**Known dev-combo gotcha:** `bhk` + `otp-hardcoded-master-key` together is a *broken* feature combo — `bhk` implies `saes-dhuk`, but the `main.rs` BHK boot wiring is gated `not(otp-hardcoded-master-key)`, so the BHK is never loaded/locked at boot while `derive_into_bhk` still routes to `cmac_bhk` → `KeySel::Bhk` → `KeyInvalid` at first use. If a future test wants "real BHK for SE050 but stable dev PBS for OPTIGA", either (a) loosen that boot-wiring guard to just `cfg(all(feature = "bhk", not(feature = "bhk-hardcoded-master-key"), not(feature = "saes-self-test")))` (the OTP-hardcoded axis and the BHK axis are orthogonal), or (b) get a fresh OPTIGA shield and use `dual-se-bhk-e2e` as-is.

(`se050_scp03_enc/mac_key` are still defined-but-never-called — the SE050 driver uses the AN12436 default SCP03 keys (`KEY_VERSION=0x0B`); wiring those through SCP03 PUT KEY is work-todo #20, separate from Phase 2C.)

#### Early-adopt: derive SE050 admin PIN from the device root (historical — now BHK, post-Phase-2C)

**Status — current state in one line:** `se050_admin_pin()` → `derive_into_bhk("pqsigner/se050-admin-pin-v1")` ⇒ `SAES-CMAC(BHK, …)` in a `bhk` build (the shipping target; falls through to `SAES-CMAC(DHUK, …)` with `saes-dhuk` alone, `HKDF(OTP-master/const, …)` legacy), never persisted to flash; the page-125 PIN slot is deleted. The DHUK→BHK move is the "Phase 2C — caller migration" section above; it's been hardware-validated (`dual-se-bhk-e2e`, 2026-05-12: `factory_reset_admin → Ok`, `store_objects OK`, `Admin factory reset complete`, full 8/8). Provisioning-order caveat: the admin UserID (and any BHK-derived secret) must be written at RDP ≥ 1 — the BHK's flash wrapping is DHUK-keyed and the DHUK changes at RDP0→RDP1. The rest of this sub-section is the historical record of the original cut-over.

**CORE PATH DONE (2026-04-23).** Both `Se050::store_objects` (provisioning) and `Se050::factory_reset_admin` (wipe) derive the 16-byte admin PIN via `hw::secret_keys::se050_admin_pin()` (HKDF-over-OTP-master at the time; now `derive_into_bhk` per the build). The flash page 125 PIN slot is no longer consulted on any production path. `dual-se-admin-wipe-e2e` PASSES all 8 steps on real silicon (commits `1bfb572` + `e6b8c2f` + `38982c7`).

**Cleanup DONE (2026-05-11).** The whole pre-v6 page-125 admin-PIN flash mechanism is removed: `hw::flash::write_admin_pin`, `read_admin_pin`, `is_admin_pin_blank`, and `ADMIN_PIN_OFFSET` are all gone (commits `482969d` + `da18f29`). The SE050 admin PIN is re-derived on demand via `secret_keys::se050_admin_pin()` — `SAES-CMAC(BHK, …)` in a `bhk` build (post-2C), `SAES-CMAC(DHUK, …)` with `saes-dhuk` alone, `HKDF(OTP-master/const, …)` on the legacy fallback — and never touches flash, so it survives reflashes and a flash mass-erase (one exception: the BHK lives in flash page 126, so an RDP regression loses it → re-pair afterward — but a lost *admin* PIN is non-fatal, not needed for recovery, and an RDP2 production unit can't be regressed). The `se050-crash-safety-e2e` test's cross-phase admin PIN is its own compile-time literal (`*b"crashsafetypin00"`), used directly in both phases (no flash round-trip). The three e2e pre-clean cascades (`dual_se.rs` `[DUAL-E2E-ADMIN]` + `[DUAL-MULTI]`, `main.rs` `[S][e2e]`) call `Se050::factory_reset_admin()` (the v6 path → arm wipe flag → admin-auth wipe → conditional page-125 erase) instead of reading a pre-v6 flash PIN; stages (b) user-PIN candidates + (c) unauthenticated sweep stay as fallbacks. Page 125 still holds the wipe-in-progress flag at `WIPE_FLAG_OFFSET=16` (a separate slot from the now-dead QW0). The 2026-04-21 footgun (page-125 erase bricked the bench SE050) is structurally impossible now — nothing reads the page-125 admin-PIN slot. Verified: `dual-se-admin-wipe-e2e` / `dual-se-multi-unlock-e2e` / default-QEMU builds clean, 105/105 host tests pass; the e2e pre-clean cascades + the BHK-rooted derivation were bench-re-validated 2026-05-12 (`dual-se-bhk-e2e`).

**The gap.** `secure/src/se050/mod.rs:35-42` already carries an aspirational docstring:

> The admin PIN itself is never persisted in plaintext anywhere — derived on demand via `crypto::derive_se050_admin_pin(&pbs)`.

But `crypto::derive_se050_admin_pin` was never written. The actual implementation at `se050/mod.rs:1196-1224` does `rng::fill(&mut admin_pin); flash::write_admin_pin(&admin_pin)` on first provision, and `flash::read_admin_pin(...)` on every wipe. The PIN exists only in flash page 125, and any action that erases page 125 without simultaneously deleting the on-chip admin UserID renders the chip permanently un-wipe-able.

This bit us on 2026-04-21: the first iteration of the OPTIGA admin-wipe e2e test erased page 125 as post-test hygiene (to clear the armed-wipe flag). The bench SE050 still had its production admin UserID but the flash now held zeros. All subsequent dual-SE admin-wipe attempts on that chip fail at `policy_roundtrip_selftest` because the newly-generated admin PIN doesn't match what the chip is holding.

**The fix.** Add `hw::secret_keys::se050_admin_pin() -> [u8; 16]`:

```rust
pub fn se050_admin_pin() -> Result<[u8; 16], OtpError> {
    let mut out = [0u8; 16];
    derive_into(b"pqsigner/se050-admin-pin-v1", &mut out)?;
    Ok(out)
}
```

Rewire `Se050::provision` to call it instead of `rng::fill` + `write_admin_pin`. Rewire `Se050::factory_reset_admin` to call it instead of `read_admin_pin`. Delete the `ADMIN_PIN_OFFSET=0` slot from page 125 entirely — the wipe-in-progress flag at `WIPE_FLAG_OFFSET=16` moves to a new home (either a dedicated 16-byte page or an OTP one-shot bit; OTP is more aligned with "this action is permanent").

**What this unlocks:**
- Bench chips that lose page 125 still boot with a reproducible admin PIN → wipe path works through cross-test contamination.
- First step of work-todo #7 Tier 1 lands ahead of the full SAES migration. When DHUK goes live, this helper flips from `HKDF(OTP_master, ...)` to `SAES-CMAC(DHUK, ...)` with no caller changes.
- Removes a production attack surface: a flash-extraction attacker no longer gets the admin PIN out of a live page 125 read.

**Dev/migration trap** (document + guard, don't silently change):
- Any chip already provisioned with a TRNG-random admin PIN (i.e., every current dev unit) has a chip-side admin UserID whose PIN doesn't match the derived value. Upgrading those chips requires admin re-provisioning under the OLD (flash-read) PIN — which works only if the flash hasn't been erased. Once this change lands, there's a one-shot "migrate existing admin PIN" step at first boot of the new firmware that reads the old PIN from page 125, deletes the on-chip admin UserID under admin auth, and rewrites with the derived PIN. If page 125 is already blank at that point, migration fails and the chip joins the "cannot auto-recover" set (this bench chip is the first member).
- Once DHUK-based (Tier 1), the derivation produces a DIFFERENT value from the HKDF-over-OTP-master version of the same label. Document the one-shot rotation as part of the DHUK migration plan; do NOT silently bump the derivation algorithm without a paired on-chip rotation.

**Files to create:** none — extend `secure/src/hw/secret_keys.rs` in place.
**Files to change:** `secure/src/hw/secret_keys.rs` (add helper), `secure/src/se050/mod.rs` (provision + factory_reset_admin), `secure/src/hw/flash.rs` (retire admin-PIN slot, keep wipe flag).

**This work-todo is explicitly pre-Tier-1:** landing it does NOT require the SAES driver. HKDF over the existing OTP master is good enough until DHUK comes online. The API will NOT change when we later move to SAES — only the internal `derive_into` implementation does.

---

#### Tier 3 — OTP randomness (software-readable, per-device salt)

**What it is today.** `hw::otp::read_device_master` returns 32 TRNG bytes burnt at first-boot into `0x0BFA_0080..0x0BFA_00A0`. Used via `hw::secret_keys` for all SE pairing derivations.

**What Trezor does with OTP.** 256 TRNG bytes at factory into `FLASH_OTP_BLOCK_RANDOMNESS` (`otp_layout.h:4-12`), consumed as `hardware_salt = SHA256(OTP_randomness)` in the PBKDF2 PIN-stretching chain (`storage.c:76,978` `derive_kek_v4`). Independent of DHUK/BHK.

**Direction after DHUK lands.** Repurpose OTP bytes 128..160 solely as PBKDF2 salt input for any future PIN-gated MCU-side storage — not as a direct derivation root. All SE pairing secrets move up to tiers 1+2. Specifically:
- [ ] Expose `hw::otp::read_otp_randomness() -> [u8; 32]` (public read; bytes are non-secret — they're salt, not key).
- [ ] When a future PIN-gated MCU storage wrap lands (e.g. for at-rest cached companion objects), its KDF is `PBKDF2(user_pin, SHA256(otp_randomness) || storage_salt, 20_000)` matching Trezor's `derive_kek_v4`.

#### Ordering / dependencies

1. `otp-hardcoded-master-key` stays as the dev-bench safety valve throughout all three tier migrations. Never ship. Guarded by compile_error gate already in `nsc/mod.rs`.
2. #24 (OPTIGA pairing restructure) landed the OTP tier + `hw::secret_keys` API + re-rooted `hw::huk::derive_device_key` off `firmware_hash`. **Done.**
3. Work-todo #20 (production key rotation) still assumes the HKDF-over-OTP path for SE050 SCP03 + TROPIC01 migration. When Tier 1 lands, #20 pivots to calling the same `secret_keys` API (now SAES-CMAC-backed) — no change in #20's scope, only in the underlying implementation.
4. Tier 2 (BHK) after Tier 1, because BHK-at-rest is DHUK-wrapped.
5. After Tier 1+2, `hw::huk::derive_device_key` becomes the last software-readable root and should be deleted — see header comment in that file which already flags this retirement.

#### Files

**Create:** `secure/src/hw/saes.rs`, `secure/src/hw/bhk.rs`.

**Change:** `secure/src/hw/secret_keys.rs` (swap HKDF → SAES-CMAC under a compile-time selector), `secure/src/hw/otp.rs` (narrow the master-key region to salt duty, or deprecate entirely once DHUK lands), `secure/src/hw/huk.rs` (delete after tier 2), `secure/src/hw/flash.rs` (add BHK page region), `secure/Cargo.toml` (new dev-gate feature for hardcoded BHK under `otp-hardcoded-master-key`).

#### Wipe-flow impact (cross-ref the dual-SE admin wipe)

None visible to SE-side wipe code. The SE drivers continue to call `secret_keys::optiga_pairing_secret()` / `secret_keys::se050_scp03_enc_key()` etc. What changes underneath: Tier 1 migration makes those bytes computable only via SAES on-chip. The wipe sequence (OPTIGA `Conf(E140)` DATA overwrites + SE050 admin-UserID DELETE) is unchanged. One new cleanup question: if the BHK page becomes corrupted (brown-out mid-wipe, bit-flip), what's recovery? Options: (a) treat as permanent brick (Trezor's answer), (b) fall through to DHUK-only with every-SE-pairing re-derivation + forced re-provisioning of both SEs. Decide before shipping.

## MEDIUM -- Defense-in-depth / PQ hardening

### 9. ML-KEM-1024 Inner Wrap

**Status:** NOT STARTED (no ML-KEM crate)

Adds post-quantum confidentiality on top of the classical encrypted channels (Noise_KK1, SCP03). Even if a CRQC breaks X25519 or AES-128, the entropy halves remain protected.

- [ ] Decapsulate after reading from SE during unlock

### 10. Multi-Source RNG (3-way XOR) — **DONE for the signing path; remaining call-sites to migrate are non-secret**

**Status:** PHASE 1 LANDED (signing OptRand). Phase 2 (migrate other RNG call-sites — consumption-mask jitter, wait_random salt, dual_se entropy split) is opt-in defence-in-depth, not security-critical.

Mirrors Trezor's `core/embed/sec/rng/rng_strong.c`: `secure/src/rng_strong.rs::fill` XOR-folds the platform TRNG (STM32 hardware RNG on real silicon; semihosting `/dev/urandom` on QEMU) with each available SE backend's `random()` method. For `dual-se` builds `DualSecureElement::random` internally XORs OPTIGA + SE050 contributions first, so the final fold is `STM32 ⊕ OPTIGA ⊕ SE050`. Defends against any single broken / biased source.

The signing path consumes it via `crypto::c10_sign_verified_with_progress`: a fresh 16-byte `opt_rand` is drawn per call, fed to BOTH double-compute signs (re-drawing per sign would break the F-13 byte-equality FI gate), and zeroized on exit.

**What's needed for Phase 1:**
- [x] SE050 TRNG APDU wrapper (`secure/src/se050/apdu.rs::get_random`, AN12413 §5.13.1 with `P2_RANDOM = 0x49`)
- [x] OPTIGA `OptigaTrustM::random` high-level method (wraps existing shielded `apdu::get_random`)
- [x] Trait method `WalletStore::random` (default `Err(SlotNotFound)`) implemented on every real backend
- [x] `DualSecureElement::random` (XOR-fold OPTIGA + SE050)
- [x] `secure/src/rng_strong.rs::fill` (Trezor-pattern XOR-mix with fail-closed non-zero gate)
- [x] Wired into `c10_sign_verified_with_progress` for SPHINCS+ OptRand

**Phase 2 — DONE (commit follows).** Surveyed every `crate::rng::*` call-site and migrated the security-critical ones to `rng_strong::fill` (3-source XOR). Documentation of which sites moved and which didn't:

  * **Migrated:**
    - `secure/src/main.rs` first-boot wizard's master-entropy generation — the single most critical RNG output in the firmware. Predictable here = universal forgery.
    - `secure/src/hw/otp.rs::burn_device_master` — irreversibly burned to OTP; no second chance. `rng_strong` gracefully falls through to platform-TRNG-only at first boot if SE channels aren't up yet (strict no-regression).
    - `secure/src/dual_se.rs::provision` half_O — **open-coded** (not via `rng_strong::fill`) to avoid re-entrancy: the function is `&mut self` on the global SE while `rng_strong::fill → se_random` would re-borrow the same SE. Resolved by directly accessing `self.optiga.random()` + `self.se050.random()` and XOR-folding manually. Same 3-source defense, no UB.
    - `secure/src/hw/consumption_mask.rs::seed_prng_from_rng` — EM-mask seed; predictable seed → predictable mask → easier SCA. Migration is once-per-boot, no hot-path impact.
  * **NOT migrated (deliberate, documented in code):**
    - `secure/src/fi.rs::rng_byte` (the `wait_random` salt): called thousands of times per sign. SE round-trips per call would inflate sign latency from ~1.5 s to multi-minute. The loop-count only sets a delay duration — a biased platform TRNG weakens timing-channel defense at the margins but leaks no secret. Cost/benefit clearly favours platform-only.
    - `secure/src/ui/seed_wizard.rs::pick_three_distinct`: random word indices for backup-confirmation display. Not security-bearing.
    - `secure/src/tropic01_se.rs::*`: Tropic01 not on the shipping target ([[tropic01-excluded]] memory).

Migration validated: QEMU `make e2e` 18/18 (exercises wizard entropy + dual_se provision + sign path all routed through `rng_strong`).

**Files changed in Phase 1:** `secure/src/rng_strong.rs` (new), `secure/src/se050/apdu.rs`, `secure/src/se050/mod.rs`, `secure/src/optiga/mod.rs`, `secure/src/dual_se.rs`, `secure/src/secure_element.rs`, `secure/src/main.rs` (added `se_random` accessor + `mod rng_strong`), `secure/src/crypto.rs`.

### 11. SCP03 Key Rotation for SE050 (wire derived-root + GP PUT KEY ceremony)

**Status:** NOT IMPLEMENTED — dormant derivation infrastructure landed in #24, feature flag + ceremony still owed. Wire-up is the immediate parallel to OPTIGA's OTP-derived PBS.

Today `secure/src/se050/scp03.rs:21-30` hardcodes the NXP AN12436 Rev 2.4 defaults for SE050E OEF `0xA921` (`PLATFORM_ENC` + `PLATFORM_MAC` 16-byte constants, `KEY_VERSION = 0x0B`). Every device built from the same firmware presents identical static keys → single-firmware-extraction breaks the entire fleet's SE050 channel.

**The deterministic-root infrastructure already exists** (landed in #24, currently dormant):
```rust
// secure/src/hw/secret_keys.rs:126-137
pub fn se050_scp03_enc_key() -> Result<[u8; 16], OtpError> { ... }
pub fn se050_scp03_mac_key() -> Result<[u8; 16], OtpError> { ... }
```
Both currently `HKDF-Expand(OTP_master, "pqsigner/se050-scp03-{enc,mac}-v1")`. Under #7 Tier 1 migration the internal primitive becomes `SAES-CMAC(DHUK, label)`; under Tier 2 it shifts to `SAES-CMAC(BHK, label)` for the per-SE selector split. Callers never change.

#### The problem with a naive swap

`PLATFORM_ENC`/`PLATFORM_MAC` are what the firmware uses; the *chip* has its own copy of the same bytes at keyset `0x0B` (factory-provisioned by NXP). They have to match byte-for-byte for `INITIALIZE UPDATE` to produce a matching CardCryptogram. So:

- Changing just firmware to use derived keys → SCP03 establishment fails against a factory-default chip (key mismatch at the MAC step).
- We must do a GP `PUT KEY` ceremony against the chip THAT MATCHES the firmware's new derivation, and then commit the firmware to use the new keyset version.

This is a two-build, one-chip-operation flow.

#### Three-stage landing

**Decisions taken (2026-05-12) — were "open" in the earlier draft of this section:**
- **Root = BHK** (not DHUK, not DHUK⊕BHK). `se050_scp03_{enc,mac,dek}_key` route through `derive_into_bhk` ⇒ `SAES-CMAC(BHK, "se050-scp03-{enc,mac,dek}-v1")` in a `bhk`-on build; falls through to `derive_into` (DHUK / OTP per build) when `bhk` is off. **No call-site flip needed** — `se050_scp03_enc/mac_key` are already on `derive_into_bhk` (the Phase-2C flip); just add `se050_scp03_dek_key()` there too. Rationale: the only argument for pure-DHUK was "BHK is lost on RDP regression → unrecoverable channel", but production units ship at RDP2 (no regression path) so that brick mode is structurally impossible, while the SE050's SCP03 keyset `0x0B` is *replaceable* (you can PUT KEY it again) — so BHK is the safe choice *and* keeps the Tier-2 isolation (a silicon-DHUK extraction doesn't reach `half_E`) for free. The OPTIGA PBS stays on **DHUK** for the inverse reason — its E140 is bumped to `LcsO=Operational` (immutable), so it needs the maximally-stable root. (`derive_into_xork` / `KeySel::DhukXorBhk` not needed anywhere — see `docs/architecture/trezor-comparison.md §6.5`.)
- **`KEY_VERSION` stays `0x0B`** — the PUT KEY ceremony *replaces keyset `0x0B` in place* (data-field KVN = `0x0B`), it does NOT add a new `0x11`. Adding `0x11` would leave the published `0x0B` keys live and authenticatable → the rotation buys nothing; and there's no command to delete a keyset → replace-in-place is the only real option.
- **Detection = probe-on-boot, no persistent flag.** `establish()` tries the derived keys at `KVN=0x0B`; on a card-cryptogram mismatch / `0x6A88` it retries with the hardcoded constants. One firmware handles both rotated and factory-default chips; one extra failed `INITIALIZE UPDATE` per boot on un-rotated chips (sub-ms; zero on rotated ones); no flash state, so no page-125-colocation hazard.
- **Provisioning-order constraint** (because the root is BHK): the BHK first-write AND the PUT KEY ceremony MUST run *after* the unit is at its final per-die-DHUK RDP level (RDP ≥ 1) — the BHK's flash wrapping is DHUK-keyed and the DHUK changes at RDP0→RDP1, so provisioning anything BHK-derived before that step bricks the SE050. Factory sequence: step RDP→1 → provision (BHK first-write) → OPTIGA provision → SE050 provision → SCP03 PUT KEY → … → burn RDP2. (Already the case for the Phase-2C admin PIN; the SCP03 rotation inherits it.) See `docs/production-todo.md` §"SE050 — SCP03 + ADMIN provisioning".

**Stage A — derivation plumbing (REVERSIBLE — code + Cargo features only, no chip writes). LANDED 2026-05-12.**
- [x] `secure/src/hw/secret_keys.rs`: `se050_scp03_dek_key()` via `derive_into_bhk(b"pqsigner/se050-scp03-dek-v1", …)` (parallel to `_enc`/`_mac`).
- [x] `secure/src/se050/scp03.rs`: `PLATFORM_DEK` const (`67 02 DA C3 09 42 B2 C8 5E 7F 47 B4 2C ED 4E 7F`, OEF `0xA921`) + `load_platform_keys() -> Result<([u8;16],[u8;16],[u8;16]), Se050Error>` — default returns the published triple, the derived triple under `se050-derived-scp03`.
- [x] `secure/src/se050/scp03.rs`: `establish()` refactored to `establish() → establish_with_keys(enc, mac)`; `establish()` probes `load_platform_keys()`'s preferred set, on `Scp03`/`Status(_)` retries once with `PLATFORM_*` (only in a `se050-derived-scp03` build). `KEY_VERSION` unchanged at `0x0B`.
- [x] `secure/Cargo.toml`: `se050-derived-scp03 = ["se050"]` (production-allowed; **not** in the fence).
- [~] Host tests for `scp03_kcv` / `load_platform_keys` round-trip / `build_put_key_apdu` layout are *written* (`#[cfg(test)] mod tests` in `scp03.rs`) but **don't run under `cargo test -p sphincs-tz-secure`** — `mod se050;` is `#[cfg(all(feature="se050", not(test)))]`-gated in `main.rs`, so the SE050 module (and `scp03.rs`) is excluded from host test builds by existing design. **TODO:** sort a host-test config for the SE050 module, or relocate the pure-logic SCP03 helpers (`aes128_ecb_encrypt`, `cmac_aes128`, `build_put_key_apdu`, `scp03_kcv`) to a host-testable spot. The build itself is verified across `dual-se,…,se050-derived-scp03`, `se050-rotate-scp03,bhk,…`, and plain `dual-se` (all clean); 105/105 of the existing host tests still pass.

**Stage B — PUT KEY ceremony: code REVERSIBLE (LANDED 2026-05-12), *running it* IRREVERSIBLE (deferred):**
- [x] *(reversible — code)* `secure/src/se050/scp03.rs`: `build_put_key_apdu(new_enc, new_mac, new_dek) -> ([u8;72], usize)` — pure builder. **Replaces keyset `0x0B` in place** (data-field KVN = `0x0B`). Layout `CLA=0x80 INS=0xD8 P1=0x0B P2=0x81 Lc=67`, data `[0x0B] ([0x88][0x10][AES-ECB-Enc(PLATFORM_DEK, key)][0x03][scp03_kcv(key)]) × 3` (ENC/MAC/DEK). `scp03_kcv(key) = AES-ECB-Enc(key, {0x01}×16)[..3]`. **The `P2` encoding / encrypted-key-data length byte / KCV filler / DEK-encryption mode are best-effort from GP 2.3 / AN12436** — the doc-comment on `build_put_key_apdu` enumerates exactly what to confirm on a sacrificial-part rehearsal (the chip recomputes everything and rejects on mismatch, so a rehearsal returning `SW=0x9000` is the proof).
- [x] *(reversible — code)* `secure/src/se050/mod.rs`: `Se050::rotate_scp03_keys()` (`#[cfg(feature="se050-rotate-scp03")]`) — `init()` (establishes SCP03, falling back to the factory keys on a fresh chip) → `load_platform_keys()` (derived triple) → refuse if it equals the published constants (`scp03::keys_are_factory_default`) → `build_put_key_apdu` → `apdu::send_apdu` (wrapped in the running session) → check `SW=0x9000`.
- [x] *(reversible — code)* `secure/Cargo.toml`: `se050-rotate-scp03 = ["se050-derived-scp03"]`; added to the `compile_error!` fence in `nsc/mod.rs`. `secure/src/main.rs`: one-shot `#[cfg(feature="se050-rotate-scp03")]` dispatcher (pattern of the `se050-admin-wipe-e2e` block — log + `loop { wfi() }`). `Makefile`: `flash-hw-se050-rotate-scp03` (`--features se050-rotate-scp03,bhk,stm32u585,ui-oled,debug-log,e2e-test`) with a loud sacrificial-chip warning + the dual-se-e2e flash/OB shape.

From then on that chip only opens with firmware that re-derives the matching keys (probe-on-boot makes that "any `se050-derived-scp03` build whose BHK reproduces"). **Production-provisioning-only** — never run on a board that still moves RDP around (the RDP1↔RDP0 dance mass-erases the BHK page → dead SE050). Even *bench-testing* the ceremony carries the brick risk (PUT KEY at RDP0 to BHK-derived-at-RDP0, then a regression → dead) — so the live ceremony is validated on sacrificial parts as part of building the real production-provisioning flow, not on the working bench SE050. See `docs/production-todo.md` §"SE050 — SCP03 + ADMIN provisioning" for the full irreversible item + pre-commit checklist.

Defeats "clone a device's OTP master to a different SE050" — the derivation is only valid for the specific SE050 UID it was rotated against.

### 12. Signature parameter security level — DECISION RECORDED (closed 2026-05-20)

**Status:** CLOSED. The original "SLH-DSA-SHA2-128f → 192f migration" framing was stale — it predated the all-C10 cutover and referenced a codebase that no longer exists (the generic `slh-dsa` crate with `Sha2_128f`/`Sha2_192f` type parameters, and the 17,088 / 35,664-byte standard-param signature sizes). Verified 2026-05-20: there is no `slh-dsa` crate or `Sha2_*f` type parameter anywhere in the tree; the production signer is the custom `sphincs-c10` crate (`SIGNATURE_LEN = 4008`, `N = 16` ⇒ **128-bit security**, NIST Category 1).

**Decision: C10 / 128-bit is the production parameter set.** It was deliberately tuned for **on-chain verifier feasibility** — the 4,008-byte signature is what keeps the Yul `SPHINCsC10Asm` verifier's per-`validateUserOp` gas viable; the SHA-256-precompile-based verify cost scales with signature size. Doubling to a 192-bit (Category 3) variant would roughly double signature size → ~2× on-chain gas + ~2× signing time + a new on-chain verifier + re-based CREATE2 addresses (every key changes under a new parameter set, so it would also break launch invariant #6). For a hardware wallet, 128-bit SLH-DSA is the standard, defensible choice: hash-based 128-bit security has no quantum lever beyond Grover, which the construction is explicitly designed against.

**If a Category-3 (192-bit) requirement ever materializes**, the cost is understood and bounded: a `sphincs-c-192`-style variant (N=24), a matching Yul verifier, ~2× sig/gas/sign-time, and — because it re-bases addresses — it must land at a launch/KDF-generation boundary, never as a retrofit. No such requirement exists today; recorded here so a future "should we go 192-bit?" question starts from this analysis instead of the stale migration checklist.

Also fixed during closure: the stale `proto/src/lib.rs::CMD_SIGN_BOOTSTRAP` docstring (a reserved, non-dispatched legacy command) that still claimed a "17,088-byte SLH-DSA signature" — corrected to the C10 4,008-byte reality with a historical note.

## LOW -- Production infrastructure

### 12b. NSC `register_heartbeat` uses a divergent, weaker NS-pointer validator (TZ4)

**Status:** OPEN (LOW). Surfaced 2026-07-02 by the TrustZone adversarial-review-playbook survey (`docs/security/adversarial-review/trustzone-gateway-adversarial-review.md` TZ4).

`nsc_register_heartbeat(addr)` (`secure/src/nsc/mod.rs:1376` → `hw::iwdg::register_ns_heartbeat`, `iwdg.rs:176`) range-checks the NS-supplied heartbeat address with its **own inline** predicate (`addr & 0x3 != 0 || addr < NS_SRAM_BASE || addr.saturating_add(4) > NS_SRAM_END`) instead of routing through the central `validate_ns_read_ptr`. Relative to the hardened validator it is missing: (a) the shared-mailbox-disjoint check, (b) the hardware `TT`/SAU re-classification, and (c) the FI-doubling through `check_true_into_sentinel`. The stored address is later `read_volatile`'d every SysTick (`iwdg.rs:209`).

**Impact is bounded** — it is a 4-byte secure→NS *read* of a liveness counter that stays within NS SRAM (the mailbox is inside NS SRAM, so even an aliased address reads NS memory, no secret egress); worst case the stall-detect logic reads attacker-chosen bytes. But it is the **one gateway entry point that bypasses the central FI-doubled validator**, so it is a hardening/consistency wart worth closing.

- [x] **DONE 2026-07-02.** `nsc_register_heartbeat` now validates the NS-supplied address through `ns_ptr::NsPtr::<u8>::new(addr).validate_read(4)` — the same FI-doubled typestate every other veneer uses (adds mailbox-disjoint + hardware `TT`/SAU reclassification + double-checked sentinel); `iwdg`'s inline alignment+window check stays as defense-in-depth. rustc type-checked the veneer clean under the `stm32u585,iwdg` cfg (`secure/src/nsc/mod.rs:1376`).

### 12c. Trusted-path + surface findings from the adversarial-review-playbook survey (2026-07-02)

Surfaced by the second adversarial-review-playbook batch (`docs/security/adversarial-review/{trusted-ui,firmware-update-secure-boot,onchain-contracts}-adversarial-review.md`). All are claim-vs-code / hardening residuals, not live fund-theft; ranked by severity.

- [x] **[MED] Confirm result is not FI-hardened (trusted-UI UI1). DONE 2026-07-02 (fault-sweep follow-up open).** All 9 sign-path `confirm()` call sites (`cmd_sign_userop.rs:1121,1187`, `cmd_sign_userop_batch.rs:630,873,928`, `cmd_sign_offchain.rs:630,772`, `cmd_offchain_sync.rs:145`, `fw_update/mod.rs:119` — the last via `matches!`) accept on a plain `match`/`matches!` of `ConfirmResult` with no voted read / redundant check / `fi` barrier — a single instruction-skip on the `Cancelled`/`IdleWipe` arm's `return` can fall through into signing. This is the one link in an otherwise heavily-FI-hardened sign chain (double-compute, CFI counter, sentinel gates) that is *not* fault-hardened, and there is **no `fault_sweep_confirm`**. FIXED: `confirm()` now delegates to `confirm_checked() -> (ConfirmResult, sentinel)` (`secure/src/ui/confirm.rs`), which **borns the sign-gate sentinel at the affirmative accept branch from `seen_last`** (every non-Confirmed path yields `FAIL_SENTINEL`); all 9 sign sites gate on `verdict == OK_SENTINEL` and fail closed (zeroize + reject) — so a skipped reject-arm return AND a value-fault flipping the verdict are each caught (two consistent faults now required). Source-text pins in `ui_under_test`/`fw_update_boot`/`nsc_batch_offchain` updated to enforce the hardened form; full secure host suite 2078/0. **Remaining follow-up (LOW):** add `tools/sca/fault_sweep_confirm.py` (rainbow single-skip sweep of the accept gate) — deferred, needs a working bin build for the target ELF.
- [x] **[MED] `ui-noop` has no `mode-production` fence (trusted-UI UI2). DONE 2026-07-02.** The leaky-feature denylist (`nsc/mod.rs:114-133`) fences `ui-semihosting`/`ui-mirror`/`ui-capture` but **not `ui-noop`**. A hand-composed `mode-production,ui-noop,usb` set compiles; today it only *hangs* (`noop::wait_button` returns `(Right,Short)` → infinite loop under the scroll-gate `confirm()`), masked because every `ui-noop` Makefile target also carries `e2e-test`. Hazard: if anyone "fixes the hang" by returning `(Right,Long)`, every sign auto-confirms with zero physical consent. FIXED: added a `#[cfg(all(feature="mode-production", feature="ui-noop"))] compile_error!` guard in `nsc/mod.rs` (mirrors the 5 existing mode-production guards). Verified it FIRES on `mode-production,ui-noop` and no existing target combines them (all `ui-noop` targets carry `e2e-test`/`mock-se`).
- [~] **[LOW/doc] Claim 8 advertises an OPTIGA E1E0 anti-rollback counter cross-check that `cmd_fw_commit.rs` does not implement (fw-update FW10).** `threat-model.md:167` said a downgrade is rejected by "OPTIGA monotonic counter (E1E0, Conf(0xE140))" cross-checked at COMMIT, but the COMMIT handler has no OPTIGA counter read — anti-rollback rests **entirely on STM32 OTP** (which is sound). Not a live vuln. **DOC HALF DONE 2026-07-02**: added a dated CORRECTION note under Claim 8 in `threat-model.md` documenting that anti-rollback is STM32-OTP-only. **Remaining (user's call):** decide whether to *implement* the second OPTIGA-counter layer for defense-in-depth (would add an E1E0 monotonic-counter read/bump to `cmd_fw_commit.rs`) or leave it OTP-only and close.
- [x] **[LOW/confirm-intended] Cross-slot `removeOwnerAtIndex` not bound to the signing slot (on-chain SOL6). DONE 2026-07-02 (documented + pinned; tighten-decision remains user's call).** The H-3 calldata-parity check is deliberately skipped for the remove selector (`PQSmartWallet.sol:469-474`), so any slot key at index *i*≥1 can `removeOwnerAtIndex(j, owner_j)` for any *j*≥1 (`ownerAtIndex(j)` is a public getter). Impact is intra-wallet **availability**, not theft (bootstrap index 0 is unremovable and can re-add). `theft_free` is silent (no funds move) and no forge/Kontrol/Halmos test exercises cross-slot *authorization*. The code comment indicates it is intended. DONE: added a descriptive design note to `threat-model.md` §8 (availability-not-theft; bootstrap index-0 unremovable so recovery holds; framed as an **open** owner-management design decision, not ratified) + `test_sol6_crossSlotRemoveIsAcceptedByDesign` forge test that PINS current behavior (slot-1 signer removes slot-2; `vd==0`) so binding `i==j` later is a deliberate flip — passes (2/2 with the H-3 reject test). Whether to tighten to "a slot may only remove itself" is the remaining **user design decision**; it would require a wallet codehash re-freeze.

### 12d. Enroll the silicon-lockdown enforcement in the `verify-gate-enforcement` meta-gate (SL3-meta)

**Status:** OPEN (LOW). Surfaced 2026-07-02 by the silicon-lockdown adversarial-review-playbook survey (`docs/security/adversarial-review/silicon-lockdown-adversarial-review.md` SL3). The production-hardening enforcement — the `nsc/mod.rs` `compile_error!` fence wall + `make prod-check-ship` (a blocking CI step, `ci.yml:106-126`) — is the lockdown's build-time gate. Unlike the FV gates, it is **not registered in `scripts/gate_enforcement.json`**, so the `verify-gate-enforcement` meta-gate (which exists precisely to catch a gate that silently stops firing — e.g. `prod-check-ship` losing its `run:` step or being made `continue-on-error`) does not police it. Not a live vuln (the gate fires today), but a G1-class consistency gap.

- [x] **DONE 2026-07-02.** Added a `prod-check-ship` entry to `scripts/gate_enforcement.json` (`polices_paths`: `secure/src/nsc/mod.rs` + `secure/Cargo.toml` + `Makefile`; `runs_in: ci.yml`; `enforcement: per_pr_blocking`). `check_gate_enforcement.py` now validates it (18/18 gates green + self-test negative controls pass) — so the fence wall + `prod-check-ship` can no longer silently lose their `run:` step or gain `continue-on-error` without the meta-gate failing.

### 12e. Silicon-lockdown class-(c) hardening — RDP-verify-in-boot DONE; S-3 soft-counter fence REFINED (not a quick fence)

Implemented 2026-07-02 from the silicon-lockdown playbook's class-(c) (documented-but-unenforced) targets. The irreversible (b) burns (RDP2/LcsO/WRP1A/OTP/PUT-KEY) remain deferred-by-design factory ceremony.

- [x] **RDP-verify-in-boot (SL7 / HARDENING §4.2) — DONE.** Added `hw::flash::{rdp_level_from_byte (pure), rdp_level (MMIO read of FLASH_OPTR.RDP)}` + a `#[cfg(feature="mode-production")]` boot check in `main.rs` (right after clock init) that **WARN-and-continues** if RDP != Level 2 (a hard halt would brick a device during factory rehearsal before the RDP2 burn; RDP2 already disables SWD/JTAG in silicon, so this is belt-and-braces). Opt-in `rdp-enforce-halt` feature hard-refuses instead. Host test `rdp_byte_decode_matches_rm0456_and_only_cc_is_level2` pins the SL1 property (only `0xCC` decodes as RDP2). Validated: normal + `mode-production`+`rdp-enforce-halt` thumbv8m builds compile; secure host suite 2089/0.
- [ ] **S-3 soft-counter fence — REFINED: it is NOT a quick `compile_error!` fence.** production-todo.md:188 prescribes "make `build_metadata_counter` a compile_error under mode-production, then write F1E1 as frozen-junk or skip it." **BUT** tracing F1E1 (`OID_COUNTER`) revealed it is deeply integrated: read at 5+ sites (`read_counter_raw`, the reconcile `pin_attempt_count`), **written by `factory_reset_body`** (which the metadata's `Change=Conf(E140)` exists to permit), and LcsO-ratcheted (`verify_and_lock`). The driver already cfg-splits enforcement (`#[cfg(optiga-hw-counter)]`→E120, `#[cfg(not)]`→F1E1), so in a production (hw-counter) build the soft counter is NOT the lockout — the residual is marginal. A safe fix must gate the WHOLE F1E1 soft-counter subsystem (provision + reads + the factory_reset sentinel write) out under `mode-production`, replaced by E120 — a provisioning change touching invariant #2 that **needs on-silicon `optiga-hw-counter-e2e` validation**, not a one-line fence. Deferred to a hardware-validated pass; do NOT ram in a partial gate (would break OPTIGA provisioning/factory-reset on real silicon).

Surfaced by evaluating the [Strix](https://github.com/usestrix/strix) AI-pentest tool for portable *testing* patterns (verified by a 9-agent sweep + adversarial refutation of every "we already have this" claim). **Headline:** Strix is web-app-focused; ~90 % of its *transferable* architecture is already here (the CS/TZ/SE/FI adversarial-review playbook family, the mandatory `honest_residual` — which Strix has no equivalent of, backend-agnostic swarm, PoC-or-downgrade discipline, per-transition + single-fault replay/rollback tests, and the `CMD_OFFCHAIN_SYNC` page-123 exhaustion VULN is *itself* a textbook Strix business-logic/limit-slicing find). Its web vuln skills (SQLi/XSS/SSRF/…), offensive toolchain (Caido/sqlmap/ffuf/nuclei/browser), CVSS/CWE customer-report machinery, Docker sandbox, and the refusal-avoidance system prompt are **not applicable** and were discarded. **Guardrail:** do NOT port Strix's auto-*fixing* agent — an agent editing FI-hardened signing / CT compares / TrustZone veneers can silently weaken exactly what CLAUDE.md forbids weakening; keep fix *generation* human. (Was §12e in the 2026-07-02 draft; renumbered 12f after a concurrent commit claimed 12e for silicon-lockdown.)

### 13. OEMiROT Secure Boot (ML-DSA-65)

**Status:** SUPERSEDED (closed 2026-05-28). We do NOT use ST's OEMiROT. We built our own immutable FSBL (`fsbl/`, WRP1A-locked pages 0-3) that verifies each A/B slot with the vendor **SPHINCS+C10** key + renders the measured-boot fingerprint before branching (see §16, commit `7d0ec47`). That covers the secure-boot role OEMiROT would have filled, on our single PQ primitive (invariant #5) rather than ML-DSA-65. Re-open only if a concrete need for ST's OEMiROT toolchain integration appears.

Custom bootloader that verifies S-world and NS-world firmware images with ML-DSA-65 + Ed25519 hybrid before any code runs.

### 16. Immutable Bootloader (Defense-in-Depth)

**Status:** DONE (label corrected 2026-05-28). This is exactly what the FSBL is.

Split the firmware measurement code into a separate immutable bootloader in WRP-locked flash pages. Protects against a compromised update that replaces both the firmware and the measurement code simultaneously. **Realised by the FSBL** (`fsbl/`, pages 0-3, WRP1A-locked): it independently SHA-256-verifies each A/B slot against the SPHINCS+C10-signed manifest and renders the 8-word measured-boot fingerprint *before* branching into the slot, so a malicious update can't forge the measurement (commit `7d0ec47`, the manufacturer-trust-elimination work; see `docs/security/measured-boot.md`).

**What's needed:**
- [ ] Separate bootloader binary with its own linker script (ORIGIN = 0x0C000000, ~16-32 KB)
- [ ] Minimal OLED driver, SHA-256, BIP-39 wordlist, I2C init, GPIO buttons in bootloader
- [ ] Main firmware linker script updated: ORIGIN starts after bootloader region
- [ ] WRP option bytes to permanently protect bootloader flash pages
- [ ] Bootloader: hash firmware region, display 8 words, verify signature, jump to main firmware
- [ ] HDP activation: bootloader becomes execute-only after measurement (optional, for attestation key protection)

**Files to create:** `bootloader/` (new binary crate), `bootloader/memory.x`, `bootloader/src/main.rs`
**Files to change:** `secure/memory-stm32u585.x` (adjust ORIGIN), `Makefile` (bootloader build target)

### 18. SLH-DSA side-channel + fault-injection hardening

**Status:** NOT STARTED (research complete — see `docs/security/production-security.md`)

> See also #23 for PIN counter hardening gap surfaced by Bundle F: OPTIGA OID 0xF1D5 is firmware-managed decrement-before-auth (glitch during decrement can revert the counter); TROPIC01 uses physically irreversible fused slots. Either migrate to OPTIGA monotonic counter objects or add a secure-flash-anchored shadow counter as an anti-rollback check.

Research-derived mitigations from the deep-research round of 2026-04-14. Critical finding: **both verify-after-sign is inadequate** (RFC 9814 / Genêt TCHES 2023) and **OptRand = 0 (deterministic signing) enables PRF recovery**. These are not theoretical.

**STM32U5 is confirmed-vulnerable to voltage glitching**, not merely presumed. The Masaryk U Simonik thesis (verified real in the 2026-04-15 round) demonstrates ~76% PIN-glitch bypass on STM32U5 silicon (same family as our STM32U585). Ledger Donjon's March 2025 statement that no public attack existed was correct at publication but invalidated within months. Plan the FihInt / fail-in / double-compute items in this backlog as **must-ship**, not optional.

**SHAKE-vs-SHA2 decision — CLOSED 2026-05-20: STAY ON SHA-2, SHAKE REJECTED.** Three independent reasons, all confirmed this round: **(1) Masking is infeasible on either hash.** The on-silicon masked-SHA2 benchmark put masking the hot path at 278–2800× the HASH peripheral, and the 70,754 secret-touching PRF calls/sign make masking even just the secret subset ≈ 60 s/sign — 1–2 orders past the 5–10× viability line. **(2) SHAKE has NO hardware acceleration on STM32U5.** The HASH peripheral accelerates SHA-1/SHA-224/SHA-256 only — NOT SHA-3/Keccak/SHAKE (RM0456). So SHAKE would run entirely in software, forfeiting the measured 485-cyc/block HW path (vs 4265-cyc software SHA-2, 8.8×); even *unmasked* SHAKE is a net slowdown, and masked-SHAKE — though Keccak's single χ nonlinear step masks cheaper per-permutation than SHA-2's adders — is still ~70k software permutations/sign → also infeasible. **(3) The only cheap-SHAKE argument is unsubstantiated.** Fluhrer ePrint 2024/500's "1.7× backward-compatible PRF-tree" is not verifiable and technically implausible per production-security.md §3. **(4) Switching cost is launch-breaking:** a parameter-set change re-derives every key, moving the CREATE2 address (frozen-wallet invariant #6). Net: SHAKE offers no *realizable* SCA win on this MCU at a launch-breaking cost. The SCA posture stays on SHA-2 + the landed structural/FI mitigations (F-13 double-compute, F-16 WOTS/FORS shuffle, F-17 rate-limit, consumption-mask, multi-source OptRand); the **v2 PRF restructure** (pad sk_seed to a full 64-byte first block → constant cacheable midstate) remains the SHA-2-preserving KDF-revision escape hatch IF step-(b) leakage measurement ever shows an exploitable leak.

- [x] **SHAKE-vs-SHA2 architectural decision** — **DECIDED: STAY ON SHA-2** (closed 2026-05-20, see Completion Log; user-confirmed three times). Masked-SHA2 and SHAKE are both too expensive for the STM32U5: masked-SHA2 = 278–2800× the HASH peripheral (~60 s/sign even masking only the secret subset), the U5 HASH peripheral accelerates SHA-1/SHA-2 only (NOT SHA-3/Keccak/SHAKE per RM0456) so SHAKE forfeits the HW path AND a parameter switch would re-derive every key + move the CREATE2 address (breaks frozen-wallet invariant #6). **Do not re-open.** SCA posture stays SHA-2 + landed F-13/F-16/F-17 + consumption-mask + multi-source OptRand.
  - [x] **#2 measurement (masked-SHA2 cost) — DONE, run on silicon 2026-05-20.** New `masked-sha2` workspace crate: first-order (2-share) boolean-masked building blocks — ISW masked AND (`sec_and`, Ishai-Sahai-Wagner CRYPTO 2003) + Kogge-Stone secure boolean adder (`sec_add`, Coron-Großschädl-Vadnala family CHES 2014), generic over an RNG closure. Per the advisor-reviewed plan we do NOT build a full masked SHA-256 (correctness-risk + code volume); instead we measure the two gates that dominate the cost and PROJECT the per-block number from SHA-256's fixed structure (`ADDS_PER_BLOCK = 600`, `ANDS_PER_BLOCK = 320`). 7 host correctness oracles (sec_and == plain AND over 50k random; sec_add == wrapping_add over 50k random + full-ripple edge cases; Ch/Maj composition over 20k) all pass → Kogge-Stone indexing proven correct. Bench firmware `secure/src/bench_masked_sha.rs` (`bench-masked-sha` feature), run-and-halt at boot, DWT-times one-compression (55-byte single-block input) of HASH peripheral + `sha2` software + sec_and/sec_add BOTH ways (TRNG-inline / rand-pre-drawn). `make bench-masked-sha-hw` (note: NO debug-log — `hw::rng::fill` secure_log!s on every call, which floods the semihosting channel under a TRNG-heavy bench; results print via unconditional `hprintln!`). **Decision rule** (advisor): masked/HW ≲ 3× → SHA2 viable; ≳ 5–10× → SHAKE worth building.

    **RESULT (STM32U585 @ 160 MHz, 20k iters/measurement):** HASH peripheral 1-block = **485 cyc**; sha2 software 1-block = 4265 cyc (8.8×); TRNG draw 1 word = **214 cyc**; sec_and = 22 cyc gate / 214 cyc TRNG-inline; sec_add = 213 cyc gate / 2147 cyc TRNG-inline. **Projected masked SHA-256 block: 134,840 cyc (~842 µs) DRBG-floor; 1,356,680 cyc (~8.5 ms) raw-TRNG.** Slowdown vs HASH peripheral: **~278× (DRBG) to ~2797× (raw TRNG)**; vs sha2 software ~31× to ~318×.

    **VERDICT — masking the SHA-2 hot path wholesale is INFEASIBLE** (1–2 orders of magnitude past the 5–10× line). A full SLH-DSA sign is hundreds of thousands of compressions; even at the DRBG-floor 842 µs/block that's minutes-to-hours/sign. Three takeaways: **(1) The TRNG draw dominates** (214 cyc/word; sec_add's 10 words = ~2140 of 2147 cyc) — any masking path MUST use a DRBG (~10× win, 8.5 ms→0.84 ms/block); raw-TRNG masking is never right. **(2) This de-prioritizes the SHAKE migration** — SHAKE masks ~2–5× cheaper than SHA-2 (bitwise, no carry chains) but 278÷5 ≈ 56× is still infeasible for the full hot path, and there's no SHAKE hardware peripheral, so SHAKE-masked-in-software loses the HASH-peripheral speed without buying feasibility. **(3) The viable SCA architecture is "mask only the secret-touching hash subset"** (the sk_seed PRF + the first hash of each WOTS/FORS secret chain; the vast majority of SLH-DSA hashes operate on PUBLIC pk_seed-derived data and need no masking) composed with F-16 shuffle + consumption-mask + (deferred) RDI, driven by the `sca-trigger` on-silicon leakage measurement (F-18b). **Next step is NOT the SHAKE #3 benchmark** — it's (a) quantify the secret-touching hash count per sign, (b) on-silicon leakage-characterize just those calls, (c) mask only that subset (SHA-2, via DRBG) if it leaks. ~~At a few thousand secret-touching calls × 134k cyc ≈ ~1 s added, that's plausibly acceptable.~~ **← this "~few thousand / ~1 s" estimate was WRONG (see step (a) below).**

  - [x] **Step (a) — secret-touching hash count MEASURED 2026-05-20: 70,754/sign (NOT ~200).** Added a `hash-counters` feature to sphincs-c10 (atomic per-category counts, zero-cost when off) + `tests/hash_counts.rs`. One C10 sign: **wots_secret 44,118 + fors_secret 26,636 = 70,754 sk_seed-touching PRF calls (13.1% of all 539,616 hashes)**; chain_hash 44,204; th* (tree nodes) 364,273; other 60,385. **CORRECTION: my prior-turn "~200, mask the subset, ~1 s added" was wrong by ~350×** — I let a casual "~200" estimate past without verification. The count is large because SLH-DSA signing regenerates *every leaf of every signed subtree* (auth-path Merkle reconstruction: `build_subtree_with_auth` = D=2 × 512 leaves × L=43 = 44,032 wots_secret) and *every FORS tree leaf* (K=13 × 2^11 = 26,624 fors_secret) — each a `PRF(sk_seed, ADRS)` call, inherent to the algorithm. **CONSEQUENCE: "mask only the secret-touching subset" is INFEASIBLE** — 70,754 × 134,840 cyc (DRBG-masked block) ≈ **~60 s/sign**; raw-TRNG ~10 min. Same wall as masking everything. **(c) is dead at every granularity.**
    - **Why midstate caching doesn't rescue it** (advisor-checked): the PRF byte layout is `sk_seed(32) ‖ "wots"(4) ‖ layer(4) ‖ tree(32) ‖ kp(4) ‖ chain_idx(4)` = 80 B = 2 SHA-256 blocks. First block = `sk_seed(32) ‖ "wots"(4) ‖ layer(4) ‖ tree[0..24]`, so W[0..7]=sk_seed but W[8]=`"wots"‖layer` is *varying public ADRS*. You can cache the SHA-256 state after rounds 0..7 (which absorb only the sk_seed words W[0..7]), but rounds 8..63 still run on secret-derived state and still need masking — saves ~14% of round cost, doesn't drop the per-call multiplier. No cacheable post-sk_seed midstate because sk_seed isn't block-aligned.
    - **Why F-16 shuffle is only partial here**: it randomizes which ADRS pairs with which trace (defeats correlation DPA that needs the known-input pairing), but sk_seed sits at the *same intra-call sample offset* in all 70,754 calls, so horizontal averaging on rounds 0..7 doesn't care about call order. Real residual.
  - [ ] **Step (b) — REFRAMED as a standalone residual-characterization measurement (NOT a precursor to (c), which is dead).** One-number question: does the PRF actually leak sk_seed exploitably on *this* silicon, with F-16 shuffle + consumption-mask + the hardware noise floor combined? Wire `sca-trigger` (F-18b) around the PRF region, capture power traces, TVLA max|t| on the sk_seed-absorption window. Two dispositions: **leaks** → document the residual; the v1 answer is the system-level bar (dual-SE XOR split + PIN-unlock required before any sign + per-chain `MAX_SLOT_USES = 65,536`), and flag the v2 PRF restructure below; **doesn't leak at measurable sensitivity** → declare existing mitigations sufficient, close the §18 SCA thread. Needs the on-silicon power-SCA rig (scope + scared/lascar) — parked alongside the RDI measurement until a bench session is set up for SCA *capture* (not just probe-rs flashing).
  - [ ] **v2 PRF restructure (KDF-revision candidate — NOT retrofittable to the frozen wallet).** If (b) shows an exploitable leak, the real fix is to pad sk_seed to a full 64-byte first block in the PRF (`sha256(sk_seed_padded_64 ‖ label ‖ adrs)`) so the first compression absorbs ONLY sk_seed → a constant post-sk_seed midstate computed ONCE per sign (one masked compression) reused across all 70,754 calls, which then each run only the public second-block compression (unmasked). Turns 70,754 masked compressions into 1. **Changes the PRF input layout → re-bases every key + CREATE2 address → violates launch invariant #6.** A v2 / next-KDF-generation item, recorded so the v2 design has a concrete candidate. **(c) — "mask the subset" — is shelved permanently; this restructure replaces it.**

**What's needed — P0 (must ship with these):**
- [x] **SLH-DSA double-compute** (commit `7f2f2e7`, F-13). `secure/src/crypto.rs::c10_sign_verified_with_progress` signs twice, constant-time compares, releases on match. RFC 9814 §A.2 / Genêt TCHES 2023 verify-after-sign-alone-is-insufficient class closed.
- [x] **Non-deterministic OptRand** on every signature (commit follows F-13). 16 B drawn per sign-call via `rng_strong::fill` (STM32 ⊕ OPTIGA ⊕ SE050 XOR-fold; Trezor-parity multi-source RNG). The OptRand=None deterministic path is gone; both double-compute signs see the SAME freshly-drawn randomiser so the FI byte-equality gate still holds.
- [x] **Signing rate limiter** (F-17, commit `6fcc3cb`) — **complete as architecturally achievable**. `secure/src/sign_rate.rs` enforces (a) 1-second minimum interval between signs (busy-wait via `cortex_m::asm::wfi()` against the SysTick-driven `timeout::now()`), and (b) per-unlock-session cap of 250 sigs (refuse past cap; user must re-PIN-unlock). Hard-rotate after 2^16 is enforced via on-chain `MAX_SLOT_USES`. The "500/day" half of the original spec **is not implementable on the current platform** — it requires VBAT-backed wall-clock time, and the B-U585I-IOT02A (and any USB-powered hw-wallet topology generally) has no battery backup. STM32U5 RTC resets on every power-off, so any "day" boundary is bypassable via a reset cycle. The effective long-term rate ceiling becomes 250 sigs/session × ~5 s PIN-unlock cycle ≈ **~50 sigs/min sustained** for an attacker with the PIN — same architectural bound Trezor accepts (also USB-powered, no VBAT). Adding VBAT to the board design + RTC anchor is a hardware-revision item, not a firmware deliverable.
- [x] **WOTS chain + FORS tree shuffling** (F-16, commit follows). `sphincs-c10/src/shuffle.rs::fisher_yates` + new `SigningKey::sign_with_shuffle(msg, opt_rand, &ShuffleSeed, progress)` API. Per-call seed drawn from `rng_strong::fill` (3-source XOR) and fed UNCHANGED to both F-13 double-compute signs. Internally label-derived into per-purpose sub-seeds: `wots-0`, `wots-1`, `fors`. WOTS chain processing order shuffled per layer (43! ≈ 10^52); FORS tree processing order shuffled (13! ≈ 6×10^9). Output sig bytes are byte-identical to the un-shuffled path (regression-tested in `tests/shuffle_byte_equality.rs` — 4 oracles, all pass). `contracts/smart-wallet/test/c10_test_vectors.json` is byte-identical pre/post — every Foundry test that passed before still passes by construction. **Defeats profiled-DPA trace alignment**: with the same SK chain landing at a random sample index per signature, the averaging premise the attack depends on no longer holds. No on-chain change, no API break for external consumers; internal `sphincs-c10::wots::sign` and `hypertree::sign_inner` gain a shuffle-seed parameter.
- [x] **FihInt-style complement-storage** for `pin_verified` (F-14, commit follows). `secure/src/fih.rs::FihBool` stores `(val, complement)` with `SEC_TRUE=0x1AAA_AAAA` / `SEC_FALSE=0x1555_5555`, double-read via `read_volatile` with `wait_random` between, composed with the existing F-2 `check_true_into_sentinel` for caller-side branch-skip resistance. `has_signed`, `slot_master_derived`, `blob_cached` deferred — first two are write-only as of this pass; third gates a read whose downstream decryption fails on all-zero, not a security bypass.
- [~] **PIN-lockout fail-in pattern** (F-15, commit `6fa69bd`) — **MITIGATED, not provably fixed**. Both `nsc::gated_unlock` and `cmd_request_unlock::verify_pin_with_chip` now structure the lockout gate as FAIL-IN with `check_true_into_sentinel` Hamming-distant gating and double-read of the page-124 counter. The Masaryk-thesis 76 %-reliable single-branch-skip primitive is closed. Residuals (see `tools/sca/README.md §F-15`): stale OK_SENTINEL in r0 from a prior gate, specific-value EM injection, 2-coordinated-skip on `check_true_into_sentinel` internals, post-scan value-fault on `pre_count`/`remaining_after`, and the lack of F-12 forward+reverse hardening inside `pin_attempts_read` itself. Items 1 and 5 are cheap follow-ups (~30 LoC); 2–4 are accepted as "multi-fault out of scope."
- [x] **Compiler fence + DSB barrier after every zeroize** of master_secret, entropy, PIN buffers (commit follows). `crate::fi::zeroize_barrier()` helper: `compiler_fence(SeqCst)` (forbids LLVM reorder across the boundary) + `cortex_m::asm::dsb()` (forces CPU store-buffer commit before subsequent ops; ARM-only via cfg-gated inline asm; no-op on host tests). Applied to 50 critical-secret zeroize call sites (`master_secret`, `entropy`, `half_o` / `half_e` / `full_entropy`, `pin`, `opt_rand_buf`, `slot_master_entropy`). Post-patch linked ELF has +23 `dsb` instructions vs pre-patch (the rest are inlined into shared epilogues by LLVM). The `zeroize` crate's `Zeroize::zeroize()` already does the `compiler_fence` internally; this commit's value is the explicit `dsb` for peripheral-bus ordering (SHA / SAES / PKA accelerators are separate AHB masters) and for asynchronous-wipe-interruption ordering (idle-wipe ISR / panic handler).

**What's needed — P1 (strongly recommended):**
- [x] Architectural decision: **SHAKE vs SHA2 parameter set — CLOSED 2026-05-20: STAY ON SHA-2** (full rationale in the §18 lead above). Summary: masking infeasible on either hash (278–2800× / ~60 s/sign at 70,754 secret calls); SHAKE has no STM32U5 HW acceleration (HASH peripheral is SHA-2-family only) so it's a net slowdown even unmasked; the Fluhrer 1.7× cheap-PRF-tree claim is unverifiable; and a parameter switch re-derives every key (frozen invariant #6). SCA posture stays on SHA-2 + landed F-13/F-16/F-17 + the v2-PRF-restructure escape hatch.
- [x] **Control-flow-integrity step counters** (F-18, commit follows). `secure/src/fi.rs::CfiCounter` + `cfi_expected!` macro. Each critical-path function bumps a running u32 with a unique per-step magic; final check compares against compile-time-summed expected via the F-2 sentinel idiom (`check_into_sentinel(EXPECTED) != OK_SENTINEL → reject`). Volatile reads/writes prevent constant-folding. Applied to `c10_sign_verified_with_progress` (7 steps: rate-limit, opt_rand, shuffle, sign-A, sign-B, ct_eq, verify-gate). Defends the "skip an entire function call" attack class that the F-2 sentinel pattern doesn't reach (F-2 hardens the `if`-branch AFTER a function returns; CFI hardens the function call itself being skipped). Multi-fault attack that writes the expected value directly to the stack-resident counter would still bypass (out of scope per the single-fault threat model). Future: extend to `gated_unlock` + the gateway command dispatchers (similar 3-5 step shape each).
- [x] **Random delays before security-critical ops (entry jitter at the PIN gate)** — DONE 2026-05-20. After advisor review, scoped to explicit `wait_random()` at the entry of `nsc::gated_unlock` + `cmd_request_unlock::verify_pin_with_chip` ONLY — NOT the sign path (F-16's 43!×13! internal shuffle already jitters the secret-dependent timeline there) and NOT an always-on RDI timer (Trezor's `systimer`-driven `rdi_handler` burns random cycles in an ISR that would land mid-USB-bulk / mid-T=1' / mid-Shielded-handshake — Trezor's own `rdi_disabled = sectrue` default + enable/disable infra exist because they hit those bugs; not worth the risk pre-silicon-SCA). The PIN gate is the high-value target: linear from external trigger (USB `CMD_REQUEST_UNLOCK` dispatch, boot-unlock, PendSV re-unlock) to the F-15 sentinel with no internal shuffle, so a Masaryk-class profiled single-fault attacker glitches at a fixed offset; entry jitter desyncs that offset by 0..255 `wait_random_loop` iterations (~0..19 µs at 160 MHz). **Honest bound**: meaningful against an uncalibrated single-shot attacker, does NOT defeat a profile-then-attack rig with multi-attempt statistical recovery — F-15 sentinel + F-17 rate limiter remain the load-bearing defenses. Verified on the linked ELF: real `bl ...fi11wait_random` in both functions (no prologue folding — both `wait_random` and `wait_random_loop` are `#[inline(never)]`). 2 source-grep regression tests pin that the entry jitter precedes the gate it desyncs. The RDI-timer path stays available as a follow-up if on-silicon SCA shows the gap matters.
- [x] **Redundant volatile reads (2-3×) on critical state with OR-based fail-in** (F-19, commit follows). New helper `secure/src/fi.rs::read_volatile_voted<T: Copy + Eq>(p) -> Result<T, ()>` — three independent `read_volatile`s with `compiler_fence(SeqCst)` between, `Ok(v)` iff all three agree, `Err(())` otherwise. Defends single-fault glitches on the `ldr` instruction (which would otherwise clamp the loaded register to an attacker-chosen value). Applied at `sign_rate::wait_for_min_interval` — the rate-limit reference timestamp `LAST_SIGN_MS` AND the SysTick counter `now()` are both critical-timing reads whose disagreement now forces the wait loop to spin until a clean read agrees. Composes with F-14 FihBool (stored-value defense) and F-18 CFI (function-skip defense) as the third leg of the load-time / call-time / value-time triad. Pattern documented in the helper's docstring for future application at other single-load sites.

- [x] **RDI (Random Delay Interrupt) timer — RE-SCOPED 2026-05-28: NOT MOTIVATED for the non-sign secret paths. Do NOT build the subsystem.** The one piece of on-silicon work that remains (value-channel confirmation) lives under §18b (`sca-trigger`), and RDI is not its answer either way. Originally investigated 2026-05-20 as "net-beneficial-for-SCA-not-FI, deferred pending an on-silicon leak measurement." The *emulation half* of that prerequisite is now done — and it argues against RDI rather than for it (see "Emulation re-scope" below). The detailed Trezor / TrustZone / cost analysis is retained below as the record of why even a flag-gated port is a poor fit.

  **What Trezor does** (`trezor-firmware/core/embed/sec/random_delays/stm32/random_delays.c`): a periodic `systimer` fires every tick; `rdi_handler` burns `drbg_random8() + session_delay` cycles (0..510, ~3 µs) of NOP-equivalent delay, preempting whatever runs. Source is a ChaCha DRBG (not raw TRNG — per-tick TRNG draws would exhaust it), reseeded every 1000 calls. `random_delays_start_rdi()` is called ONCE at `kernel/main.c:141` + `secmon/main.c:94` and left on; `random_delays_refresh_rdi()` re-seeds the session base delay on every USB `DataIn`/`DataOut` (`usb.c:728/748`) so the timeline stays unpredictable across a session. `FEATURE_FLAGS["RDI"] = True` for kernel + secmon + firmware — enabled on all Trezor core devices (T / Safe 3 / 5 / 7). The `rdi_disabled = sectrue` default + start/stop scaffolding exist for the boot window before the DRBG is seeded, not because they ship it off.

  **The decisive architectural difference — TrustZone.** Trezor runs USB + crypto + RDI in one secure context, so always-on jitter is uniform and they accept it. **Our USB stack runs in the NON-secure world.** A secure-world RDI timer firing on a fixed schedule preempts NS-world USB bulk transfers whenever it lands during them — the intermittent-stall risk (USB enumeration glitch, SE050 T=1' inter-byte / OPTIGA Shielded-Connection sequence timing) is *worse* for us than for Trezor because the ISR crosses the NS boundary they don't have. The naive always-on port is a genuinely bad fit. The viable design for us is **flag-gated RDI**: an `IN_SECURE_CRITICAL` flag set around signing / PIN / SE-unlock, handler burns cycles only when the flag is live, no-op otherwise. That dodges the USB stall but is a richer version of the entry-jitter + per-step `wait_random` we already have, not a categorically new defense.

  **Where it adds value we lack — SCA, not FI.** *FI*: marginal — our security gates are already hardened (F-2 sentinel, F-14 FihBool, F-15 fail-in, F-18 CFI, F-19 voted reads, entry jitter); RDI only covers un-instrumented paths, and a glitch in non-gate code (KDF, XOR) yields a wrong key that fails verification, not a bypass. *SCA*: real gap — F-16's shuffle misaligns power traces ONLY for the signing path. The other secret-touching ops have NO timeline jitter today: **AES-GCM entropy-blob decrypt**, **SAES-CMAC(DHUK) KDF** (`hw/saes_cmac.rs`), and the **dual-SE XOR reconstruction** (`dual_se.rs`). RDI would misalign traces against all three. Complementary to the consumption-mask (TIM2 PWM adds amplitude noise but not time jitter).

  **Cost.** ChaCha/HMAC DRBG in the secure world (~200 LoC + we have no chacha20 dep today — sha2/sha3 present, so an HMAC-SHA256-DRBG or SHA3 sponge is the lower-friction source); a dedicated timer (STM32U585 has spares — TIM/LPTIM beyond the SysTick-timeout + TIM-sign-rate + TIM2-consumption-mask already in use); the flag-gating plumbing; and silicon validation that it doesn't break SE I²C / USB enumeration / SysTick accounting.

  **Emulation re-scope (2026-05-28) — the measurement says don't build RDI.** The prerequisite was "measure the three non-sign secret paths before building anything." The emulation half of that is now complete in `tools/sca/` (rainbow/lascar `mem_address` TVLA, 600 traces each, fresh on the current tree):

  | Path | Probe | Result |
  |---|---|---|
  | AES-GCM entropy wrap/unwrap | `sca_aesgcm_wrap` (`leakage_kdf.py`) | `max\|t\| = 0.00` → flat |
  | SAES-CMAC(DHUK) KDF wrapper | `sca_saes_cmac` / `sca_saes_kdf_one_block` ×4 (`leakage_saes_kdf.py`) | `max\|t\| = 0.00` → flat |
  | dual-SE XOR reconstruction | `sca_dual_se_xor` (`leakage_seed_derivation.py`) | `max\|t\| = 0.00` → flat |

  Two facts kill the RDI case for these paths:
  1. **The address channel — the only multi-trace channel emulation can test — is already flat for all three** (constant-time soft-AES + branchless XOR; no T-table / cache / secret-indexed access). There is no multi-trace alignment leak for RDI to misalign.
  2. **The residual is a value-channel leak that is *single-trace*.** Each of these is a boot-once operation (one entropy unwrap, one KDF derivation, one XOR reconstruction per unlock) with no attacker-chosen input → no DPA/CPA averaging surface. RDI randomises the *time offset across many traces*; it does **not** defend single-trace SPA/template attacks (a template attacker realigns one trace by pattern-matching). So RDI's mechanism does not match the threat that remains. **The same disqualifier applies to the cheaper `wait_random()`/per-op-jitter middle step** — same mechanism, same single-trace ineffectiveness; it is *not* a consolation fix here.

  This is consistent with the framework's own track record: every *real* single-trace leak it found — F-22 (BIP-39 wordlist), F-24 (seed-display), F-23 (recovery typing) — was closed with **constant-time code**, never RDI. If on-silicon measurement (§18b, `sca-trigger`) ever shows an exploitable value-channel leak on one of these paths, the correct response is constant-time/masked code or a hardware countermeasure on that specific op — not a global RDI timer that fights our NS-world USB stack (see the TrustZone analysis above) for a defense that doesn't address single-trace.

  **What still legitimately remains (tracked under §18b, not here):** on-silicon power/EM confirmation that the value channel on these three paths is acceptable at the system level (dual-SE XOR split + PIN-gated unlock + per-chain `MAX_SLOT_USES` cap). That needs a scope, not RDI.

**Files to create:** `secure/src/fih.rs` (FihInt type + read_volatile helpers)
**Files to change:** `secure/src/crypto.rs` (OptRand + double-compute), `secure/src/nsc/sign_and_emit.rs`, `secure/src/nsc/cmd_request_unlock.rs`, `secure/src/dual_se.rs` (seed XOR reconstruction hardening)
