### 24. OPTIGA pairing restructure: OTP-derived PBS + HUK re-root

**Status:** IN PROGRESS (2026-04-17). Full rationale and mechanism in `docs/secure-elements/optiga-brick-postmortem.md`.

We bricked the Shielded Connection on our TRUSTMV3SHIELDTOBO1 bring-up chip during rx_seq debugging. Root cause analysis shows the current pairing code **would brick any customer device on any firmware update** — this is a ship blocker, not a bench anomaly. Three compounding issues:

- PBS is generated as random per-provisioning (non-deterministic).
- PBS is sealed to MCU flash page 126 with an AES-256-GCM key that mixes `firmware_hash()` — so any firmware rebuild produces a different key and the seal becomes undecryptable.
- `setup_pbs_no_handshake` bumps `E140 LcsO=Operational` (irreversible) immediately after the write, committing to a pairing that only survives while that one firmware binary is running.

Fix is structural, modelled on Trezor's production design (`~/repos/trezor-firmware/core/embed/sec/optiga/` + `sec/secret_keys/stm32u5/`). See the postmortem doc for the full trace, the `firmware_hash()` attestation-vs-HUK separation of concerns, and the decision record on which Trezor patterns we port vs. skip.

**What's needed — P0 (this week, unblocks continued dev):**

- [x] **`optiga-no-shield` dev feature**: skip `setup_pbs_no_handshake` entirely + make `ensure_shield` a no-op. Lets the current bricked chip exercise all non-PRL paths (provisioning F1Dx OIDs, PIN HMAC via `DecryptSym`, entropy reads, factory-reset) while new shields are in transit.
- [x] **Gate `setup_pbs_no_handshake`'s LcsO=op bump** behind a `optiga-lock-operational` Cargo feature. Default OFF — future dev chips stay at LcsO=Creation (rewriteable) and cannot be bricked by rebuild. Production builds opt in once OTP is burned.
- [x] **Remove the `optiga-bringup-fresh` feature.** Its flash-page-126 erase was what finished off our test chip; once PBS is OTP-derived (below) the feature is meaningless. (Landed 2026-04-20: feature dropped from `secure/Cargo.toml`, `optiga/mod.rs::auth_ref_is_authref_typed` deleted, `lock_oid` + `provision_user_oid` + `store_objects` simplified, Makefile `flash-hw-optiga-reset` recipe updated.)

**What's needed — P0 (step 2, the actual fix):**

- [x] **`secure/src/hw/otp.rs` extension**: `read_device_master`, `burn_device_master` (one-shot), `is_device_master_burned`, `ensure_device_master`. Reserves OTP bytes 128..160 (two quad-words past the rollback tally). Feature-gated test stub under `otp-hardcoded-master-key` (ASCII pattern `"PQSIGNER-TEST-OTP-MASTER-DNS-v1!"`) so the derivation can be exercised on dev bench without consuming real OTP.
- [x] **`secure/src/hw/secret_keys.rs`** (Trezor parallel): domain-labelled HMAC-SHA256 expansions of the OTP master. `optiga_pairing_secret`, `se050_scp03_enc_key`, `se050_scp03_mac_key`, `tropic01_pairing_key`. Sets up the API surface for work-todo #7 HUK-SAES to re-implement against SAES-wrapped key material later.
- [x] **Trezor-style first-boot self-provisioning** in place of the originally-planned `make stm32-burn-device-key` factory target: on the first call of `ensure_device_master` against a blank OTP, fill 32 TRNG bytes and program the region. No factory workflow step — the device provisions itself on first power-up.
- [x] Rewrite `setup_pbs_no_handshake` to derive PBS from `secret_keys::optiga_pairing_secret()` instead of `rng::fill`. Flash-seal removed (`hw::flash::write_pbs` call deleted). Added a belt-and-braces guard that refuses the LcsO=op bump if `is_device_master_burned()` is false, so an accidental feature-flip on an unburned board cannot reproduce the brick.
- [x] Delete `hw/flash.rs::read_pbs / write_pbs / erase_pbs_page / PBS_PAGE_ADDR / PbsLoadError / PBS_WRAP_DOMAIN / PBS_BLOB_LEN / is_pbs_blank`. (aes-gcm dep still needed — other callers.)
- [x] Simplify `optiga/mod.rs::load_pbs`: drop the flash-seal + blank-page check path, call `self.shield.load_pbs(&secret_keys::optiga_pairing_secret()?)` directly.

**What's needed — P0 (step 3, decouples firmware_hash from HUK):**

- [x] **Re-root `secure/src/hw/huk.rs::derive_device_key`** off `firmware_hash()` and onto `hw::otp::ensure_device_master()`. **`measured_boot::firmware_hash()` unchanged** — 8-BIP-39-word OLED attestation + #22 manifest binding still depend on it. `derive_device_key` now returns `Result<[u8; 32], OtpError>` to propagate first-boot OTP failures; currently no in-tree caller (flash-seal deleted), retained as the documented primitive for any future at-rest sealing need.

**What's needed — P1 (Trezor ports beyond PBS, finish the OPTIGA layer):**

- [ ] **Hardware monotonic counter for PIN attempts**: migrate `OID_COUNTER` from `0xF1E1` (software, glitch-fragile, `Conf(E140)`-gated) → `0xE120` (OPTIGA built-in monotonic counter with `Auto(LUC)` access conditions). Drop the firmware-side decrement-before-verify gymnastics in `authenticate_and_read`. Closes the concern noted in `project_optiga_bringup.md` memory.
- [ ] **Typed `OptigaMetadata` struct** replacing the tag-by-tag `push_ac_simple`/`push_lcso_op` builders in `apdu.rs`. Pure refactor; makes merge-vs-overwrite semantics explicit. Mirror of Trezor's `optiga_metadata`.

**What's needed — P2 (reversible validation, dev-safe):**

- [x] End-to-end Phase-A test on a fresh TRUSTMV3SHIELDTOBO1 shield (2026-04-20): flashed with `otp-hardcoded-master-key` + `e2e-skip-unlock`, LA1010 on PB8/PB9/PE4. Verified: (a) 64-byte PBS derivation matches expected fingerprint `8ca52e4bc284d822` across multiple reflashes with different `firmware_hash` values, (b) E140 accepts 64-byte PBS + metadata at LcsO=Creation (Sta=0x00), (c) hard-RST pulse on PE4 reaches silicon (LA-confirmed 22 ms falling-edge on CH2 / Arduino D5), (d) all six user OIDs (F1D0 AUTH_REF, F1D1 ENTROPY, F1D2 MASTER_SECRET, F1D3 VK, F1D4 BOOTSTRAP_VK, F1E1 COUNTER) provisioned cleanly (user-OID `lock_oid` calls committed on that run — since corrected to be gated by `optiga-lock-operational`, see below), (e) chip still at LcsO=Creation on E140. The `firmware_hash`-in-wrap-key brick is genuinely gone.
- [x] `lock_oid` gated behind `optiga-lock-operational` (2026-04-20 follow-up): default dev builds (`optiga-lock-operational` OFF) no longer commit user-OID LcsO=op. Every OID stays at LcsO=Creation through all normal Phase-A iteration. Logs `[OPTIGA/prov] OID 0xNNNN lock_oid SKIPPED (optiga-lock-operational OFF ...)` on each gated call. This is the main "never waste another chip in dev" guardrail.
- [x] PRL handshake validated in LcsO=Creation (2026-04-20 follow-up 2): `OptigaTrustM::ensure_shield` used to bump E140 LcsO=op before `shield.establish`, on the mistaken belief that PRL required it. The Infineon reference (`example_pair_host_and_optiga_using_pre_shared_secret.c:30-35` `#define FINAL_LCSO_STATE (LCSO_STATE_CREATION)`) + SRM §"Pairing Use Case Pre-conditions" (`LcsO < operational`) + `ifx_i2c_presentation_layer.c:820-829` (no LcsO check in PRL dispatch) all say the opposite — PRL engages fine at Creation. Removed the bump. Confirmed on hardware: chip now emits a full 38-byte SlaveHello against the current (Creation-state) chip, demonstrating PRL engagement is reversible. Session-key exchange still fails at MasterFinished — that's a separate issue tracked below.
- [x] Investigate MasterFinished handshake rejection (2026-04-21, commit 43b6937). Root cause was our PRL-layer implementation diverging from Infineon's `ifx_i2c_presentation_layer.c:285-621` in three places: (1) PRF seed was `random_M ‖ random_S` but reference uses only `random_S` (no master-side random exists in Infineon's PRL), (2) MasterFinished plaintext was `random_M ‖ slave_seq` but must be `random_S ‖ slave_seq`, (3) MasterFinished nonce/AAD/header seq were all zero but must be the `slave_sequence_number` received in SlaveHello. SlaveFinished verification similarly needed `master_sequence_number` extracted from the response header. `establish: DONE` on real B-U585I-IOT02A + OPTIGA Trust M V3.
- [x] Shielded record I/O end-to-end (2026-04-21, commit a1ac0cd). Three additional bugs uncovered once handshake closed: (a) `send_command` sent wrapped records via `ifx.transceive` (no PRESENCE_BIT) — frames hit the APDU parser instead of the PRL layer, chip responded with SCTR=0x40 fatal alert. Now uses `transceive_prl`. (b) `hard_reset_and_reinit` didn't clear `shield.active`, so after every RST pulse we tried to send records encrypted with keys the chip had discarded. Now clears the flag so `ensure_shield` re-handshakes. (c) `store_objects` never called `ensure_shield` at all despite its docstring claiming idempotent re-provisioning via Conf(E140); now threads it after step-1 PBS write and after every `hard_reset_and_reinit`. Validated: six shielded `set_data_object` calls (F1D0..F1D4 + F1E1) through the Shielded Connection on real silicon.
- [x] PIN unlock via AUTHREF HMAC on real silicon (2026-04-21, commit 3d34101). Two more bugs: (a) `PARAM_HMAC_MODE` in the DecryptSym APDU was 0x02 but `optiga_hmac_type_t::OPTIGA_HMAC_SHA_256 = 0x20` per `common/optiga_lib_common.h:213` — this is the `cmd_param` byte selecting HMAC variant. Sending 0x02 made the chip interpret the frame as a different sym mode and reject with Status=0xFF. (b) Missing `GenerateAuthCode` APDU — `optiga_crypt_hmac_verify` is specified to consume a session acquired by `generate_auth_code` (`optiga_crypt.h:2390`); a bare `GetRandom` leaves the session slot empty and the verify rejects. Added the APDU (GetRandom with `store_in_session=TRUE`) and rewired `authenticate_and_read` to use it with 16 B host_nonce + 32 B chip random + 16 B host_tag mirroring the reference example. Log reaches `hmac_verify OK` → `[OPTIGA] Unlocked: entropy + VKs cached` → `gateway pre-unlocked, ready for tests`.
- [x] Skip `set_metadata` when OID LcsO is already Operational (2026-04-21, commit 635e018). Partially-provisioned chips from pre-0b412b4 runs have F1D0..F1DF at LcsO=Op; re-running provisioning would hit the one-way ratchet with Status=0xFF. Guards `provision_auth_ref` / `provision_user_oid` / `provision_counter` to read the current metadata, check tag 0xC0 via `apdu::is_metadata_operational`, and skip both `set_metadata` and `lock_oid` when the stored functional AC tags already match `build_metadata_*` output.
- [ ] Rerun Phase-A on a third fresh chip with the gate in place and confirm the chip stays fully at LcsO=Creation post-provision (all 7 OIDs readable + rewriteable afterwards). Completes the reversibility proof.

**What's out of scope here — moved to `docs/production-todo.md`:**

- Phase-B (E140 LcsO=op commit + full PRL handshake) — one-way.
- First-boot STM32 OTP master-key burn validation — one-way.
- User-OID LcsO=op commits — one-way (our Phase A flipped these accidentally on the 2026-04-20 chip; follow-up landed the `optiga-lock-operational` gate so the default flow no longer does).
- RDP=2, WRP1A, SECBOOTADD0, vendor-key bake-in, SE050 SCP03 rotation, supply-chain manifest signing — all silicon- or fleet-committing actions.

Everything in `docs/production-todo.md` must be validated on sacrificial parts and only executed during explicit factory / end-to-end production provisioning runs with `optiga-lock-operational` (and the other relevant gates) enabled. Dev day-to-day iteration never flips those.

**What we're NOT porting from Trezor** (decision record; full rationale in §6.4 of the postmortem):

- Multi-OID PIN stretching chain (`OID_PIN_CMAC` E200 + `OID_PIN_HMAC` F1D8 + `OID_PIN_ECDH` E0F3). Trezor uses it to defeat offline brute force after flash extraction; our design never stores PIN material in MCU flash so the threat is closed elsewhere.
- ECDSA signing-key masking with an OTP-derived mask. We don't sign with OPTIGA ECC keys. The general pattern may be worth revisiting for work-todo #18 (SLH-DSA SCA/FI), not for the OPTIGA layer.
- `optiga_suspend`/`optiga_resume` with RTC wakeup. Irrelevant for a USB-bus-powered device.

**Cross-references:**

- `docs/secure-elements/optiga-brick-postmortem.md` — full rationale (primary reference for this item)
- #7 HUK-SAES — the OTP + `secret_keys` infrastructure introduced here is what #7 needs; they merge
- #22 Supply-chain attestation — `firmware_hash()` stays intact and is still this manifest's firmware-identity input
- `project_optiga_bringup.md` memory — has the earlier bring-up quirks + a note about PIN counter hardening that this item closes

**Files to create:** `secure/src/hw/otp.rs`, `secure/src/hw/secret_keys.rs`, `scripts/burn_device_key.py` (helper for `stm32-burn-device-key`)
**Files to change:** `secure/Cargo.toml` (new features), `secure/src/optiga/mod.rs` (setup_pbs rewrite, load_pbs simplification, LcsO gate), `secure/src/optiga/apdu.rs` (OptigaMetadata refactor, OID_COUNTER change), `secure/src/hw/huk.rs` (OTP-backed), `secure/src/hw/flash.rs` (delete PBS seal), `secure/src/main.rs` (load_pbs call simplification), `Makefile` (new target)

### 25. PIN counter sync polish (three-way lockstep hardening)

**Status:** Gaps 1 + 3 landed 2026-04-22. Three items below remain.

Context: the dual-SE PIN lockout runs three counters in lockstep (MCU page-124 pre-commit + OPTIGA E120 LUC + SE050 UserID chip-side attempt counter). `pin-gate-hw-counter-e2e` (5 phases) and `pin-gate-wipe-e2e` (destructive 10-iteration burn + factory_reset_admin + re-provision) validate the steady-state and wipe-dispatch paths on real silicon. See `memory/project_optiga_hw_counter_validated.md` for the wire-format facts and cross-boot/LcsO=Op caveats; see commits `7574218` (three-way sync), `0ecfe69` (boot-time SE050 cache re-sync), `d0dda77` (wipe-dispatch e2e) for the code.

Three gaps deliberately not closed by the four validation runs on our last bench chip:

- [ ] **Gap 2 — E120 exhaustion lockout on silicon.** The `curr >= HW_PIN_CTR_LIMIT` check in `authenticate_and_read` (`secure/src/optiga/mod.rs:1357-1358`, carries a SAFETY/COVERAGE comment) is validated by inspection only. Running it consumes 32 consecutive wrong-PIN counter slots on the chip with unknown cumulative wear from repeated reset-on-success cycles. Per advisor consult 2026-04-22: the risk of an unknown-unknown on the last bench chip outweighs the marginal coverage versus inspection + LUC increment proven by `optiga-hw-counter-e2e`. Revisit when (a) a fresh spare TRUSTMV3SHIELDTOBO1 arrives and (b) we have either a second bench chip to cross-validate wear behaviour or access to Infineon's datasheet wear budget for the E120 counter OID.

- [x] **Gap 4 — SE050 silicon-locked SW → `UnlockError::PinLocked` mapping** (code landed; on-silicon empirical confirmation deferred to next bench session). Closes the corruption/desync stuck-loop case: MCU page-124 reset (flash fault, RDP regression mid-cycle, dev-tool intervention) while SE050 UserID is silicon-locked → fresh PIN attempt previously hit the `Status(sw)` catch-all → `UnlockError::InternalError` → gateway treats as transient, NO WIPE → device stuck in retry loop. Investigation: original work-todo guess "0x6983" was the **A71CL** legacy code (`SE_HAL_Lib.h::A71CL_SE_HAL_ERR_KEY_LOCKED = 0xCC // 6983`) — SE050's own `se05x_tlv.h::smStatus_t` does NOT include 0x6983. SE050 uses **0x6986** (`SM_ERR_COMMAND_NOT_ALLOWED`, AN13030 wording "Command not allowed — access denied based on object policy") for policy-denial when an auth object stops accepting VERIFY. Mapping is documented-by-deduction (AN12413 is behind NXP login; on-silicon SW capture deferred to next bench session — run `iterative_wipe` against a throwaway UserID, then VERIFY, capture SW; if not 0x6986 update both arms). Fix: (1) new `Se050Error::AuthMethodBlocked` variant in `secure/src/se050/apdu.rs`; (2) `apdu::verify_session` translates `Status(0x6986) → AuthMethodBlocked` alongside the existing `0x6985 || 0x63xx → PinIncorrect` arm; (3) new pure helper `classify_se050_unlock_error(&Se050Error) -> UnlockError` in `secure/src/se050/mod.rs` maps `AuthMethodBlocked → PinLocked`; (4) `Se050::unlock` map_err refactored to call the pure helper (side-effect arm zeros `self.remaining` on `AuthMethodBlocked`). Dispatch chain end-to-end: `apdu::verify_session` → `Se050Error::AuthMethodBlocked` → `Se050::unlock`'s map_err → `UnlockError::PinLocked` → `DualSecureElement::unlock`'s propagation (line 197-202, no transformation) → `nsc::gated_unlock` → `cmd_request_unlock`'s `Err(UnlockError::PinLocked) => trigger_lockout_wipe()` arm (line 117-122) → `factory_reset_admin` + zeroize SRAM secrets + return `NscStatus::PinLocked` to NS. 4 new host pin-tests in `secure/src/se050_under_test/pure_tests.rs::gap4_*` (1841 total, was 1837): (a) `apdu::verify_session` contains the 0x6986 arm, (b) `classify_se050_unlock_error` maps AuthMethodBlocked→PinLocked, (c) PinIncorrect still maps to PinIncorrect (regression guard against wrong-PIN→PinLocked broadening that would wipe on first mistyped digit), (d) catch-all still maps to InternalError (no over-eager wipe on transport glitches). Conservatively narrow per advisor review: only 0x6986 mapped, not 0x6982 (generic security-status which could be a transport glitch — false-positive wipe risk worse than the rare desync). On-silicon validation pending — if SW differs the existing `pin-gate-wipe-e2e` infrastructure already covers the wire (just need to extend it to capture the SW returned after the 10th wrong PIN's lockout completes).

- [ ] **Gap 5 — behaviour under `optiga-lock-operational=ON` (production ratchet).** Every validated test run to date has kept every OID at `LcsO=Creation` per the user's non-negotiable directive while we're still iterating. The production build bumps OIDs to `LcsO=Operational` — an irreversible one-way ratchet per the OPTIGA SRM. All metadata writes become no-ops after the bump (chip rejects). This is fine for the happy path (no metadata rewrites needed post-provision), but needs a deliberate dry-run on a throwaway chip that's been bumped to `LcsO=Op` to confirm: (a) `reset_hw_pin_counter` still works (the Change AC is `Auto(F1D0)` which is preserved across LcsO), (b) `factory_reset` still works (the user-OID Change ACs include `Conf(E140)` which is preserved), (c) the `is_metadata_operational` check in `provision_hw_pin_counter` short-circuits correctly, (d) three-way sync still passes phase-1..4 with E120 operating on a locked-metadata chip. Consumes a second bench chip (the LcsO bump is one-way).

### 27. ~~`bench-key-speed` NS test regression in HEAD~~ — RESOLVED

**Resolved 2026-05-06.** Root cause was build-hygiene, not a code bug. The two observed failure modes (`[NSC] sign_offchain (len=46)` returning `NotInitialized`, and the `u64_div_rem` exception in NS bench init) were both **stale NS veneer addresses** in a manual build flow.

**Mechanism.** Each secure-side build with a different feature set produces a different `target/veneers.o` because the secure binary's veneer thunks are placed at addresses that depend on the surrounding code layout. The NS-side build links against `target/veneers.o` to resolve `extern "C" { fn nsc_sign_userop(...); ... }` declarations to specific veneer addresses.

If the NS ELF is older than the current `veneers.o`, its thunks point to STALE addresses. Calling `nsc_sign_userop` from NS lands at the address that was the userop veneer **at NS build time** — but in the freshly-flashed secure binary, that same address might now be the offchain veneer or the middle of an unrelated function. Result: silent dispatch to the wrong handler.

**Concrete trace.** Verified by comparing `arm-none-eabi-nm target/veneers.o | grep nsc_sign_userop` (`0x0c0673b8`) against the NS ELF's veneer-thunk target (`.word 0x0c0675f9` — Thumb bit included → `0x0c0675f8`). 1088-byte mismatch → calls land 8 entries downstream in the veneer table.

**Fix.** Always rebuild NS with the same `veneers.o` it'll be running against. The Makefile `test-key-speed` target already does this:

```make
rm -f $(NONSECURE_ELF) target/nonsecure/$(TARGET)/release/deps/sphincs_tz_nonsecure-*
$(RUSTFLAGS_VAR)="$(RUSTFLAGS_NONSECURE_HW)" cargo build ... -p sphincs-tz-nonsecure ...
```

The pre-build `rm` of the NS ELF + dep files forces cargo to relink against the current `veneers.o`. **As long as you use `make test-key-speed`, this works.** Manual builds (`cargo build -p sphincs-tz-secure ... && cargo build -p sphincs-tz-nonsecure ...`) need the same `rm`-then-rebuild discipline if the secure side's feature set changed since the last NS build.

**Validation post-fix.** Bench passes cleanly with TAMP IRQ-mode + tamp + e2e-test (commit `f5e6a8a` + fresh NS link):

```
[S] TAMP initialised (IRQ, log-only)
[NS][bench] A) chain=1 first-sign:    9,159 ms
[NS][bench] B-avg) type2-only:        4,001 ms
[NS][bench] C) chain=2 first-sign:   10,282 ms
[NS][bench] === PASS ===
```

Within ±2% of polled-mode baseline. No spurious `[TAMP] irq:` or `[IRQ] unexpected:` lines across the entire bench — IRQ-mode passes through thousands of SAES + RNG operations cleanly.

**Documentation tightening that fell out of this:** any future secure-side feature-flag matrix CI / dev-board run that builds secure separately from NS should treat them as a single build unit (rebuild both, in lockstep). A bare `cargo build -p sphincs-tz-secure` followed by `probe-rs run` on a previously-flashed-NS chip will silently invoke wrong veneers and look like an arbitrary firmware regression. Worth a sentence in `docs/hardware/dev-board-setup.md` once that file gets a refresh.

**Second build-hygiene gotcha surfaced 2026-05-06 during tamp-irq soak.** `cargo build -p sphincs-tz-secure --features <new-set>` does NOT always rebuild the secure ELF — Cargo's incremental cache may keep the previous feature set's binary when the only change is a CLI feature flag. Symptom: `cargo build` reports "Finished in 0.06s", the secure ELF stays at the OLD timestamp, and `probe-rs download` flashes the OLD binary. Combined with a fresh NS rebuild (which links against the FRESH `veneers.o`), the chip runs an old secure with new NS thunks → arbitrary handler dispatch failures that look like new-feature regressions but are actually stale builds. Fix: `rm -f target/secure/<target>/release/sphincs-tz-secure target/secure/<target>/release/deps/sphincs_tz_secure-*` before `cargo build -p sphincs-tz-secure` whenever the feature set changes. The `make` targets do this implicitly via the `rm` of the NS ELF triggering a full rebuild graph; bare `cargo build` does not.

### 26. TAMP polled → IRQ migration (Trezor-parity latency)

**Status:** Polled-mode landed in commit `aecc1cc` (2026-05-05). IRQ-mode landed behind `tamp-irq` feature in `f5e6a8a` (2026-05-06) + soak-validated 2026-05-06. Default-on flip remains deferred until production-hardening branch (per "When to do this" below).

`secure/src/hw/tamp.rs` currently runs in polled mode: `tamp::init()` arms `TAMP_CR1` (detection) but leaves `TAMP_IER` masked, and `tamp::poll()` from the SysTick handler drains `TAMP_SR` ~1 kHz. Validated on real STM32U585 — no spurious triggers across `make test-key-speed`-shape runs.

**Why polled today, not IRQ.** PQSigner has zero peripheral-IRQ infrastructure today (no PAC crate, no `#[interrupt]` handler scaffold). An unmasked TAMP IRQ would land in cortex-m-rt's WEAK `DefaultHandler` and HardFault. Polling is dev-board-safe; IRQ-mode is a production hardening item.

**Migration path: `DefaultHandler` dispatch (~30 LOC, recommended).**

```rust
// secure/src/main.rs
#[cortex_m_rt::exception]
unsafe fn DefaultHandler(irqn: i16) {
    match irqn {
        #[cfg(all(feature = "stm32u585", feature = "tamp"))]
        2 => hw::tamp::on_tamp_irq(),  // already in tree, gated `_unused`
        _ => panic_unexpected_irq(irqn),
    }
}
```

Plus three lines added to `tamp::init()`: `write_volatile(TAMP_IER, ITAMP_FLAG_MASK); write_volatile(NVIC_ICPR0, 1 << TAMP_IRQN); write_volatile(NVIC_ISER0, 1 << TAMP_IRQN);`. The `tamp::on_tamp_irq()` function is preserved in tree (renamed to `_*_unused` + `#[allow(dead_code)]`) so re-introducing it is a single uncomment.

**Why this matters.** IRQ latency (~hundreds of cycles) beats SysTick polling (~1 ms) by an order of magnitude. Doesn't matter in dev (log-only) but matters in production where the wipe is racing an attacker reading residual-power side-channels off the backup SRAM.

**Why not default-on yet.** Two reasons remaining (the "operational soak" one is satisfied; see below):

1. **DefaultHandler picks up every IRQ source we accidentally unmask, not just TAMP.** Without a firmware-wide audit of "which peripherals have IER bits set right now," that's a footgun. The audit must land in the same diff as the default-on flip.
2. **Production should flip the wipe trigger in the same commit as the default-on flip.** Migrating polled→IRQ while still log-only is half a change; we'd then have to re-validate the trigger surface again when production wipe lands. Bundle them.

**Operational-soak criterion: SATISFIED 2026-05-06.** Test plan was "a few clean `make e2e-hw` + `dual-se-multi-unlock-e2e` runs with no spurious `[TAMP] irq:` or `[IRQ] unexpected:` lines." Results on B-U585I-IOT02A:

| Test | Build features | Spurious IRQ events | Result |
|---|---|---:|---|
| dual-se-multi-unlock-e2e — boot 1/3 | `dual-se-multi-unlock-e2e,stm32u585,ui-oled,debug-log,e2e-test,otp-hardcoded-master-key,tamp,tamp-irq` | 0 | 5/5 unlocks PASS |
| dual-se-multi-unlock-e2e — boot 2/3 | (same) | 0 | 5/5 unlocks PASS |
| dual-se-multi-unlock-e2e — boot 3/3 | (same) | 0 | 5/5 unlocks PASS |
| e2e-hw (full unified-sign suite + PIN-lockout brute-force) | `mock-se,debug-log,ui-semihosting,e2e-test,stm32u585,tamp,tamp-irq` | 0 | `=== All scenarios passed! ===` |

Total: **15 unlocks, 1 multi-scenario sign suite, 1 PIN-lockout brute-force test, 0 spurious IRQ events.** TAMP-IRQ passes through thousands of SAES + RNG accesses + CMSE veneer crossings cleanly. `DefaultHandler` dispatch invariant holds (no `[IRQ] unexpected irqn=N` lines).

**Build hygiene gotcha surfaced during validation.** Cargo's incremental build does NOT always rebuild the secure ELF when the secure-side feature set changes via CLI args alone. The symptom is identical to a "tamp-irq regression" — secure ELF is from an older feature set, NS is built fresh against current `veneers.o`, and the chip ends up running a secure binary whose veneer addresses don't match what NS expects. Fix: `rm` the secure ELF + deps directory before each rebuild, not just the NS one. Documented in #27.

**Alternative paths considered + rejected:**
- **Adopt PAC crate (`stm32u5`).** Would let us use `#[cortex_m_rt::interrupt] fn TAMP() { ... }` directly. Rejected: PQSigner has historically refused PAC adoption (raw-register pattern everywhere — see `hw/rcc.rs`, `hw/usb_hw.rs`, `hw/saes.rs`). Architectural drift the codebase actively avoids.
- **Hand-rolled `__INTERRUPTS` array with linker-script trick.** ~100 LOC of boilerplate. Fragile. Skip unless cortex-m-rt drops `DefaultHandler` support.

**When to do this:** during the production-hardening branch, alongside the parallel `production-todo.md` item ("TAMP escalation: log-only → `trigger_lockout_wipe()`"). Both flips MUST land together so review can verify the trigger surface end-to-end.

**Production-flip register-by-register plan (added 2026-05-19 from `docs/architecture/trezor-comparison.md §8.4`).** The deeper Trezor analysis surfaced the specific TAMP enable list + handler shape we'd port:
- `TAMP->CR1` enable bitmask: ITAMP1 (VBAT), 2 (temp), 3 (LSE CSS), 5 (RTC overflow), 8 (monoctr overflow), **9 (SAES/AES/PKA/TRNG crypto fault — the biggest FI defense win)**, 11 (IWDG-with-tamper). External pin via `TAMPER_INPUT_2` GPIO macro if we wire a tamper pin.
- `TAMP->CR3 = 0` — all enabled sources fire in *confirmed mode* (no warn/restrict layering). Single terminal action.
- `TAMP->FLTCR` filter: 8-cycle pre-charge, 4-sample debounce, RTCCLK/256 sampling (per Trezor `tamper.c:132-136`).
- Handler body (drop-in replacement for the current log-only `on_tamp_irq`): re-init MPU/SAU (TAMP erased part of SRAM2), disable external pin to prevent debounce re-trigger (`TAMP->CR1 &= ~TAMP_CR1_TAMP2E`), clear status (`TAMP->SCR = sr`), call `trigger_lockout_wipe()`.
- Coordinate `TAMP->CR2 |= TAMP_CR2_BKERASE` at controlled-reboot paths so firmware can force backup-domain erase when we choose to.

The §8.4 agent-derived plan is the missing concrete porting content that §26 referenced as "production hardening" without specifying. Adopt verbatim with the default-flip when production-hardening branches.

**Files to change for default-flip (when):** Remove `tamp-irq` feature gate and make IRQ-mode unconditional under `tamp`. The `DefaultHandler` fn in `secure/src/main.rs` and `enable_tamp_irq()` + `on_tamp_irq()` in `secure/src/hw/tamp.rs` are already in tree (commit `f5e6a8a`); the default-flip is a Cargo.toml + cfg-gate cleanup, not a behavioral change.

### 28. SPI LCD migration — ZT165M017AT (NV3007 driver, 142×428 RGB565)

**Status:** Phase A (scaffold) 2026-05-19 `a576480` → **Phase B (bench bring-up) DONE 2026-06-09** → **Phase C (Display backend + dialogs + F-24 CT gate) DONE 2026-06-09** → **Phase C #3 production-switch DONE 2026-06-09: `RELEASE_FEATURES` now uses `ui-lcd` (was `ui-oled`); `ui-lcd` is the shipping display backend.** `ui-oled` is **demoted to dev-only** (kept for SSD1306 dev boards + the `ui-capture`/`ui-mirror`/`decoy-frames` tooling that rides on it — NOT deleted). `make play-hw-lcd` runs the full interactive wallet on the NV3007; `make play-hw-display` is the OLED equivalent. See `docs/hardware/nv3007-wiring.md` for wiring.

**Why the migration.** Two motivations:

1. **F-24 stage E Phase 1 (decoy frames) needs slow-response pixels.** OLED pixels are bistable (hold state until repainted) — bench-tested 2026-05-19, decoys are fully visible to the user at any tunable cadence. The ZT165M017AT TFT has Tr+Tf typ 35 ms max 40 ms per datasheet, so a decoy painted for ~5-10 ms and immediately overwritten by real never reaches full pixel transition. Bus carries the decoy (defense works); pixels barely move (UX preserved). The decoy-frames feature already exists in tree behind a Cargo flag; just needs a Display backend that's actually slow-response.

2. **Larger usable display area.** 142×428 = 60,776 pixels vs SSD1306's 128×64 = 8,192. Lets us show longer addresses, multi-line transaction confirmations, and richer typed-call decoding without scrolling. UX win independent of the decoy defense.

**Phase A — driver scaffold (DONE, 2026-05-19)**: `secure/src/hw/lcd_nv3007.rs`. Byte-level command/data primitives over SPI1 (PE12/13/15 + new PE3 DC + PE1 RES), production init sequence ported byte-for-byte from `dgen1`'s `nv3007_142x428_4line_8bit.c`, `set_window` + `fill_screen` + `write_pixels` helpers. `ui-lcd` Cargo feature. `make build-hw-lcd-bringup` target.

- [x] **Phase B — bench-validate on real silicon. ✅ DONE 2026-06-09** (panel alive + clean image, 40 MHz / 41 fps full-frame, 0.65 ms partial updates; see Completion Log + `docs/hardware/nv3007-wiring.md`). Wire the LCD per the pin map (see lcd_nv3007.rs module header + the build-hw-lcd-bringup Makefile comment). Add a `lcd-test` Cargo feature + `make lcd-test-hw` target that short-circuits `main()` after `ui::init()` into a small loop: `hw::lcd_nv3007::init()` → `fill_screen(0x07E0)` (green) → 1s pause → `fill_screen(0xF800)` (red) → 1s → `fill_screen(0x001F)` (blue) → loop. Mirror the `decoy-flicker-test` pattern. Validation: LCD lights up with the 3 colors cycling. If it doesn't, the init sequence or SPI timing needs tuning. Estimated effort: ~1-2 hours once the LCD is in hand. Risk: SPI prescaler may need adjustment from ÷32 (5 MHz) to ÷8 (20 MHz) for acceptable refresh; the bring-up loop above will surface this immediately.

- [ ] **Re-up LCD SPI to ÷4 (40 MHz) on the EVT board (no LED on SCK).** The bring-up dev board (B-U585I-IOT02A) hardwires its blue Arduino LED **LD2 to PE13 = SPI1_SCK**; the LED + series resistor loads the SCK line and rounds off 40 MHz edges (12.5 ns half-period vs the NV3007's 10 ns setup/hold) → intermittent UI flicker. So `ui-lcd` was dropped to **÷8 (20 MHz)** on 2026-06-09 (`spi_hw.rs` MBR, `ui-lcd`-gated; full repaint ~50 ms instead of ~24 ms — fine for a near-static UI, partial updates still < 2 ms). The custom EVT board has no LED on SCK + proper PCB traces, so flip the `ui-lcd` MBR back to `0b001` (÷4, 40 MHz) there to halve full-repaint time. Update the `positive_spi_hw_cfg1_baud_gated_dsize_8bit` source-pin test in lockstep, and validate no striping/flicker at 40 MHz on the EVT wiring before locking it in. (If 20 MHz is ever still not clean on a given board, ÷16 = 10 MHz is the next step down.)

- [~] **Phase C — Display trait integration. BACKEND LANDED 2026-06-09** (`secure/src/ui/lcd.rs`); rendering validated on real hardware. Implemented: (a) `build.rs` + the `nsc/mod.rs` fence now count `ui-lcd` as a standalone 4th Display backend (no `ui-noop` pairing — that's now a build error); (b) `lcd::Display` renders the **same 16×4 char grid** (so `confirm`/`pin_entry`/`seed_wizard`/`measured_boot` port with ZERO edits) via a per-glyph 720 B cell buffer + `lcd_nv3007::set_window`/`write_pixels`; (c) `FONT_5X8` upscaled **3× → 15×24** glyphs, landscape, white-on-black, centered; (d) `flush_with_secret_rows` routes secret rows through a **constant-time** path (`secret_text::secret_glyph_cols`, the 96-entry scan + `black_box` barriers, branchless RGB565 expansion) — the "CT comes for free" assumption in the original plan was WRONG (the rasterization is the leaky surface), so it reuses the CT scanner explicitly. Orientation = software rotation onto the proven native coords (flip-X, resolved on bench); SPI ÷8 = 20 MHz (flicker traced to dev-board LED on SCK — see the EVT re-up item above); flicker eliminated by dropping the per-flush full-screen clear. **Bench-validated 2026-06-09:** (1) ✅ the three trusted-UI screen types (confirm / PIN / seed-mnemonic-via-`flush_with_secret_rows`) render correctly on real hardware, AND the **interactive `pin_entry::enter_pin` dialog drives off the physical buttons** (no input-path functionality loss vs the OLED); (2) ✅ **the F-24 constant-time gate PASSES** — new harness `make -C tools/sca glyph-leak` (leaky baseline `max|t|=650.90`→LEAKAGE, the `ct_glyph_col` scan + branchless blit `max|t|=0.00` over 60,173 mem-address samples→flat); independently re-run, exit 0. This also closes the long-standing "F-24 harness (when wired)" TODO and validates the original OLED `ct_glyph_col`. **STILL OPEN:** (3) flip production profile to `ui-lcd` + OLED retirement (demote, keep `oled.rs` as the CT reference + dev tooling — `ui-capture`/`mirror`/`decoy-frames` ride on it).

- [~] **Phase D — decoy-flicker on the LCD: BENCH-TESTED 2026-06-09 @ 20 MHz → DEFENSE FAILS (repaint-limited); re-test @ 40 MHz on the EVT board.** New `make decoy-flicker-lcd-hw` (the `decoy_flicker_test_loop` now SWEEPS `DECOY_HOLD` = 40/25/15/8/3/0 ms, logged). Result on the dev board at the production ÷8 = 20 MHz: **decoys are visible (the words switch) at every hold, including DECOY_HOLD=0.** Root cause differs from the OLED (bistable pixels) — here it's the **repaint speed**: a full seed-page blit is ~20 ms at 20 MHz, above the eye's ~10–13 ms content flicker-fusion threshold, so each decoy frame is registered as a distinct content change regardless of the hold (the hold sits on top of the ~20 ms repaint floor). The LCD's slow pixel RESPONSE (~35 ms) would *help* (a brief decoy wouldn't fully transition) but only if the repaint were fast enough to exploit it. **Lever = repaint speed, not hold:** to get the decoy on-time under ~10 ms you need ~40 MHz (≈11 ms repaint, borderline) + likely DMA and/or repainting only the 3 word rows. 40 MHz on THIS dev board has the LED-on-SCK flicker (confounds the observation), so the clean decisive test is on the **EVT board** (no LED on SCK → ÷4 = 40 MHz clean; see the EVT 40 MHz re-up item). Until then `decoy-frames` stays OFF (default) — it is NOT viable at 20 MHz. If 40 MHz still can't get under the threshold, F-24 stage E Phase 1 is dropped (the sub-channel-4 bus-EM defense is moot if the user can read the decoys). Bonus (unchanged): the LCD's constant-current backlight (3 white LEDs in parallel, ~60 mA, content-independent) structurally weakens the power-rail sub-channel 3, so Phase 2 (constant-pixel-count encoding) stays deferred.

- [ ] **Phase C+ — touch (CST816D) and backlight (VLED) control.** Currently deferred: the wallet UX is button-driven (GPIO-buttons feature), so the CST816D capacitive touch on pins 19-23 of the FPC isn't wired. Backlight VLED+/VLED- can be PWM-controlled for dimming (low-power idle) but the prototype hard-wires to 3V3 for full brightness. Both items are ergonomics, not security — pick up only if user-facing concerns warrant. **Order of priority if revisited**: backlight PWM first (power savings + thermal management — 60 mA × 3.0 V = 180 mW continuous is meaningful in a battery-powered final product), touch second (would replace physical buttons in a future product variant).

- [ ] **Phase C++ — `ui-lcd` becomes a Display backend in its own right (drop the ui-noop pairing).** Phase A pairs `ui-lcd` with `ui-noop` to satisfy the `build.rs` UI-backend check. Phase C implements the Display trait on LCD, but the build check still requires one of `{ui-semihosting, ui-oled, ui-noop}`. After Phase C, update `build.rs` to count `ui-lcd` as the fourth option and drop the pairing requirement. Trivial — one diff to `secure/build.rs` and one to the matching pin test.

- [ ] **F-24 stage E Phase 2 (constant-pixel-count encoding) reconsideration.** Once Phase D validates the LCD-based defense closes sub-channel 4 (bus EM), the question is whether Phase 2 (sub-channel 3, power-rail) is still worth implementing. Per the F-24 stage E analysis, the LCD's constant-current backlight already weakens this channel structurally — pixel transmittance changes affect ambient light, not supply current. If Phase D confirms acceptable defense breadth on the LCD, Phase 2 can stay deferred indefinitely.

- [ ] **(VERY LOW PRIORITY) GPDMA-overlapped blit — the ONLY remaining lever for nebula; NOT needed for clock speed.** ⚠️ Corrected: the original premise here ("80 MHz needs DMA") was WRONG. VALIDATED on the dev board 2026-06-16 — the splash build runs SPI at ÷2 = **80 MHz polled**, blit = **13.2 ms** (the lean byte-wise blit keeps the FIFO fed at the 100 ns/byte rate — the "80 MHz starves the polled FIFO" claim predated that blit), **no flicker** despite the LD2 LED on PE13=SCK. Final committed result: **hyperspace 68 / horizon 46 / nebula 33 fps** (blit ~13 ms). The clock lever is exhausted (80 MHz polled, done) and the compute levers have been worked hard: **unchecked field reads** (37→33 ms) AND **half-resolution intensity / full-res dither** (the deep-research fill-rate lever, APPLIED 2026-06-16) — the latter evaluates bilinear+carve+pow once per 2×2 block (4× fewer) while the cheap Bayer dither+set stay full-res, cutting nebula compute **33→16 ms (21→33 fps)**, image visually ~identical (host-verified bounds + stepping). That split now reveals the floor: of the ~16 ms left, the expensive math is only ~5 ms and the **full-res dither+set is ~11 ms** (inherent — 60 776 Bayer+bit-set ops). The remaining GPDMA lever is now MORE attractive than before: nebula compute (~16 ms) ≈ blit (~13 ms), so GPDMA compute/transfer overlap (`frame = max(compute, blit) + expand`) → nebula ~33 → **~50 fps** (vs the old ~27 projection — the gap closed because compute dropped to near the blit). It still does NOT help hyperspace/horizon (already compute≪blit), and nebula still can't trivially hit 60 (the ~16 ms compute caps it). **GPDMA register specs gathered 2026-06-16 (from `STM32U585.svd`; CMSIS header has NO GPDMA bitfields):** GPDMA1 secure base `0x5002_0000`; channel-x regs at `+0x50 + x*0x80` (CTR1 `+0x40`, CTR2 `+0x44`, CBR1 `+0x48`, CSAR `+0x4C`, CDAR `+0x50`, CLLR `+0x7C`, CCR `+0x14`, CSR `+0x10`, CFCR `+0x0C`); `RCC_AHB1ENR.GPDMA1EN` = bit 0; `SPI_CFG1.TXDMAEN` = bit 15; SPI1_TX request id = **7**. 16-bit SPI items (DSIZE=15) make the frame 60 776 items → fits one `TSIZE` ≤65535, but 121 552 B > the 16-bit GPDMA block size → needs a **2-node linked list** (or block-repeat) for one autonomous fire-and-forget transfer. **De-risk when picked up:** gate behind a `splash-dma` feature (polled stays default + instant fallback); timeout the DMA-done wait so a misconfig is recoverable not a hang; adversarially review every GPDMA register write vs the SVD/RM0456 before first flash; it's the FIRST DMA in the firmware (no precedent), written without HW access → expect a debug iteration or two. Cross-ref Phase D: a sub-10 ms repaint (DMA + 3-row-only blit) is also the decoy-frames lever, so if F-24 stage E Phase 1 is revived this DMA work is shared.

- [ ] **(LOW PRIORITY) Leverage the Chrom-ART (DMA2D) accelerator where it fits — currently UNUSED.** The STM32U585 has Chrom-ART / DMA2D (secure base `0x5002_B000`, `DMA2D_IRQn` 118; full register set: `CR.MODE[2:0]` = R2M-fill / M2M-copy / M2M+PFC / M2M+blend; color modes L4/A4/L8/A8/CLUT/RGB565/ARGB8888) — confirmed present in `stm32u585xx.h`, and `grep` shows **zero** uses in our tree. It is a fixed-function 2D **blitter/converter/blender**, NOT a shader: it can fill rects, copy, convert pixel format, and alpha-blend layers in RAM, but it has **no procedural compute, no scaling/interpolation** (the scaler is the NeoChrom GPU2D, only on U5F/U5G — NOT the U585), and **no dithering** (min input depth 4-bit). So it CANNOT accelerate the procedural splash (the nebula's per-pixel bilinear+carve+dither is exactly what DMA2D can't do — verified 2026-06-16). Where it WOULD earn its keep: the **production trusted-UI compositing path** (`ui::lcd` — glyph blits, background fills, format conversion, layer/icon/QR rendering, any real-UI animation) — classic GUI work that maps directly onto DMA2D fill/copy/blend and would offload the CPU. Not a bottleneck today (partial UI updates < 2 ms), so LOW priority — but the right tool if the trusted-UI rendering ever gets perf-sensitive (richer tx screens, animated UI). When picked up: enable `RCC_AHB1ENR.DMA2DEN`, mind the secure/NS GTZC governance of the new peripheral, and confirm the framebuffer model (DMA2D writes to a RAM buffer, which still needs an SPI-DMA stream to the panel — it composites, it doesn't drive the SPI bus).

**OLED retirement path — ✅ DONE 2026-06-09 (demote, not delete):** `RELEASE_FEATURES` flipped `ui-oled` → `ui-lcd`, so production firmware uses `ui-lcd` exclusively. `ui-oled` is kept as a build option for dev / debug boards that have the SSD1306 OLED instead of the LCD (and because `ui-capture`/`ui-mirror`/`decoy-frames` + the proven CT-reference `secret_text::render_secret_row` ride on it). `oled.rs` is NOT deleted. The `decoy-frames` Cargo feature stays a no-op on the OLED build (bench-documented it doesn't work there); the LCD's slow-response pixels are what make decoy frames viable — re-validating that on the LCD is the remaining Phase D item.

---

### 31. Trezor-derived USB / FW-update hardening

Items sourced from a 2026-05-19 review of `trezor-firmware/core/src/trezor/wire/{codec,thp}` against our stack. Our threat model differs (PIN never on wire, trusted-UI confirms every signature, transactions are public post-broadcast) so much of Trezor's THP (CPace pairing for encrypted wire, CRC-32 per frame, alternating-bit ACK protocol) earns minimal incremental security benefit at high engineering cost — skipped. The items below ARE worth adopting because each closes a concrete failure mode the existing stack doesn't defend against.

#### 31a — USB HID layer (Tier 1, defense-in-depth)

- [x] **Channel-ID isolation in `HidFrameAssembler` — already implemented; add regression test.** Re-audit on 2026-05-19: `process_frame` at `shared/src/apdu_framing.rs:364` ALREADY rejects continuation frames whose channel_id doesn't match the captured one from frame 0 (`if channel != self.channel_id || seq != self.rx_seq { self.reset(); return FrameOutcome::Dropped; }`). The proptest harness exercises this path via `hid_frame_random_input_never_panics` but no explicit positive-/negative-channel-ID test pins the behavior. Action: add a hand-written regression test that constructs frame 0 on channel A, frame 1 on channel B, asserts `FrameOutcome::Dropped` + reset state. ~20 LoC. Done so a future refactor can't accidentally weaken this without the test failing loudly.

(Items 2 + 3 from the initial Tier-1 list moved to §31c after re-audit — see below for rationale.)

#### 31b — Firmware-update trusted-UI confirm (DONE 2026-05-19)

Re-audit on 2026-05-19 corrected the original premise: `secure/src/ui/confirm.rs` was NOT a WIP — it's the production sign-confirm path used by `cmd_sign_userop` / `cmd_sign_userop_batch` / `cmd_sign_offchain`. The "stable again" comment on the `confirm_commit` stub was stale. Wiring the four §31b fields just needed building the page array and calling `ui::confirm::confirm(&pages)`. All four landed:

- [x] **New firmware version**. Manifest's `fw_version` (u32) decoded as four BE bytes → `to vA.B.C.D` on row 1 of page 1 (mirrors Trezor's MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH.BUILD).
- [x] **New firmware fingerprint** as 8 BIP-39 words. `verify::measurement_words_for_inactive_slot` (already plumbed) → 4-row × 2-col layout with `'f'` discriminator per cell (`"1f abcde 5f fghij"` etc.). 5-char truncation safe because BIP-39 English wordlist has unique 4-letter prefixes by spec.
- [x] **Upgrade / downgrade indicator** — `otp::rollback_floor()` vs `manifest.fw_version()` → `UPGRADE` / `SAME VERSION` / `DOWNGRADE!` on row 2 of page 1. Verify chain already rejects downgrades; this is user-visible defense-in-depth.
- [x] **Signing-key fingerprint** as 8 BIP-39 words. Build.rs pre-computes `vendor_pubkey::VENDOR_PK_FPR = SHA-256(pk_seed || pk_root)`; runtime is one `hash_to_word_indices` call → 4-row × 2-col layout with `'k'` discriminator. The `'f'` vs `'k'` discriminator next to each digit means pages 2 and 3 are visually distinguishable at a glance.

Page order: version+direction → fw fingerprint → key fingerprint → final yes/no. User taps R to advance, L to back up; long-R confirms, long-L cancels. Updated host test `positive_confirm_commit_calls_trusted_ui_outside_e2e_test` pins the new behavior (each of the four page builders must be referenced + `rollback_floor()` consulted). The old `negative_confirm_commit_defaults_to_user_cancel_outside_e2e_test` test (which pinned the stub's `false` return) is replaced.

Once these 4 fields are wired, the COMMIT confirm dialog matches Trezor's defense-in-depth: even a valid signature + non-rollback firmware can't install without the user visually approving the version, fingerprint, direction, and signer.

#### 31c — Skipped from Trezor's stack at the USB HID layer (closed)

These two were evaluated for the USB HID transport surface and rejected on a non-FW-update analysis. They don't apply to FW-update wire design either (their failure modes don't manifest on the FW-update path), so they're truly closed.

- **Frame-arrival timeout in `HidFrameAssembler`** (initially proposed as a Tier-1 add). Re-audit 2026-05-19: a malicious host CAN wedge the assembler by sending frame 0 then no continuation, but `process_frame` at line 336 unconditionally overwrites assembler state on ANY subsequent seq=0 frame, so the wedge can't persist past the next legitimate stream. The wedged bytes in `rx_buf` aren't exposed to dispatch (only `ApduComplete` returns a slice), so no info leak. No multi-stream state means no resource exhaustion. The "real" failure mode is "next legitimate seq=1 from the original host gets dropped, host retries from frame 0" — that's UX-equivalent to any USB transient, not a security event. Skipping. **No.**

- **Per-channel rate limit at USB transport** (initially proposed as a Tier-1 add). Re-audit 2026-05-19: USB FS bulk caps at ~1200 frames/sec at 64 B, our frame-processing is constant-time + statically allocated, CPU ceiling sits at ~6%. The device is in the user's hand; unplug is recovery. Rate-limiting at this layer would just add code without changing what's already bounded. Skipping. **No.**

#### 31d — Deferred: re-evaluate during FW-update-over-wire design

The four items below were rejected for the USB HID transport surface (sign-command path) but the FW-update-over-wire flow is a different threat model — confidentiality of release metadata, signing-key risk model, chunk-stream integrity, replay semantics for chunk delivery all read differently when the surface is "shipping a new firmware binary" rather than "signing a transaction the user just confirmed on-device". KEEP these items open for explicit re-evaluation when the FW-update wire flow gets designed in detail. Each entry documents the original rejection rationale AND the questions to revisit.

- [x] **CPace pairing / encrypted USB wire.** **RE-EVALUATED 2026-05-28 → REJECT (confirmed).** The threats a PAKE-encrypted channel addresses are already covered: MITM-substituting-a-tx is defeated by trusted-UI clear-signing (every sig confirmed on-device with decoded intent); eavesdropping is moot since our wire payloads (sign requests + sigs) are on-chain-public within seconds; FW-update downgrade-replay is OTP-blocked + manifest forgery is signature-blocked (a hub observer learns only version/timing metadata = privacy-marginal). The one genuinely-confidential case (per-device personalisation / factory attestation manifest / serial-binding tokens) is a provisioning-time concern in a controlled factory, not the runtime protocol. Cost ~2-3 weeks + a wire-format change (companion rework) + session-key/replay machinery — premature for a device whose wire output is public by design. Revisit only if the product later carries confidential non-on-chain data over USB. (Original 2026-05-19 rejection stands.)
- [x] **CRC-32 per HID frame.** **RE-EVALUATED 2026-05-28 → REJECT (confirmed), and doubly moot.** (1) The USB PHY already CRC-16s every packet at the bus level → app-layer CRC is redundant for the realistic corruption source. (2) FW-update's COMMIT re-hashes the written flash vs the signed manifest hash (`verify_images`), so any corruption that slips the PHY CRC is caught before the image is trusted. (3) It wouldn't even help the flaky-cable failure mode we actually hit this session — those are *dropped* frames (read timeouts), not *corrupted* frames; CRC detects corruption, not absence, so the reassembly/response **timeouts** (landed `0d59b5f`/`1059296`) are the right tool. (4) The "fail earlier → less flash wear" angle is weak: the slot erase is at BEGIN (before any chunk), so per-frame CRC would only save some mid-stream chunk *writes*, not the erase. Net: marginal gain for a wire-format change. (Original 2026-05-19 rejection stands.)
- [ ] **Vendor key hierarchy** (SatoshiLabs root → vendor key → firmware key). Original rejection (2026-05-19): single SPHINCS+C10 fwsign key is fine for launch; complexity without near-term threat reduction. **FW-update re-eval questions**: (a) What's our CI key compromise recovery plan? Today: single key compromised → adversary signs malicious updates that pass on-device verification → §31b confirm dialog catches it visually (key fingerprint mismatch) but only if user actually compares fingerprints. (b) Trezor's solution: root key (offline, rarely used) signs vendor keys; vendor key compromise → root key signs revocation that ships in next firmware. Circular dependency: device can't learn about revocation without an update. Trezor solves by baking multiple vendor keys + minimum-N policy. (c) Worth the complexity for a single-key launch wallet? Probably yes if we're shipping at 5k units — that's enough to make CI key rotation operationally necessary at some point.
- [~] **Alternating-bit ACK protocol.** **RE-EVALUATED 2026-05-28 → REJECT the full ACK machine; the useful slice is companion-side.** A full alternating-bit ACK protocol is a wire-format change for marginal benefit (per-command finality + on-chain counter bumps already defeat replay; the manifest-hash COMMIT-time recheck verifies the complete image). The ONE worthwhile adjacent improvement — letting the companion resume a flaky FW-update from the last-good chunk instead of retrying from BEGIN — is implementable purely **companion-side** via the existing `CMD_FW_STATUS` (which already returns per-image received-length progress); no firmware/wire-format change. Tagged `[~]` (not `[x]`) only because that companion-side resume helper isn't written yet — but it's not firmware work.

When work on FW-update-over-wire design begins, walk through each `[ ]` item above, answer the questions, and either land the change or move the item to §31c (truly closed) with refreshed rationale.

---

### 30. Factory production-line test (prodtest) firmware

**Status:** Phases A through G landed 2026-05-19 except Phase F's photos which need a hardware-on-bench session. Phase F's text contribution (troubleshooting matrix, pre-flight checklist, TODO photo markers, operator wallchart narrative) landed in the same commit. The prodtest main-loop short-circuit runs the final-stage init (`usb_hw::init` + `setup_systick` + DWT) then calls `boot_ns::boot` so the NS USB stack comes up and routes `INS_V2_PRODTEST_*` codes to the CMSE veneers. All 10 commands are reachable over USB. Hardware bring-up is now blocked only on the user's hardware-on-bench session.

Goal: a single-purpose firmware the factory operator flashes BEFORE `factory_provisioning`. It validates every hardware component (OLED, SAES, BHK, TRNG, flash, eventually OPTIGA/SE050/USB/button) via USB-driven commands so the fixture can decide per-component pass/fail. Defective chips set aside BEFORE the one-way provisioning ceremony runs. Justified at the 5k-unit production target the project is sized for — RMA + yield loss costs dominate the prodtest dev cost.

**Phase A — Architecture (DONE)**: new `prodtest` Cargo feature, `secure/src/nsc/prodtest.rs` module with command handlers, 6 CMSE veneers in `secure/src/nsc/mod.rs::nsc_prodtest_*`, main.rs short-circuit that replaces wizard/unlock with "PRODTEST READY" + WFI, `make build-hw-prodtest` target, compile fence (mirrors factory_provisioning's opt-in pattern), `docs/provisioning/factory-prodtest.md` operator manual + command reference, `tools/factory-prodtest-runner.py` Python scaffold. 6 CMD_PRODTEST_* constants in `proto/src/lib.rs` (IDs 100-105). 4 host pin-tests in the module.

**Phase B — Compute-only commands (DONE)**: SAES_SELFTEST (returns DHUK fingerprint via `hw::saes::encrypt_ecb_block` under `saes-dhuk`), BHK_SELFTEST (BHK fingerprint under `bhk + saes-dhuk`), FLASH_RW (stub — designated test page helpers not yet wired; returns InternalError so fixture knows to skip), TRNG_SAMPLE (1..=256 bytes from MCU TRNG via `rng::fill`, no SE XOR mix). All four reuse existing primitives so test surface is minimal.

- [x] **Phase C — Communication tests (DONE)**. Three new commands at IDs 106-108: `CMD_PRODTEST_OPTIGA_HANDSHAKE` (lazy `OptigaTrustM::init()` + `GetRandom(16)` over the plain APDU path, no PBS / no shielded connection — catches missing chip / broken solder / wedged I²C / wrong RST line / power-rail issues / chip RNG defect); `CMD_PRODTEST_SE050_HANDSHAKE` (lazy `Se050::init()` with NXP default SCP03 platform keys + `GetRandom(16)` — catches missing chip / wrong ENA line / cold-boot timing / pre-rotated SCP03 keys / RNG defect); `CMD_PRODTEST_USB_LOOPBACK` (echo N≤256 bytes — catches USB TX corruption / HID fragmentation bugs / buffer overflow). All three: 3 new dispatch arms + 3 new CMSE veneers in `secure/src/nsc/mod.rs`, 3 new handlers in `secure/src/nsc/prodtest.rs` (using existing `crate::SE.optiga.random()` / `crate::SE.se050.random()` primitives so test surface is minimal). Host fixture runner gets `test_optiga_handshake` / `test_se050_handshake` / `test_usb_loopback` (with deterministic XOR-with-0xA5 pattern for byte-identity verification + first-mismatch-offset diagnostics). Pin tests pin `OPTIGA_HANDSHAKE_RNG_LEN = SE050_HANDSHAKE_RNG_LEN = 16` and `USB_LOOPBACK_MAX = 256`. 4 build paths still clean.

- [x] **Phase D — Button test (DONE)**. `CMD_PRODTEST_BUTTON_TEST` (109): 3-step LEFT → RIGHT → BOTH sequence, 10 s per step, returns 4 bytes (step_status:1 + reserved:3). Compact nibble encoding: upper=step (1/2/3), lower=error (1=timeout, 2=wrong button = swapped wires). Wrong-button detection added on advisor recommendation — diagnostically distinct from timeout (swapped solder vs dead solder are different fixes). No latency reporting (human reaction time dominates GPIO timing). `gpio-buttons` added to the `prodtest = [...]` feature; `hw::buttons::{left_pressed, right_pressed, busy_wait_ms}` promoted from private to `pub(crate)`. Button init wired into the prodtest short-circuit in `secure/src/main.rs`. Host runner gets `test_button_test` with `BUTTON_STEP_DECODE` table that maps each status code to operator-readable diagnostic. 3 new pin-tests pin the step-code layout, timeout, and output length. 4 build paths still clean; 1847/1847 default-features host tests pass.

- [x] **Phase E — Host-side fixture runner + USB launch fix (DONE)**. **Firmware:** the prodtest main-loop short-circuit was halting in WFI before `boot_ns::boot`, so the NS USB stack never started. Rewired to run the final-stage init that NS world depends on (`hw::usb_hw::init`, `setup_systick`, DWT cycle counter) then call `boot_ns::boot(NS_FLASH_BASE)` — same exit primitive the production main flow uses. **Per-CMD INS allocation:** added 10 `INS_V2_PRODTEST_*` constants (0x80-0x89) in `proto/src/lib.rs` — one INS per command (no 4-byte cmd_id overhead inside the APDU body; full LC=255 budget per command). **NS-side wiring:** added 10 extern `nsc_prodtest_*` declarations in `nonsecure/src/nsc_api.rs` plus transport-side wrappers + public API. **NS USB dispatcher:** added 10 `#[cfg(feature = "prodtest")]` match arms in `nonsecure/src/usb/commands.rs` plus 10 `cmd_prodtest_*` methods that wrap the appropriate `nsc_api` call. New `prodtest_finalize(n, status)` helper writes output to `RESP_BUF[..n]` + 2-byte SW (`SW_OK = 0x9000` on Ok, `SW_INTERNAL_ERROR = 0x6F00` otherwise). USB_LOOPBACK capped at 254 B (255 - 2 B SW headroom inside the 256 B RESP_BUF). **Nonsecure Cargo:** new `prodtest = ["stm32u585", "usb"]` feature; Makefile `build-hw-prodtest` updated. **Python host runner:** `ProdtestTransport` rewritten with real APDU-over-HID framing — `_build_apdu` (CLA=0xF0 + INS + P1=0 + P2=0 + LC + data), `_frame_apdu` (chan/tag/seq/total_len header, 57 B first chunk + 59 B continuation chunks, mirrors `webhid_test.html`), `_read_response` (reassemble incoming frames per `shared/src/apdu_framing.rs::HidFrameAssembler`). Linux hidapi quirk handled (leading 0x00 report-ID byte on `write`, per advisor). Status code mapping: SW_OK → STATUS_OK = 0; other SW values pass through unchanged so error logs preserve the wire SW. 60 s read timeout to accommodate BUTTON_TEST's ~30 s budget. **4 build paths pass** (default, prodtest+dev-testkey, prodtest+factory-production-irreversible-im-sure, prodtest-no-opt-in→compile error). 1847/1847 default host tests still pass. `make build-hw-prodtest` builds both secure + nonsecure clean. Python `--help` works; AST clean. Target NOT silicon-validated by intent — awaits hardware-on-bench session.

- [x] **Phase F — Operator manual production-ready text (DONE)**; **photos pending hardware-on-bench session**. Added "Pre-flight checklist" (5 items: fixture USB cable golden-unit verify, probe-rs flash speed, operator station lighting, anti-static wristband continuity, defective-unit bin labeled), "Troubleshooting matrix" (every per-command failure mode mapped to likely root cause + operator action — reseat / rewire / re-solder / set aside / REPORT VENDOR), and TODO photo markers (`<!-- TODO photo: ... -->`) at each visual-aid moment in the manual. Per advisor: markers describe what the photo should show; they do NOT paraphrase the visual into prose (descriptions of UI states age into wrong-but-shippable docs the moment the firmware changes a glyph). Photos land in a follow-up when the user runs a hardware-on-bench session with a USB-C camera capturing the fixture display.

- [x] **Phase G — Compile fence (DONE)**. Same opt-in pattern as factory_provisioning: refuses to build `prodtest + bhk` or `prodtest + optiga-lock-operational` or `prodtest` (without `dev-testkey` / `otp-hardcoded-master-key`) unless `factory-production-irreversible-im-sure` is also set. Empirically validated by attempting the four build combinations.

- [ ] **Flash test page helpers** (`hw::flash::test_page_*`). The Phase B FLASH_RW command currently returns InternalError because the designated test page + write/read/verify helpers aren't carved out. Implementation: pick a flash page in bank 1 that's outside both SLOT_A/B regions and the existing reserved pages (123-127), add helpers `test_page_erase()`, `test_page_write(pattern)`, `test_page_verify(pattern)`. ~1-2 hours.

- [ ] **Production prodtest build profile** — once Phases C-E land, define a `make build-hw-prodtest-production` Makefile target that enables `prodtest,saes-dhuk,bhk,factory-production-irreversible-im-sure` for the full Tier-1 + Tier-2 self-test coverage. Current Phase A target uses `dev-testkey` for safe iteration; production replaces that with real OTP+BHK paths.

### 29. Factory provisioning ceremony — single-purpose firmware the factory operator runs

**Status:** Phase A (scaffold) landed 2026-05-19 in commit (this commit). Phase A is a buildable factory image the user can ship to the factory; subsequent phases are deferred to before real production runs. NOT YET silicon-validated by intent — Phase A is the "send to factory for trial flash + iterate on error reports" milestone.

Goal: a single firmware build the factory operator flashes to a fresh device, runs once, and either ships ("FACTORY OK") or sets aside and reports a numbered code ("FACTORY FAIL @ STEP X/6 EXXXX"). Operator does not need to be a developer; they read the OLED, photograph the failure code, send the photo back. Vendor (us) maintains an error-code → diagnosis table in `docs/provisioning/factory-provisioning.md`.

**Phase A — scaffold (DONE)**: new `factory-provisioning` Cargo feature, `secure/src/factory_provisioning.rs` state machine with 6 steps (HW selftest → OTP master → pre-populated-state check → dual-SE provision → wipe dummy user state → post-wipe validation) + 12 distinct error codes covering the failure surface, OLED display helpers, `make build-hw-factory-provisioning` target, full operator manual `docs/provisioning/factory-provisioning.md`. 5 host pin-tests inside the module (step count vs enum, format-string layout, hex codes all in [0-9A-F], hint length fits OLED row, step labels fit OLED row, codes are pairwise distinct). Implementation pattern: provision-with-deterministic-zero + immediate `factory_reset_admin` to wipe the dummy user state, leaving SE infrastructure (SCP03, admin UserID, PBS, F1Dx metadata) in place. The zero user state never leaves the secure world.

- [ ] **Phase B — silicon trial run (next session with hardware in front of you).** Flash `make build-hw-factory-provisioning` to a fresh-state board (after `make wipe-for-wizard` to clean any prior state), power-cycle, observe the OLED. Expected output sequence: 6 `[X/6] STEPNAME` panels then a `FACTORY OK` panel. Any failure surfaces a numbered code; map the code back to its FactoryErrorCode variant and decide whether it's a real production-blocking issue or a bench-only artifact. Estimated effort: ~½ day including the inevitable first-fix-on-silicon cycle.

- [ ] **Phase C — SE driver refactor: split admin-only provisioning from user-state provisioning.** Today's `WalletStore::provision()` does both admin setup AND user object storage in one call. Phase A works around this with the provision-then-wipe pattern, but the wipe burns flash cycles + leaves transient residue inside the secure world. A clean refactor: add `Se050::factory_provision()` (admin + SCP03 + canary, no user objects) and `Optiga::factory_provision()` (PBS + F1Dx metadata + E120 LUC, no user data) — both called from `DualSecureElement::factory_provision()`. Then `factory_provisioning.rs::step_dual_se_provision_infrastructure` calls the dedicated path, no wipe step needed, and the post-wipe validation becomes redundant (step 5 + 6 collapse into a single sanity check). Estimated effort: ~1-2 days incl. host tests + on-silicon revalidation.

- [ ] **Phase D — `optiga-lock-operational` + SE050 admin UserID lock.** Currently the factory firmware keeps OPTIGA OIDs at `LcsO=Creation` (rewriteable) — same as dev. Production should bump to `LcsO=Operational` (irreversible) once Phase B + C have validated the ceremony works end-to-end. The lock makes subsequent metadata writes silently fail (chip rejects) — useful as a tamper signal but unrecoverable. Lock semantics: `optiga-lock-operational` Cargo feature (already exists, currently off); flip on for factory builds AFTER bench validation. Similarly the SE050 admin UserID's `max_attempts=0` is already permanent at provisioning time, but a deliberate post-Phase-C audit confirms no other admin-residue paths exist.

- [ ] **Phase E — §22 attestation manifest burn at factory.** When work-todo §22 (SLH-DSA-128s factory manifest + triple-UID binding) is ready, the factory ceremony grows a step 7: read 3 UIDs (STM32 + SE050 + OPTIGA), present them to the offline factory HSM for signing, burn the signed manifest into the device (SE050 + OPTIGA + STM32 OTP anchor). Adds attestability without changing the SE infrastructure flow. Pair with `make build-hw-factory-provisioning-attest` if the manifest path isn't a default-on feature.

- [ ] **Phase F — production OTP-master burn (drop `dev-testkey` from the factory build).** Current `factory-provisioning` Cargo feature implies `dev-testkey` (deterministic OTP master, constant across boards — useful for bring-up). Real production needs `hw::otp::ensure_device_master()` to burn 32 random bytes from TRNG on first boot of each device. The boot path already does this when `dev-testkey` is OFF; the factory ceremony just needs to validate it ran successfully (currently a TODO in `step_otp_master_key`). Estimated effort: ~half day including silicon validation of a non-`dev-testkey` factory run.

- [ ] **`flash-hw-factory-provisioning` Makefile target.** Currently the build target exists; the corresponding flash+run target is a TODO referenced in the build target's epilogue. Mirror the `flash-hw-dual-se-oled-standalone` pattern (probe-rs download + STM32_Programmer_CLI option-byte config + probe-rs run for the post-flash semihosting). Phase B will need this anyway.

- [ ] **Validation cycle: `wipe-for-wizard` + factory rerun.** Confirm that after a wipe (`make wipe-for-wizard`), the factory firmware can re-run successfully on the same device. Today's pre-populated-state check (step 3) rejects any chip with user state; a wiped chip should pass through cleanly. Bench-validate this loop before the factory operator starts seeing intermittent reruns.

## Trezor critical-port pass (2026-06-30) — defense-in-depth from `docs/architecture/trezor-comparison-critical-port-2026-06.md`

Top 3 (rr-1/sca-3/tz-2) hand-verified vs source. Full rationale + file:line cites + "considered & rejected" ledger live in that doc's "Proposed work-todo rows" section (durable home if this list is ever reverted).
