### 32. Duress PIN → decoy wallet (plausible deniability, Keycard-pattern)

**Status:** SCOPED 2026-05-20; **feasibility VALIDATED on silicon 2026-05-20** (`make duress-probe-hw`, PASS on real B-U585I-IOT02A + OPTIGA Trust M + SE050). The probe confirmed every load-bearing assumption, all at LcsO=Creation (nothing locked, fully recoverable): (1) a SECOND OPTIGA AuthRef at F1D8 with `Execute=ALW` (no E120/LUC binding) provisions cleanly **without moving E120**; (2) HMAC-auth against F1D8 succeeds and **leaves E120 at 0** — a duress AuthRef with no LUC binding never bumps the silicon lockout counter; (3) the real F1D0 still bumps E120 (0→1) on wrong PIN + resets on correct PIN with F1D8 present — adding the duress credential didn't break the real lockout; (4) a SECOND SE050 UserID (`max_attempts=0`, unlimited) provisions + auths alongside the real one. The probe lives behind the `duress-probe-e2e` feature (`OptigaTrustM::probe_provision_duress_authref` / `probe_hmac_auth_at`, `Se050::probe_provision_and_auth_duress_userid`) + `make duress-probe-hw`. **Implication for the counter/wipe open question (P3):** since F1D8 auth is decoupled from E120, the duress path itself never drifts the real E120 — the only drift source is the always-run real-F1D0 verify failing during a duress entry (the timing-uniformity requirement), exactly as analysed. Implementation (P1/P3/P4) not started. Supersedes §23's "BIP-39 passphrase" line as the chosen deniability mechanism — same goal ($5-wrench / coercion defence), but a second numeric PIN reusing the existing PIN-entry UI instead of an ASCII passphrase keyboard. Modelled on Keycard's duress PIN (`keycard-shell/app/keycard/keycard.c`): a second PIN unlocks a decoy wallet; always-provisioned (random if the user declines) so its presence reveals nothing.

**Decoy-entropy model: SEPARATE entropy (user decision 2026-05-20, the stronger option).** The duress credential gates a DISTINCT XOR-split entropy; the real seed never enters SRAM during a duress session. Robust even against an attacker who duress-unlocks then dumps SRAM. Bonus: this *removes* the "duress flag must flow through every NSC handler" requirement that the same-entropy model would have needed — handlers just operate on whichever entropy got loaded, and the decoy entropy produces only the decoy wallet through the unchanged derivation path.

**Design:**
- **Two SE credentials per chip.** Real PIN = existing lockout-protected credential (SE050 UserID with `max_attempts`, OPTIGA F1D0 AuthRef bound to E120 LUC). Duress PIN = a SECOND credential provisioned with **unlimited attempts** (SE050 `max_attempts=0` like the admin UserID; OPTIGA AuthRef WITHOUT the E120-LUC lockout binding) so it never itself triggers the wipe. Real PIN ≠ duress PIN enforced at setup.
- **Two XOR-split entropies.** Real: `real_half_o` (OPTIGA, gated by real AuthRef) ⊕ `real_half_e` (SE050, gated by real UserID). Decoy: `decoy_half_o` ⊕ `decoy_half_e` under the duress credentials. Invariant #1 (neither chip alone reveals a seed) holds for BOTH entropies.
- **Timing-uniform unlock (mandatory).** `gated_unlock` must run BOTH credential verifies on every unlock — never short-circuit on duress success — else the real path's extra SE round-trip lets a coercion attacker *time* which PIN is real and defeat the deniability. Select which entropy to release from which credential matched; discard the unused verify's result.
- **Counter / wipe — semantics (locked) + drift fix (open design decision).**
  - *Lockout semantics (settled):* an entered PIN is classified by which credential it matches. **Real PIN → real wallet, counter resets. Duress PIN → decoy wallet, counter ALSO resets** (a coerced user must enter it smoothly + repeatedly; it must NOT bump or wipe). **Neither (genuinely wrong) → both verifies fail → MCU page-124 counter stays bumped.** The "bump" is keyed on matching *neither* credential, so a correctly-entered duress PIN never counts toward lockout. **10 consecutive genuinely-wrong → `trigger_lockout_wipe()` → `factory_reset_admin`, identical to today.** The unlimited duress credential (`max_attempts=0` / `Execute=ALW`) does NOT weaken brute-force protection: a brute-forcer hits "neither" on every random guess → the MCU master counter climbs → wipe at 10; the unlimited credential never helps an attacker who doesn't know the duress PIN (and if they do, it just opens the decoy). The wipe destroys **both** wallets' state — the real is recoverable from the 24-word seed; the **decoy (separate random entropy) is NOT recoverable**, so any bait funds are lost on wipe and a re-provisioned device gets a *new* decoy (acceptable for a sacrifice wallet, inherent to the separate-entropy model).
  - *Timing-channel measured on silicon 2026-05-20 (`make duress-timing-hw`):* one OPTIGA HMAC verify = **~160 ms** (157–167, n=15), one SE050 UserID verify = **~103 ms** (102.6–105.4), so the skip-vs-run "extra real verify" delta = **~263 ms, ~6% jitter**. Unlock is SE-bound (the ~1–3 s C10 keygen runs later on first sign/address, NOT during unlock), so 263 ms is a large, low-jitter, **observable** fraction of unlock time. **Conclusion: timing uniformity IS load-bearing** — the advisor's "verifies are ~10–50 ms so just skip the real verify" path is OFF the table (the verifies are 2–3× slower than assumed). We cannot skip the real verify on a duress match without leaking ~263 ms.
  - *Drift fix — three options, the data narrows it to two real ones:*
    - **(A) Run both verifies every unlock (perfect timing uniformity, real drift).** The real F1D0/UserID verify runs on a duress entry (fails) → bumps the real counters → drift. Fix the drift by binding the reset to the duress credential: OPTIGA E120 `Change = Auto(F1D0) OR Auto(F1D8)` (validate the reset-via-F1D8 path on silicon), and accept SE050 user UserID reduced to **informational** (it can't be reset by another credential — silicon has no such primitive) → three-way lockstep becomes **two-of-three** (MCU + OPTIGA E120). Stronger on timing, weaker on the direct-I2C-attacker defence.
    - **(B) Skip the real verify on a duress match + busy-wait a calibrated ~263 ms ± jitter (approximate timing uniformity, NO drift).** The real verify NEVER runs on a duress entry → no counter touched → **full three-way lockstep preserved**, no E120 metadata change, no SE050 weakening, simpler. Cost: the dummy delay must match the real-verify distribution (~263 ms mean, ~6% jitter) or a residual remains; mitigate by drawing the delay from the measured distribution and/or adding randomized jitter to BOTH paths. Stronger on lockout integrity, slightly weaker on timing perfection (~±10 ms residual vs A's perfect match).
    - **(C) Skip + no padding (~263 ms leak) — REJECTED** (defeats the deniability the feature exists for).
    **DECIDED 2026-05-20: option B.** Spec check (`plug-and-trust` AN12413-derived `se05x_APDU_apis.h`) confirmed SE050's `MAX_ATTEMPTS` is **creation-only** ("only when the object identifier is not in use yet") and there is **no `POLICY_OBJ_*` bit / APDU to reset one auth object's attempt counter via another credential** — so A's SE050 drift is genuinely unfixable and A is forced to reduce SE050 to informational (two-of-three). B avoids the drift on both chips and keeps full three-way lockstep. The old MCU-only-wipe "alternative" is subsumed by A and moot.

  - *B — minimizing the timing residual (the "real-operation pad"):* do NOT pad with a NOP busy-wait (that residual = "my fixed-delay estimate vs reality"). Instead, on a duress match, run a **second verify against the duress credential** as the pad — a real SE operation of the same type, with the same natural jitter, that touches only the unlimited duress counter (harmless, no drift). Structure: (1) verify duress credential FIRST; (2a) if it SUCCEEDS → decoy unlock + run a second duress verify as the pad (skip the real verify entirely → real counters untouched); (2b) if it FAILS → run the real verify (real PIN → real wallet + counter resets; wrong PIN → real counter bumps → lockout). Every path runs exactly two SE verifies → timing uniform; the real counters are touched only on real/wrong entries, never on duress → no drift. **SE050 side is a perfect match** (duress UserID verify and user UserID verify are the identical operation, both ~103 ms). **OPTIGA side — plain-vs-auto-state delta MEASURED on silicon 2026-05-20 (`make duress-timing-hw`):** F1D8 **plain** verify = ~160 ms (157.6–167.2, n=15); F1D0 **auto-state/LUC** verify = ~149 ms (147.2–151.0, n=15); **delta ~10.4 ms** (auto-state is *faster* — leaner Trezor 16 B-nonce shape vs the plain path's 64 B compound input). So a simple second-plain-F1D8 pad leaves a **~11 ms CONSISTENT residual** (duress path runs ~11 ms slower) — a 24× reduction from the raw 263 ms, and practically unexploitable for coercion (an attacker can't calibrate a baseline without already knowing which PIN is which), but consistent (not jitter) so a precision rig averaging many unlocks could in principle detect it. **DECIDED 2026-05-20: matched-LUC duress credential** (null the residual). Bind the duress F1D8 to its OWN LUC counter at a second LUC slot (OPTIGA has 4: E120–E123 → use e.g. E121) so the duress verify uses the same **auto-state** path (~149 ms) as the real F1D0 verify → the pad is a byte-for-byte timing twin → ~0 residual. The duress LUC is **unenforced** (never `curr>=limit`-checked) — it just counts harmlessly. No drift: the real E120 is only touched on real/wrong entries; the duress LUC absorbs the duress-path bumps. Cost: one extra LUC counter object + the duress credential uses the auto-state path (both within P2 provisioning). The simple-plain pad (~11 ms residual) was the rejected fallback.
  - *Dual-LUC coexistence — VALIDATED ON SILICON 2026-05-20 (`make duress-probe-hw` step 10, PASS).* The matched-LUC config (F1D8 `Execute=LUC(E121)` re-provisioned alongside F1D0 `Execute=LUC(E120)`) was the one untested OPTIGA AC expression; the probe now confirms all three load-bearing assumptions on a real B-U585I + OPTIGA Trust M: **(a) two LUC-bound AuthRefs coexist** at LcsO=Creation — F1D8 re-provisioned from plain to `Execute=LUC(E121)` cleanly (Change=ALW, never locked); **(b) bump isolation holds** — one duress auto-state verify moved E121 0→1 while the real E120 stayed at 15 (zero drift on the real lockout counter); **(c) timing twin holds** — F1D8 auto-state mean **22428 µs** vs F1D0 auto-state **21598 µs**, ~830 µs delta, well inside the ~10 ms per-verify jitter band → the option-B pad is a byte-for-byte timing twin and the ~1179 µs plain-vs-auto-state residual is nulled. Probe scaffolding: `OptigaTrustM::probe_provision_duress_authref_luc` / `probe_read_counter` + apdu `build_metadata_auth_ref_luc_oid` / `build_metadata_pin_ctr_oid` (the latter two double as the P1 production builders). The matched-LUC layout is now safe to bake into P1/P2 code. *(Absolute per-verify cost here is ~22 ms OPTIGA / ~104 ms SE050, not the ~149 ms placeholder previously written — corrected.)*

**Phases (medium-large, multi-session — touches the validated three-way PIN lockstep + provisioning ceremony):**
- [x] **P1 — derivation + provisioning of a second entropy — LANDED 2026-05-20 (`duress-pin` feature).** `crypto::provision_from_mnemonic` now takes `duress_pin: Option<&[u8;8]>` and, after the real wallet, generates a FRESH independent 256-bit decoy entropy (separate-entropy model: STM32 TRNG ⊕ SE-combined TRNG) and provisions a decoy wallet via the new `WalletStore::provision_duress`. **Always-provision**: `None` (user declined) provisions a decoy with a fresh random 8-byte PIN, so "duress configured vs not" is indistinguishable on-chip. Host tests pin the always-provision + independent-entropy + invariant-#1-on-the-decoy properties (`negative_dual_se_duress_no_full_entropy_to_a_single_chip`, `positive_crypto_duress_always_provisions_decoy`). All 1916 secure-crate host tests pass.
- [x] **P2 — second SE credential (storage) — LANDED 2026-05-20 (`duress-pin`).** Coexistence feasibility was proven on silicon (`make duress-probe-hw`); production storage now wired: `WalletStore::provision_duress` (default no-op; real impls on OPTIGA / SE050 / DualSE). OPTIGA `store_duress_objects` provisions E121 matched-LUC counter + F1D8 AuthRef (`Execute=LUC(E121)`) + 4 decoy data OIDs (F1D9/F1DA/F1DB/F1D6) gated on F1D8, never locking (stays LcsO=Creation). SE050 `store_duress_objects` provisions `DURESS_USERID_OBJ` (max_attempts=0, unlimited) + 3 decoy data objects, all carrying the admin-delete policy so `factory_reset_admin` reclaims them; includes an admin-auth stale sweep for idempotent re-provision. DualSE XOR-splits the decoy entropy (fresh 3-source half) and dispatches to both. `is_provisioned()` stays real-only; `duress_is_provisioned()` is the new diagnostic. Builds clean across `duress-pin` / `duress-probe-e2e` (no duress-pin) / `mock-se+ui` matrices. **Remaining for P2:** (a) ~~on-silicon validation of the FULL production `provision_duress`~~ — **DONE 2026-05-20 (`make duress-provision-hw`, PASS).** Provisioned a real wallet + an independent decoy via the production `provision`/`provision_duress` path with a known decoy entropy, read both decoy halves (OPTIGA F1D9 via F1D8 auto-state auth + SE050 `DURESS_ENTROPY_OBJ` via duress UserID auth): `half_o XOR half_e` == known decoy entropy (production XOR-split correct end-to-end); the decoy read bumped ONLY E121 (16→17), real E120 untouched at 15; real wallet still unlocked with the real PIN. E121 idempotency-skip worked; all at LcsO=Creation. (b) ~~re-provision idempotency~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-05-20 (`make duress-provision-hw` steps 15–19, PASS).** The feared brick was a false alarm: the duress data OIDs use `Change=Auto(F1D8) OR Conf(E140)` (via `build_metadata_protected`), so the shielded-connection `Conf(E140)` arm rewrites them on re-provision exactly like the real OIDs — no F1D8 auth needed. E121's `Change=Auto(F1D8)` (no Conf escape) is handled by the idempotency-skip (metadata persists across a wipe → skipped) + the high `DURESS_CTR_LIMIT` (stale current value is unenforced). `factory_reset_body` now ALSO best-effort blanks the duress OIDs (F1D8/F1D9/F1DA/F1DB/F1D6) for hygiene/parity (E121 left intact). Silicon-validated end-to-end: wipe (`factory_reset_admin`) → re-provision real + a NEW decoy entropy/PIN (mnemonic-change case) → read both halves back → reconstruct the NEW entropy → `gated_unlock(new duress)` + `gated_unlock(real)` both work. (c) **Provisioning wall-time** roughly doubles under `duress-pin` (E121 + F1D8 + 4 decoy OIDs on OPTIGA, each with the hard-reset dance, + the SE050 duress objects) — expect the first-boot wizard to run noticeably longer. The unlock-side dispatch is P3.
- [x] **P3 — timing-uniform dual-verify `gated_unlock`** + the counter/wipe design decision above. **DONE 2026-05-20 — implemented + advisor-reviewed (diff) + SILICON-VALIDATED (`make duress-provision-hw` steps 10–14, PASS on real B-U585I + OPTIGA + SE050):** `gated_unlock(duress)` → decoy master through the production dispatch with the real E120 untouched (real verify genuinely skipped, no drift); `gated_unlock(real)` → real master (falls through after the duress verify fails); `gated_unlock(wrong)` → rejected; real unlock recovers after a wrong+duress sequence. Landed: `WalletStore::unlock_duress` (default `Err(PinIncorrect)`) + `duress_pad` (default no-op); `DualSE::unlock_duress` reads both decoy halves via the production `duress_read_half` (promoted from e2e gating) with NO short-circuit (both chips always verified → uniform op-count), XOR-reconstructs the decoy entropy, caches the decoy entropy-blob, returns the decoy master; `DualSE::duress_pad` runs one duress verify per chip; OPTIGA/SE050 `duress_verify` (verify-only); `gated_unlock` feature-gated duress-first dispatch (try `unlock_duress`; on Ok run `duress_pad` + return; else fall through to real `unlock`) preserving the existing FI hardening + the MCU pre-commit/reset (a duress-correct unlock resets the MCU counter exactly like a real success). Both correct paths execute 4 SE verifies + 2 reads → uniform total latency. 2 new source-grep host tests; 1918 host tests pass; builds clean across duress-pin / duress-provision-e2e / mock-se. **Advisor-reviewed 2026-05-20 (diff); two fixes landed (`0fc97ff`):** (i) **E121 lifetime lockout** — the duress F1D8 verify fires LUC(E121) on EVERY unlock, INCLUDING the failed duress verify `unlock_duress` runs on a real-PIN entry (the no-short-circuit timing structure); that bump is UNRESETTABLE (E121.Change=Auto(F1D8) needs a *successful* F1D8 auth), so E121 climbs ~1/unlock regardless of duress use. The load-bearing fix is the high limit `DURESS_CTR_LIMIT = 0x00FF_FFFF` (~460 yr @ 100 unlocks/day); a duress-correct unlock ALSO resets E121 to 0 (F1D8 auth active) to bound the decoy-heavy case — but that reset only covers duress use, NOT the real-entry climb, so the doc's "reset E121 on duress success" must be read with that qualification. (ii) **FI master cross-check** — `unlock_duress` now reads the stored decoy master (F1DA) in the F1D8 session and cross-checks `kdf(reconstructed)==stored` via two `ct_eq` + the hamming-distant sentinel (parity with `DualSE::unlock`). *Note:* the E121 reset write rewrites `(0, DURESS_CTR_LIMIT)`, so the first duress-correct unlock on an already-provisioned bench chip (E121 limit 0xFFFF from the probe era) silently MIGRATES the limit up to 0x00FF_FFFF — expected, free, one-time. **STILL NOT validated on silicon** (the gated_unlock dispatch — real/duress/wrong PINs — has not been run on hardware), and the read-position sub-ordering differs between the duress (V,R,V…) and real (V,V,R) paths (total time uniform; fine-grained I2C order differs — out of the coercion threat model). **DESIGN (as implemented):**
  - *Dispatch — every path does exactly 2 SE verifies → uniform timing (an observer can't tell real vs duress vs wrong from latency):*
    1. Pre-commit bump MCU page-124 (unchanged from today's `gated_unlock`).
    2. Verify the **duress** credential first (OPTIGA F1D8 auto-state + SE050 duress UserID).
       - **Duress match** → SKIP the real verify; run a SECOND duress verify as the matched-LUC timing pad (fires LUC(E121), the validated ~0-residual twin of the real F1D0 auto-state verify). Reconstruct the DECOY entropy (`unlock_duress` read path: OPTIGA F1D9 + SE050 `DURESS_ENTROPY_OBJ`, XOR, cross-check vs decoy master). Return the **decoy** master. Reset MCU page-124 + E121.
       - **No duress match** → verify the **real** credential (F1D0 auto-state + SE050 real UserID).
         - **Real match** → reconstruct REAL entropy (existing `DualSE::unlock`). Return real master. Reset MCU page-124 + E120.
         - **Neither** → wrong PIN. Leave MCU bumped (10 ⇒ `factory_reset_admin` wipe, unchanged). E120 (real verify) + E121 (duress verify) already bumped by the silicon LUCs.
  - *Why duress-first:* lets the duress-match path SKIP the real verify (no E120 drift) while the pad keeps timing uniform. The real-match + wrong paths both run duress-verify + real-verify = 2 verifies; the duress-match path runs duress-verify + pad-duress-verify = 2 verifies. Symmetric.
  - *Counter semantics:* a CORRECT duress PIN is a *successful* unlock (of the decoy) — it MUST reset the MCU counter exactly like a real success, else timing/lockout-state would distinguish duress from real (breaks deniability). Reset E121 on duress success (Change=Auto(F1D8) authorises it). E120 is never touched on a duress unlock (real verify skipped) — frozen-but-fine. A wrong-on-both entry bumps all counters (per the user's "duress wrong PIN must also count toward the 10").
  - *Transparency:* `gated_unlock` returns the master (real OR decoy); the caller / `SecureState` caches it as `master_secret` and ALL downstream (signing, address, off-chain) use it unchanged — the decoy wallet simply *is* "the wallet" for that session. No caller learns which wallet unlocked (deniability).
  - *New production surface needed:* `unlock_duress` read path on OPTIGA / SE050 / DualSE (the `duress-provision-e2e` `duress_read_half` helpers are the validated preview — promote to production, add the master cross-check + entropy-blob cache like `DualSE::unlock`). Restructured `gated_unlock` must preserve the existing FI hardening (F-2 sentinels, double-reads, `wait_random` on both sides of each verify, fail-in verdict) across the new two-verify shape — extend, don't weaken.
  - *Open questions for review:* (1) ordering of the two SE chips within each verify (OPTIGA-then-SE050) for timing symmetry across the duress/real branches; (2) whether the pad must be a full dual-SE duress verify (OPTIGA + SE050) or OPTIGA-only suffices given SE050's ~104 ms dominates — likely must pad BOTH to stay uniform; (3) FI structure for the branch select (which-credential-matched) so a single glitch can't force the decoy→real or real→decoy mis-route.
- [x] **P4 — wizard UI — DONE 2026-05-20.** `ui::seed_wizard::collect_duress_pin` — yes/no chooser → `enter_pin_with_confirm`, bounded to 3 attempts, confirmed-DISTINCT from the main PIN. Runs ONLY on the unprovisioned first-boot path; threads `Some(pin)` into `provision_from_mnemonic` (declined/exhausted → `None` → random decoy, always-provision preserved); duress PIN zeroized after provisioning. Dialog flow is interactive-only (`e2e-test` short-circuits `confirm`/`enter_pin`), validated by source-grep + an interactive harness `make play-hw-duress-ui` (`duress-ui-test` feature — mock-se, drives JUST the dialogs via the physical gpio-buttons; main PIN fixed to `12345678` so the distinct-check reject is exercisable). **Interactively validated on OLED + perfboard buttons 2026-05-20:** the `Set duress PIN?` chooser navigates/selects, a distinct PIN is accepted, `Wipe on duress?` works, `mode: WIPE/DECOY` renders, and the harness loops cleanly. **Manual-UI checklist to re-verify during HW bring-up / pre-ship (NOT covered by automated e2e — the dialogs are short-circuited there):** (a) entering the main PIN as the duress PIN holds `Same as main / pick another` ~1.8 s (readable, not a split-second flash); (b) a mismatched enter/confirm holds `PIN mismatch / try again` ~1.8 s; (c) declining at `Set duress PIN?` (or 3× distinct-fail) shows `Declined / random decoy`; (d) the wizard's duress dialog appears ONLY on first-boot, never on the already-provisioned unlock path. (a)-(c) reproducible via `make play-hw-duress-ui`; (d) needs the full first-boot wizard (`duress-pin` non-e2e build).
- [x] **P5 — wipe-on-duress option — DONE 2026-05-20 + silicon-validated.** `ui::seed_wizard::choose_duress_wipe_mode` → persisted as a 1-byte flag in secure flash page 125 QW2 (`hw::flash::arm_duress_wipe_mode`/`is_duress_wipe_mode`), blank=decoy (safe default), programmed=wipe; armed BEFORE provisioning so a crash can't downgrade wipe→decoy. `gated_unlock`: on a duress match in wipe mode → zeroize the decoy master, `factory_reset_admin` both chips, return `PinLocked` (NO `duress_pad`; NO `pin_attempts_reset` — the Err arm leaves page-124 correct). Decoy still always provisioned regardless of mode. Silicon-validated via `make duress-provision-hw` steps 20–22 (PASS): arm flag → `gated_unlock(duress)` returns `PinLocked` AND both chips wiped.

### 33. AI-aided Lean verification track (Rust ↔ SphincsCVerify)

**Status:** SCOPED 2026-06-10. Background assurance track — **does NOT gate the
October ship** (params + wire formats are frozen, so proofs landing later still
apply to shipped firmware). Full research report + tool comparison + risk list in
`docs/verification/lean-verification-research-2026-06.md` (deep-research run, 24 sources,
claims adversarially verified). Decision: **Aeneas** (Charon → LLBC → Lean) for
extraction (NOT hax — see report §2), a Lean Squad-style scheduled AI-agent CI
loop for proof grinding (Claude primary, Aristotle API/MCP fallback), and an
adversarial spec-validation layer (`plausible` counterexamples + Facade-taxonomy
audits + Lean-spec-as-oracle differential fuzzing per verified-ledger).

**Goal theorems (in value order):**
1. *Firmware↔chain composite:* the bytes the firmware signs over parsed input
   are exactly the `userOpHash` the verified wallet model checks
   (`aa` extraction ∘ existing `Wallet/ValidateUserOp.lean`).
2. *Signer/verifier correspondence:* extracted `sphincs-c10` functions ≡ the
   existing `SphincsCVerify/Spec/{Adrs,Wots,Fors,...}.lean` the on-chain
   verifier was proven sound against (SHA-256 axiomatized as the SAME
   `Spec.sha256` symbol as bridge axiom A1 — composes through one shared axiom).
3. *Panic-freedom* on every attacker-facing parser (`proto`/`shared`/`aa` input
   paths) — near-free under Aeneas's `Result` monad.
4. Tier B (post-Oct): Merkle bundle verifiers (`tx`/`pqsigner-erc7730`),
   `fw-manifest` 75-B preimage chain, `domain` derivation determinism +
   invariant-#6 salt stability, `bip39` round-trip.

**Trust framing (MUST accompany any "verified" claim, internal or external):**
kernel-checked theorems modulo an *enumerated* TCB — SHA-256 axiom (KAT-backed),
Aeneas translation fidelity, rustc. Extends the existing `AXIOM_STATUS.json`
discipline to the extracted code: every axiom named + linted, zero
`assume`-style escapes, KAT per axiomatized primitive. Says nothing about
compiler/silicon/FI/SCA — those stay with `tools/sca` + silicon validation.

      - **DECISION 2026-06-13 (workflow `verity-assessment` + 2 adversarial
        reviews, both Keep):** split into two paths. **(i) Verity EDSL
        (write-in verified compilation) → DO NOT PURSUE.** Two independent
        dispositive reasons: it cannot ingest existing Solidity/Yul/bytecode
        (it only proves its own EDSL→Yul output), so it is the wrong tool
        class for an axiom (A3.1) that is ∀ over the *deployed* runtime; and
        even a hypothetical port is a different-bytecode, different-CREATE2-
        address contract barred from the shipped wallet by invariant #6 — plus
        v0.1.0's fragment (zero-bound loops only, word-keyed single-word
        memory, no calldata/sub-word/sha256-precompile primitives) can't
        express the 233-line hand-Yul verifier (~8–12 pw upstream Phase-0
        alone, verifier port unestimated). It also leaves A1/A2/A4 + solc
        Yul→bytecode in the TCB and keeps the functional "==Rust ref" leg an
        axiom witnessed by the same KAT harness. **(ii) verity-FRAMEWORK
        refinement (the upstream `c13_refines_spec` shape: hand-transcribed
        in-Lean EVM model + framework refinement, same Lean v4.22 as
        SphincsCVerify) → REMAINS the tracked A3.1-ceiling option above**, but
        scoped honestly: it proves ∀ over a hand-transcribed EVM *model* (so
        model↔deployed-bytecode is a transcription-TCB) and still axiomatizes
        SHA-256. The complementary option that proves the *actual* deployed
        bytecode with SHA-256 INTERPRETED is **Kontrol/KEVM** (tractability at
        C10 depth ~322 hashes/verify unproven; best as a 1–3 week spike on one
        sub-routine, e.g. WOTS+C chaining, for methodological diversity). Both
        (ii)/Kontrol are DEFERRED behind the S-1/S-2/S-3 OPTIGA ship-blockers
        and are NOT primary-assurance commitments; status-quo A3.1 (KAT 10/10
        + ~250-mutant screen + Rust↔Solidity↔Lean differential) is the honest,
        exploit-free ceiling. No code/redeploy implied.

Verified: closure = {propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound, sha256_collision_resistance}, no sorryAx; the false injectivity axiom is gone (so safe before mathlib). `BreaksHash` moved to Assumptions.lean (shared token).
- [x] **P2 follow-up — upgrade the three `∀_,True` hardness shapes to opaque Props — DONE 2026-06-14.** `SM_DT_TCR_F_Shape`/`ITSR_F_Shape`/`hMsg_RO_Shape` are now `opaque Prop` (witnessed by their axioms) instead of vacuous `∀_,True` defs. Build green; theft_free closure unchanged (the shapes are defs/opaques, not axioms). Trust base now has NO false/vacuous/inconsistent axioms.
## Lean faithfulness-audit follow-ups (mutation testing + coverage matrix + falsifiability, 2026-06-14)

Findings from the deep faithfulness audit (`contracts/verification/docs/FAITHFULNESS_AUDIT_2026-06-14.md`). Mutation battery: 8/9 real defects KILLED (verification is sensitive), 1 SURVIVED. The survivor + the security-load-bearing coverage gaps:

- [x] **P1 — close the `cap-bootstrap` mutation survivor (proof-coverage hole on invariant #7) — DONE 2026-06-14.** Added `capOk_bootstrap_implies_strict` (Invariants.lean, mirror of the slot path's `capOk_slot_implies_strict` :549) + the load-bearing consumer `validateSignature_bootstrap_cap_strict` (bootstrap counterpart of `combinedCap_inductive`: a bootstrap op is accepted only if `bootstrapUses < MaxBootstrapUses` strictly). Both kernel-clean `{propext, Quot.sound}`. **Verified the fix kills the mutant:** re-applying `<`→`≤` at ValidateUserOp.lean:298 now fails to compile (`unsolved goals` in the new lemma). Two-gate parity with the slot path restored.
- [x] **P1 — model the off-chain/on-chain domain separation (Gap-3) — DONE 2026-06-14.** New `Wallet/OffchainBinding.lean`: models Solady's keccak-nested `replaySafeHash` (mirrors `aa/src/eip1271.rs`) + the nested EIP-1271 entry point, and proves `offchain_nested_disjoint_from_userop_digest` — an off-chain `replaySafeHash`-nested value is never any UserOp `sphincsDigest` (∨ BreaksHash), so an off-chain C10 sig is never a valid UserOp sig (the RAW32 forgery-oracle defense). Reduces to ONE new cited axiom `keccak_sha256_cross_separation` (cross-hash separation, same `∨ BreaksHash` shape as `sha256_collision_resistance`; `keccak256` is `opaque`, not a named axiom). `theft_free`'s 11-axiom closure is **unchanged** (verified); AXIOM_STATUS.json A6 added.
- [x] **P2 — strengthen Claim 4 toward per-call attribution (Gap-2) — DONE 2026-06-14 (honest scope).** Added per-execute-STEP injective attribution to `Wallet/TxFlow.lean` (re-exported in `Spec/Theorems.lean`): `growing_executes_le_verified_validates` / `every_call_attributed_to_distinct_validate` prove `#(stack-growing execute steps) ≤ #(verifier-true validates)` via a transient-token "ledger" walk (`ledger_state`/`ledger_of_runTrace` prove the ledger agrees with the real `runTrace`, so it's an invariant of the genuine execution model, not a fiction), and `call_traces_to_authorising_validate` traces any live transient back to the unique most-recent verifier-true validate with no execute in between. This rules out attacks the existential gate can't (1 validate → 2 executes). **HONEST SCOPE:** this is per-execute-STEP injective, NOT strict per-individual-CALL-element distinctness — `executeBatch` appends many calls under one validate, so batch-internal calls share one authorising validate; a literal call→step provenance map would need a model change (tag each `Call` with its producing step index). Disclaimed in the docstrings. All new theorems kernel-clean `{propext,Classical.choice,Quot.sound}`, builds on the EXISTING Step/applyStep/runTrace/StepVerified model (faithful), no new axioms, theft_free unchanged.
  - **UPDATE 2026-06-16 (credits-model migration + research):** the single-token "ledger" form above (`growing_executes_le_verified_validates` / `every_call_attributed_to_distinct_validate` / `call_traces_to_authorising_validate`) was RETIRED when the GAP-11 credits refactor's per-index `credits : Nat→Nat` model superseded the single `validatedOwnerPlusOne` transient (the merge `b9f2e59` had stranded it, breaking the build). Replaced by the credits-faithful **per-index exactly-once** form (`every_call_consumes_its_own_validated_credit` + `credit_lift_implies_verified_validate`, `Spec/Theorems.lean`). A 3-stream deep-research workflow confirmed per-index is strictly MORE faithful (deployed credit is a per-ownerIndex counter) and strictly STRONGER (rules out cross-index substitution; subsumes the global aggregate). **Do NOT restore the single-token ledger.**
  - [x] **DONE 2026-06-18 (FV-#5) — global credits-native aggregate corollary.** `Wallet/CreditLedger.lean`: `exec_count_le_validate_count` (re-exported as `Spec.Theorems.exec_count_le_validate_count`) — in any successful clean-transient trace, `trace.countP isExec ≤ trace.countP isValidate`. Built on the tight `creditConservation` (`countExec ≤ countValidate + sumOver σ.credits S`) via a self-contained mathlib-free finite-support sum (`sumOver`/`carrierNodup`/`insertIdx` + congr/not-mem/stamp/consume lemmas) over a RUNNING duplicate-free carrier (dedup IS load-bearing, as flagged — handled by `insertIdx` keeping nodup rather than the pre-planned `List.eraseDups`). Single induction over the trace generalizing `σ`+`S`; no separate instrumented runner. Kernel-only `{propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound}` (verify-audit + dump_axioms_claim4 + per-module lean4checker exit 0). Framed strictly as the COARSENING/completeness complement (counts ALL validates incl. non-stamping; the per-index exactly-once form remains operative) and explicitly NOT a revival of the deleted single-token ledger. Gated in `dump_axioms.lean` + `dump_axioms_claim4.lean`; doc note at `Spec/Theorems.lean` updated.
  - [ ] **FOLLOW-UP (SHARED across the Claim-4 / Invariants families, surfaced by the FV-#5 advisor pass 2026-06-18) — non-vacuity witness.** Every Claim-4/Invariants theorem is conditional on `runTrace … = some _` / `decodeWrappedSig … = some d`; kernel-cleanliness proves the implications VALID but not that the hypotheses are SATISFIABLE by a trace with a successful `execute`. **Status: NOT vacuous (argued + partially mechanized).** Each guard on the `[validate-stamp, execute]` path is independently satisfiable (decode has a non-empty slot-index preimage; `ownerAtIndex 1` via `Storage.initialised`; `capOk` via slot-allowed index-matching selector + clean caps; `verify_fn` is a `Step` param = `fun _=>true`; `bumpSlot`/`setOffchain` succeed from zero). The DECODE-FREE half is mechanized: `CreditLedger.execute_step_satisfiable` (a live credit funds one successful execute, kernel-clean). The blocker to a FULLY-CONCRETE in-kernel witness is that `decodeWrappedSig` runs `readWordBE` over the 4128-byte wrapper, so `decide`/`rfl` would force the whole array through the kernel — only `native_decide` (BANNED on proof paths) makes it tractable; hence NO concrete decode witness exists anywhere in the dev. **Options:** (a) prove `aggregate_nonvacuous_modulo_decode` — clean-init `[validate, execute]` runs to `some` with `countP isExec = 1` GIVEN `decodeWrappedSig op.signature = some ⟨1,…⟩ ∧ isSlotAllowed ∧ ≠removeOwner ∧ calldataOwnerIndex=1` (reduces vacuity to the one decode-preimage fact; ~50 LoC, started but bookkeeping-heavy — name the bumped storage explicitly, no `set`/`get!`); (b) a `readWordBE`-of-`Array.replicate…set` spec lemma to make ONE concrete decode `decide`-tractable; (c) accept the prose argument + the decode-free witness as sufficient. Owner: verification (Claim-4 / wallet-invariants).

Removed from `known_true_axioms.txt`; `lint_axioms.sh` made zero-match-tolerant. **Verified:** build 0-sorry; theft_free closure = SAME 11 names (A4 content-bearing), no sorryAx; detonator type-errors; `lint_axioms` PASS reporting **True-typed axioms: 0** (no `: True` placeholder remains in the ledger); KATs 10/10. - [x] **P3 — close the I-3 closed-world gap (Gap-1) — DONE 2026-06-14 (CI lint).** Added `contracts/verification/scripts/check_storage_mutators.sh` + `make -C contracts/verification verify-storage-mutators` (wired into `verify-three-claims.sh`): extracts every Storage-returning def AND every `{ s with … }` in-place mutator from `Wallet/Storage.lean` and FAILS if either drifts from a pinned allow-list (the 5 mutators wired into `no_reset_path`). A future reset-introducing mutator can't be added without flipping CI red + being forced to add a matching no-decrease lemma. ### 34. Security-tooling adoption — 2026-06 SOTA research

Source: `docs/verification/security-tooling-sota-2026-06.md` (3-pass deep-research sweep + local read of `SPHINCS-`/`LeanLoop`/`crypto.rs`). The **headline finding is a non-action** (already correct): `crypto::c10_sign_verified_with_progress` already implements the literature-endorsed SPHINCS+ redundant-recomputation FI countermeasure (Genêt TCHES 2023 — verify-before-release is *insufficient* alone); CLAUDE.md doc corrected 2026-06-16. Items below ranked adopt-now → pilot.

**FI/SCA (relates to §18 / §18b):**
- [ ] **Prove no single fault bypasses both gates in `c10_sign_verified`.** Run the rainbow `fault_skip` sweep over `tools/sca/fi_target` + `tools/sca/c10_sign_target` (mirror it bit-for-bit). Residual: SIGN_A/SIGN_B share a shuffle seed → register-identical, so a *reproducible* fault could pass `ct_eq` (the verify gate is the backstop — prove it holds under a single fault).
- [~] **`cargo-checkct` (Ledger Donjon) on the thumbv8m secret paths** — the ONLY tool that proves constant-time on the shipped M33 ISA (Binsec/Rel has **no ARMv8-M decoder** — confirmed NO-GO). Targets `verify()`, SAES-CMAC/DHUK KDF, `subtle` compares. **BROUGHT UP 2026-06-16** (binsec 0.10.0 RelSE backend; setup/run/results in `tools/sca/DONJON-RUST-TOOLING.md`; vendored driver workspace `tools/sca/checkct/`). Ran on the CT-1-fixed `fisher_yates` (thumbv8m): **independently confirms the fix** — disasm is `lsrs r3,#0x10` (the Lemire `>>16`), no `UDIV`, no new secret-dependent branch (517/518 CF + 5359/5361 mem checks pass). The residual INSECURE verdict is the inherent secret-indexed `buf[j]` swap — **by design** (the shuffle relies on statistical 43!/13! trace-misalignment, not bitwise CT). Fixed an in-repo cargo-config gotcha (root `target.<triple>.rustflags` shadows `build.rustflags`, dropping `-Cpanic=immediate-abort` → binsec `Not_found`). **DONE 2026-06-16: three SECURE machine-checked CT proofs** — `domain::kdf` (117/117 CF + 750/750 mem), `fors_secret` (52/52 + 575/575), and the `th` tweakable hash (141/141 + 1290/1290), via `tools/sca/checkct/driver_{kdf,fors,th}/` (the `sim-internals` feature). So the KDF, the FORS secret-key PRF, and the core tweakable hash are proven constant-time over their secret inputs on thumbv8m. `verify`/`keygen` are out of binsec's reach (full hypertree = millions of symbolic instructions). REMAINING (optional): a CMAC/SAES software-mirror driver + a `make checkct` CI gate.
- [ ] **dudect-style DWT Welch t-test on real STM32U585** for `verify()`/KDF — the on-silicon last mile rainbow can't model (exact M33+SAES/flash timing).
- [ ] **`lascar`/`scared` CPA over rainbow-emulated `hw/hash.rs` traces** during secret-seed load — confirm the F-16 shuffle suffices vs SHA-2 PRF DPA (Kannwischer COSADE 2018, ≥32 sk bits); if leakage shows, shuffle WOTS+ chain order (cheap) — do NOT reopen masked-SHA2 (§18).
- [~] **Pilot Muscat** (Donjon's current-gen SCA; `lascar` is now Donjon-"legacy") + dtw + Turboplot for SPHINCS+-sized traces. **BROUGHT UP 2026-06-16** (harness `tools/sca/muscat/pqsigner_tvla_cpa.rs` + npz→npy converter; setup in `tools/sca/DONJON-RUST-TOOLING.md`). Reproduced the **F-16-holding TVLA on the real `f9_traces.npz`** (windowed max|t|=3.24 < 4.5) and recovers a ground-truth key on synthetic data. **DONE 2026-06-16:** full-10M TVLA reproduces lascar **exactly** (`max|t|=4.931 @ sample 6.8M` vs ≈4.93) — Muscat validated as a lascar drop-in; the peak is the known F-9 grind_r PUBLIC-input timing leak. First-order CPA over the random-group window is **flat** (best corr 0.355 in a 0.29–0.36 noise cluster, no spike) — the address channel + shuffle hold, corroborating the checkct address-proofs. A real register-value attack is on-silicon SCA territory (ChipWhisperer). `SKIP_CPA` env added to the harness for fast TVLA-only runs on 10M-wide traces. Pilot `erc7730-analyzer` (lints the ERC-7730 descriptor bundle).
- [ ] **Hardware bench:** ChipWhisperer-Husky + ChipSHOUTER-PicoEMP (the lascar/scared loop transfers from rainbow to silicon). **Open empirical question:** no public STM32U5 RDP/TrustZone glitch result exists (all are F1/F2/F4/L0/L5) — settle on-bench whether a U585 RDP downgrade is achievable (governs dual-SE trust budget). SECGlitcher = starting methodology.

**On-chain Yul verifier (relates to §33 / A3.1):**
- [ ] **Port `SPHINCsC10Asm.sol` into the `SPHINCS-/verity` workbench.** C13 is already proved (`c13_refines_spec`); the C10 SHA-256 port inherits exactly the SHA-256-precompile-bridge + opaque-hash residual the §33 A3.1 track is already discharging — a second, independent route to A3.1. (Framework `lfglabs-dev/verity` is MIT; the `SPHINCS-` main repo is all-rights-reserved — coordinate with nconsigny.)
- [ ] **hevm equivalence** of `SPHINCsC10Asm` vs a reference Solidity verifier (SHA-256 uninterpreted) + **halmos** for surrounding invariants — bytecode-level, so they see the Yul that Slither can't. Complements Kontrol/KEVM.
- [~] **C10 differential KAT oracle:** generate C10-specific vectors from the `SPHINCS-` reference signers (`signers/*/crosscheck.py`); feed the same set to three legs — Rust `sphincs-c10`, the Lean spec via **`leanloop kat`**, and the Yul verifier via a Foundry JsonKAT test (C10 is non-FIPS-205 — no off-the-shelf vector matches). Gas context: poqeth shows a full-budget SPHINCS+ verify is ~5–13M gas; C10's ~115K is the "minus" payoff. **INDEPENDENT-SOURCE leg DONE 2026-06-18** (work-todo #7): a clean-room SHA-256-C10 signer `contracts/verification/scripts/independent_c10_signer.py` (written from `SPHINCsC10Asm.sol` + the PRF preimages, NOT transliterated from the Rust signer) reproduces the Rust `pk_root` + all 4 valid KAT vectors BYTE-FOR-BYTE + round-trips fresh msgs; `sphincs-c10/tests/independent_signer_xcheck.rs` confirms the EXISTING Rust verifier accepts FRESH independent output. `make -C contracts/verification verify-independent-signer` + CI host-tests step. Still open: the `leanloop kat` Lean leg + the Foundry JsonKAT Yul leg wired into ONE shared-vector differential (the existing `verify-interp`/`verify-test-vectors` already cover the Lean+Yul legs on the committed corpus).

**Firmware formal verification (relates to §33 / LeanLoop):**
- [x] **Kani + Miri (host Rust FV) — ADOPTED 2026-06-17.** Kani 0.67.0 (its own toolchain + CBMC, via `cargo install --locked kani-verifier && cargo kani setup`); Miri via `rustup component add miri --toolchain nightly`. Both wired as `make kani` / `make miri`. **Kani panic / arithmetic-overflow / slice-OOB-freedom proofs** over `kani::any()` bytes on the untrusted-companion-bytes parse surface (a panic there = DoS / parse-desync), all in host-buildable pure-logic crates, each VERIFIED in seconds: (1) `tx-core::rlp::decode_item` — panic-free **+** the load-bearing `used <= input.len()` invariant (this is what keeps `ListIter`'s `&rest[used..]` re-slice in bounds, so the nested EIP-1559 / access-list walkers inherit panic-freedom — a separate `validate_access_list` harness was DROPPED as intractable-for-CBMC, nested-loop path explosion, AND redundant given this invariant); (2) `tx-core::rlp::bytes_to_u256` — the int leaf decoder (`32 - len` underflow + `copy_from_slice` length-match); (3) `domain::deserialize_pin_state` — the flash recovery parser, proving the `num_slots > MAX_ATTEMPTS` guard keeps `encrypted_secrets[i]` in bounds over all 481-byte blobs (also surfaces its `blob_len <= blob.len()` caller precondition). Harnesses live in `#[cfg(kani)]` modules. **Miri UB detection** (0 UB): `pqsigner-fi` volatile helpers 12/0, `pqsigner-tx-core` decoders 27/0. **HONEST SCOPE (per advisor):** runs on the HOST toolchain over HOST-REACHABLE logic. The highest-risk `unsafe` — CMSE veneers, raw MMIO, NS-pointer deref — is `thumbv8m`/hardware-`cfg`'d and COMPILED OUT of the host build, so this does NOT prove those UB-free; it proves the parse arithmetic/slicing + recovery-parser indexing panic-free, and the FI-volatile helpers + decoders UB-free on host. **Follow-ups:** (a) cap-monotonicity on the FIRMWARE off-chain counter (`secure/src/offchain_state.rs` `MAX_OFFCHAIN_GAP` + combined-cap arithmetic) — secure-crate-bound, needs a host-buildable extraction (the on-chain caps are already halmos/Kontrol-proven — don't re-count those). **DEFERRED 2026-06-17 (advisor-reviewed, deliberately NOT done):** the only part that host-extracts is the single-call gate arithmetic, and proving `Allowed ⟹ gap < MAX_OFFCHAIN_GAP && userop_sigs + new_count ≤ MAX_SLOT_USES` is just the contrapositive of the two early-return guards — Kani discharges it trivially (a guard-restatement, ~zero content beyond `saturating_add` overflow-safety). The VALUABLE property — counters never decrease / cap holds across the call sequence (invariant #7/#9) — is a property of the `unsafe` FLASH read-modify-write (`offchain_count_bump`/`promote_to`), which does NOT host-extract. Worse, unifying the two F-10 recheck sites into one fn could silently weaken the F-10 FI hardening (double-read + `wait_random` + recheck-reusing-`new_count`) in a way the 2076 host suite would NOT catch — the real gate is the `tools/sca/.../cap` FI sweep, not unit tests (same tests-pass≠property-holds trap as the codehash + kontrol-lib bugs this session). Net: would refactor the most FI-sensitive path to prove a restatement while the real property stays out of reach, and the on-chain caps (the PRIMARY defense; the firmware gate is explicitly belt-and-braces below it) are already symbolically proven. If ever revisited: single source of truth at BOTH F-10 sites, recheck reuses `new_count` byte-identically, preserve all distinct returns, re-run the `cap` FI sweep + 2076 suite, and scope the claim to overflow-safe single-call enforcement (NOT "cap-monotonicity proven"). (b) Miri on the secure-crate `ns_ptr`/`fi` host-reachable test modules — **DONE 2026-06-17 (now in `make miri`):** Miri runs the secure crate under the interpreter (`--no-default-features --features mock-se,debug-log,ui-semihosting`, `-Zmiri-permissive-provenance` for the legitimate int→ptr NS boundary) and reports **0 UB** on the GENUINE host-reachable `unsafe` — `ns_ptr` 24/0 (the `read_volatile(src.add(i))` TOCTOU snapshot deref, `from_raw_parts` `as_slice`, the write deref) + `ptr_validate` 3/0. This is the unsafe that the pure-logic Kani/Miri pass could not reach; the FI volatile helpers are separately Miri-clean via `pqsigner-fi` (12/0). Scope note still applies: host execution of the NS-ptr logic — the CMSE veneer ABI itself is still thumbv8m-only; (c) more parser harnesses (ERC-20 calldata in `tx`, ERC-7730 TLV). NOT wired into CI (Kani's toolchain is heavy — gate as a manual/nightly job). **(c) DONE 2026-06-17 — 3 more harnesses, all verified, now in `make kani` (6 total):** `tx::erc20::parse_erc20_calldata` panic-freedom over arbitrary calldata **+** a `transfer` NO-MISDECODE display-integrity proof (a well-formed `transfer(to,amount)` always decodes to exactly the encoded `to`/`amount` — the trusted display can't show a recipient/amount different from the signed bytes, over every (to,amount)); and `pqsigner-erc7730::Erc7730Ir::parse` panic/OOB-freedom (the non-trivial discharge: the symbolic u16 `metadata_off`/`pool_len`/`formats_off`/`formats_len` offsets + the layout checks must keep the final `pool`/`formats` slices in-bounds — `parse` is loop-free, 47 s). Follow-ups (a) + (b) still open below.

Differential is empirically our most productive technique — it caught the two real A3.1 reconstruction bugs (`5055d66d`) and the extraction-corruption catch. *Unifying insight (second-agent review):* a KAT that runs the same vectors through the firmware Rust **and** the extracted/spec Lean is simultaneously the bounded check, the only honest validator of §33 extraction faithfulness, **and** the closest thing to grounding the protocol/info-theoretic abstractions in code. *Cost:* months-not-years, B/B+ coverage at a fraction of full functional-proof cost.

- [ ] **The model→Rust span for device invariants #1–#4 is a cited-TCB transfer-by-assumption (honest residual, NOT buildable as Aeneas work).** *Correcting an earlier mis-scoping (2026-06-29):* the **design** layer for #1–#4 is already proven *as far as it can soundly go* — #1 (seed-split secrecy) by the info-theoretic `contracts/verification/lean/SphincsCVerify/Crypto/SplitSecrecy.lean` (kernel-clean OTP combinatorial core; the SOUND replacement for the symbolic `tamarin/seed_split_xor.spthy`, which carries an Unruh-2010 XOR-unsoundness caveat) + `cryptoverif/seed_split_secrecy.cv`; #2/#3/#4 (PIN-lockstep reachability / tunnel authenticity / isolation) by the protocol models (`proverif/{dual_se_unlock,optiga_shield_handshake,scp03_handshake,scp03_replay,fw_update_authenticity}.pv` + `tamarin/{pin_lockstep,scp03_replay,seed_split_xor}.spthy`). **These are secrecy/reachability/authenticity/isolation properties, NOT functional correctness, so Aeneas extraction is the wrong tool** (Aeneas proves "this Rust computes this spec" — invariant #5/#8-shaped). The genuine standing gap is that **there is no spanning theorem from those abstractions down to the secure-crate Rust** — a documented multi-tool composition gap / transfer-by-assumption, an honest cited-TCB interface (track it like A2/A4), not a cheap mechanization "one link away." *Where Aeneas/§33 actually applies (and is substantially delivered, NOT blocked):* invariant #5/#8 functional correctness — the `contracts/verification/extracted/` tree is **79 files / ~9.6k lines, ~12 ranks proven** (bip39, RLP, Merkle, WOTS, FORS, hash specs, slot-KDF, FW-update preimage, `deserialize_pin_state`); `aeneas-probe/UPSTREAM_ISSUES.md` is a **scoped** gate on the heaviest SHA-256 crypto kernels (array-equality-before-loop, nested-array writes), not a wholesale wall. The natural §33 continuation is more invariant-#5/#8 ranks (slot-chain `derive_c10_slot_seeds`, the counter-arithmetic functional spec) per `fv-soundness-roadmap-2026-06.md` T1.3 — separate from the #1–#4 composition gap above. **Both captured in `contracts/verification/docs/FV_VALUE_AND_GAPS.md`.**

**Protocol verification (NEW):**
- [~] **Tamarin/ProVerif models** of the three-way PIN lockstep + SCP03 + OPTIGA Shielded Connection + XOR seed-split. Nobody has modeled multi-SE PIN lockstep — EMVerify (found real Visa/Mastercard PIN bypasses) is the template. Prove no reachable state lets a desolder/substitute attacker (S-1/S-6 threat) reset the counter, and that the split leaks nothing if one SE is compromised. Novel, publishable. **PROVERIF MODEL LANDED 2026-06-17** (`contracts/verification/proverif/dual_se_unlock.pv` + `make proverif` + README with the S-tier→Claim mapping; ProVerif 2.05 CLI built from source — GTK not needed). Symbolic Dolev-Yao proof of the dual-SE seed-unlock, queried in layers, all 6 RESULTs as designed: **Claim 1 (dual residency)** — one extracted half leaks nothing (seed secret); **Claim 2 (channel access ≠ read access)** — both channel keys leaked (S2/DHUK-PBS) + no PIN still cannot read a half (the silicon PIN gate is orthogonal to the tunnel crypto — modelled as a separate control so the query is non-vacuous); baseline bus-attacker secrecy; **PIN-gate authentication** — `ReleasedHalf ⇒ PresentedPin` (no half released without a correct PIN first → "every PIN attack is online, no offline grind", §6.2); plus a load-bearing **positive control** (both halves extracted ⇒ seed NOT secret) that proves the model is not vacuously safe (the protocol-model analogue of the F-9 SCA positive control). **STILL OPEN (Tamarin/GSVerif territory — ProVerif can't count):** the ≤10-attempt monotonic-counter bound + the desolder/substitute counter-RESET reachability (the actual S-1/S-6 threat); and the tunnel key-establishment handshakes (TLS-PRF/CCM-8 + CMAC/CBC, abstracted as pre-shared per-channel keys here). The stateful counter half is the natural next protocol-verification step (Tamarin). **TAMARIN MODEL LANDED 2026-06-17** (`contracts/verification/tamarin/pin_lockstep.spthy` + `make tamarin` + README; Tamarin 1.12 + Maude 3.5.1 prebuilt binaries, no sudo/GHC). Stateful model of the three counters (MCU page-124 / OPTIGA F1E1 / SE050 UserID), the attacker's single-counter reset, and the boot reconcile that wipes on disagreement — **all 4 lemmas verified**: anti-vacuity (`honest_boot_possible`); **CORE** `fresh_synced_means_no_reset` + `zero_synced_means_all_reset` (a surviving boot is reachable ONLY via no-reset or all-three-reset ⇒ a PARTIAL reset of one/two counters can NEVER survive a boot — every single-side reset, incl. S-1 OPTIGA alone, is caught); and `full_reset_bypass` (the documented residual: all-three reset survives). The model also QUANTIFIES ship-blocker S-3: the hardware-monotonic OPTIGA counter removes `Reset_OPT`, dropping the attacker to "reset 1 of 3" which the core lemma catches. Modelling notes (honest scope): counter VALUE abstracted to status `fresh`/`zeroed` (the exact ≤10 numeric cap is the per-SE silicon premise this reconcile DEFENDS, not re-proves — Tamarin's natural-number arithmetic over the unbounded attempt loop is intractable, confirmed across value/status/loop-free variants); boot is terminal (per-boot reconcile, one boot/trace). **SCP03 HANDSHAKE GAP FILLED 2026-06-17** (`contracts/verification/proverif/scp03_handshake.pv`, in `make proverif`): symbolic ProVerif model of the SE050 SCP03 mutual-auth + session-key derivation (`INITIALIZE UPDATE`/`EXTERNAL AUTHENTICATE`, session key = `KDF(static, host_chal, card_chal)`, PIN VERIFY wrapped under the session key). All 4 results as designed: baseline `attacker(pin)` **secret** + mutual authentication (`HostAccepted⟹CardSent`, `CardAccepted⟹HostSent`) both **true** — so the channel protects the PIN and is mutually authenticated when the static keys are secret, **justifying the pre-shared-key abstraction** in `dual_se_unlock.pv`; and the **residual** `attacker(pin)` **NOT secret** under a static-key leak (DHUK/PBS = S2) + session capture — the formal statement of Claim 7's residual (re-derive `KDF(static, captured challenges)` → decrypt the PIN VERIFY), exactly what the ML-KEM inner wrap closes, doubling as the model's anti-vacuity control. Converged in 2 iterations (ProVerif, no install — its sweet spot). **OPTIGA SHIELD HANDSHAKE + SCP03 REPLAY WINDOW DONE 2026-06-17** (in `make proverif`/`make tamarin`): (1) `contracts/verification/proverif/optiga_shield_handshake.pv` — the OPTIGA Shielded Connection (PBS→TLS-1.2-PRF→AES-128-CCM-8) modelled analogously to SCP03; `half_O` secret + mutual auth vs bus, PBS-leak+capture residual (strictly weaker — half_O alone is useless by Claim 1). So BOTH SE tunnels now have a handshake model. (2) SCP03 in-session anti-replay, split tool-by-strength: `proverif/scp03_replay.pv` proves NO FORGERY (`event(Accept)⟹event(Send)` — no forged command accepted; ProVerif over-approximates the injective version so it's left to Tamarin), and `tamarin/scp03_replay.spthy` proves NO REPLAY (`no_replay` — each counter accepted ≤ once, via the explicit linear `Expected` counter token consumed on accept) + `can_accept` (anti-vacuity; MAC check as an equality `restriction` not a pattern-match → no `--auto-sources` needed). Together: an attacker can neither inject nor replay a wrapped command within a session; cross-session replay is separately covered by the handshake's fresh per-session keys. **Protocol-verification track now COMPLETE** (ProVerif: seed-unlock secrecy + 2 handshakes + replay-no-forgery; Tamarin: lockstep + replay-no-replay; `make proverif` 15 RESULTs / `make tamarin` 6 lemmas, all green). NOTE (priorities, advisor-flagged + user-acknowledged): these last bits re-derive documented residuals (low-yield); the actual production gate is the OPTIGA ship-blockers S-1/S-2/S-3, still untouched (parts are doable as compile-fences without the bench).

Promote the full secure-suite + `forge test` into CI once these are green. **RED #1 FIXED + GATED; RED #2 WAS A FALSE ALARM (corrected 2026-06-16):** (1) `4d1d6476` migrated the usb_hw raw `read/write_volatile` RMW sites to `hw::mmio::Reg32::modify` → `make test-unit` secure suite 2076 passed / 0 failed; gated by the new `secure-tests` CI job. (2) **The "4 codehash-pin drifts" were NOT a real drift** — diagnosed via a clean-room rebuild: the canonical build (the 7 `foundry.lock`-pinned libs) produces the *already-committed* pins (verifier `0xf1ef…`, wallet `0x43c654…`), which match `AXIOM_STATUS.json` and reproduce Base Mainnet exactly. The local `forge test` red was a **local-environment artifact**: this machine has an 8th lib, `kontrol-cheatcodes` (installed for the Kontrol verification work, NOT in `foundry.lock`), and Foundry auto-generates remappings from `lib/` contents, which solc folds into the metadata-IPFS hash appended to the runtime bytecode → a different codehash. Proven: with 7 libs the verifier is `0xf1ef…`; adding kontrol-cheatcodes flips it to `0x18402d26…`; stripping the CBOR metadata tail shows the verifier **code is byte-identical** across both (1280 B runtime, only the trailing IPFS digest differs). **The real CI failure was unrelated:** the `contracts` job died at COMPILE time because `lib/` is gitignored and the deps are git-pinned by `foundry.lock` but are NOT submodules, so a clean checkout had nothing to compile against — this had been red on EVERY push since CI was first added (`b9d30d23`), masked because no one had run the contracts job in CI before. **Fix:** `ci.yml` `contracts` job now restores each lib at its exact `foundry.lock` rev (`jq`-driven, lib-exact), then `forge test` → 109 passed / 0 failed / 1 skipped, born-green verified in a fresh clean-room clone. **The earlier erroneous re-pin (`4b3f4f85`) + the false "stale codehash" AXIOM flag (`76f70f5e`) were REVERTED** (`79a235ff`, `1b39d65d`): they had pinned to the local 8-lib metadata artifact and would have turned CI red once libs were restored. There is **NO deploy divergence**: under `FOUNDRY_PROFILE=deploy` in a clean 7-lib tree, `DeployedBytecodeReproCheck` PASSES (canonical deploy verifier = on-chain `0xeb1e3fcd…`); the earlier "local FORS fix ahead of Base Mainnet / `0xc5c8938b…`" claim was the same kontrol artifact and is RETRACTED. The deploy profile + `make verify-bytecode` symbolic discharge stay OUT of CI (slow + need the patched halmos toolchain). **Residual RESOLVED 2026-06-17:** `kontrol-cheatcodes` (the 8th lib that poisons the codehash pins) was being installed into `contracts/smart-wallet/lib` by `contracts/verification/kontrol/run_kontrol.sh` (the sole installer — no Makefile/other path) and left to linger. Fixed in `run_kontrol.sh`: the install is now (a) deferred until after harness staging and CONDITIONAL on a staged harness actually importing `kontrol-cheatcodes`/`KontrolCheats`/`KEVMCheats` (the current harnesses use only forge-std → never installed), and (b) removed by an `EXIT` trap so it never lingers for a later `forge test`. Verified: with kontrol-cheatcodes removed, local `forge test` returns to the canonical 109/0/1 (verifier `0xf1ef…`). Both `lib/kontrol-cheatcodes` and the staged `test/kontrol/` harnesses are gitignored, so CI/clean-room never saw them anyway — this fix is purely for local dev hygiene (no phantom codehash reds after running Kontrol).

Installed it + ran the full parse surface (`make fuzz-all`, 30 s/target against seed corpora): **all 11 targets non-vacuous (cov 23–133), 0 crashes** (~10–27 M execs/target). So the adversarial decoders (tx-core RLP/EIP-1559, ERC-20 calldata, ERC-7730 IR/walker/bundle/render, aa-userop, apdu, hid-frame) survive coverage-guided fuzzing — extends the Kani panic-freedom proofs onto the unbounded/large-input paths. ### 35. VF tooling + coverage roadmap (2026-07 surveys)

Captured from two 2026-07-01 surveys (a VF-landscape gate-health sweep + a tooling-currency web-research pass). **Gate health:** firmware Kani/Miri/cargo-fuzz + the Lean on-chain track are healthy + CI-gated; CT/SCA (`cargo-checkct`) and the ProVerif/Tamarin/CryptoVerif protocol models are **capability-only, not gated** (they regress silently between manual re-runs). **Currency:** we are CURRENT — Kani 0.67.0 is the latest release, CBMC 6.8.0 is pinned-by-Kani + irrelevant to us, Aeneas `nightly-2026.06.10` is ~3 wk behind (low value). The real lever is three experimental Kani `-Z` features **already shipped in 0.67.0** and switched off. **Fundamental / un-closable:** SHA-256/SPHINCS+C10 opacity — every FV tool (Kani stub loses `H(x)==H(x)`, CBMC uninterpreted-fn not exposed, Aeneas `--opaque`, even hax axiomatizes SHA-256) reproduces the same boundary; keep `--opaque` + fuzz `verify_digest`/`verify_signature`.

Prioritized (P = do-now candidate, F = future; ordered by unblock-real-surface × low cost):

- [ ] **P1 — `-Z function-contracts` → re-prove `records_pages_total` modularly.** Closes the composition wall dropped in slice 10 (which was proved-components + argued-composition): one `#[ensures]` per inner decoder + a `proof_for_contract` harness each, then `stub_verified` at the call site — machine-checks the composition on the CI-gated tool. ANTI-VACUITY: `-Z` is unstable → run under `verify-proof-mutation` + negative controls (mitigant: `stub_verified` *requires* a matching `proof_for_contract` = built-in check; the research spike showed wrong invariants fail *loudly*). **MECHANISM CONFIRMED 2026-07-01** (spike 2/2 — needs BOTH `-Z function-contracts` AND `-Z stubbing`). But an advisor pressure-test flags three catches that shrink the payoff: **(a)** the scan-heavy targets `classify_safe_mgmt`/`parse_erc20_calldata` are PRODUCTION fns (compile into thumbv8m) → annotating them with `#[kani::requires/ensures]` risks a broken firmware build — confirm the annotation path first, OR use plain `#[kani::stub]` (over-approximating, `#[cfg(kani)]`-only, sound for the panic/overflow property, no production annotation); **(b)** stubbing `classify` closes only ONE of `records_pages_total`'s two walls — `decode_multisend`'s symbolic-framing scans (what the slice-10 N=180 attempt *actually* died on) remain, needing concrete framing or `-Z loop-contracts`; **(c)** NARROW DELTA — either way the record-walk stays ≤2-record-bounded, so this re-fences an already-unit-tested 10-liner, and the slice-10 prose argument (per-record bound × payload length) already carries the many-records overflow. NET: the reusable value is the confirmed **mechanism** (for a future *load-bearing* contract proof), not this specific proof → **DEPRIORITIZED below P3/P4**. Effort med-high.
- [ ] **P2 — `-Z loop-contracts` on the fw-manifest reserved scan — CHECKED 2026-07-01, recommend SKIP.** Mechanism RE-VERIFIED on our toolchain: a symbolic `[u8; 3995]` explicit-`while` scan with `#[kani::loop_invariant(i <= N)]` verifies in **0.051 s, NO unwind bound** (matches the spike) — `-Z loop-contracts` genuinely closes the ≥3996-unwind wall. BUT the cost to apply it to the REAL `verify_structural`: (a) a crate-level `#![cfg_attr(kani, feature(stmt_expr_attributes, proc_macro_hygiene))]` NIGHTLY-feature gate; (b) rewrite the production `.any()` → an explicit `while` carrying a `#[kani::loop_invariant]` attribute that must itself be cfg-gated (kani has the attr; the stable/thumbv8m build has no `kani` crate → production loop DUPLICATION); (c) a QUANTIFIED soundness invariant (relate the scan result to the bytes), not the trivial `i <= N`. All for a NON-authority DoS-hygiene check already covered by proptest + libfuzzer. VERDICT: disproportionate cost — mechanism BANKED (like P1's function-contracts) for a future HIGH-value big-loop surface; not pursued now.
- [x] **P3 — Kani-decoder anti-vacuity gate — DONE 2026-07-01.** Institutionalises the per-slice manual mutation checks as a standing gate — the Kani-side mirror of the Lean `verify-proof-mutation`. `scripts/check_kani_mutations.py` + `scripts/kani_mutations.json` (a curated manifest, NOT full cargo-mutants×kani which would be hours): each entry breaks a decoder/gate the way a specific harness must catch and asserts that harness flips to `VERIFICATION:- FAILED` — a green-when-it-should-be-red = a vacuous/under-constrained proof. Same robustness as the Lean gate (materiality-guarded find-EXACTLY-once, always-revert via finally+atexit+signal, an always-run CANARY whose survival voids all results, tiers quick/default/full, exit 0/1/2). 6 mutations across 3 crates: canary + rollback off-by-one (`>`→`>=`) on `fw-manifest`; the execute-ABI-offset (`0xa0`→`0xc0`) on `pqsigner-aa`; multiSend cursor (`+= data_end`→`+1`), no-hidden-value (`| MsRecordKind::Blind`), and CoW field-offset shift on `pqsigner-tx`. `make verify-kani-mutation` (root Makefile) + wired into the nightly `kani` job (reuses its kani install). Validated: quick tier 3/3 caught (canary 5 s / off-by-one 2 s / aa 50 s), full tier 6/6. **Add a manifest entry whenever a new load-bearing harness lands** (this pins the specific behaviours, it does not prove harness completeness).
- [x] **P4 — AA execute-calldata Kani proof — DONE 2026-07-01.** 2 harnesses on `reconstruct_execute_calldata` (`aa/src/userop.rs:149` — the pure, no-hash byte-assembly the on-chain wallet actually executes): `reconstruct_execute_panic_free` (panic/overflow/OOB-free over symbolic ownerIndex/offchainCount/tx/data) + `reconstruct_execute_layout` (EXHAUSTIVE for dlen ≤ 64: byte-exact `execute(...)` ABI encoding — selector‖ownerIndex‖offchainCount‖target‖value‖offset(0xa0)‖dataLen‖data‖zero-pad; a layout bug = the userOp runs something OTHER than what was signed). `cargo kani -p pqsigner-aa` = 2/2 SUCCESSFUL; MUTATION-confirmed (ABI offset byte 0xa0→0xc0 → FAILED, reverted); host suite 102/0; wired into the `make kani` echo. RESIDUAL: `compute_user_op_hash`'s userOpHash-PREIMAGE assembly (the pack before the keccak) is a smaller follow-up — the keccak itself is the opaque boundary, but the preimage LAYOUT is Kani-able like fw-manifest's `signed_preimage_layout`.
- [x] **P5 — protocol-model regression GATE + CI job — DONE 2026-07-01.** Two parts: **(1) the gate** (`scripts/check_protocol_models.py` + `make verify-protocol-models`) — the third anti-vacuity sibling of `verify-proof-mutation`/`verify-kani-mutation`. KEY finding: `make proverif`/`tamarin`/`cryptoverif` RUN the tools but exit 0 whether a query is true/false, and the models carry DESIGNED `is false` residuals — so a bare run is NOT a gate; a security query flipping true→false would pass silently. The gate asserts each model's verdict pattern vs a committed **per-file** baseline (advisor: per-file kills cross-file cancellation) — proverif per-`.pv` (true,false): dual_se(5,1)/scp03_handshake(3,1)/optiga_shield(3,1)/scp03_replay(1,0)/fw_update(1,1); tamarin per-`.spthy` verified: pin_lockstep 4 / scp03_replay 2 / seed_split_xor 2, all falsified=0; cryptoverif "All queries proved". Honest scope in the docstring: verdict-COUNT tripwire, not identity. `PROTOCOL_MODELS=` family selector. Validated all-pass locally + **go-red confirmed** (perturb a baseline → FAIL). **(2) the CI job** — nightly `protocol-models` (checkct-mirror: `ocaml/setup-ocaml` + `opam install proverif.2.05` + prebuilt tamarin-1.12.0/maude-3.5.1 binaries, all version-PINNED to the baseline), runs `PROTOCOL_MODELS=proverif,tamarin` (the 8 symbolic models). CryptoVerif EXPLICITLY local-only (nix `-lib` install; its one property is already gated by tamarin/seed_split_xor.spthy) — a stated subset, not a silent skip. CI job unverified-until-first-nightly (the durable deliverable is the locally-validated gate; the CI install may need a first-run fixup — same resolution shape as P1).
- [ ] **P6 — CT/SCA gate — CHECKED 2026-07-01, TWO open halves (neither a clean quick win).** **(a) cargo-checkct** WORKS + is locally validated (re-confirmed: 4 SECURE — kdf/fors/th/saes — + the by-design-INSECURE `fisher_yates` shuffle control), but the nightly `checkct` job is `workflow_dispatch`-only + `continue-on-error: true` → **NOT a real gate**. Blocked on a from-source binsec in a CLEAN CI opam switch (the opam-released `binsec` builds the ARM decoder XOR the checkct plugin, never both — fully diagnosed, `tools/sca/DONJON-RUST-TOOLING.md` §1). Making it a real gate = fiddly OCaml/opam CI work (the unverifiable-locally kind — same risk shape as P5's CI half). **(b) ct_analyzer advisory pass** — genuinely NOT wired (grep-confirmed absent from Makefile + workflows); cheaper (source/IR pre-filter, no binsec) but it's a ToB marketplace skill so wiring/feasibility is uncertain. So CT is CAPABILITY-validated-locally but not a standing regression gate; both closure paths are non-trivial. Guards against a secret-dependent branch reappearing after `opt-level=s` (the checkct driver profile was aligned to the shipped `opt-level="s"`+`overflow-checks` 2026-07-02, CT-1 — previously it built at the cargo template default `opt-level=3`/no-overflow-checks, so the "reappearing after opt-level=s" guarantee did not exercise the shipped codegen; cross-crate LTO is not exercised — the drivers are single-crate — a documented residual, not a closed axis). SCOPE: covers the 4 kdf/fors/th/saes drivers, NOT the `subtle` compares (the c10 double-compute `ct_eq`, PIN pre-commit) — those remain source-review + rainbow/lascar TVLA.
- [ ] **P7 — clear-sign FV expansion + USB decoder — USB REASSEMBLY HALF DONE 2026-07-02.** The attacker-facing USB APDU reassembly + framing + chain-cursor was ALREADY host-extracted in `shared/src/apdu_framing.rs` (proptest-fuzzed, no ∀ proof). Added 4 Kani harnesses proving ∀ panic / out-of-bounds-write freedom over a FULLY ARBITRARY assembler state (no `kani::assume`) + a symbolic 64-byte frame — `process_frame` continuation (incl. `rx_pos` at/over the `MAX_APDU_RX` boundary, the overflow-critical write) + first-frame/scrub, `ChainState::step` cursor-within-capacity, `parse_apdu_header` soundness — + the `ApduComplete(len) <= MAX_APDU_RX` bound. Mutation-gated: `kani_mutations.json: usb_apdu_reassembly_bounds` (removing the reassembly bounds guard flips `process_frame_continuation_no_oob` to FAILED, hand-verified). Closed `S-USB-OVERFLOW` UNCLAIMED→PARTIAL (`THREAT_CLAIM_MAP.md`). **Remaining:** (a) the per-command dispatch in `nonsecure::usb::commands` (1111 LOC) is still host-uncompilable — needs a host-compile path before its command-boundary/report-framing can be Kani'd (the other half); (b) more Kani on the erc7730/multiSend layers (higher effort, diminishing returns since the renderer is `#[cfg(not(test))]`). Medium.

Gains `bv_decide` for mask proofs + std-lib breadth. Won't move the crypto / manual-effort walls.

Migrate a surface only if it's ≥ an order cheaper; else keep Aeneas.

Genuinely-new capability but heavy (F* expertise), hits the crypto boundary, carries the "verification facade" critique (ePrint 2026/670). Future protocol-assurance item, NOT a quick win.

**NOT mine / genuinely hard** (flagged for completeness — do not propose as a next step): A3.1 verifier-refinement (active user front — Halmos 38/38, Kontrol 30/30; residual = the ∀-signature symbolic ceiling); `verify_signs` completeness (3-6 person-months, explicitly *not* in `theft_free`'s closure = completeness not safety); the invariant #1-#4 spanning theorem (person-months multi-tool composition; cited-TCB residual per `FV_VALUE_AND_GAPS.md` gap #1); on-silicon CT/dudect/TVLA + register-EM leakage (bench ChipWhisperer — though the `rainbow` `fault_skip` emulation half of the CT-1 re-check IS session-doable).

**Don't chase** (hype / same-wall / wrong-direction): bumping Kani (we run the latest; CBMC pinned + delta irrelevant); any tool "that handles crypto" (all reproduce `--opaque`); Verus wholesale (invasive `verus!{}` macro on a mature `#![no_std]` base; 3rd toolchain; shifts Lean-tactic cost into Z3 trigger engineering); Prusti (self-labeled unmaintained); RefinedRust (Coq/Iris effort ≥ Aeneas); Gillian/VeriFast-Rust (target *unsafe* aliasing — future NS-ptr/CMSE, not our safe pure-logic); `BoundedArbitrary` (formalizes the bound, doesn't close it).

### FV surface-broadening 2026-07-02 — Tier 1/2 delivered + **Tier 3 evaluation**

Executing round + 5 ground-truth scouts (A3.1 / Aeneas §33 / Kani / WYSIWYS / checkct), then session-tractable increments. Verdict framing: the proofs were already sound; broadening = *more independently-verified surfaces, each honestly bounded* (no device-wide spanning theorem is achievable — SoK ePrint 2019/1393).

**Tier 1/2 DELIVERED (7 commits, all on `origin/master`, each locally verified):**
- [x] **CT leak closed** — extending checkct to the shipped `opt-level=s` caught a REAL side channel: `cmac.rs::double_l`'s GF(2¹²⁸) reduction was a secret-MSB branch under `-Os` (SAES-CMAC(DHUK) subkeys). Branchless mask; `driver_saes` SECURE + 29 CMAC KATs. `1d2c68f7`.
- [x] **ERC-7730 resolver no-misdecode** (Kani) — `resolve_structured` FollowOffset leaf byte-binds to the exact ABI offset word ∀ (was panic-free-only; the documented walker-slot-confusion HIGH path) + non-vacuity witness + negative control. `1d2c68f7`.
- [x] **ABI two-dynamic-tail canonical packing** (Kani) — the cross-tail `tail_cursor` carry the 7 single-tail harnesses never exercised: two dyn args pack disjointly, no aliasing ∀. `2c6e13a5`.
- [x] **driver_ct_eq** — CT-proves `subtle::ct_eq` (c10 double-compute / dual_se master / SCP03-MAC / FW-verify compares); 5th SECURE checkct driver; nightly gate 4→5. `59ec0af0`.
- [x] **SlotKdf §33 re-prove** — re-extracted + re-proved the 4 byte-layout theorems against the zeroize-hardened Rust (`+ zeroize` in the `simp`, `step*` absorbs); closes the extracted-F3 stale-body drift; `make verify-extracted` green. `ce369fdf`.
- [x] **format_decimal no-panic** (Kani) — panic/OOB-free + well-formed output over ∀ (decimals,frac,trim) format shapes (round-carry/trim/structural-zero — where M-6/F14 lived). Value CONCRETE (symbolic-U256 CBMC ran > 10 min at four bounds down to 2¹⁶ — the symbolic-VALUE ∀ is deferred). `a1c71df3`.
- [x] **A3.1 doc reconciliation** — corrected a public UNDER-statement: `execC10Asm_eq` (model↔spec ∀) is KERNEL-COMPLETE (2026-06-18), not "underway/corpus-only"; sole residual is R1 (bytecode↔AST). `3b1d4655`.
- [x] **A3.1 R1a source↔AST STRUCTURAL check (the time-boxed Yul-parser bet)** — `check_c10_transcription_ast.py` parses BOTH the `.sol` `assembly{}` block AND the committed `c10Program` literal into the `Interpreter.Yul` AST and asserts **tree equality** (564 nodes each side), closing the statistical lint's documented intra-fragment hole (reorder / var-swap / operand-swap). Negative-control harness proves 3 such mutants are invisible to the statistical lint yet caught by the structural check (old-lint PASS / new-check FAIL each). Wired into `make verify-transcription` + the `a31-transcription.yml` CI gate. **Honest scope: upgrades R1a (source↔AST) statistical→structural; R1b (solc source→bytecode) untouched; parser + 2 normalization rules are now the TCB.** See `A3_1_CLOSURE_PATH.md` §8 (2026-07-02). *(commit pending)*.

**Tier 2 tail (session-scoped OUT — the honest multi-week frontier):**
- [ ] **A3.1 R1a in-KERNEL Yul parser** — the *host-side structural* check above landed (R1a is now structural, CI-gated). The remaining multi-week piece is the in-kernel `parseYul(sol) = c10Program` by `decide` (moves the parser from a trusted Python script into a kernel-checked Lean function). Real risk the kernel-reduction of a KB-scale-string parser over ~200 statements swamps (`native_decide` banned — drags `Lean.ofReduceBool` into the audit closure). *Moves* trust to the parser, doesn't eliminate; R1b bytecode↔source (solc) stays cited-TCB regardless. Lower ROI now that R1a is structurally gated.
- [ ] **ERC-7730 formatter-∀ faithfulness** — extract `formatters.rs`+`Pages` to a pure crate (precedent exists). **RE-SCOPED 2026-07-04 (scout):** the restructure half is SMALLER than "multi-week" — `primitives.rs`+render/* already extracted, the whole formatter tree already host-compiles via the `display_under_test` mount, and the move is pre-scoped in `docs/erc7730-renderer-fuzzability.md` §Follow-up (M2, 1–2 sessions, gated on the golden-grid-hash + 60 render tests byte-identical). Remaining milestones: M2 crate move → M3 Tier-P0 formatter ∀ (raw-hex exactness flagship, unlimited-gate, loud markers, addr-hex, const/enum chunking) → M4 amount-decision factoring + decision-ladder ∀ + Tier-P1 (`write_decimal_into`/`unix_to_ymdhms` round-trips). Amount-paint ∀ stays gated on the `format_decimal` swamp (separate item below). **M1 resolver VALUE-soundness: ✅ DONE 2026-07-04** (see Completion Log — slice/array-elem byte-binding, slot-sum + region-chain laws ∀, resolve_array count-binding; the "panic-free-only on the value-that-executes half" asymmetry is CLOSED).
- [ ] **format_decimal symbolic-VALUE ∀** (+ injectivity / no-collapse) — the byte-wise `div10_inplace` is a CBMC solve-time swamp; multi-session (bit-blast tuning / a different engine).
- [ ] **checkct CI-rewire** — replace the opam-install-binsec step (which the job's own header calls broken) with from-source binsec + drop `continue-on-error`/`workflow_dispatch`; authorable, but CI-green needs one dispatch iteration (container-only, not locally reproducible).
- [ ] **WYSIWYS end-to-end last mile** — the render↔signed-buffer identity (`pick_sign_pages`/`cmd_sign_userop.rs` dispatcher glue) is NOT Kani/Lean-closable; it stays an e2e/differential obligation. Do not read "render proven" as "WYSIWYS proven".

**TIER 3 EVALUATION (2026-07-02) — verdict: DO NOT chase Tier 3 as verification work.** These are gated by MISSING FOUNDATIONS or RESEARCH-FRONTIER scale, not effort; the primary controls (on-chain caps, per-SE silicon lockout, cited crypto) already bound the risk without them, and every Tier-3 interface is an explicit cited-TCB defeater in `ASSURANCE_CASE.md`/`TRUST_ASSUMPTIONS.md`. Restated from the existing "NOT mine / genuinely hard" + "Don't chase" blocks above, with the honest ROI call per item:
- **T3a — Invariant #1–#4 model→Rust spanning theorem (protocol model → secure-crate Rust).** No sound tool composes a Dolev-Yao symbolic model down to the implementation (Aeneas proves functional correctness, not secrecy/reachability). Same interface seL4 leaves as assumption. **Partial in-road already exists** (`Crypto/SplitSecrecy.lean` kernel-proves the IT core of #1); more combinatorial cores are incrementally possible, but a *spanning* theorem is not. `hax→ProVerif/F*` (F3 above) is the only credible down-payment and is heavy/crypto-boundary/facade-critiqued. **Move: strengthen each END (design models + Rust functional proofs) + DISCLOSE the interface. Do not build the span.**
- **T3b — TrustZone isolation (#4).** Needs a formal model of the STM32U5 SAU/GTZC silicon — none exists, narrow reuse. **Ceiling: silicon-E2E + the compile-time subset assert (`sau.rs` `const _:()`).**
- **T3c — C10 bit-security (A5 quantitative).** Closing it = extending the Barbosa'24 EasyCrypt SPHINCS+ development to the +C variant — **person-years**. The generic-attack floor (`Quantitative.lean`, 96-bit) is the tractable part, DONE. **Qualitative reduction stays cited; full mechanized C10 EUF-CMA is out of scope.**
- **T3d — Verified hash silicon (A1) + keccak.** No verified SHA-256/keccak for the STM32 HASH peripheral or geth's `0x02` precompile in any prover; even Verity stops at Yul. **Permanent cited-TCB — irreducible.**
- **Recommendation:** keep investing in Tier-1/2 increments (where verified assurance actually grows) + maintain the defeater register. The single Tier-3-adjacent bet worth considering is T3-adjacent A3.1 R1 (the Yul-parser above), time-boxed.
