## Trezor delta pass 2026-07-02 (see `docs/architecture/trezor-comparison-critical-port-2026-06.md` §UPDATE 2026-07)

**Meta finding — the June-2026 critical-port backlog is unimplemented.** Of the ~18 validated
items from that pass, only `rr-1` (HardFault→zeroize+reset, `main.rs:3907`) shipped. Verified
still-open with grep evidence 2026-07-02: `tz-2` (0 `CSLCKR`/`LCK` in `sau.rs`), `tz-3`
(0 `RCC_SECCFGR` repo-wide), `tz-1` (no `OPTR` read in `fsbl/`), `boot-1` (bump still at
`cmd_fw_commit.rs:268`), `rr-2` (0 `BUSY_TICKS` in `iwdg.rs`), `te-2` (`golden.rs` still only
`value_transfer`), `sca-1` (boot-only seed, no periodic reseed), `sh-3` (`is_provisioned` still
"any byte != 0xFF"), `td-1`/`td-2`/`se-3`. **The bottleneck is execution, not discovery** — pull
these into a sprint rather than commissioning another survey. All rows live in the June doc's
final `Proposed work-todo rows` block; do not re-derive.

### New / re-elevated this pass

- [ ] **[hash-fault-1, med, S] `sca-3` RE-ELEVATED with a second, stronger rationale — independent
  shuffle seed per double-compute pass.** `crypto.rs:170/173` feed the SAME `&shuffle` to both
  signs. Beyond the June SCA argument (two perfectly-aligned DPA traces), this also opens an **FI
  seam**: the HW HASH engine is a single point trusted by sign_a, sign_b, AND the verify-before-
  release, so a *deterministic/reproducible* HASH fault (position-triggered) corrupts all three
  identically → passes `ct_eq` and verify → exactly the Genêt grafting-leak class the double-
  compute exists to block. Independent seeds decorrelate the computation ORDER between passes, so a
  position-triggered fault hits different WOTS/FORS positions per pass → divergent sigs → caught.
  Output is byte-invariant across seeds (PROVEN + tested: `shuffle_byte_equality.rs:65`
  `multiple_random_shuffles_all_equal` covers many nonzero seeds vs the zero reference, transitively
  covering any two distinct nonzero seeds), so `ct_eq`/verify still hold. **Also correct the
  misleading `crypto.rs:138-154` comment** — it defends same-seed by claiming re-drawing yields
  "divergent internal hash streams" as a *cost*; that divergence is the *benefit*, and the comment
  contradicts the crate's own `shuffle.rs:16-19` byte-invariance invariant. Add an explicit
  two-distinct-nonzero-seed byte-equality assertion for clarity. ~3 LOC + 1 test + comment fix.

- [ ] **[hash-fault-2, med, S] Fail LOUD on HASH-engine timeout (currently silent stale-digest).**
  `pqsigner_sha256_final` DCIS-timeout `return`s at `hash.rs:331` BEFORE the HR-read loop, leaving
  the caller's 32-byte `out` untouched (stale prior digest / stack garbage) with no error channel
  (`debug_log_safe` is a no-op without a debugger). Same in `wait_ready` (`hash.rs:186`, used by
  `_init`). A glitch-induced hang thus degrades to silent wrong-digest. Fix: on timeout, park the
  CPU like the boot-KAT FAIL path (`hash.rs:160` `loop { wfe() }`) or poison `out` with a sentinel
  — a hash that timed out mid-signature has no safe continuation. Also assert FIFO-drained
  (`NBWP==0`/`DINNE` clear) + `BUSY` cleared before trusting HR. ~10-15 LOC. Note: STM32U5 `HASH_SR`
  has NO hardware fault flag (SVD-confirmed), so this catches the *stalled/incomplete* class (DoS),
  not wrong-but-complete — belt-and-braces, but closes a real silent-corruption path.

- [ ] **[hash-fault-3, low, S] Optional per-sign KAT.** Re-run the "abc" KAT inside
  `c10_sign_verified*` before releasing the sig (~1 µs) to catch a latched/deterministic engine
  defect that arose *between* boot and sign — closes the "deterministic fault → self-consistent
  verify" sliver that hash-fault-1 mostly covers. Lower priority than hash-fault-1.

- [ ] **[keygen-fi-1, low, M] Double-compute keygen `pk_root` on the provisioning path only.**
  `c10::keygen` is single-compute through the HW engine; a HASH fault at first-ever
  `GET_WALLET_ADDRESS`/provisioning → wrong `masterPkRoot` → wrong CREATE2 address → funds sent
  there are unspendable (later re-derivations give the correct, different address). Only path where
  one HASH fault survives to an externally-visible artifact. Fault-safety, not attacker-profit.

- [ ] **[rng-1, low, S] Per-word TRNG sanity check in `rng.rs::fill`.** Add a `DR != 0` check
  (RM0456 seed-error-race can read 0) and a Trezor-style CRNGT repeat-word compare (one `static`
  word of state). U5 on-die SP800-90B tests + `SECS/CECS` polling (`rng.rs:100-106`) cover most
  cases, but this hardens the **non-SE-folded** secret paths — BHK generation (`bhk.rs:223`) and
  ML-KEM message `m` (`pq_wrap.rs:87`) call raw `rng::fill`, not `rng_strong` — against a
  degradation subtle enough to evade the hardware tests. Also assert `SECS/CECS==0` after warmup in
  `rng::init()`. Cheap parity-with-Trezor defense-in-depth.

- [ ] **[rng-2, low, S] Align `provision_duress_wallet` (`crypto.rs:331-352`) with `rng_strong`
  strict semantics.** It hand-rolls the entropy mix with a SILENT SE fall-through
  (`if store.random(...).is_ok()`) and the declined-duress random PIN is platform-TRNG-only —
  inconsistent with the module's own fail-closed philosophy (the decoy is meant to be fundable).

- [ ] **[rng-3, DESIGN] External-entropy verifiability at seed creation.** PQSigner's RNG stack is
  on-device-only; an attacker-rigged RNG is unauditable. Trezor mixes host external entropy +
  an internal-entropy HMAC commitment + interactive entropy-check (`reset_device/__init__.py`).
  Does NOT conflict with invariant #6 (entropy source doesn't affect determinism after the 24 words
  exist). Partially mitigated by reproducible builds + measured boot. Record as deliberate
  design decision (adopt vs decline), not silently drop.

- [ ] **[erc7730-arr-elem, low, S] Optional: add Date/Enum arms to `render_array_element`
  (`formatters.rs:1090`).** Currently rejects array-of-date / array-of-enum → decline-to-blind. No
  shipping descriptor uses these, so not a vulnerability — reuse `render_date`/`render_enum`
  per-word logic with the same loud-reject-on-overflow discipline if a future descriptor needs it.

**Recorded as PQSigner LEADS Trezor (no port):** ERC-7730 clear-signing. Trezor's four new
clear-signing commits (multi-value arrays `f9f6eb9778`, date/raw formatters `600cf34e40`,
const-token-address `c5aa28511f`, `f52bea0a03` chunkify) are all already present in PQSigner and
PQSigner is AHEAD on date-overflow/year/block-magnitude guards, raw two-page byte-spill (WYSIWYS),
Kani-proven array exact-placement + tail-hiding defense, and a 14-op FormatOp table vs Trezor's 6.
The 27-commit Trezor delta (`a0fe1eccc2..HEAD`) is otherwise all Evolu/Tropic-prodtest/UI-cosmetic/
translations — nothing new portable. RNG stack is at parity-or-ahead (stricter all-zero gate,
broader strong-RNG coverage of signing randomness).

| 2026-06-16 | Animated splash-screen preview on the NV3007 LCD (`splash-test` feature + `make splash-test-hw`) | Ported the three `assets/splash-1{6,7,8}-*.standalone.html` revisions (hyperspace / horizon / nebula) — which share one identical `<canvas>` animation registry — to a no_std Rust renderer in `secure/src/ui/splash_test.rs`. Renders into a 1-bit packed **landscape** framebuffer (428×142, 7.6 KB — a full RGB565 native frame would be 119 KB vs the 192 KB secure SRAM budget) then blits via the SAME landscape→native transpose the trusted-UI text path uses (`ui::lcd` `FLIP=(true,false)` → native `(nx,ny)=(141-ly, lx)`). f32 math via a new **optional** `micromath` dep (pure-Rust no_std approximations; `num-traits` off; only compiled under `splash-test`). `main()` short-circuits into `ui::splash_test::run()` after `ui::init()` brings the panel up + SysTick starts (so `timeout::now()` is the animation clock); cycles the 3 revisions ~12 s each forever. One intentional divergence: integer-LCG PRNG (the browser's f64 multiply loses precision before masking) → star coordinates differ but field density/character match. **Verified:** clean `cargo check` for thumbv8m (zero diagnostics in the new module), non-splash builds unaffected, `WORDMARK` byte-identical to source, and a 4-agent adversarial fidelity workflow (per-animation + scaffolding/orientation reviewers, each finding independently verified) returned **0 confirmed divergences**. **On-silicon confirmed (all 3 render correctly), then an optimization pass landed for smoothness:** (1) blit rewritten from 428 per-row `set_window` calls/frame to ONE full-frame `set_window` + a single continuous chunked RAMWR stream via a new `lcd_nv3007::write_pixels_with(n, closure)` primitive (mirrors the proven `write_pixels_solid` framing; scan-order verified pixel-identical against the HW-validated `blit_glyph` multi-row fill) — makes all 3 animations SPI-bound (~24 ms/121 KB-at-40 MHz floor ⇒ ~40 fps for hyperspace/horizon); (2) nebula per-pixel `powf(n,2.2)` → 256-entry LUT and per-pixel `sqrt` for `carve` eliminated via a squared-distance branch that skips the carved-out centre AND the saturated body (`sqrt` only in the 104..172 annulus) — the dominant nebula cost; (3) per-revision avg-FPS logged for bench readout. A 2-agent workflow verified both changes behavior-preserving (`safe:true`, no bugs; one optional `get` bounds-guard applied for `set`/`get` symmetry). **On-silicon FPS readout then exposed the REAL bottleneck:** hyperspace 16 fps / horizon 6 fps / nebula ~0.8 fps (1.2 s/frame) — far worse than any blit/compute estimate, because the firmware is otherwise all-integer so **the Cortex-M33 FPU was never enabled** and the default `thumbv8m.main-none-eabi` (soft-float ABI, no FPU feature) compiled every `sin`/`cos`/`sqrt`/`powf` to soft-float emulation (~24 µs/transcendental). **Fix: build `splash-test-hw` with `-C target-feature=+fp-armv8d16sp` (the M33 FPU, soft-float ABI KEPT so the CMSE veneer / NS interface is byte-unchanged — "softfp") + `enable_fpu()` flips `CPACR` CP10/CP11 at the top of `run()` before the first VFP op.** Binary-verified: 698 hardware VFP instructions (incl. `vmla.f32` in the noise/bilinear hot loops), zero f32 soft-float in the render bodies (the only residual is `fmodf` for the float `%` in hyperspace drift — inherent, no VFP modulo exists, negligible). This is the dominant smoothness win (nebula compute ~30× faster). **Post-FPU FPS (20/17/10) + a new compute-vs-blit DWT split** (logged per-revision) then localised the remaining bottleneck precisely: blit = **constant 48.6 ms for all three** (vs `fill_screen`'s 24 ms floor for the same byte count) while compute was tiny (hyperspace 1.4 ms) — i.e. the blit was **CPU-bound on the per-pixel transpose+bit-unpack**, not SPI-bound; also bumped `micromath` + splash hot fns to `opt-level=3` / `#[optimize(speed)]`. Restructured the framebuffer to **native scan order** (`set` does the `FLIP=(true,false)` transpose once per *lit* pixel: `k = lx*FRAME_WIDTH + (141-ly)`) so the blit became a branch-light **byte-wise expand** (verified pixel-identical to the prior workflow-blessed blit by an exhaustive host program over all 60 776 pixels — perfect bijection, 0 mismatches, no OOB; binary shows 0 multiplies in the blit body). **But the next on-silicon readout showed the blit UNCHANGED at exactly 48.6 ms** — disproving the CPU-bound hypothesis. Root cause: `spi_hw::init` runs the `ui-lcd` SPI at **÷8 = 20 MHz** (chosen for the *trusted UI* because the dev board's LD2 LED on PE13=SCK shimmers at 40 MHz), and 121 552 B × 8 / 20 MHz = exactly 48.6 ms — the blit was **SPI-clock-bound**, not CPU-bound, which is why the closure work was free (hidden in the FIFO-wait shadow). **Fix: the `splash-test` build alone runs SPI at ÷4 = 40 MHz** (mutually-exclusive cfg from the 20 MHz trusted-UI default; binary-confirmed `CFG1=0x10000007`) → blit ~halves to ~24 ms. The native-layout byte-wise blit, while not the bottleneck at 20 MHz, is what keeps the polled FIFO fed at 40 MHz (the heavier old closure would have starved). Net expected: hyperspace ~38 / horizon ~30 / nebula ~14 fps. On-silicon confirmed: hyperspace **38** / horizon **30** / nebula **14** fps, blit now a constant 24.3 ms (the 40 MHz floor) — hyperspace/horizon maxed for a full-frame repaint, **nebula compute-bound at 43.8 ms**. Took the safe compute lever first: **coarsened the nebula noise grid step 4→6** (`GW/GH = div_ceil(STEP)+1` so the bilinear's +1 neighbours stay in-grid even when STEP doesn't divide LW — host-verified bounds-safe across all 60 776 pixels, 0 OOB; **2.1× fewer `noise()` calls/frame**, 1825 vs 3888; also now renders the full 142 rows vs the old 2-row bottom skip). Scoped DMA fully (GPDMA1 secure base `0x5002_0000`, SPI1_TX req 7, `CFG1.TXDMAEN` bit 15; 16-bit SPI items make `TSIZE=60776` fit one transaction; CMSIS lacks GPDMA bitfields but the SVD has them) — but at 40 MHz DMA-overlap helps ONLY nebula (hyperspace/horizon are transfer-bound), and it's a from-scratch blind GPDMA+linked-list driver needing on-silicon debug iterations, so it's deferred in favour of the safe compute lever; DMA's real payoff is a future 80 MHz "all-three-faster" push (signal-integrity risk on the LD2-on-SCK dev board). Next on-silicon readout will show how much the grid coarsening recovered (implicitly the field-vs-pixel split) before deciding further compute cuts (step→8 / octaves 3→2) vs the DMA+80 MHz project. |

| 2026-06-13 | R-derivation hardening — secret-keyed, message-bound R (chosen-message FORS-saturation defense, TRNG-failure-robust) | `fors::grind_r` now derives `R = sha256(sk_seed ‖ "R_grind" ‖ [opt_rand] ‖ message ‖ nonce)[0..16]` (was `sha256("R_grind" ‖ [opt_rand] ‖ nonce)` — no secret, no message). Binding `sk_seed` + `message` makes `ht_idx = bits[143..161] of h_msg(pk_seed,pk_root,R,message)` unpredictable to anyone without the secret key for any chosen message, closing the chosen-message FORS-saturation avenue (upstream SPHINCS- SECURITY-ANALYSIS.md §2 "Avenue B") EVEN under a biased/predictable TRNG — production was previously protected only by mandatory fresh opt_rand. Mirrors FIPS 205 `PRF_msg` keying R on a secret. `opt_rand` retained as defense-in-depth (F-9 iteration-count leak + Genêt). **Signer-side only**: the frozen on-chain Yul verifier reads R from the signature and is unaffected; deployed contracts unchanged. Threaded `sk_seed` through `grind_r` (only caller `hypertree::sign_inner` has it). Updated the Lean signer model `Spec/Signer.lean::grindR` to match (faithfulness; `verify_signs` is parametric on the consistency hypothesis so unaffected) and lifted `secure/src/crypto.rs`'s opt_rand justification to cite the cryptographic argument, not just SCA/F-9. **Validation** (all green): sphincs-c10 56/56 (sign→verify roundtrip with new R); regenerated c10_test_vectors.json + bulk_vectors.json (new R, each self-verified under the unchanged Yul-matching `verify`); re-embedded KatVectors.lean + BulkVectors.lean; Lean `make verify` 10/10 (digest+htIdx+full) and `make verify-bulk` 384/384; secure host suite 2030 passed / 1 pre-existing unrelated usb_hw lint; Lean build 0 sorry. **forge NOT run** (absent in dev env) — but the Solidity KAT tests only assert `verify()==expectValid` (no exact-byte pinning), the verifier is unchanged, and every regenerated vector self-verifies in Rust + passes the Lean differential,  so forge CI will pass. Diverges from the legacy upstream Python (which is the OLD design) — the deferred patched-Python-oracle leg must apply the same hardening. **UPDATE: forge installed (v1.7.1) + run 2026-06-13 — validation CONFIRMED on the deployed Yul:** `SPHINCsC10Asm.t.sol` 13/13 incl. `test_verifyAllKatVectors` AND the pinned verifier codehash 0xf1ef…fef5 (the verifier built locally is byte-identical to the deployed/audited one and ACCEPTS the R-hardened KATs), `SPHINCsC10AsmAdversarial` 6/6 mutant screen, `PQSmartWalletRealSig` 4/4 real-UserOp C10 sig. 105/108 forge tests pass; the 3 failures are wallet/factory codehash pins (build-env, see below), NOT this change (zero .sol touched). |

(2) `Spec/Bytes.lean::loadWord32` returned an all-zero word whenever a 32-byte read window overran the 4008-byte blob, silently zeroing the final 16-byte auth-path entry (offset 3992, window [3992,4024) overruns by 16) — unlike EVM `calldataload`, which returns available bytes + zero-pads the tail; fix = zero-pad. This is exactly why layer 0 verified perfectly and only layer 1's last merkle step was corrupted. | 2026-06-12 | §33 12-rank verification catalog COMPLETE (ranks 5+8 close it) — and a REAL 32-bit RLP parse-desync bug found by the rank-8 proof, FIXED | Frontier grind session 2 (lean-lsp-mcp proof loop): **rank 5 `pk_from_sig_spec`** (the catalog's hardest) PROVEN — verifier-side WOTS+C: digit-sum-205 gate exact in both directions, each of the 43 chains advanced exactly `(start=digit, steps=7-digit)` with every ADRS byte pinned, th_multi compress; content axioms exactly the three hash boundaries `wots_digest/chain_hash/th_multi _pure`. loop0 invariant = partial digit-sum; loop1 invariant = per-chain `chain_hash_pure` with `BitVec.ofNat`-lifted args (new `u32_eq_ofNat` helper); `congr 1` whnf-times-out on the opaque axiom — `congrArg` instead. **Rank 8 `decode_item_spec`** PROVEN kernel-clean (zero content axioms — all five core stdlib helpers DEF'd via the into_iter pattern) against the independent #eval-ground-truthed `decodeRef` RLP grammar. **The proof found a real bug:** `decode_length_be` used `acc.checked_shl(8)`, and rustc's `checked_shl` returns None only when the SHIFT AMOUNT ≥ BITS — overflowing VALUE bits are silently discarded. On a 32-bit usize (thumbv8m = the STM32U585!) a `len_of_len ≥ 5` length field WRAPPED (e.g. claim 2^56+5 → accept a 5-byte payload) — parse-desync vs every 64-bit decoder; the spec was only provable against a deliberately-stricter model. **Fixed** `tx-core/src/rlp.rs` to `acc.checked_mul(256)` (extracts to Aeneas stdlib `Usize.checked_mul` — hand model deleted entirely), re-extracted (drift guards green ×4 incl. line-number refresh of the rank-7 Rlp Funs), re-proved the loop lemma against the real semantics, added regression test `negative_length_field_exceeding_usize_errors_not_wraps` (tx-core 140/140 green). Exploitability bounded: a wrapped-length tx is invalid to the real network (64-bit geth demands the un-wrapped byte count), so this was sign-garbage/desync, not theft — but the trusted display's parse MUST match the network's. All 12 ranks now in the sorry-free default target + `AxiomCheck.lean`; full `lake build` green (1737 jobs); content axioms across the whole catalog are exactly the declared hash boundaries; ranks 4,7,8,9,10,12 fully kernel-clean. | 2026-06-12 | SCP03 counter desync on bare-error responses (silicon-found, wizard-bricking) FIXED — and first full LCD-standalone first-boot wizard E2E | Symptom: `flash-hw-dual-se-lcd-standalone-debug` first-boot wizard looped forever at PIN entry — `rng_strong::fill FAILED` on every NewWallet attempt. **Two stacked causes.** (1) *OPTIGA leg:* the chip's PRL was wedged in the SCTR=0x40 alert state from an earlier failed handshake in the same powered session (this build's `dev-testkey` PBS ≠ the chip's DHUK pairing from 2026-05-12; **probe-rs flash/reset never power-cycles the SEs**, so the wedge survives reflashes) — every plaintext APDU bounced with the 7-byte `08 40` alert frame. `Optiga::random` is the one OPTIGA op with no `ensure_shield`, so wizard entropy went plaintext into the wedge; the bounce also made the still-provisioned OPTIGA read as blank (counter probes → None → "fresh chip"). Cleared by USB power-cycle; provisioning then re-paired E140 to the dev-testkey PBS via `setup_pbs_no_handshake` (LcsO=Creation). (2) *SE050 leg (the code fix):* `scp03::unwrap_response` did **not** advance the encryption counter on bare-error-SW responses. On the wiped chip the FIRST secured command (reconcile's ReadObjectAttributes on the deleted USERID) legitimately returned bare `0x6985` → host counter froze one behind the card → next command's C-ENC ICV was garbage to the card → `0x6982` (card terminates session) → every later APDU `0x6985` → wizard rng dead. **This empirically refutes the 2026-05-28 row's "counter-desync alarm was a false positive" dismissal**: silicon shows the card consumes the counter when its SM layer processes a command, regardless of applet outcome (GP Amd D §6.2.6 counts per command sent). Fix: `session.inc_counter()` in the bare-SW arm of `unwrap_response` + corrected doc comment (`secure/src/se050/scp03.rs`). Why no e2e ever caught it: provisioned-chip flows never error mid-session; the wiped-chip wizard is the natural trigger. **Validation on real B-U585I + NV3007 LCD:** full first-boot wizard E2E — 3-source entropy OK (STM32⊕OPTIGA⊕SE050), 24-word display + spot-check, `[DUAL] Provisioned` (E120 found already-provisioned → idempotent skip), `[DUAL] Unlocked: entropy reconstructed from XOR split`, NS boot + USB polling. SCP03 session survived dozens of secured commands **with interleaved benign applet errors** (the exact desync trigger) — also the first end-to-end functional silicon validation of the S-5 `P1=0x33` channel (R-ENC responses decrypted throughout); the S-5 LA capture stays open as a separate item. Host suite 2030 passed / 1 pre-existing `usb_hw.rs` failure. **Follow-ups (not landed):** `Optiga::random` should `ensure_shield` once paired (CRIT-8 gap — chip-TRNG bytes plaintext on I²C when shield down; needs the virgin-chip pair-then-draw ordering decision); generalize the 2026-05-12 `factory_reset` fix — drop `optiga.ready` on any PRL alert so the next op re-inits the chip out of the wedge; wizard should surface an actionable diagnostic on rng failure instead of silently looping to PIN entry (`pin_entry.rs:75` `let _` + generic "RNG failed"). |
| 2026-06-12 | ZK byte-pack commitment non-binding — proof-forgery fix (CRITICAL) across all 3 clear-sign circuits | Soundness audit of the circom clear-signing stack found the byte→field commitment `H_tx = Poseidon(pack31(bytes))` is **not byte-binding**: `PackBytes31` folded `acc <== acc*256 + byte` with **no `Num2Bits(8)` range check**, while the firmware computes the same hash over a genuine `[u8]`. Because byte signals were unconstrained field elements (and `256` is invertible mod r), the packing is non-injective — a malicious companion could present an in-circuit `canonical` of out-of-range "bytes" that pack to the **same** field elements as a *different, real* on-chain order, so Poseidon matches and the proof verifies while the device signs an order it never displayed (e.g. show "SELL 0.20 USDC", sign "SELL all WBTC" — `receiver`/`fee`/`validTo`/`appData` are bound ONLY via `H_tx` and were the forgery scratch). Full analysis + block-by-block constructibility (only block B3 couples; trivially met) in `docs/security/vulns/VULN-cowswap-zk-bytepack-nonbinding.md`. **Distinct from** the 2026-06-10 amount-overflow VULN (that range-checked the amount *value*; this is upstream of all decoding). **Fix:** `Num2Bits(8)` per input byte BEFORE the fold in all three byte-commitment packers — `circuits/lib/poseidon_bytes.circom::PackBytes31` (covers `cowswap_eip712_order`, i.e. both direct CoW **and** the Safe-wrapped presign path, which share the v3 VK), `circuits/cowswap/set_pre_signature::CsPackBytes31`, `circuits/aave_v3/clear_signing_proof::PackBytes31`. Every calldata/canonical/readable byte flows through one of these, so the check transitively covers all downstream decoders (registry pack, `Uint256BytesToField`, ABI slots). **Regen:** all 3 circuits recompiled (circom 2.2.3; constraints 21027/4937/9049, fit pot16/pot14/pot14), seed-pinned `groth16 setup`+`contribute` (added `circuits/aave_v3/contribution.seed`), new `circuit_final.zkey` + `.vk.bin` ×3; `cargo run -p dbgen` → new `VK_DB_ROOT=1380fc86…` + `nonsecure/src/vk_db.bin` + `vks.review.txt`; `vk_bin_to_rust.js` → `secure/src/zk/vk_data.rs`; aave `TEST_PROOF_A/B/C` regenerated against new zkey (`TEST_H_TX`/`TEST_H_STR` unchanged — same in-range bytes). **Validation:** `cargo run -p zk-test` PASS (firmware Groth16 verifier accepts the new aave proof+VK, both pairing paths); 59 `zk_under_test::pure_tests` PASS incl. `positive_verify_clear_signing_proof_known_good` + the unchanged-hash asserts; regenerated v3 CoW order proof verifies against new VK with `H_root == ERC20_POSEIDON_ROOT`; **negative proof the forgery is dead** — witness gen with `canonical[i]=256` now fails `Num2Bits_0` assert while `=255` succeeds; full secure host suite 2030/2030 (the 1 fail is the pre-existing unrelated `usb_hw.rs` raw-volatile lint, untouched here). Pre-deployment (no devices, no on-chain funds) so VKs overwritten freely. Files: `circuits/lib/poseidon_bytes.circom`, `circuits/cowswap/set_pre_signature/circuit.circom`, `circuits/aave_v3/clear_signing_proof.circom`, `circuits/{aave_v3,cowswap/eip712_order,cowswap/set_pre_signature}/circuit_final.zkey`, `circuits/aave_v3/contribution.seed`, `secure/data/vks/*.vk.bin`, `secure/src/db_roots.rs`, `secure/src/zk/{vk_data.rs,test_vectors.rs}`, `nonsecure/src/vk_db.bin`, `secure/data/vks.review.txt`, `docs/security/vulns/VULN-cowswap-zk-bytepack-nonbinding.md`. |
| 2026-06-11 | Safe-wrapped CowSwap pre-sign clear-signing (Groth16 `setPreSignature` via Gnosis Safe) | New feature: when a Safe flow's inner call is `GPv2Settlement.setPreSignature(orderUid, true)`, the firmware now verifies the companion-attached CoW v3 trailer (Groth16 proof or 204-B AddrOnly) bound to the **SafeTx's inner calldata** with `orderUid.owner == the Safe` (not the wallet `sender`), and renders a **combined** confirmation — Safe context (banner, address, nonce, gas-refund pages) + the full order intent + a "CowSwap order / for this Safe: 0x…" linkage banner. **Zero new wire/crate/command:** the existing `cowswap::verify_and_bind_trailer(bundle, calldata, chain_id, owner)` already takes the owner as a parameter (owner sits outside the EIP-712 digest AND the proof), so the entire 5-step CoW pipeline is reused as-is; both trailers (`zk_v3` kind 3 + `safe_v1` kind 4) already coexist on both the single and batch wires. **New code is just the seam:** (1) `secure/src/tx/eip712/safe/cow_binding.rs` — one pure resolver (`safe_inner_is_cow_presign` + `resolve_cow_binding → CowBinding{owner (owned), calldata, via_safe}`), shared by both handlers and their gates so the predicate has one definition (fail-closed: a wrong selection yields a pair the cross-check rejects, owner returned by value to avoid borrowing through the batch `routed[idx]` mutable write); (3) `safe_display.rs` `InnerKind::CowswapPresign` + context banner + body append; (4) dispatcher `pick_sign_pages_inner` routes `v3 && safe` through the Safe surface (a bare CoW render would HIDE the signed refund pages — a drain channel); (5) single-handler reorders Safe-verify above the v3-verify + adds the `via_safe` downgrade gate + closes the pre-existing FI-sentinel gap on the single-handler v3 verify (batch already had it); (6) batch splits TLV dispatch into two passes (ZK_V3 deferred to a second pass so the same-tx Safe context — wherever it sits in record order — is routed first) + render-loop gate; (7) NS VK injector (`inject_vk_bundle_at`) relaxed to shift-insert mid-payload (`copy_within`) so a proof-mode `zk_v3` followed by a `safe_v1` section still gets its VK auto-appended. **Non-goals:** revocation (`signed==false`, refused both paths), multiSend (DELEGATECALL-refused), off-chain EIP-1271 Safe (no Safe decode on that path). **Validation:** host suite 2030/2030 pass incl. 22 new (`cow_binding` 11-case matrix + `cowswap/test_vectors` owner=safe cross-check + full host pipeline compose); production firmware (`dual-se,stm32u585,mode-production,…`) builds clean; QEMU e2e Scenarios 5q (AddrOnly safe-wrapped presign → signs, combined pages verified pixel-by-pixel: "CowSwap order / for this Safe / kind=SELL / Receiver: (= the Safe)") + 5r (same without `zk_v3` → `CoW sign / v3 required` refusal) both PASS when run ahead of the **pre-existing** Scenario-2 blocker (reproduces identically on the unmodified tree — second-sign entropy/state reconstruction in QEMU/mock-se, same as documented in the 2026-06-10 firmware-signing-audit row; | 2026-06-11 | Off-chain `raw32` UserOp-forgery fix (CRITICAL) + EIP-712 encoded-data exact-length (MEDIUM) | Audit of the EIP-1271/ERC-6492 off-chain path found `CMD_SIGN_OFFCHAIN` kind=RAW32 **bare-signed** the companion's 32-byte payload with the slot key. The on-chain Type-2 path (`PQSmartWallet._validateSignature`) verifies a bare slot C10 sig over `sphincsDigest(userOp)` (plain SHA-256 of UserOp fields = firmware's `compute_sphincs_digest_v06`), so `raw32(sphincsDigest(drainOp))` was a valid UserOp signature → **full wallet drain behind a blind "! Raw 32-byte" page**, bypassing the trusted display (contradicted threat-model.md:172/:257). **Fix:** the firmware now performs the Solady replay-safe EIP-712 nesting itself for every off-chain kind. Added `aa::eip1271::replay_safe_hash(chain,wallet,H)` = `keccak(0x1901‖domainSep‖keccak(PERSONAL_SIGN_TYPEHASH‖H))` (no inner EIP-191 prefix; matches `ERC1271.sol:240-290`); refactored `personal_sign_replay_safe_hash` to `replay_safe_hash(prefixed(msg))` (kind=1 byte-identical). kind=RAW32 now sends the dapp's RAW hash H (companion no longer pre-nests) and the firmware nests it; kind=EIP712_TYPED switched from `personal_sign_replay_safe_hash(final_eip712)` (spurious double-prefix → would fail on-chain verify) to `replay_safe_hash(final_eip712)`. raw32 display/fingerprint now show H, not the internal nested hash. | 2026-06-09 | F-24 stage E Phase 1 (decoy frames) — bench-tested on the LCD @ 20 MHz, DEFENSE FAILS (repaint-limited) | New `make decoy-flicker-lcd-hw` + `decoy_flicker_test_loop` now sweeps `DECOY_HOLD` = 40/25/15/8/3/0 ms (logged). On the dev board at the production ÷8 = 20 MHz the decoy words are VISIBLE (switch) at every hold incl. DECOY_HOLD=0 — the defense fails. Root cause ≠ OLED (bistable pixels): here it's **repaint speed** — a full seed-page SPI blit is ~20 ms, above the eye's ~10–13 ms content flicker-fusion threshold, so each decoy frame reads as a distinct content change regardless of hold. The LCD's slow pixel response (~35 ms) would help only if the repaint were fast enough to exploit it. Lever = faster repaint (~40 MHz ≈ 11 ms + DMA / word-rows-only), but 40 MHz on this dev board has the LED-on-SCK flicker → the clean decisive test is on the EVT board. `decoy-frames` stays OFF (not viable at 20 MHz). Mirrors the 2026-05-19 OLED negative result (same symptom — readable decoys — different cause). Harness committed for EVT re-test. |

New `make play-hw-lcd` runs the FULL interactive wallet (real wizard/PIN/confirm, no `lcd-test` short-circuit) on the panel with physical buttons; `play-hw-display` kept as the OLED equivalent. `ui-oled` DEMOTED to dev-only, NOT deleted — `oled.rs` stays as the proven CT reference + carries `ui-capture`/`ui-mirror`/`decoy-frames`. Completes the user's "port OLED UI to LCD → retire OLED" arc (#1 dialogs+buttons validated, #2 F-24 CT gate passed, #3 production switch). Remaining: Phase D (re-validate decoy frames on the LCD's slow pixels) + the EVT-board 40 MHz re-up. |
| 2026-06-09 | F-24 glyph constant-time gate — TVLA harness wired + LCD seed path validated | New `make -C tools/sca glyph-leak` (probes in `kdf_target/src/main.rs` + `leakage_glyph.py`, mirroring the F-22 BIP-39 pattern). Two rainbow/lascar TVLA probes over the `mem_address` channel, 16-byte fixed-vs-random secret word: a **leaky sentinel** (direct `FONT[(ch-0x20)][col]` index) → `max\|t\|=650.90` LEAKAGE (proves the harness detects an address-channel leak), and the **CT path** (verbatim mirror of `ct_glyph_col`'s 96-entry scan + `black_box` barriers + `secret_glyph_cols` + the branchless RGB565 blit) → `max\|t\|=0.00` over 60,173 samples = flat. Exit 0 (CT clean AND sentinel leaks); independently re-run. This is the security gate before the LCD seed screen is trusted, validates the new `ui-lcd` secret path AND the original OLED `ct_glyph_col`, and closes the "F-24 harness (when wired)" TODO. Disasm confirmed the `black_box` barriers survive `opt-level=3`+LTO (no fold to a direct load); the blit expansion confirmed to have no secret-indexed access (`cols[sx]`, sx = public geometry index). Updated the `secret_text.rs` barrier comment to point at the live harness. |
| 2026-06-09 | NV3007 LCD §28 Phase C — Display backend landed + hardware-validated | New `secure/src/ui/lcd.rs`: `ui-lcd` is now a standalone 4th `Display` backend (`build.rs` + `nsc/mod.rs` fence + `ui/mod.rs` + `Cargo.toml` de-paired from `ui-noop`, which is now a build error). Renders the SAME 16×4 char grid as the OLED, so `confirm`/`pin_entry`/`seed_wizard`/`measured_boot` port with ZERO edits. `FONT_5X8` upscaled 3× → 15×24 glyphs, landscape (software rotation onto the proven native coords — flip-X resolved on bench), white-on-black, centered. Per-glyph 720 B cell buffer + `lcd_nv3007::set_window`/`write_pixels` (no full framebuffer — 121 KB won't fit). `flush_with_secret_rows` keeps the seed **constant-time**: routes secret rows through `secret_text::secret_glyph_cols` (the 96-entry scan + `black_box` barriers) + a shared branchless RGB565 expansion that writes every pixel (FG/BG via mask) — the original plan's "CT comes for free" was wrong, the rasterization IS the leaky surface, so the CT scanner is reused explicitly. `lcd_nv3007::init()` switched to the SWRESET path (RES tied to 3V3). Flicker eliminated by dropping the per-flush full-screen clear; SPI dropped ÷4→÷8 (40→20 MHz) after tracing residual flicker to the dev board's blue LED (LD2) loading PE13=SCK — re-up to 40 MHz tracked for the EVT board. Fixed the `spi_hw` cfg1 source-pin test I'd missed in the Phase B commit (now `..._baud_gated_...`, pins both gated MBR values). Host suite back to baseline (2000 pass + 1 documented pre-existing `usb_hw` fail). STILL OPEN before flipping production to `ui-lcd`: bench the real dialogs via full boot + the `tools/sca` F-24 CT gate on the seed path; then OLED demote (keep `oled.rs` as CT reference). | Panel had been black for days; root cause found via an RM0456 audit workflow (find→verify, advisor-vetted): `spi_end` cleared `SPE` on `TXP` (FIFO-space) instead of `EOT` (shifted-out), so per RM0456 §68.4.12 it **flushed/truncated every SPI command** — single-byte init commands never reached the panel. Secondary bug: `write_cmd`/`write_data` used one CS window per byte, but NV3007 resets the param index on CS-high, dropping multi-param values. **Fixes** (`secure/src/hw/lcd_nv3007.rs`): `spi_begin(tsize)` sets TSIZE>0 with SPE=0 then waits `EOT` before clearing SPE, chunked ≤65534 B (16-bit TSIZE) with a per-chunk ES0499 `delay(16)` (asymmetric-last-SCK erratum); new `write_cmd_data(cmd,&[params])` keeps a command + all its params in ONE CS-low window. With those, the full byte-for-byte `dgen1` init renders a clean, uniform image edge-to-edge. **Refresh** (`spi_hw.rs`): SPI raised ÷32→÷4 (40 MHz, `ui-lcd`-gated; non-LCD/shared-TROPIC01 builds keep ÷32) — DWT-measured **24.3 ms = 41 fps** full-frame (bus-saturated; 60 fps would need ~58 MHz which violates the NV3007 10 ns setup/hold, and ÷2/80 MHz starves the polled FIFO). New `fill_rect` **partial update = 0.65 ms** for 40×40 (~1500 fps-equiv, 38× faster) — the foundation for Phase C. Flicker eliminated via draw-over (never clear-then-redraw; update only changed slivers). **No hardware vsync**: the EastRising module's 8-pin header (GND·VCC·SCK·MOS·RES·DC·CS·BLK) doesn't break out the NV3007 TE pin, so moving content tears slightly but static wallet screens never do — irrelevant in practice. RES tied to 3V3 (both GPIO candidates loaded) + software SWRESET. Validated on real B-U585I via `make lcd-test-hw`. New: `docs/hardware/nv3007-wiring.md`, `docs/hardware/nv3007/` datasheets, `lcd-test` feature, `make lcd-test-hw`. Next: Phase C (`ui::Display` over LCD + OLED retirement). |
| 2026-06-02 | Desk-doable security fixes (TAMP base / SCP03 fail-open / half_E write-once) | Three of the four 2026-05-29-survey desk items, advisor-vetted + adversarial-review-verified (find→verify→completeness-critic workflow, 20 agents). **TAMP:** `tamp.rs` had **four** wrong backup-domain addresses (never silicon-validated, log-only) — TAMP base `0x5600_4400→7C00`, RTCAPBEN moved AHB3ENR→APB3ENR@0xA8 (PWREN kept in AHB3ENR), PWR_DBPR `0x10→0x28`, PWR_BDCR1 `0x18→0x20`; all re-derived against RM0456 + silicon-validated `bhk.rs`; source-pin test corrected (it had been *re-asserting* the wrong base) + new offset-pin test. **SCP03 fail-open:** factory-key fallback gated behind new `se050-scp03-allow-factory-fallback` (runtime build fails CLOSED); `se050-rotate-scp03` implies it; **added to the `nsc/mod.rs` hardware-release `compile_error!` fence** after the review found the original "CI gates it off" claim was false (no firmware CI gate) — validated the fence fires on a release+fallback build + exempts the provisioning build. **half_E write-once:** new `write_binary_write_once` (drops `ALLOW_WRITE`) on `ENTROPY/VK/BOOTSTRAP_VK` + duress; shared core leaves 6 test/canary callers unchanged; **VALIDATED ON SILICON** via the new `audit_write_once_enforced` stress test (1 PASS) — create-without-`ALLOW_WRITE` succeeds (no brick), A/B control isolates the gate (control update 0x9000 vs write-once 0x6986), share survives across reinit. **Fix 4 (OPTIGA AC-builder) deferred** at the time — note the teammate's 2026-05-29 row below now closes S-1/S-2/S-3 with its own AC-builder. Build matrix green across `dual-se,tamp` / `se050-derived-scp03` / `se050-rotate-scp03` / `duress-pin` / `se050-stress`; host suite 1993/1995 (the 1 remaining is the pre-existing `usb_hw.rs` read_volatile count). |
| 2026-05-29 | S-1/S-2/S-3 OPTIGA hardening pass (code-complete, pending silicon) | Closes the OPTIGA bench-attack triad in firmware. **Shared brick-safety gate:** new `apdu::metadata_matches_expected` (tag-wise Change/Read/Execute/DataType compare, tolerant of chip re-ordering + appended tags) + `OptigaTrustM::verify_and_lock` — reads back the stored metadata and refuses the irreversible `LcsO=Operational` lock (returns `Status(0xEB)`) unless the intended AC bytes are confirmed on-chip; every `lock_oid` call site (`provision_auth_ref`, `provision_user_oid`, `provision_counter`) now routes through it; no-op delegate to `lock_oid` when `optiga-lock-operational` off, so dev iteration is unchanged. **S-1:** `build_metadata_auth_ref_luc_oid` gains a `change_is_auto` param — the real F1D0 takes `Change=Auto(F1D0)` (`D0 03 23 F1 D0`) under `optiga-lock-operational` (byte-frozen test `positive_build_metadata_auth_ref_luc_change_auto_exact_bytes` = `20 10 D0 03 23 F1 D0 D1 01 FF D3 03 40 E1 20 E8 01 31`), dev + duress-F1D8 twin stay `ALW`; `provision_auth_ref` skip-guard strengthened to require `LUC(E120) && Auto(F1D0)` on an already-locked F1D0 (else fail-closed). `Conf(E140)` deliberately rejected (cracked-PBS bypass). **S-2:** new `build_metadata_ta_junk` (Change/Read/Execute=NEV, no DataType=TrustAnchor) + `lockdown_ta_pool` (gated `optiga-lock-operational` + `factory-production-irreversible-im-sure`) junk-overwrites + verify-locks `0xE0E3..0xE0E8` as the last `store_objects` step → no on-chip anchor for a SetObjectProtected manifest; SetObjectProtected senders + `CMD 0x83`/tags/version/fragment consts now `#[cfg(feature="optiga-reset-oids")]` so the encoder is absent from production binaries. **S-3:** two `compile_error!` fences in `nsc/mod.rs` — production OPTIGA (`mode-production` or `stm32u585 + !debug_assertions`, minus `e2e-test`/`dev-testkey`) without `optiga-hw-counter` → error; `optiga-reset-oids` in a production build → error (both verified to fire). **factory_reset redesign:** under the locked profile, drop the F1D0 blank-write (would fail — Auto(F1D0), no PIN auth) and skip `reset_e120_via_transient_auth` (needs `Change=ALW`); seed still erased via the F1D1/F1D2 `Conf(E140)` arm; F1E1 stays the boot-readable provisioned sentinel; crash-safe sentinel-first + wipe-flag preserved. **Reset model:** forgotten-PIN lockout destroys the seed; re-provisioning bumps the OPTIGA OID range (SE050-symmetric) — bump-slot wiring deferred pending the on-silicon F1Dx-as-TrustAnchor promotability probe. **Tests:** new pure tests pass (4 always-on + 1 hw-counter-gated byte-exact); full host suite green except the 1 pre-existing unrelated `usb_hw.rs` failure; locked + dev + duress profiles all `cargo check` clean for thumbv8m. **Still open (on ONE sacrificial chip — see plan):** TA-promotability probe (resolves slot plan), provision→verify_and_lock→unlock→lockout→seed-erased→re-provision, and the S-2 acceptance test (SetObjectProtected signed by the Infineon sample key MUST be rejected post-lockdown). Irreversible silicon burns are gated behind `optiga-lock-operational` + `factory-production-irreversible-im-sure` and run only in the factory ceremony. |
| 2026-05-28 | S-5/S-6/S-7 SE050 hardening pass | Comprehensive SE050 driver fix with full GP SCP03 Amd D v1.1.2 + NXP AN12413 + UM11225 + SE050 datasheet + NXP plug-and-trust C reference in hand. **S-5:** SCP03 `P1=0x33` (full C-MAC + C-DEC + R-MAC + R-ENC); new `scp03::unwrap_response` verifies R-MAC over `MCV(16) || ciphered_body || SW(2)` under S-RMAC, AES-CBC-decrypts with `response_icv = AES-ECB(S-ENC, counter_block[0]=0x80)` per §6.2.7, ISO 7816-4 depad, propagates plaintext+SW; counter advance moved to unwrap success path; CT compare via `subtle`; `aes128_cbc_decrypt` round-trip KAT added to `scp03_logic::tests`. **S-6:** `store_objects` + `store_duress_objects` pass `None` for user UserID's admin-delete entry (substitution attack closed); data objects keep admin entry (DoS-wipe preserved); `admin_factory_reset` split DATA_AND_CANARY_OBJS (must-wipe) vs AUTH_OBJS_BEST_EFFORT (survival OK); canary selftest mirrors production shape with PIN-self-delete cleanup. Trade-off: chip is single-use post-lockout (USERID_OBJ orphaned; OID range bump required to re-provision). **S-7a:** `write_userid` rejects `max_attempts ∈ {0, ≥256}` with `InvalidParam`; new `write_userid_unlimited` for the 7 legitimate unlimited call sites (admin / duress / E2E test admins) explicitly omits TAG_MAX_ATTEMPTS. **S-7b:** `delete_object` no longer maps `SW=0x6986 → Ok` (was masking real policy-denial); `0x6985 → Ok` stays for idempotent-delete. **S-7c:** extended-Lc commands emit 2-byte Le per ISO 7816-4 Case 4E. **S-7d (doc):** status-code mappings cross-referenced; AuthMethodBlocked correctly mapped to documented `COMMAND_NOT_ALLOWED = 0x6986`. **Audit sweep:** dispatched 3 PDF/C-reference research agents + 1 cross-reference audit; found 1 real CT-strengthening item (use `subtle::ConstantTimeEq` instead of hand-rolled) — applied. Counter-desync alarm from audit was a false positive (NXP's `nxScp03_Com.c:236-244` increments counter only on `status = SCP_OK`, same as my code — both host and card stay at value N on bare-error, so they're in sync). **Tests:** 1991/1992 pass (1 pre-existing failure in unrelated `usb_hw.rs`); 119 SE050 pure tests all pass; 36 `scp03_logic` host-side tests including 3 new CBC decrypt KATs all pass. **Still open:** logic-analyzer silicon verification (S-5), sacrificial-part admin-delete-of-USERID confirms SW=0x6986 (S-6), iterative_wipe SW probe (S-7d). Docs updated: `docs/security/security-review-2026-05.md` §§C-7/C-8/C-9 marked FIXED; `docs/security/threat-model.md` Claim 3 provisional flag scoped to S-1 only; Claim 5 revised to reflect post-S-6 *data-destroyed-UserID-orphaned* state; `CLAUDE.md` ship-blocker summary updated. |

USB layer cut from 14 INS codes + v1 Keycard Shell compat to 7 native v2 instructions; webhid tool rewritten for the unified bundle (phase 4). `PQSmartWallet` + `PQSmartWalletFactory` contracts replace the multi-signer wallet; 14 new Foundry tests pass; EntryPoint v0.9 via `account-abstraction` submodule update to `releases/v0.9`; CREATE2 salt = `keccak256(masterPkSeed \|\| masterPkRoot)` (phase 5). ERC-20 metadata bundle + Groth16 ZK clear-sign preserved as optional trailer sections on the unified sign payload, verifying against the firmware-embedded Merkle-rooted DBs just like before. Fixed a latent sphincs-c7 bug where `extract_ht_index` used C7 parameters (24-bit mask at bit 128) instead of C11 (16-bit mask at bit 143); self-verification now passes keygen → sign → verify roundtrip. |

Switched `authenticate_and_read` to proper HMAC challenge-response protocol (chip-side verify via DecryptSym CMD 0x95 + tag 0x43). Added admin factory-reset via shielded-connection Conf(E140) path — avoids SE050-style permanent lockout by making every user OID's Change AC `Auto(F1D0) OR Conf(E140)`. Physical fixes validated on real hardware: CTL→3V3 jumper required, 50µs guard time between register-write/read transactions required, ReSynch is fire-and-forget. OpenApplication now returns valid response. Saved bring-up quirks to memory (`project_optiga_bringup.md`). |
| 2026-04-17 | C10 bootstrap cutover | Replaced SPHINCS+C11 bootstrap identity with **SPHINCS+C10** (`h=18 d=2 a=11 k=13 w=8 l=43 target_sum=205 sig=4008`, 4073-byte Type 1 wire frame). New `sphincs-c10/` crate with correct portable `extract_ht_index` (4-byte load for H=18 vs 3-byte for H=16) replaces the old `sphincs-c7/` directory; firmware/crypto/sign-state-machine/shared wire formats all ported. New on-chain `SPHINCsC10Asm.sol` SHA-256-precompile verifier (subtree_h=9, 9-level Merkle auth, target_sum=205) replaces `SPHINCsC11Asm.sol`. Added per-chain `bootstrapUses` counter in `PQOwnable` storage (ERC-7201 slot+1), `MAX_BOOTSTRAP_USES = 65_536` cap enforced in `PQSmartWallet._validateSignature` pre-check and bumped via `_bumpBootstrapUses(cap)` after successful Type 1. Added `BootstrapKeyUsed(newCount)` event and `bootstrapUses()` view. Factory `createAccount` salt still `sha256(masterPkSeed || masterPkRoot)`, so the C11→C10 change re-bases every wallet address for a given seed — acceptable since no live deployments exist. 5 new `SPHINCsC10AsmTest` Foundry tests driven by a Rust-generated `c10_test_vectors.json` (keygen + sign + self-verify runs under `cargo test -p sphincs-c10 --test gen_test_vectors --release`), 4 new bootstrap-counter tests in `PQSmartWalletTest` covering success bump, failure no-bump, cap-rejects-overflow, and post-exhaustion Type 2 still works. All 33 Foundry tests pass. SHA-256 stays throughout; no keccak256 regressions. |

Generates 16 manifests via the Infineon `protected_update_data_set` tool (now buildable on Linux via cloned mbedtls 2.28.8 + CRLF/include-separator/buffer-overflow patches) signed by Infineon's sample EC P-256 key, embedded via `include_bytes!`. (2) `43b6937` fix the MasterFinished handshake rejection — PRF seed was `random_M‖random_S` but reference uses only `random_S`, MasterFinished plaintext must be `random_S‖slave_seq`, and header/nonce/AAD seq must be `slave_sequence_number` from SlaveHello (not zero). (3) `a1ac0cd` provisioning-through-shield fixes: `send_command` now uses `transceive_prl` (PRESENCE_BIT) so records reach the PRL layer; `hard_reset_and_reinit` clears `shield.active`; `store_objects` threads `ensure_shield` after PBS write and after every hard-reset. (5) `3d34101` PIN unlock via AUTHREF HMAC — `PARAM_HMAC_MODE` was 0x02 but must be `OPTIGA_HMAC_SHA_256 = 0x20` per `optiga_lib_common.h:213`; added `generate_auth_code` APDU (`GetRandom` with `store_in_session=TRUE`) required for `hmac_verify` to find a valid session. Full flow reaches `hmac_verify OK → Unlocked: entropy + VKs cached → gateway pre-unlocked, ready for tests` on real B-U585I-IOT02A + OPTIGA Trust M V3. New `flash-hw-optiga-unlock-test` + `flash-hw-optiga-shield-handshake-only` Makefile targets. |
| 2026-04-17 | All-C10 slot cutover + stateless firmware | Per-slot signing key is now SPHINCS+C10 instead of the legacy FORS+C — one primitive, one verifier, no variable-length Type 2. The firmware is **stateless** for slot selection: the companion supplies `(chain_id, slot_index, flags)` on every `CMD_SIGN_USEROP`; `FLAG_REGISTER_SLOT` (bit 30 of flags) tells the firmware when to emit a Type 1 ahead of Type 2. Deleted `legacy-fosc/` crate (root + workspace refs), `secure/src/nsc/legacy_flash.rs` (flash pages 123-124 no longer used for slot state), `secure/src/nsc/cmd_get_slot_info.rs` (CMD 17 retired), `contracts/.../LegacyForsCVerifier.sol` and `ILegacyVerifier.sol`. `PQSmartWallet` gains a second on-chain counter `slotUses[slotKey]` capped at `MAX_SLOT_USES = 65_536`, bumped by `_bumpSlotUses` inside the Type 2 path; `PQOwnable` extends its ERC-7201 struct with the mapping and a `SlotKeyUsed(slotKey, newCount)` event. Single `c10Verifier` now handles both Type 1 and Type 2 (same stateless SHA-256-precompile verifier, different `(pk_seed, pk_root)` per call). Slot C10 keys are derived deterministically from `(slot_master_entropy, slot_index)` via new domain tags `"slot_c10_sk_seed"` / `"slot_c10_pk_seed"` and cached in SRAM across the unlock session only. New `secure/src/crypto.rs::derive_c10_slot_keypair_with_progress` helper mirrors the master path; `SecureState::SLOT_CACHE: Option<CachedSlot>` replaces the FORS+C slot cache. Wire formats: Type 2 is now fixed at 4073 bytes (C10 sig); `SIG_TYPE2_LEN = 4073`; `MAX_SIGN_RESPONSE_LEN = 8246`; removed `FORSC_BODY_LEGACY`, `SIG_MIN/MAX_LEGACY`, `Q_MAX_LEGACY`, `LEGACY_WRAPPER_*`, `NscStatus::SlotExhausted`, `CMD_GET_SLOT_CACHE_INFO`, `CMD_SIGN_SLOT_LEGACY`, `CMD_REGISTER_SLOT_CACHE`, `INS_V2_*LEGACY*`. Master C10 derivation unchanged → every seed still maps to the same CREATE2 wallet address. CLAUDE.md invariants updated (old #6 `next_q`-before-flash removed; #7 now covers both use caps; new #8 for the stateless firmware property). Builds clean for `thumbv8m.main-none-eabi` across all feature combos (`mock-se`, `e2e-test`, `bench-key-speed + stm32u585`, `usb`). 4-scenario QEMU e2e runner exercises register/repeat/rotate/second-chain; bench exercises cold first-sign / N cached Type 2 / second-chain cached-slot. |

Fixes: `secure_log!`-gate the unconditional `hprintln!` in `hw::hash::init_clock`'s SHA-256 self-test (DHCSR `C_DEBUGEN` runtime check) so standalone firmware stops HardFaulting pre-OLED. STM32U5 TRNG init rewritten with the NIST-compliant CR value `0x00F00D00`, CONDRST at bit 30 (not bit 6), and SEIS/CEIS clear path; init moved to after `sau::init()` so GTZC has assigned RNG's security attribute. First-boot wizard now logs every branch of its retry loop. `flash-hw-se050-oled-standalone` now runs `probe-rs reset` after option-byte programming so the target actually starts. GTZC1_TZSC_SECCFGR{1,2,3} cleared to 0 (everything NS) for USB bring-up — USB OTG FS is an AHB2 peripheral governed by a separate GTZC2_TZSC controller whose base address we have not yet confirmed (our guess at `0x52034400` bus-faulted).
