| 2026-05-12 | `tools/sca/` — first lascar leakage target: TVLA + CPA on the AES / AES-GCM entropy-blob wrap (+ positive control) | New `leakage_kdf.py` + `kdf_target/` (standalone thumbv8m crate, own `[workspace]`; uses the same crates.io deps as `pqsigner-domain`s entropy-blob path — `aes-gcm` 0.10 / `aes` 0.8 / `sha2` 0.10 — so the AESs leakage behaviour matches). Three subjects, analysed via `lascar` TVLA (Welch fixed-vs-random t-test) + CPA on the `mem_address` channel: **(1)** `sca_leaky_sbox` — `out[i]=AES_SBOX[in[i]^KEY[i]]`, a deliberate table leak — the positive control: TVLA `max|t| ≈ 24` at the `SBOX[]` load address → leakage detected; CPA over `HW(&SBOX + (in[i]^guess))` recovers **16/16** key bytes (the FIPS-197 vector key `2b7e1516…`) → **lascar pipeline verified end-to-end** (TVLA + CPA both work). **(2)** `sca_aes256_encrypt_block` — `AES-256-ENC(fixed_key, plaintext)` via the `aes` crate (the AES `pqsigner-domain`s entropy wrap uses): TVLA `max|t| = 0.00` over 600 traces → **flat**: the bitsliced "soft" backend on thumbv8m is constant-time w.r.t. its plaintext (no data-dependent memory addresses → no T-table / cache mem-address side channel). **(3)** `sca_aesgcm_wrap` — structural mirror of `pqsigner_domain::encrypt_entropy_blob`s AES-256-GCM wrap of the 32-B entropy under a fixed key+nonce: TVLA `max|t| = 0.00` over 600 traces → **flat** (constant-time AES + constant-time GHASH). **No findings** — the production AES / AES-GCM-wrap path is clean on the `mem_address` channel in emulation; `make kdf` exits 0. Caveats documented in README §"Leakage analysis": emulation only (analog power/EM + register-HW DPA of the AES round keys still need a scope); the deployed entropy wrap is a *single* encryption with a *fixed* nonce → no attacker-chosen-input DPA surface, the residual is single-trace SPA/template of the wrap key/keystream during the boot-time wrap; `sca_aesgcm_wrap` is a *mirror* (not `#[path]` — `pqsigner-domain` uses `{ workspace = true }` deps, not path-dep-able from a detached workspace), KEEP-IN-SYNC if `encrypt_entropy_blob` changes; the `register` channel is unusable here (rainbows per-instruction `reg_read` of every capstone-named dest reg hits an invalid id inside the bitsliced AES on unicorn 2.1.x; `mem_address` works universally and is the meaningful "is it constant-time" channel). Makefile: `make kdf` / `make build-kdf` / `make clean` (both crates); README §Layout + §Roadmap + new §"Leakage analysis" updated. Files: `tools/sca/{kdf_target/{Cargo.toml,build.rs,memory.x,src/main.rs},leakage_kdf.py,Makefile,README.md}`, `docs/work-todo.md`.
| 2026-05-12 | `tools/sca/` — fault sweep over the *real* `sphincs_c10::verify` (forge-a-signature direction) | New `fault_sweep_c10v.py` + `c10v_target/` — a standalone thumbv8m crate (own `[workspace]`) that **path-deps the real `sphincs-c10`** (software SHA-256 path) and wraps `sphincs_c10::verify` under a `#[no_mangle]` symbol. (The path-dep-from-a-detached-workspace works: `sphincs-c10`s only `{ workspace = true }` dep, `zeroize`, resolves from `sphincs-c10`s *own* workspace = the repo root, not from `c10v_target`s.) The harness loads the `wrong-message` vector from `contracts/smart-wallet/test/c10_test_vectors.json` (the same JSON the Solidity verifiers Foundry tests use) — a *structurally-valid* C10 signature for a *different* message, so `verify` runs the full FORS + WOTS + hypertree recomputation and then fails the final `computed_root == pk_root` check (the failure is at the *end*, so the sweep exercises the whole pipeline incl. the classic SPHINCS+ FI spot, the final root comparison). The Rust `verify` takes the 16-byte `pk_seed`/`pk_root` (the JSON stores them right-zero-padded to 32 — N-mask convention — so the harness slices `[:16]`); `message` is the 32-byte msg-hash directly, `signature` 4008 bytes. The whole `verify` is only **~7521 instructions** in emulation, so the sweep covers **all of it** (not just the tail) under all 3 fault models (skip / stuck-at-0 / stuck-at-FF) — ~22.5k emulations, a few minutes. **RESULT: CLEAN** — sweeping every one of the ~7521 instructions × 3 models, **no single fault made a forged signature verify as good** (per model: ~209/250/243 crash on an invalid instruction, 16 hangs for skip, the rest ~7270-7295 correctly rejected). `make c10v` exits 0; it exits 1 (with the offending instruction indices + PCs + a verbose repro) the moment any single fault flips a reject into an accept. Caveats (in README §"Full C10 verify fault sweep"): emulated single-fault only (multi-fault / on-device clock-EM glitches out of scope); this is the `verify` direction — a fault inside C10 *signing* (leaking `sk_seed`, emitting a malformed sig) is a separate not-yet-wired target (sign slower to emulate but tractable); only the `wrong-message` invalid shape is swept — the other invalid vectors (`wrong-root`, `mutated-R`, `mutated-FORS-auth`, `mutated-WOTS-sigma`, `mutated-WOTS-count-target-sum-fail`) exercise different early-exit paths and are one-line additions. Makefile: `make c10v` / `make build-c10v` (+ `make clean` cleans it); README §Layout + §Roadmap + new §"Full C10 verify fault sweep" updated. Files: `tools/sca/{c10v_target/{Cargo.toml,build.rs,memory.x,src/main.rs},fault_sweep_c10v.py,Makefile,README.md}`, `docs/work-todo.md`. Net of this 3-target arc (PIN / lascar-KDF / C10v): one prior production fix landed (F-1, the `crypto.rs` `check_true(|| v)` CSE collapse → `|| black_box(v)`); residual findings F-2/F-3/F-4 documented as maintainer-decision items; everything else clean.
| 2026-05-12 | `tools/sca/` — F-3 confirmed addressed (`gated_unlock` `13c194e` guard, harness re-synced) + C10-verify sweep extended to all 6 invalid vectors | **(a) F-3 follow-up**: `nsc::gated_unlock` had already been FI-hardened by `13c194e` (post-`se.unlock` guard: read the `Ok/Err` discriminant twice / `wait_random` / `fi::check_true` sentinel / `match result { Ok(master) if both_ok => …, Ok(_) => Err(InternalError), Err(e) => Err(e) }`) — but the earlier `tools/sca` `gated_unlock` mirror still modelled the *old* plain-`match` version. Re-synced the mirror (`sca_pin_gated_unlock` in `fi_target/src/main.rs`) to track the current `gated_unlock`; the `[skip]` sweep now confirms **no single instruction-skip makes a wrong PIN unlock** (even though LLVM CSEs the two `is_ok()` reads into one, `both_ok` is computed from them — `false` for a genuine `Err` — *before* the `match`, so a single skip of the `match` discriminant lands in `Ok(_) => Err(InternalError)`). A `stuck-at-FF` on the status-return path still forces an "unlocked"-looking return — the inherent result-register-corruption class (same as the `fi::check_true` stuck-at INFO), not a `[skip]` bypass, not a regression; and even a flip would only have the wallet read `Err`-variant garbage as the "master", not the seed (SE does the PIN compare in silicon). Belt-and-braces noted: `core::hint::black_box(&result).is_ok()` would defeat the `is_ok_1`/`is_ok_2` CSE if a future compiler ever hoists the `match` load to coincide — the harness would catch that regression. So **F-3 = ADDRESSED**; F-2 / F-4 remain documented residuals (no clean *localised* fix — F-2 = "skip the `bl check_true`" needs a sentinel-encoded callee return + caller compare, i.e. a new `fi.rs` helper + caller change, a perf/hardening judgment call; F-4 = a refused-but-uncharged attempt under a single fault, ≤10 free guesses = a 1-in-10^6 lottery, fix = sentinel-encoded bump result + caller compare and/or charge-on-refuse, or accept — both are maintainer decisions, not applied). `fault_sweep_pin.py`s status footer rewritten to reflect this; README §Findings F-3 entry updated to "ADDRESSED (upstream `13c194e`)". **(b)**: extended `fault_sweep_c10v.py` to sweep **all 6 invalid vectors** in `contracts/smart-wallet/test/c10_test_vectors.json` (`wrong-message`, `wrong-root`, `mutated-R`, `mutated-FORS-auth`, `mutated-WOTS-sigma`, `mutated-WOTS-count-target-sum-fail`) — each fails verification at a *different* point (H_msg / FORS index extraction / FORS auth path / WOTS chain / WOTS digit-sum check / final root compare), so every reject path of `sphincs_c10::verify` is exercised (not just "fails at the very end"); all 3 fault models per vector, per-vector instruction count (early-bailing vectors are cheap), `make c10v` exits 1 the moment any single fault flips a reject into an accept. README §"Full C10 verify fault sweep" updated. *Not done* (deferred, with reasoning, in the Roadmap): a fault sweep over C10 *signing* (the meaningful FI threat there is *differential* — two glitched signs reusing the same FORS/WOTS one-time keys → universal forgery — not a single-trace "did the output flip" check, so a simple sweep would be weak; sign is also slower to emulate), and a two-fault sweep over `fi::check_true` (rainbow is single-fault — would need a custom `UC_HOOK_CODE` counting hook). Files: `tools/sca/{fi_target/src/main.rs,fault_sweep_pin.py,fault_sweep_c10v.py,README.md}`, `docs/work-todo.md`.
| 2026-05-12 | `tools/sca/` — two-fault [skip,skip] pair sweep over `fi::check_true` (`make fi-twofault`) + Finding F-5 | New `fault_sweep_fi.py --two-fault` (/`make fi-twofault`): a `UC_HOOK_CODE`-driven pair sweep — for every *ordered pair* `(i, j)` of the ~205 instructions a `sca_fi_check_true(0)` call executes, inject an instruction-skip at `i` AND at `j` (the hook counts instructions and calls `rainbow.fault_models.fault_skip` at those two indices) and check whether `check_true(false)` then returns a non-zero value. `fi::check_true`s doc-comment claims a glitch must skip **all four** decision points to flip false→true; this quantifies how many it really takes. **Finding F-5**: it takes **~2 coordinated skips**, not 4. BUT almost every 2-skip route is *outside `check_true`s claimed scope*: corrupting *both* `cond()` evaluations (the boolean source — `check_true` explicitly does not promise to protect that), and in the harness the trivial synthetic closure `sca_fi_cond(x)=black_box(x)!=0` makes "skip the `mov r0, want` before `bl sca_fi_cond` → r0 holds a nonzero stack pointer → reads as true" an easy route — which does **NOT** transfer to a real `bl sphincs_c10::verify(pk_seed, pk_root, msg_hash, &sig)` closure (4 pointer args; skipping an arg-load makes `verify` *fail*, not return truthy). The pairs that matter are the handful with **both** faults in `check_true`s verdict/return code (e.g. skipping the result `mov r0, r4` / the fail-path result-zeroing — leaving FAIL_SENTINEL/garbage in the return register): for those, sentinel-encode `check_true`s return; or just soften the doc-comments "4" to "~2". Maintainers call — not applied (touches `fi.rs`). Exploratory test: `make fi-twofault` exits 0 (the single-fault `[skip]` sweep — the *contract* test — still passes, 0 `fi.rs` bypasses; F-5 is the harder 2-coordinated-skip case). The harness buckets pairs: scaffolding-only / "touches `fi::check_true` but also the synthetic closure call → harness artifact, doesnt transfer" / "both faults in `check_true`s verdict/return code → candidate-real". Also updated: all 3 single-fault models were already swept (`stuck-at-0`/`stuck-at-FF` alongside `skip`); `make fi-twofault` target added; README §Roadmap `fault_sweep_fi.py extensions` bullet updated (stuck-at + two-fault marked done, F-5 noted; 3-fault sweep + an FI-guard *leakage* pass still open). | 2026-05-12 | `tools/sca/fault_sweep_c10v.py` — multi-vector sweep now completes (reused-emu optimisation) + clean result + Finding F-6 | The 6-invalid-vector C10-verify sweep added in `6659e43` timed out (`timeout 1500` killed it after ~24 min — each iteration re-parsed the `sphincs-c10`-linked ELF, and `mutated-FORS-auth` / `mutated-WOTS-sigma` / `mutated-WOTS-count-target-sum-fail` make `verify` run **~1.2–1.8 MILLION instructions** — see F-6 — so emulating the full thing per sweep position is infeasible). **Fixes**: (1) reuse one persistent emulator (`reset()` + re-write the 4 input buffers + zero `verify`s ~32 KB stack region between iterations — the latter makes the reuse safe even on the fault path: a fault that skips a stack write then reads a *zero*, not a previous iterations value); the canonical rainbow pattern, ~0.2 ms/iter vs the per-iter ELF-reload bottleneck. (2) split the instruction budget: `COUNT_BUDGET = 3_000_000` for the one-time `instr_count`/baseline runs, `SWEEP_BUDGET = 25_000` per faulted sweep emulation (a normal `verify` returns in ~7.5 k); `SWEEP_CAP = 8_000` max sweep positions per vector — so the three multi-million-instr vectors get their first 8 k instructions swept (the early checks, where a forge-relevant fault would live) and each emulation caps at 25 k → a fault during the multi-million-instr chain shows up as "hung" (verify never returns → non-forge). **Result: CLEAN** — across all 6 invalid vectors (`wrong-message` / `wrong-root` / `mutated-R` at full ~7521 instr; `mutated-FORS-auth` / `mutated-WOTS-sigma` / `mutated-WOTS-count-target-sum-fail` at first 8 k) × all 3 fault models (skip / stuck-at-0 / stuck-at-FF), **no single fault made a forged C10 signature verify as good** — ~188–241 crash on an invalid instruction, ~15–43 hang, the rest correctly rejected. `make c10v` exits 0, runs in ~1–2 min; exits 1 the instant any single fault flips a reject into an accept. **Finding F-6 (low-severity observation)**: `sphincs_c10::verify` (the Rust reference impl) does ~1.2–1.8 M instructions on a malformed FORS-auth-path / WOTS-sigma / WOTS-digit value — i.e. its chain/loop lengths arent bounded defensively against an out-of-range value. Still a *reject* (correctness holds — a malformed sig never verifies). On-chain `SPHINCsC10Asm.sol` would do ~1–2 M SHA-256 precompile `staticcall`s → blow past `verificationGasLimit` → OOG revert → the bundlers `eth_estimate…`/simulation catches it → the userOp is dropped before it lands → no DoS in the ERC-4337 flow. But worth a fail-fast `digit < W` / bounded-loop assertion in `sphincs-c10::verify` (and a look at whether the Yul verifier has the same shape) so an auditor isnt surprised. **Not fixed** — `sphincs-c10` / the Yul verifier, out of scope of this tooling pass; flagged for the maintainer. README §"Full C10 verify fault sweep" + new §"F-6" updated. Files: `tools/sca/{fault_sweep_c10v.py,README.md}`, `docs/work-todo.md`.
| 2026-05-12 | `fi::check_true_into_sentinel` — sentinel-encoded FI-guard return; migrated all ~13 `check_true` callsites (mitigates F-2; partly F-5; F-4 re-framed) | Acting on the recommendations. The `if !guard() { err }` / bool-return idiom (Findings F-2/F-5) lets a single fault defeat a verify-before-release gate at the *call site* — skip the `bl check_true`, the post-check branch, or stuck-at the bool-return register. `secure/src/fi.rs`: new `pub fn check_true_into_sentinel<F: FnMut()->bool>(cond) -> u32` — same guarded body as `check_true` (double-eval `cond` / `wait_random` / hamming-distant sentinel commit / re-check) but returns `OK_SENTINEL`(0xA5A5A5A5)/`FAIL_SENTINEL`(0x5A5A5A5A) instead of a `bool`, so a garbage/`FAIL_SENTINEL` return register is overwhelmingly `!= OK_SENTINEL` and the caller's `if … != OK_SENTINEL { err }` still takes the error path. Both fns kept as standalone bodies (~10 lines duplicated): making `check_true` a *wrapper* over `check_true_into_sentinel` adds a `== OK_SENTINEL -> bool` reduction that is itself a one-skip-to-a-truthy-`FAIL_SENTINEL` step — `make fi` caught and rejected that on the first build. `check_true`'s doc-comment rewritten (points at the sentinel form; honest re F-5). Migrated all ~13 `check_true` callsites in the `secure` crate to `if crate::fi::check_true_into_sentinel(C) != crate::fi::OK_SENTINEL { err }`: `crypto.rs::c10_sign_verified_with_progress`; `nsc/mod.rs::gated_unlock` (`match result { Ok(master) if verdict == OK_SENTINEL => …, Ok(_) => Err(InternalError), Err(e) => Err(e) }`); `nsc/cmd_sign_userop.rs` x3; `nsc/cmd_sign_userop_batch.rs` x3; `nsc/cmd_sign_offchain.rs`; `dual_se.rs`; `hw/otp.rs`; `hw/flash.rs` x2. 12 of the 13 are the identical regex-applied edit; `gated_unlock` done by hand. Effect: F-2 -> 'skip the bl' and 'stuck-at the bool return' are no longer call-site bypasses at any gated site; residual = the irreducible 'skip the caller's `if … != OK_SENTINEL { err }` branch' + the boolean-source routes (corrupting `cond`, out of scope). F-5 -> the result/return-path 2-skip route and the stuck-at route gone; residual = the 2-coordinated-skip route inside `check_true_into_sentinel` itself — accept. F-4 -> re-framed to 'minor': the page-124 free-guess only hits the MCU's *redundant* counter; `gated_unlock` bumps page-124 *before* `se.unlock(pin)`, so a glitched bump still leaves `se.unlock` running and the SE counts the wrong PIN in silicon (invariant #2); boot reconciles to the strictest counter -> the SE rate-limit (10->wipe) holds -> recommend accept. Harness mirrors (`tools/sca/fi_target/src/main.rs`): `sca_c10_verify_release` / `sca_pin_attempts_bump` / `sca_pin_gated_unlock` updated. Verified: thumbv8m build clean (mock-se+…+stm32u585 and dual-se+…+stm32u585); `cargo test -p sphincs-tz-secure --release` 105/105 (incl. `glitched_unlock_returns_internal_error`, the `gated_unlock`-path cases, `fi::tests`); `make fi` / `make c10` / `make pin` all exit 0 (the `make c10` F-2 residual shrank — 'skip the bl' gone; remaining 4-6 hits = the irreducible post-check-branch / F-5-internal stuff). `make e2e` (QEMU unified-sign) timed out at the 10-min budget — QEMU's software-SHA C10-sign x2 being slow, not a regression (and likely pre-existing); `make run` smoke pending. README §Findings updated (F-2/F-4/F-5). NOT done: F-6 (`sphincs-c10::verify` ~1.2-1.8M-instr unbounded chain on a malformed digit — touches `sphincs-c10` + its Solidity/Lean twins; harmless on-chain (OOG revert -> bundler simulation catches it); flagged for the maintainer); the deferred 3-fault / 2-sign-differential harnesses. Files: `secure/src/{fi.rs,crypto.rs,nsc/mod.rs,nsc/cmd_sign_userop.rs,nsc/cmd_sign_userop_batch.rs,nsc/cmd_sign_offchain.rs,dual_se.rs,hw/otp.rs,hw/flash.rs}`, `tools/sca/{fi_target/src/main.rs,README.md}`, `docs/work-todo.md`. |
| 2026-05-15 | F-21 correction — SE050 PIN-counter reconcile is feasible after all; three-way reconcile shipped | User challenged the F-21 commit's framing ("SE050 silicon counter can't be peeked without burning an attempt"). On re-investigation that claim was wrong: NXP `Se05x_API_ReadObjectAttributes` (CLA=0x80 INS_READ P2_ATTRIBUTES) returns the USERID auth object's `auth_attempts` / `max_attempts` TLV fields over the transport SCP03 channel, no user-session auth required, no attempt consumed. SDK parsing reference: `sss_pkcs11_pal_helpers.c:603-613 pkcs11_parse_atrribute` (wire format: `obj_id(4) ‖ obj_type(1) ‖ auth_attr(1) ‖ auth_attempts(2) ‖ auth_obj_id(4) ‖ max_attempts(2) ‖ policy…`). New `Se050::pin_attempt_count_raw()` parses bytes [6..8] for `auth_attempts`, returns the **failed-attempts USED** count (aligns to OPTIGA's F1E1 semantics so reconcile is a straight `!=` against MCU page-124 — also caught and fixed an interim "remaining" semantics regression I almost shipped). Wired into the `SecureElement::pin_attempt_count` trait impl behind a `MAX_ATTEMPTS` cap. **`DualSecureElement::pin_attempt_count`** now returns `max(OPTIGA_used, SE050_used)` (strictest = highest used count). New **`SecureElement::pin_attempt_counts_divergent()`** trait method (default false; Dual overrides to flag OPTIGA↔SE050 mismatch). **`nsc::reconcile_pin_attempts`** upgraded to three-way: wipe iff `MCU != strictest_SE` OR intra-SE disagreement. Defense bound improves from "attacker resets ≤1 side" to "attacker resets ≤2 sides" — coordinated three-side reset still bypasses, but that needs OPTIGA E140 PBS rewrite + SE050 USERID delete + MCU page-124 erase in one campaign. Touched: `secure/src/{se050/mod.rs,dual_se.rs,secure_element.rs,nsc/mod.rs,main.rs}` + `docs/work-todo.md §4`. cargo check passes on `stm32u585+dual-se+bhk` and `mock-se` QEMU shapes; on-silicon `pin-gate-hw-counter-e2e` re-validation pending (silicon path covered by the unchanged validation hooks). Lesson recorded — work-todo entries that say "X can't be done" deserve a primary-source check before they become invariant docstrings; the §4 claim and the F-21 commit's docstring had each reinforced the other for weeks. |
| 2026-05-18 | F-9 scared convergence — quantitative noise-floor confirmation | New `f9_collect_for_scared.py` (lascar venv, dumps 600 × 10M × stride=1 to a 6-GB `.npz`) + `f9_scared_convergence.py` (scared-sca venv, replays `TTestAnalysis` on 30 incrementing trace-count prefixes) builds on `f9_retest.py` to test the 'max|t|=4.93 is noise floor' claim against a √N-scaling alternative hypothesis. **scared reproduces lascar's final-N max|t| byte-exactly (both 4.931 at N=600)** — independent venv, independent estraces+ttest implementation, same trace tensor, same answer. **Convergence shape**: max|t| 6.80 → 4.93 from N=20 to N=600 (slight decrease, no √N growth); 30 prefix points all stay in [4.9, 6.0]; multi-testing-corrected threshold over 10M samples is ≈ 5.6σ which the curve never crosses. **Reads cleanly as noise**: under H1 (real msg-dependent signal) we'd expect max|t| ≈ 37 at N=600 given N=20's reading; observed flat-or-decreasing rules out a √N-scaling channel. **Stronger claim than lascar's single point**: not just 'max|t|=4.93 at full N' but 'no detectable √N-scaling msg-dependence anywhere in [N=20, N=600]' — eliminates the 'we just didn't take enough traces' objection. Trade-offs caught: scared's `TTestAnalysis` only takes `precision=` (no `convergence_step=`, which is Attack-only), so the convergence loop is rolled by replaying `TTestAnalysis()` on `ths[:k]` per step; pre-casting uint8→float32 OOM-kills at 24 GB → keep uint8 + `scared.set_batch_size(64.0)`; matplotlib added to scared-sca's venv. Makefile targets `f9-scared-collect` + `f9-scared`; out/ gitignored (6 GB `.npz`). README §F-9 re-test extended with the convergence table + interpretation. |
| 2026-05-18 | SAES-DHUK CMAC wrapper leakage — 4-mode TVLA, all clean | New `saes_kdf_target/` (thumbv8m crate that `#[path]`-includes `secure/src/cmac.rs` byte-for-byte under a software-AES-256 closure standing in for the unemulable SAES coprocessor) + `leakage_saes_kdf.py` (4-mode TVLA via lascar). Tests both `cmac_generic` and `kdf_cmac_counter_generic` under: vary-key fix-msg, fix-key vary-msg, vary-key fix-label, fix-key vary-label. **All four max|t| = 0.00 over 600 traces × 7-9K samples**: rainbow's `mem_address` channel records the Hamming weight of memory access ADDRESSES (not loaded values), and neither the bitsliced soft AES nor the CMAC wrapper bookkeeping (`double_l`, CBC chain XOR, final-block branch dispatch, KDF label+counter packing) makes any input-dependent memory address. Narrow but useful claim: rules out one specific production leak class (data-dependent memory addresses in the CPU wrapper, on top of whatever the SAES silicon itself leaks). Power/EM leakage on register VALUES and the SAES coprocessor's own side-channel surface still require on-silicon SCA with a scope. Makefile targets `build-saes-kdf` + `saes-kdf`; README §SAES-DHUK KDF wrapper leakage carries the full table + interpretation + production-scope caveats. |
| 2026-05-18 | wait_random loop-count leakage — flat (max|t| = 2.68 over 736 samples) | New `leakage_wait_random.py` + `sca_fi_wait_random_n(byte)` symbol in fi_target. TVLA fixed-vs-random byte fed to `pqsigner_fi::wait_random_loop` — fixed group at byte=200, random group in [180, 220] (tight range so all traces have similar lengths and don't get truncated to the shortest). **Result: max|t| = 2.68 over 736 samples, well below the 4.5 TVLA threshold**. The loop body's per-iteration mem_address pattern is identical across iterations regardless of the RNG byte (vread/vwrite to fixed stack-resident `i_storage` / `j_storage` addresses; only the VALUES change, not the addresses). Closes work-todo §18b 'Leakage pass over the FI guards themselves' as **clean**: the FI guard is not itself a side channel on this measurement. Caveat: rainbow's mem_address channel is address-only, not value-leakage; this rules out address-side leakage but power/EM scoping on real silicon would be needed to characterise register-value leakage of the counter itself. Makefile target `wait-random-leak`. |
| 2026-05-18 | F-5 two-fault sweep re-run on merged tree — known residual confirmed, harness-only | `make fi-twofault` against the post-merge tree (MMIO refactor + 85 colleague commits + my F-21/scared/scared-saes/wait_random/c10-sign work). Results: (a) **single-fault [skip] sweep**: 1 fi.rs hit at instr 9 (pc=0x21294), which disassembles to a `b.w` tail-call from the local `fi::check_true` shim into `pqsigner_fi::check_true` — skipping the branch falls through into the adjacent `check_true_into_sentinel` shim at 0x21298 which returns `OK_SENTINEL = 0xA5A5_A5A5` (non-zero r0 → truthy bool). Layout-induced fall-through, not a new logic bug. (b) **single-fault [stuck-at-FF]**: 3 hits in `check_true` result-register positions — exactly the F-2 stuck-at-on-return-register class documented in the README; bool-returning fns are inherently stuck-at-defeatable. (c) **two-fault [skip,skip]**: 322 total pair flips → 217 in scaffolding (expected) + 4 harness-artifact (closure cond() bypass) + **101 in pure check_true verdict/return code** — vs prior README count of 1. The 100× jump is a codegen-layout effect from the merge, not a new mechanism. (d) **wait_random sweeps**: 33-50 crashes per single-fault model on the post-loop halt check, 0 returned-normally — invariant guard fires as designed. **Production impact: none.** `grep \bcheck_true\b` outside of fi.rs's own definition confirms **zero production callers of the bool-returning check_true** — all ~13 call sites already use `check_true_into_sentinel` per F-5's documented mitigation. The bool variant exists only for the harness's testing convenience. F-5 README's 'Action taken: migrated all ~13 `check_true` callsites to compare a sentinel rather than a bare bool' is in effect. Harness flagged this as 'REGRESSION' because the single-fault [skip] sweep used to exit 0; the underlying weakness is the documented F-5 residual, just exposed more thoroughly by the merged tree's binary layout. Follow-up options (deferred): (1) update fi_target's `sca_fi_check_true` to probe `check_true_into_sentinel` instead (probes the actually-used primitive); (2) place a `udf`/long-branch padding between shim functions to defeat the fall-through class; (3) accept and update harness expected-exit-code semantics to INFO rather than REGRESSION for fi.rs check_true hits since the doc already says it's expected. |
| 2026-05-18 | **F-22 — BIP-39 wordlist lookup leaks 264 bits of entropy (production-critical)** | New `leakage_bip39.py` + `sca_bip39_word_indices` / `sca_bip39_wordlist_lookup` symbols in `kdf_target`. Control case (SHA-256 + 11-bit unpacking, NO wordlist load): **max|t| = 0.00**. Wordlist-lookup case (adds `WORDLIST[idx]` for each of 24 words): **max|t| = 27.98** (6.2× the 4.5 threshold), 5 peaks at samples 615/671/703/727/751 consistent with the 24 individual indexed loads. **Mechanism**: `Mnemonic::to_seed` (`bip39/src/lib.rs:183`) calls `self.words()` → `self.indices.iter().map(\|&i\| WORDLIST[i as usize])`, each load at `&WORDLIST[0] + idx * sizeof(&str)` — the load address encodes the secret index. 24 × 11 bits = 264 leaked bits > 256-bit BIP-39 entropy, so single-trace seed reconstruction is sufficient. **Severity HIGH (production-critical)**: this is the FIRST CPU operation on raw entropy in the first-unlock path; bypasses dual-SE split (entropy AT REST defense), FI guards (signature integrity defense), and hardware PIN gate (SE-storage defense). **Attack model**: power/EM scoping of the cold-boot first-unlock — single trace. After unlock the bip39_seed is zeroized so steady-state signs are unaffected — only the once-per-power-on derivation leaks. **Fix design**: constant-time wordlist scan (load all 2048 entries per word slot, `subtle::ConditionallySelectable` mask on idx) — ~393 KB reads per to_seed, ~1 ms on Cortex-M33; PLUS constant-time variable-length password assembly (interleaved-write per byte position, expensive but preserves BIP-39 compat — padding-based variants break compat). **Status**: documented as F-22 in tools/sca/README.md, fix DEFERRED pending crypto-eng review — implementation is substantial and high-stakes (BIP-39 hot path). Regression harness `make bip39-leak` MUST stay flat after fix AND `Mnemonic::to_seed("")` MUST stay byte-identical with the existing BIP-39 test vectors. |
| 2026-05-18 | **F-22 FIXED — BIP-39 wordlist leak closed (constant-time scan)** | `bip39/src/lib.rs` rewritten: new `WORDLIST_FLAT: [[u8; 8]; 2048]` + `WORDLIST_LENS: [u8; 2048]` (compile-time flat representation derived from the existing `WORDLIST: &[&str; 2048]` via a `const fn`); new `ct_load_word(target_idx)` does a 2048-entry mask-OR scan with `subtle`-style `(mask & bytes) | (~mask & acc)` folding (manual `ct_eq_u16` for the mask, no `subtle` dep). `Mnemonic::to_seed` rewritten in three constant-time phases: (1) resolve all 24 indices via `ct_load_word` → stack-resident `words[24][8]` + `lens[24]`; (2) cumulative offsets via pure arithmetic; (3) for each output byte position p in 0..215, scan all (word, byte) candidates and mask-OR the right byte into `password[p]` — every store goes to a loop-counter-derived address. **Critical implementation note**: `core::hint::black_box` barriers on each iteration's mask/entry/accumulator are LOAD-BEARING — without them LLVM folds the scan into a direct `WORDLIST_FLAT[target_idx]` lookup and the fix regresses to max|t| ≈ 5000 (vs the 0.00 we now get). Verification: `to_seed_matches_trezor_vectors` + 8 other bip39 tests pass (byte-identical to Trezor reference → recovery contract preserved); 1835/1835 secure host tests pass; `make bip39-leak` post-fix probe `sca_bip39_wordlist_lookup_ct` over 24,851 samples shows **max|t| = 0.00** (perfectly constant). Pre-fix probe `sca_bip39_wordlist_lookup` retained as regression sentinel (max|t| = 17.88 — confirms the harness still detects the originally-leaky pattern). Cost in production: ~393 KB stack reads + 41K inner-loop iters → ~2 ms wall on Cortex-M33, single-digit-percent of `derive_keypair_from_entropy`'s overall cost (dominated by SPHINCS+C10 keygen ~10s) and called once per first-unlock. Residual **F-23**: PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA512's length-padding byte embeds the password's bit-length (sum of word lengths, ~7 bits of entropy — 30× smaller magnitude than F-22's 264 bits). Closing F-23 requires padding the word slots to fixed-length, which would break BIP-39 byte-equality with non-PQSigner verifiers; accepted as residual, flagged in the user-facing trust model. |
| 2026-05-18 | F-22 follow-up audit: other secret-bearing derivation paths all clean | New `leakage_seed_derivation.py` + three `sca_*_seed_from_bip39` targets in `kdf_target/src/main.rs` mirror the production `pqsigner_domain` derivation chain: (a) `slhdsa_seed_from_bip39` (2× kdf_sha256 with `sphincsc7-{sk,pk}-seed` tags) → 48 B SLH-DSA seed; (b) `bootstrap_seed_from_bip39` (2× kdf_sha256 with bootstrap tags) → 48 B bootstrap signer seed; (c) `slot_master_entropy_from_bip39` (1× kdf_sha256 with `pqwallet-slot-master` tag) → 32 B slot master. TVLA fixed-vs-random 64-byte bip39_seed input, 600 traces each. **All three max|t| = 0.000** over 2522 / 2528 / 1241 samples respectively — perfectly constant on mem_address. The `sha2::Sha256` bitsliced soft backend on thumbv8m is constant-time on address-channel (same property already shown for HMAC-SHA512 and AES-256). **Audit thread closes cleanly: F-22 was the ONLY leak in the entire secret-bearing CPU derivation chain** (PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA512 → `Mnemonic::to_seed` → 3× SHA-256 derivations → `SigningKey::keygen`). Per-derivation-path coverage now: PBKDF2 ✓ (sca_hmac_sha512_kdf clean), wordlist lookup ✓ (was F-22 leak, now FIXED via ct scan), slhdsa-seed ✓ (this run), bootstrap-seed ✓ (this run), slot-master-entropy ✓ (this run), AES-256 ✓ (sca_aes256_encrypt_block clean), AES-GCM wrap ✓ (sca_aesgcm_wrap clean), SAES-DHUK CMAC wrapper ✓ (saes-kdf clean), C10 keygen ✓ (sca_c10_keygen clean), C10 sign ✓ (sca_c10_sign clean post-F-13/F-16/F-9 fixes), wait_random ✓ (clean). Remaining gaps tracked as F-23 (PBKDF2 length-padding, accepted residual) and the display-rendering paths (seed_wizard, measured_boot — separate sweep). |

Full per-channel breakdown + mitigation options in tools/sca/README.md §F-24. |
| 2026-05-18 | PIN-verify CPU chain leakage TVLA — clean | Extended `leakage_seed_derivation.py` + new `sca_optiga_pin_derive` target in kdf_target. Mirrors the OPTIGA PIN-verify hot path: `derive_pin_secret(pin) = kdf_sha256("optiga-pin-auth-v1", pin, 0)` → `hmac_sha256(pin_secret, FIXED_NONCE)`. TVLA varying 8-byte PIN, 600 traces, **max|t| = 0.000** over 3,137 samples. SHA-256 + HMAC-SHA256 via `sha2` 0.10's bitsliced soft backend on thumbv8m is constant-time on address-channel — same property already shown for the AES + HMAC-SHA512 + AES-GCM paths. **Production interpretation**: an attacker EM-scoping the CPU during a successful PIN unlock cannot recover the 8-digit PIN from `mem_address` patterns. The 10-attempt silicon lockout (MCU page-124 + OPTIGA E120 + SE050 USERID) is the actual rate-limit; the CPU-side leak surface is empty. Caveats unchanged from F-22's documentation: rainbow's mem_address channel only measures load-address Hamming weight; value-side leakage (S-box outputs, HMAC state) is invisible to this emulator and would need on-silicon power/EM scoping. Audit summary now: every secret-handling CPU computation that the firmware does is constant-time on the address channel (PBKDF2, BIP-39 wordlist [F-22 fix], slhdsa-seed/bootstrap-seed/slot-master derivations, OPTIGA PIN-verify chain, AES + HMAC-SHA512 + AES-GCM, SAES-CMAC wrapper, C10 keygen, C10 sign post-F-13/F-16/F-9 fixes, C10 verify, wait_random, font-glyph blit [F-24 stage D fix]). Remaining residuals: F-23 length-padding via PBKDF2 (accepted) and F-24 stage E sub-channels (hardware-addressable: OLED EM/power/bus). |
| 2026-05-18 | **F-25 — bip39::lookup_prefix recovery-path leak — FOUND + FIXED** | New `leakage_recovery.py` + `sca_bip39_lookup_prefix` baseline target. Discovered the symmetric pair to F-22: during RECOVERY, when the user types each of 24 mnemonic words back, `bip39::lookup_prefix` did a binary search over WORDLIST (`partition_point` + walk-forward) with addresses keyed on the typed prefix. **Baseline max|t| = 45.43** (10× the 4.5 threshold) — even larger than F-22's 27.98. Peaks at samples 196/197/218/172/221 consistent with the 11 binary-search midpoint loads + the forward walk. **Fix**: rewrote `lookup_prefix` to scan all 2048 entries unconditionally via constant-time mask-OR over the F-22 fix's `WORDLIST_FLAT` + `WORDLIST_LENS` tables (`(entry[i] == needle[i]) | (i >= plen)` per position, `entry_len >= plen` len check, first-match-idx captured via conditional-select, count incremented per match). `core::hint::black_box` barriers per F-22's load-bearing pattern. **Post-fix max|t| = 0.000 over 45,089 samples** — perfectly constant. `case_insensitive_lookup` regression test passes (CT scan byte-equivalent to old binary-search behavior). **No migration needed**: `seed_wizard.rs`'s recovery path calls `lookup_prefix` directly — the fix lands in the shared callee. **Cost**: ~0.25 ms per keystroke (≤ 5/s keystroke rate during typing → no UX impact). **Severity**: HIGH (symmetric to F-22). Threat model: an attacker EM-scoping the CPU during the 60-120 s recovery-typing window can collect per-keystroke traces and reconstruct each word. Camera-blocked attacker with EM access is now defended against in BOTH directions (provisioning via F-22/F-24, recovery via F-25). Full §F-25 in tools/sca/README.md. 1837/1837 secure host tests pass; cargo check clean on both stm32u585+dual-se+bhk and mock-se shapes. |
| 2026-05-19 | **F-26 — Groth16 verifier tight FI sweep — NO FORGE** | New `tools/sca/groth16_target/` (thumbv8m crate mirroring `secure/src/zk/groth16.rs::groth16_verify` verbatim) + `fault_sweep_groth16.py` running rainbow `fault_skip` over the BAD-path tail. Setup: real VK from `secure/src/zk/vk_data.rs` + real proof from `test_vectors.rs`, paired with a `pub0`-bit-flipped public-signal vector so the unfaulted run rejects (r0=0). A single fault flipping r0 to 1 would be FORGE_RELEASE — letting an attacker forge ZK clear-sign verification, which the trusted UI uses to gate human-readable Aave/CowSwap action rendering. Auto-bisects BAD-path instruction count to 1K precision (161,346,878 ± 1K). Sweeps `[bad_total-5000, bad_total-900)` at step 25 = 164 positions. **Result: 124 rejects, 0 accepts, 26 crashes, 14 anomalies — no FORGE_RELEASE.** The final `result == Gt::identity()` compare + return path is FI-robust against single instruction-skips in this window. Two harness corrections landed: (1) sweep upper bound respects rainbow's ~800-instr `IndexError: reached end of function before faulting` boundary (sits ~800 instr before the bisected `bad_total`); diagnosed via a throwaway probe walking deltas 100/200/300/.../5000. (2) Fix `NameError: TIGHT_DEPTH` in success-summary print path; same `FULL_LEAD` bound applied to `--full` (overnight) mode. Deferred: `make groth16-full` documented for the ~25-100 h overnight sweep (last 30K instr × skip + stuck-at-0 + stuck-at-FF — covers the broader compare class). README §F-26 carries the full setup + result + what's NOT covered (wider window, stuck-at, multi-fault, drift watch). Commit `b6eb4e1`. |
| 2026-05-19 | **F-27 — recovery candidate-pick gate leaks (2 leaks, both FIXED)** | Sweeping `measured_boot.rs` + `tx/display/*` (per user request) confirmed those paths are clean (data they render is public — firmware_hash words, tx amounts/addresses). The same sweep landed on `secure/src/ui/seed_wizard.rs` and turned up two real production leaks in the recovery candidate-pick gate, both on secret typed-prefix input. **Leak A — `prefix_is_exact_word`** (a second `WORDLIST.binary_search_by` that F-25 didn't reach — used inside the candidate-pick gate when a typed prefix is itself an exact wordlist entry AND a prefix of others, e.g. "act" / "actor"). **Leak B — `render_candidate_screen`** stacked two channels on the same secret-derived idx: `WORDLIST[idx].as_bytes()` (F-22-class load) + `d.draw_line(..., ascii_str(&row))` (F-24-stage-C-class non-CT embedded-graphics font expansion that the seed-display path migrated away from in F-24 but the candidate-pick path didn't). **TVLA**: baseline binary search max|t| = 42.905 over 239 samples (peaks at 197/195/171/194/217 = 11 binary-search midpoint loads); CT scan max|t| = 0.000 over 69,662 samples — same shape as F-25 result. **Fix**: two new public CT primitives in `bip39/src/lib.rs` — `word_bytes_at(idx) -> ([u8; 8], u8)` (thin pub wrapper over F-22-era `ct_load_word`, same 2048-entry mask-OR scan + black_box barriers) and `is_exact_wordlist_entry(needle: &[u8]) -> bool` (adds an explicit `entry_len == needle.len()` check — without it a zero-padded oversized needle "act\0\0\0\0\0" would falsely match the 3-byte entry "act" whose WORDLIST_FLAT storage is itself padded to 8 zero bytes; regression test caught this). `seed_wizard::prefix_is_exact_word` collapses to a one-liner calling `is_exact_wordlist_entry`. `seed_wizard::render_candidate_screen` follows the `render_mnemonic_page` template: `word_bytes_at` for the wordlist load + `flush_with_secret_rows` for the framebuffer paint (rows 0 + 3 stay on `draw_line` — title + hint are public text). **Cost**: ~0.25 ms / `prefix_is_exact_word` call + 2× `word_bytes_at` per `render_candidate_screen`; bounded by keystroke cadence (≤5/s long-presses), no UX impact. **Verification**: 1837/1837 secure host tests pass; 6 new tests in `bip39/tests/lookup.rs` lock in the public-API contract (`word_bytes_at` round-trips every wordlist entry; `is_exact_wordlist_entry` accepts every entry, rejects obvious non-words + the zero-padding trap + over-long inputs + ASCII case-sensitivity at the byte level); `make seedwizard-exact-leak` validates baseline-leaks + CT-clean post-fix. **Deferred hygiene**: `bip39::lookup_word_exact` still uses leaky binary search but its sole caller is `Mnemonic::from_words` invoked only from the e2e-test path with fixed TEST_WORDS (no production secret); `measured_boot::render_all_words` still uses `WORDLIST[idx]` but the displayed words derive from firmware_hash which is public by construction. Both flagged for a separate "delete leaky WORDLIST[i] access pattern" hygiene commit so this F-27 diff stays focused on the production-critical fix. Full §F-27 in tools/sca/README.md. |
| 2026-05-19 | F-27 hygiene cleanup — delete leaky `WORDLIST[idx]` pattern from non-secret paths | Follow-up to the F-27 fix: migrate the remaining `WORDLIST[idx].as_bytes()` / `WORDLIST[idx]` call sites in production code (non-test-only) to the constant-time `word_bytes_at(idx)` API so the leaky address-keyed-load pattern doesn't survive in the codebase to be copy-pasted into a future secret-bearing context. Three call sites: (1) **`bip39::lookup_word_exact`** rewritten from `WORDLIST.binary_search_by` to a 2048-entry CT scan with the same `(byte_eq, len_eq) + black_box` shape as `is_exact_wordlist_entry`; conditional-select captures the first hit's idx via mask-OR. (2) **`secure/src/measured_boot.rs::render_all_words`** + the `run()` `secure_log!` loop migrated to `word_bytes_at(indices[li/ri])`; the displayed 8 BIP-39 words derive from `firmware_hash` which is public by construction (user is supposed to read and compare them against `fwmeasure`), so no security regression. Cost: ~128 KB stack reads at boot (~8 × 16 KB), well under 10 ms on Cortex-M33. (3) **`secure/src/ui/seed_wizard.rs:259`** debug-log MISMATCH branch migrated to `word_bytes_at` + `Mnemonic::word_bytes` for both got/expected sides; the bare `WORDLIST` import dropped (only F-27 fix comments still reference it). Source-text pin tests in `secure/src/fw_update_boot_pure_tests.rs` updated to assert the new pattern + a belt-and-braces `!MEASURED_BOOT_SRC.contains("WORDLIST[indices[")` check so a future regression-PR can't reintroduce the leaky shape. **Verification**: 1837/1837 secure host tests pass; cargo check clean on `stm32u585+dual-se+bhk+ui-oled` and the `+debug-log` variant. **Remaining `WORDLIST[i]` uses**: only in `bip39/src/lib.rs` (the `Mnemonic::word()` / `Mnemonic::words()` public accessors — documented "address-leaks the index", callers wanting CT use `word_bytes` instead) and in test files (`bip39/tests/*`, `secure/src/ui_under_test/pure_tests.rs`, `secure/src/fw_update_boot_pure_tests.rs`); plus host tooling `fwsign/src/subcommands/sign.rs` which runs on the developer's machine, not the device — no SCA threat. |
| 2026-05-19 | §25 Gap 4 — SE050 silicon-locked SW → `UnlockError::PinLocked` (code landed; bench validation deferred) | Closes the corruption/desync stuck-loop case where MCU page-124 has been reset (flash fault, RDP regression mid-cycle, dev-tool intervention) but SE050 UserID is silicon-locked — pre-fix, a fresh PIN attempt hit the `Status(sw)` catch-all → `InternalError` → gateway treated as transient, no wipe → device stuck. Investigation: original work-todo guess "0x6983" was the **A71CL legacy** code (`SE_HAL_Lib.h::A71CL_SE_HAL_ERR_KEY_LOCKED = 0xCC // 6983`); SE050's `se05x_tlv.h::smStatus_t` does NOT include 0x6983. SE050 uses **0x6986** (`SM_ERR_COMMAND_NOT_ALLOWED`, AN13030 wording "Command not allowed — access denied based on object policy") for policy-denial when an auth object stops accepting VERIFY — the documented-by-deduction match (AN12413 is behind NXP login, on-silicon SW capture deferred). Fix: new `Se050Error::AuthMethodBlocked` variant in `apdu.rs`; `apdu::verify_session` adds `Status(0x6986) → AuthMethodBlocked` arm alongside the existing `0x6985 || 0x63xx → PinIncorrect`; new pure helper `classify_se050_unlock_error(&Se050Error) -> UnlockError` in `mod.rs` maps `AuthMethodBlocked → PinLocked`; `Se050::unlock` map_err refactored to call the helper (side-effect arm zeros `self.remaining` on AuthMethodBlocked). End-to-end dispatch: `apdu::verify_session` → `AuthMethodBlocked` → `Se050::unlock` map_err → `UnlockError::PinLocked` → `DualSecureElement::unlock` propagation → `cmd_request_unlock`'s `Err(UnlockError::PinLocked) => trigger_lockout_wipe()` arm. 4 new host pin-tests `gap4_*` in `se050_under_test/pure_tests.rs` (1841 total, was 1837): (a) apdu arm exists; (b) classifier maps AuthMethodBlocked→PinLocked; (c) PinIncorrect still maps to PinIncorrect (catastrophic-UX regression guard); (d) catch-all still on InternalError (no over-eager wipe on transport glitches). Conservatively narrow per advisor review — only 0x6986 mapped, not 0x6982 (generic, false-positive wipe risk worse than the rare desync). On-silicon validation: next bench session, run `iterative_wipe` against a throwaway UserID to drive it to attempts=0, send VERIFY, capture SW — if not 0x6986 update both the `verify_session` arm and the `AuthMethodBlocked` doc. `pin-gate-wipe-e2e` infrastructure already covers the wiring; just needs SW-capture on the post-lockout attempt. |

| 2026-05-19 | F-24 stage E sub-channel 4 — **Phase 1 (decoy frames) LANDED**; bench flicker validation pending | F-24 stages A-D (2026-05-18) closed the CPU-side leak surface for seed display. The four hardware-only residuals were documented but not implemented. Phase 1 closes sub-channel 4 (I²C bus-EM emission) via persistence-of-vision decoy frames. Implementation: `secure/src/ui/seed_wizard.rs::show_mnemonic` generates N=4 valid BIP-39 mnemonics from independent `rng_strong::fill` entropy at wizard entry. New `show_mnemonic_page_with_decoys` frame loop interleaves the real mnemonic (200 ms hold) with one decoy per cycle (40 ms hold), cycling through all 4 decoys round-robin — 5:1 real:decoy time ratio. Every frame (real OR decoy) routes through the same constant-time `render_mnemonic_page` so the per-frame CPU trace is content-independent (F-24 stages A-D contract preserved). Button events return immediately from the frame loop; idle timeout triggers wipe via the existing path. **Mnemonic's Drop zeroizes indices** so decoys wipe automatically on wizard exit; the raw 32-byte entropy buffers are explicitly zeroized after Mnemonic construction. **Failure path**: if rng_strong::fill fails for any decoy (extremely rare — 3-source XOR'd TRNG would need multiple simultaneous faults), show_mnemonic returns Cancelled with "RNG failed retry" status; wizard outer loop retries. 5 new source-text regression sentinels in `secure/src/ui_under_test/pure_tests.rs::negative_*decoy*` pin: (a) rng_strong as entropy source (not deterministic), (b) Mnemonic::from_entropy for valid BIP-39 checksums (un-filterable from real), (c) entropy zeroize post-construct, (d) 5:1 time ratio constants pinned, (e) frame loop routes through render_mnemonic_page (CT path). 1847/1847 secure host tests pass (was 1842; +5 sentinels). cargo check clean on stm32u585+dual-se+bhk+ui-oled with and without debug-log. **Hardware flicker validation outstanding** — bench user reads 24 words off real silicon (SSD1306 @ 400 kHz I²C, ~36 ms per flush_with_secret_rows call) and reports whether the 200ms:40ms cadence is readable. If unreadable, increase REAL_FRAME_HOLD_MS to 400-500 (smooths visual, weakens defense). **Phase 2 deferred**: constant-pixel-count encoding (sub-channel 3, power-rail) requires per-word XOR-complement tables that bring every row's lit-pixel count to a constant — significantly more work than Phase 1; revisit after Phase 1's UX is validated. Threat-model boundary: Phase 1 defends an EM-bus attacker without optical access (foil-wrapped device, dark room, internal RMA scenarios). The realistic threat (camera attacker) is unaffected — decoys are still visible to the camera; the user's responsibility for physical privacy during provisioning is unchanged. tools/sca/README.md §F-24 sub-channel 4 updated to reflect Phase 1 landing + Phase 2 deferred. |
| 2026-05-19 | F-24 stage E Phase 1 — bench-tested on OLED, defense doesn't work on bistable displays; code gated behind `decoy-frames` feature pending LCD hardware | New `decoy-flicker-test` Cargo feature + `make decoy-flicker-hw` Makefile target. Short-circuits `main()` into a forever-loop that renders page 0 of a fixed test mnemonic interleaved with 4 fixed decoys at the production cadence — no wizard, no buttons, no SE access, just OLED rendering for bench visual inspection. Result: at 5:1 (200ms:40ms) the user sees N+1 mnemonics cycling, NOT a stable real with subliminal decoys. Bumping REAL_FRAME_HOLD_MS to 2000 didn't help (user still perceives content changes, not flicker). **Root cause**: OLED pixels are bistable — once painted, they hold state indefinitely until repainted. Persistence-of-vision (Trezor's referenced mechanism) works on CRTs / movie projectors (natural decay between frames); on OLEDs the eye locks onto every painted frame. We cannot paint faster than ~25 ms over I²C @ 400 kHz, and a 25 ms content change is well above the eye's flicker-fusion threshold (~10 ms for content). **Code disposition**: Phase 1 decoy-frame code stays in tree (`show_mnemonic_with_decoys`, `show_mnemonic_page_with_decoys`) gated behind a new `decoy-frames` Cargo feature (default OFF). `show_mnemonic` now splits to `show_mnemonic_simple` (default) vs `show_mnemonic_with_decoys` (feature-gated). 5 source-text regression sentinels preserved. Production wizard reverts to the simple stable-render path so users can read their seed. `make decoy-flicker-hw` target stays for future revalidation. **Path forward**: re-test on the ZT165M017AT TFT LCD (NV3007 driver, SPI, 142×428, Tr+Tf typ 35 ms max 40 ms) when it's wired. On slow-response LCDs the pixel response time IS the persistence-of-vision mechanism — a decoy painted for ~5-10 ms and immediately overwritten by real never reaches full pixel transition. Tune REAL_FRAME_HOLD_MS / DECOY_FRAME_HOLD_MS empirically on the LCD via `decoy-flicker-test`, then flip `decoy-frames` ON in the shipping profile if validated. Phase 2 (constant-pixel-count encoding for sub-channel 3 power-rail) also becomes less necessary on the LCD: backlight current is constant regardless of pixel state (3 white LEDs in parallel, ~60 mA typ), so the power-rail leak signature on the LCD is structurally weaker than OLED. tools/sca/README.md §F-24 sub-channel 4 updated with full bench finding + LCD path. 1847/1847 host tests pass (5 regression sentinels still match the in-tree decoy code). |

| 2026-05-19 | Factory provisioning — Phase A.1: rehearsal mode + compile fence + OTP sentinel + RDP2 sequencing docs | Three foot-gun protections added on top of the Phase A scaffold (commit `cab0530`): **(1) Rehearsal mode** via new `factory-provisioning-rehearsal` Cargo feature — steps 4 (DualSeProvision), 5 (WipeUserState), 6 (PostWipeValidation) SKIP their destructive calls in this build. OLED still cycles all 7 panels for visual validation; OTP sentinel still gets written but with `BIT_REHEARSAL` (not `BIT_PRODUCTION`). Distinct success panel "REHEARSAL OK / SE NOT changed / NOT for ship!" — operator + host fixture can't confuse it for production. New `make build-hw-factory-provisioning-rehearsal` target. **(2) Compile-time fence** — refuses to build the irreversible production profile (`optiga-lock-operational` or `bhk` without hardcoded-master-key or NEITHER `dev-testkey` NOR `otp-hardcoded-master-key`) without an explicit `factory-production-irreversible-im-sure` opt-in feature. Catches the "I forgot dev-testkey in the Makefile" class of accidental chip burns. Anyone editing Cargo can add/remove the opt-in — this is a foot-gun guard, not a security gate. Empirically validated: all 5 build paths (default, factory+dev-testkey, factory+rehearsal, factory+LcsO=Op+opt-in, factory+LcsO=Op-without-opt-in→compile error). **(3) OTP factory sentinel** at byte offset 160 of the STM32 OTP region (one 128-bit quad-word, just past the device master key). 3-bit tally encoding: bit 0 = "ran at least once", bit 1 = "rehearsal completed", bit 2 = "production completed". One-way per OTP semantics, but rehearsal-then-production is the expected dev iteration path (results in `0xFFFFFFF8` = both bits cleared). The host-side factory fixture reads the sentinel via probe-rs at `0x0BFA_00A0` BEFORE bumping the irreversible RDP=Level-2 option byte; fixture interprets `0xFFFFFFFA` (production only) or `0xFFFFFFF8` (both modes) as RDP2-ready, refuses RDP2 on any other state. Step 7 (new) writes the sentinel; the pre-populated-state check (step 3) also checks the sentinel + refuses to re-run a chip whose `BIT_PRODUCTION` is already cleared (`E0702`). New OTP API: `factory_sentinel_read() -> u32` + `unsafe factory_sentinel_record(bits_to_clear) -> Result<(), OtpError>`. `OTP_RESERVED_BYTES` bumped from 160 to 176 to include the sentinel region; matching host test updated. **Operator manual** (`docs/provisioning/factory-provisioning.md`) updated with: rehearsal-mode panel preview + interpretation table, OTP sentinel encoding + host-fixture decode table, RDP2 section explaining the post-ceremony irreversible step + why the fixture must verify the sentinel before bumping, build-profile safety guard table. 1847/1847 standard host tests pass; 7 factory_provisioning tests (was 5 — added step-count + factory_sentinel_bit_layout). cargo check clean on all 5 build paths (4 valid + 1 deliberately-refused-by-fence). |
| 2026-05-19 | Factory provisioning — Phase A.2: flash + verify + RDP2-bump Makefile targets, early "ran" sentinel | Wires the host-side orchestration on top of Phase A.1's foot-gun protections. **Three new Makefile targets** + 1 helper script: (a) `make flash-hw-factory-provisioning` chains probe-rs download (NS + S) + STM32_Programmer_CLI TZ option-byte config + probe-rs reset + the new verifier script. Does NOT bump RDP2. (b) `make flash-hw-factory-provisioning-rehearsal` same as above but uses the rehearsal build (steps 4-6 skip destructive calls). (c) `make bump-rdp2-after-factory` re-reads the OTP sentinel, refuses unless `BIT_PRODUCTION` is cleared, prompts the operator to type `BUMP RDP2` as final confirmation, then runs `STM32_Programmer_CLI --optionbytes RDP=0xCC`. (d) `make factory-status-hw` read-only sentinel inspection (no flashing, no state change). **Host-side verifier script** `tools/factory-provisioning-verify.sh`: polls the OTP factory sentinel at `0x0BFA_00A0` via `probe-rs read 32` for up to 60s, requires the sentinel value to be stable across two successive reads (guards against mid-write race), decodes into one of 5 outcomes (DID_NOT_START / STARTED_FAILED / REHEARSAL_ONLY / PRODUCTION_OK / BOTH_OK), maps each to a script exit code (0=RDP2-eligible, 1=not-yet-complete, 2=probe failure, 3=corrupt sentinel, 4=bump refused). `--bump-rdp2` flag does the RDP2 transition only if the sentinel is RDP2-eligible AND the operator types `BUMP RDP2` at the interactive prompt. Configurable via `CHIP` / `OTP_SENTINEL_ADDR` / `POLL_TIMEOUT_SECS` / `POLL_INTERVAL_SECS` env vars. **Firmware fix**: `factory_provisioning::run_and_halt` now writes `BIT_RAN` to the OTP sentinel BEFORE step 1 (was only at step 7). This lets the host verifier distinguish "chip never started" (`0xFFFFFFFF`) from "started + halted at failure panel" (`0xFFFFFFFE`) — without the early write, both states looked identical and the host fixture would timeout on every failure. The early write is best-effort (silent on OTP fault) so a stuck OTP write doesn't brick the ceremony at entry. **Operator manual** updated: new "Quick procedure" section showing the three-make-target flow, new "Source map" entries for the script + targets. All 5 cargo build paths still clean; 1847 standard host tests pass; shell-script `bash -n` syntax check clean. Target NOT silicon-validated by intent — phase B awaits the user's hardware-on-bench session. |
