| 2026-05-19 | Prodtest firmware — Phase C (communication tests: OPTIGA + SE050 + USB integrity) | Adds the third tier of prodtest commands — communication tests that exercise the I²C and USB stacks end-to-end. **3 new commands at IDs 106-108:** `CMD_PRODTEST_OPTIGA_HANDSHAKE` lazily runs `OptigaTrustM::init()` (RST pulse + `OpenApplication`) then `GetRandom(16)`. On a fresh chip there's no PBS yet so the APDU goes through the plain non-shielded path (`shield.active == false` → `send_command` falls through to `transceive` without wrapping). Catches missing chip / broken solder / wedged I²C / wrong RST line / power-rail or clock issues / chip RNG defect (all-zero or all-0xFF). `CMD_PRODTEST_SE050_HANDSHAKE` same shape — `Se050::init()` (interface_reset + ATR + SCP03 session with NXP default platform keys) then `GetRandom(16)`. Catches missing chip / wrong ENA line / cold-boot timing (the existing 3-attempt retry loop already covers this) / pre-rotated SCP03 keys (chip wasn't blank) / RNG defect. `CMD_PRODTEST_USB_LOOPBACK` echoes N≤256 bytes — the fact the firmware received the command already proves USB RX works; this proves TX + full round-trip byte integrity for non-trivial payloads. Catches USB OTG FS TX corruption / HID report fragmentation bugs in the NS-side stack / buffer-overflow in the transport layer / sustained-TX VCC instability. **Wiring:** 3 new `#[cfg(feature = "prodtest")]` dispatch arms in `secure/src/nsc/mod.rs`, 3 new CMSE veneers (`nsc_prodtest_optiga_handshake` / `nsc_prodtest_se050_handshake` / `nsc_prodtest_usb_loopback`), 3 new handlers in `secure/src/nsc/prodtest.rs` that access the global `crate::SE` via the established `addr_of_mut!(crate::SE)` pattern (serialised by the dispatcher's non-reentrant invariant). The handshake handlers reuse `OptigaTrustM::random` / `Se050::random` so the test surface is just "does the existing random primitive work" — no new code path to bench-validate. **Host fixture runner** (`tools/factory-prodtest-runner.py`) gets `test_optiga_handshake` + `test_se050_handshake` (both check status==Ok + 16 bytes received + bytes ≠ all-zero / all-0xFF, log RNG output for per-die-uniqueness DB) + `test_usb_loopback` (deterministic XOR-with-0xA5 pattern catches byte-substitution + bit-flip + buffer-overflow, reports first-mismatch offset for diagnostics). New module pin-tests: `positive_phase_c_handshake_rng_lens_pinned` + `positive_usb_loopback_cap_matches_proto_doc`. Operator manual (`docs/provisioning/factory-prodtest.md`) command table extended to 9 commands; per-command sections added with pass criteria + diagnostic value for each. **Build validation:** 4 build paths still pass — default (no prodtest), prodtest+dev-testkey, prodtest+factory-production-irreversible-im-sure, prodtest-without-opt-in→compile error (fence still fires). 1847/1847 default-features host tests pass; module-internal tests still pending host harness wiring (same situation as Phase A+B + LCD + factory_provisioning modules). `make build-hw-prodtest` succeeds. Python runner `--help` works; syntax check clean. Target NOT silicon-validated by intent — Phase B awaits user's hardware-on-bench session. |
| 2026-05-19 | Prodtest firmware — Phase D (interactive button test) | `CMD_PRODTEST_BUTTON_TEST` (109) — 3-step LEFT/RIGHT/BOTH verification, 10 s per step, returns 4 bytes (step_status:1 + reserved:3). Compact step-status encoding: upper nibble = step (1/2/3), lower nibble = error kind (1=timeout, 2=wrong button = swapped wires). Wrong-button detection (advisor): if RIGHT fires during the LEFT step or vice versa, return WRONG_BUTTON instead of TIMEOUT — operator + factory error table get a diagnostically-distinct path (swapped wires = rewire + retry; dead solder = re-solder + retry). No latency reporting — human reaction time dominates GPIO; the press-registered-or-not signal carries all the information. **Wiring:** `gpio-buttons` added to the `prodtest = [...]` Cargo feature; `hw::buttons::{left_pressed, right_pressed, busy_wait_ms}` promoted from private to `pub(crate)` so the prodtest handler can run its own poll loop without going through `wait_event`'s gesture state machine; `hw::buttons::init()` invocation added to the prodtest short-circuit in `secure/src/main.rs` (gated on `gpio-buttons` so the call is no-op on non-prodtest builds that also enable the feature). Handler runs the 3-step state machine with 30 ms debounce + 5 ms poll + clean-release-before-next-step (so a held button doesn't bleed into the next prompt). OLED shows "PRESS LEFT" / "PRESS RIGHT" / "PRESS BOTH" with 10 s reminder, then "BTN PASS" / "BTN FAIL step N". **Host runner** (`tools/factory-prodtest-runner.py`) gets `test_button_test` + `BUTTON_STEP_DECODE` table that maps each status code (0x00 / 0x11 / 0x12 / 0x21 / 0x22 / 0x31) to operator-readable diagnostic — `step 1 (LEFT) wrong button — operator pressed RIGHT or wires are swapped`. Added LAST in the test orchestration since it's the only operator-interactive command (failures from automated tests are easier to recover from without involving a human). **3 new pin-tests** pin the step-code layout (compact nibble encoding invariant), the 10 s timeout, and the 4-byte output length. Operator manual gets a full per-step-status decode table + a `<!-- TODO photo: ... -->` marker (advisor: leave honest markers rather than describing photos with prose that will age badly). **Pre-Phase-E blocker surfaced during this work**: the `prodtest` main-loop short-circuit halts in WFI before reaching `boot_ns::boot`, so the NS USB stack never starts and no prodtest command is reachable over USB yet. The button test code is correct in isolation; bench validation requires Phase E to first fix the NS-launch flow. Tracked in work-todo §30 status line. 4 build paths still clean (default, prodtest+dev-testkey, prodtest+factory-production-irreversible-im-sure, prodtest-no-opt-in→compile error). 1847/1847 default-features host tests pass. Python `--help` works; AST clean. Target NOT silicon-validated by intent — awaits Phase E + user's hardware-on-bench session. |
| 2026-05-19 | Prodtest firmware — Phase E (USB launch fix + INS dispatch + Python HID framing) | First Phase that makes the prodtest commands actually reachable over USB. **Three pieces:** (1) **Firmware USB-launch fix**: the prodtest main-loop short-circuit was halting in WFI before `boot_ns::boot`, so the NS USB stack never started — every "Phase A/B/C/D done" was firmware-only and not actually reachable from a host. Rewired to run the final-stage init that NS world depends on (`hw::usb_hw::init`, `setup_systick`, DWT cycle counter) then call `boot_ns::boot(NS_FLASH_BASE)` — same exit primitive the production main flow uses. The "PRODTEST READY" OLED panel shows briefly then NS world takes over. (2) **Per-CMD INS allocation + NS dispatch**: 10 new `INS_V2_PRODTEST_*` constants at 0x80..0x89 in `proto/src/lib.rs` (one INS per command — no cmd_id overhead inside the APDU body, full LC=255 budget per command). Mechanical mapping `INS = 0x80 + (CMD - 100)`. NS-side: 10 extern `nsc_prodtest_*` declarations in `nonsecure/src/nsc_api.rs::transport`, 10 `pub(super)` transport wrappers, 10 `pub fn prodtest_*` public API entries. NS USB dispatcher (`nonsecure/src/usb/commands.rs`): 10 `#[cfg(feature = "prodtest")]` match arms + 10 `cmd_prodtest_*` methods + new `prodtest_finalize(n, status)` helper that writes outputs to `RESP_BUF[..n]` followed by 2-byte SW (SW_OK=0x9000 on Ok, SW_INTERNAL_ERROR=0x6F00 otherwise). USB_LOOPBACK capped at 254 B (255 - 2 B SW headroom inside the 256 B `RESP_BUF`). Nonsecure Cargo gets a new `prodtest = ["stm32u585", "usb"]` feature. Makefile `build-hw-prodtest` updated to pass the new feature to nonsecure crate. (3) **Python host-side HID framing**: `tools/factory-prodtest-runner.py::ProdtestTransport` rewritten from a stub into the production transport — `_build_apdu` (CLA=0xF0 + INS + P1=0 + P2=0 + LC + data; LC ≤ 255 verified pre-build), `_frame_apdu` (chan/tag/seq/total_len header, 57 B first chunk + 59 B continuation chunks, byte-for-byte mirror of `tools/webhid_test.html::frameApdu`), `_read_response` (state machine matching `shared/src/apdu_framing.rs::HidFrameAssembler`'s expected/seq/chan_id reassembly). Linux hidapi quirk handled (advisor): leading 0x00 report-ID byte on `device.write` (kernel hidraw inspects byte 0 as report ID even when the descriptor has none); `device.read` does NOT include the byte so reassembly reads 64-byte frames directly. Status code mapping: SW_OK → STATUS_OK = 0 (so existing test_* wrappers' `status != STATUS_OK` checks Just Work); other SW values pass through unchanged so error logs preserve the wire SW. 60 s read timeout to accommodate BUTTON_TEST's ~30 s budget. INS_FOR_CMD lookup table for cmd→INS translation. **Build validation**: 4 secure-side build paths still clean (default, prodtest+dev-testkey, prodtest+factory-production-irreversible-im-sure, prodtest-no-opt-in→fence fires). New `make build-hw-prodtest` builds both secure + nonsecure cleanly with `--features stm32u585,usb,prodtest` on the NS side. 1847/1847 default-features host tests pass. Python AST clean; `--help` works. Operator manual extended with "Phase E wire format" section documenting APDU + HID framing layout + Linux hidapi quirk. Target NOT silicon-validated by intent — Phase B awaits the user's hardware-on-bench session, which can now actually drive every prodtest CMD over USB rather than being firmware-only. |
| 2026-05-19 | Prodtest firmware — Phase F (operator manual text; photos pending hardware) | Operator-facing documentation polish. Three new sections in `docs/provisioning/factory-prodtest.md`: (1) **Pre-flight checklist** — 5 items the operator runs once at shift start before any units are tested: fixture USB cable golden-unit verify (catches station-side issues vs. unit-under-test), probe-rs flash speed sanity (> 30 s indicates adapter/hub problem), DISPLAY_PATTERN visual-distinguishability under ambient lighting, anti-static wristband continuity check, defective-unit bin labeled (NEVER back on the line). (2) **Troubleshooting matrix** — every per-command failure mode mapped to a likely root cause + operator action: GET_ID UID==0/0xFF → REPORT VENDOR (silicon dead); OPTIGA/SE050 timeout → reseat shield, retry; HANDSHAKE rng==0/0xFF → I²C bus pulled high/low (rewire); USB_LOOPBACK byte-mismatch → if one unit: set aside; if multiple: REPORT VENDOR (likely firmware); BUTTON_TEST 0x12/0x22 → swapped wires (rewire); 0x11/0x21 → re-solder button; 0x31 → operator demo. "REPORT VENDOR" means tag + photograph + send chip UID + lot number to firmware team; don't attempt board-level repair on silicon-defect chips because repair time exceeds BOM. (3) **TODO photo markers** at every visual-aid moment in the manual (`<!-- TODO photo: side-by-side OLED snapshots of each of the 5 patterns ... -->`, `<!-- TODO photo: per-status decision tree as a printable wallchart ... -->`, etc.). Per advisor: markers describe what the photo SHOULD show; they don't paraphrase the visual into prose because (a) the operator's authority is the chip in front of them, not the manual, and (b) descriptions of UI states age into wrong-but-shippable documentation the moment the firmware changes a glyph. Photos land in a follow-up when the user runs a hardware-on-bench session with a USB-C camera. Phase F roadmap entry updated in the operator manual + work-todo: text DONE, photos blocked on bench. No firmware changes. 1847/1847 default-features host tests still pass (text-only change, no impact). |
| 2026-05-19 | §31b — FW-update trusted-UI confirm wired (all 4 fields) | Re-audit on 2026-05-19 corrected the original premise: `secure/src/ui/confirm.rs` was NOT a WIP — it's the production sign-confirm path used by `cmd_sign_userop` / `cmd_sign_userop_batch` / `cmd_sign_offchain`. The "stable again" comment on the `confirm_commit` stub was stale. Wiring the four §31b fields needed building a 4-page `Page` array and calling `ui::confirm::confirm(&pages)`. **Page 1** — title + "to vA.B.C.D" (u32 fw_version decoded as 4 BE bytes per Trezor's MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH.BUILD) + `UPGRADE`/`SAME VERSION`/`DOWNGRADE!` indicator (from `otp::rollback_floor()` vs manifest's fw_version) + "R-tap = next" hint. **Page 2** — new firmware fingerprint, 8 BIP-39 words in 4-row × 2-col layout, format `"1f abcde 5f fghij"`. The `'f'` discriminator next to each digit distinguishes this page from page 3. **Page 3** — signing-key fingerprint, same layout with `'k'` discriminator. `vendor_pubkey::VENDOR_PK_FPR` is pre-computed at build time so runtime is one `hash_to_word_indices` call. **Page 4** — `Confirm update? / R-hold = YES / L-hold = NO / L-tap = review`. 5-char word truncation safe because BIP-39 English wordlist has unique 4-letter prefixes by spec (so 5 chars always disambiguates). Helpers: `build_version_page` / `build_fingerprint_page(words, disc)` / `build_confirm_prompt_page` + local `format_u8_decimal` (14 lines, kept local to avoid pulling in the EIP-712 cowswap_display module). Updated host test `positive_confirm_commit_calls_trusted_ui_outside_e2e_test` pins the new behavior: each of the four page builders must be referenced AND `rollback_floor()` consulted AND the final `matches!(confirm(&pages), ConfirmResult::Confirmed)` pattern present. Replaces the old `negative_confirm_commit_defaults_to_user_cancel_outside_e2e_test` test. Build paths: `make build-hw-prodtest` clean; production HW path (`stm32u585+dual-se+ui-oled+saes-dhuk+hw-sha256`) clean; e2e-test path clean. 1847/1847 host tests pass. Together with §31a item 1 (channel-ID regression-test) this closes the Trezor-derived hardening review — §31c documents the 4 items rejected after audit with rationale. |
| 2026-05-14 | Phase 2 — host ERC-7730 IR compiler + Merkle DB + xtask + policy enforcement | EF launched Clear Signing on 2026-05-12 (`clearsigning.org`); Phase 1 (`d6beefb`) shipped the on-device `pqsigner-erc7730` crate (IR parser + bundle verifier + binding cross-check). Phase 2 lands the **host build pipeline** so the firmware-pinned `ERC7730_DESCRIPTORS_ROOT` is generated from a curated descriptor catalogue that has cleared the ERC-8176 attestation policy at compile time — preserving invariant #5 (no classical signer anywhere). **`dbgen/src/erc7730.rs`** (~1300 lines, pure-Rust): parses ERC-7730 v2 JSON (drops the deprecated `abi` section, derives parameter names from format keys like `"transfer(address _to, uint256 _value)"`); JCS-canonicalises (RFC 8785 subset — integers + strings, the only types the registry uses) to compute the 32-byte `descriptor_hash` baked into IR offset 30..62; enforces `policy.toml` (`min_attesters` / `trusted_attesters` / `allow_unattested_dev_descriptors`); compiles every format into the 134-byte BE IR header + metadata pool + formats table that `pqsigner_erc7730::ir::Erc7730Ir::parse` accepts byte-for-byte; emits one IR per `(chainId, contract)` deployment so multi-chain descriptors (USDC's 6 chains, USDT's 2, WETH's 2) expand to 20 Merkle leaves from 8 source files; computes EIP-712 `domainSeparator` per deployment via the present-field-only typehash rule; interns repeated paths/params via a per-descriptor pool cache; truncates non-ASCII owner/contractName to ≤15 ASCII per the on-device parser's anti-spoof rule. Catalog blob format: 32 B LE header (magic=`"P730"` / version 1 / entry_cnt / ir_pool_off / ir_pool_size / proof_depth / proofs_off), 72 B per entry (chain_id_le / contract / primary_type_hash / context_kind / ir_off / ir_len), IR pool, per-entry Merkle proofs. **Seed corpus** (`secure/data/erc7730/*.json`): 8 self-contained registry pulls from `ethereum/clear-signing-erc7730-registry` (tether-usdt, weth, lido-stETH, opensea-wyvern Wyvern Exchange, circle-usdc-twa/rwa, aave-v3-pool trimmed to supply+withdraw+supplyWithPermit, uniswap-v3-router trimmed to exactInputSingle) → 20 leaves, prod root `0x4b8adbb75193a7fd5fe15581cb17f5d016015a89e6ff8d2f52f58f493a7e8ff3`. `includes` resolution explicitly rejected for Phase 2 (Phase 3 wires the registry-mirror submodule for ERC-2612 permit templates). **`secure/data/erc7730/policy.toml`**: dev mode (`allow_unattested_dev_descriptors = true`) for the hand-pulled seed corpus; CI must gate production on `false` once the registry-mirror submodule lands (same pattern as `e2e-test` / `otp-hardcoded-master-key`). E2E variant (`secure/data/erc7730-e2e/`) with WETH + USDT only, gated to `ERC7730_DESCRIPTORS_ROOT` under `#[cfg(feature = "e2e-test")]` (mirror of the existing `SELECTOR_DB_ROOT` pattern). **dbgen restructure**: split into `dbgen/src/lib.rs` (public module exports + record types + loaders + helpers) + `dbgen/src/main.rs` (CLI wrapper) so integration tests in `dbgen/tests/` can reach `dbgen::erc7730::*` directly. **`xtask` subcommand `gen-erc7730-descriptors [--check]`**: re-uses the dbgen library to build prod + e2e in-memory and diff against checked-in artifacts (the binary, the review file, the root constant block in `secure/src/db_roots.rs`); same CI gate shape as `gen-solidity-constants --check`. **Round-trip integration test** (`dbgen/tests/erc7730_roundtrip.rs`, 5 tests): builds the seed corpus, synthesises the wire-format bundle the companion would ship, hands it to `pqsigner_erc7730::bundle::verify_erc7730_bundle` byte-for-byte, then runs `cross_check_contract` / `cross_check_eip712` against the IR's `(chain_id, contract, domain_separator)`. Negative tests cover wrong root, tampered proof, chain_id flip, contract flip. **Makefile**: `make test-unit` now runs `cargo test -p pqsigner-erc7730 --lib` + `cargo test -p dbgen --test erc7730_roundtrip`; new `check-erc7730-descriptors` target wraps `pqsigner-xtask -- gen-erc7730-descriptors --check`, aggregated under `make check-codegen`. **Artifacts emitted by `cargo run -p dbgen`**: `tools/companion-stub/erc7730_db.bin` (10,919 B, 20 leaves), `tools/companion-stub/erc7730_db_e2e.bin` (1,444 B, 4 leaves), `secure/data/erc7730.review.txt` (vendor-readable per-leaf summary), `secure/src/db_roots.rs` (now also carries `ERC7730_DESCRIPTORS_ROOT` cfg-gated for prod/e2e). **All gates green**: `cargo test -p pqsigner-erc7730` 24/24, `cargo test -p dbgen` 15 lib + 5 integration = 20/20, `cargo test -p sphincs-tz-secure --tests` 118/118 (no regression), `cargo build -p pqsigner-erc7730 --target thumbv8m.main-none-eabi` clean, `xtask gen-erc7730-descriptors --check` reports `erc7730: in sync` and `xtask gen-solidity-constants --check` still works. Pool TLV constants for path opcodes (0x10..0x24), format opcodes (0x01..0x0E), and formatter params (0x30..0x3F including `Visibility=0x3F`) are now stable wire constants — the Phase 5 walker MUST not renumber them. Open items intentionally left for later phases: (a) full `includes` resolution for upstream-registry ingestion (Phase 3), (b) widening the EIP-712 selector slot to a full typehash via a discriminator byte (Phase 3+), (c) the `clearsig` Python parity test (`tools/cross_parity_erc7730.py`) — recommended in handoff but punted because the host pipeline is pure Rust and self-validating via the round-trip test, (d) flipping `allow_unattested_dev_descriptors` to `false` once the registry-mirror submodule + real attestation chain land. Files: `pqsigner-erc7730/src/{ir,bundle,binding,walker,abi,lib}.rs` (already shipped in Phase 1, unchanged), `dbgen/{Cargo.toml,src/lib.rs,src/main.rs,src/erc7730.rs,tests/erc7730_roundtrip.rs}`, `xtask/{Cargo.toml,src/main.rs}`, `secure/data/erc7730/{policy.toml,*.json}`, `secure/data/erc7730-e2e/{tether-usdt,weth}.json`, `secure/data/erc7730.review.txt`, `secure/src/db_roots.rs`, `tools/companion-stub/erc7730_db{,_e2e}.bin`, `Makefile`, `docs/work-todo.md`. |
| 2026-05-14 | Phase 4 — ERC-7730 display renderer + ERC-8213 fingerprint pages + EIP-712 typed offchain sign completion | Closes the loop between Phase 3's wire format (`5f12728`) and the trusted UI: descriptor-driven pages for contract-context UserOps + offchain typed-data signs, plus an ERC-8213 calldata-digest / EIP-712-final-hash fingerprint page appended to every sign flow so users can cross-check off-device. **`tx-core/src/erc8213.rs`** (new, +8 tests): streaming-Keccak `calldata_digest(data) = keccak256(uint256(len) || data)` and `eip712_final_hash(ds, sh) = keccak256(0x1901 \|\| ds \|\| sh)`. No 4 KiB staging buffer; chained `Keccak256::update` so a 4 KiB calldata blob does not balloon stack. `tx-core/src/lib.rs` + `secure/src/tx/mod.rs` re-export shims keep the call path uniform. **`secure/src/tx/erc7730_render/{mod,params,visibility}.rs`** (new, pure logic, host-test-reachable): `RenderErr::{Reject(&'static str), NoFormat, PageBudget}`; TLV parameter parser for tags 0x30..0x3F (token_path / token / threshold / message / addr_types / addr_sources / date_encoding / enum_ref / decimals / base / prefix / suffix / nested_selector / nested_callee / fallback_label / visibility) including visibility-value-list sub-TLV (Phase 5 forward-compat); `should_render(params, resolved) -> Action::{Render \| Skip \| Reject}` with the locked Phase 4 reading of the 5 Visibility variants: Always/Optional → render, Never → skip-without-walking, IfNotIn → render (no value-list in current `dbgen` wire), MustMatch → `Reject("7730 must-match unsupported")` so the caller falls through to blind-sign rather than silently render a rule it cannot enforce. **`secure/src/tx/display/erc7730/{mod,formatters,intent,calldata_nested}.rs`** (new): `render_erc7730_pages(tx, inner_data, &VerifiedDescriptor, Option<&Erc20Metadata>, &NameResolver) -> Result<Pages, RenderErr>` for contract context; `render_erc7730_eip712_pages(chain_id, &verifying_contract, &primary_type_hash, encoded_data, &VerifiedDescriptor, Option<&Erc20Metadata>, &NameResolver)` for EIP-712 typed-data. 14 `FormatOp` dispatchers (0x01 Raw → 0x0E Encrypted) — Amount uses `write_amount_two_rows` with `params.decimals` (default 18, params.base default "ETH"), TokenAmount matches `params.token_path` / `params.token` against the supplied `Erc20Metadata` for decimals + ticker (falls back to "???" if no match), AddressName via `write_addr_full_or_name`, Date renders UTC `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS` (Howard Hinnant civil-from-days), Duration as `Xd Yh Zm Ws`, ChainId via `chain_name(chain_id)`, InteroperableAddressName as ERC-3770 long-form `eip155:<chainId>:0x<addr>` (chain short-name registry is Phase 5+ scope), Encrypted shows `params.fallback_label` only (no FHEVM on-device). Path resolution is intentionally a direct walker (sum FieldIdx → byte-slot offset → 32 B word from `inner_data[4..]`) rather than threading `pqsigner_erc7730::walker::resolve_path` — Phase 3's walker needs an `AbiView` tree and the on-device IR does not yet carry ABI type info; the direct walker is correct for the static-type seed corpus and is replaced by a full AbiView build in Phase 5 if dynamic types land. **`secure/src/tx/display/erc8213.rs`** (new): `Kind::{CalldataDigest, Eip712Final, Raw32, SafeTxHash}([u8;32])` + 2-page `append_fingerprint_page` (banner + 32-byte hash split 4 rows × 8 hex bytes per row — full digest, no truncation, vs the existing 14-hex-truncated `write_calldata_hash_rows`). **`secure/src/tx/display/mod.rs:172-238`** — `pick_sign_pages` grows an `erc7730: Option<&VerifiedDescriptor<'_>>` parameter between `safe_v1` and `erc20`; new rung between Safe-V1 (#3) and the plain-ETH check (#4) returns the ERC-7730 pages on success, surfaces a `ui::show_status("Sign", msg)` banner on `RenderErr::Reject`, and silently falls through on `NoFormat` / `PageBudget` to the existing ladder. `Pages` gains a `push_blank() -> Result<usize, ()>` method so the dynamic-field renderer doesn't pre-allocate. **`secure/src/nsc/cmd_sign_userop.rs:449-501, 791-811`**: `erc7730_verified.as_ref()` flows into `pick_sign_pages`; the Phase 3 `let _ = &erc7730_verified;` sink is deleted; `Kind::CalldataDigest(calldata_digest(inner_data))` fingerprint appended before `confirm`. **`secure/src/nsc/cmd_sign_offchain.rs:263-441`**: the Phase 3 `"phase 4 needed"` bail for `OFFCHAIN_KIND_EIP712_TYPED = 2` becomes the full sign pipeline — compute `final_eip712 = eip712_final_hash(ds, keccak256(typehash \|\| encoded_data))`, derive `wallet_addr = proxy_address(bootstrap_pubkey)`, wrap via `personal_sign_replay_safe_hash(chain_id, &wallet_addr, &final_eip712)` (passes the 32-byte EIP-712 hash AS a personal-sign message — Solady's `ERC1271` PersonalSign branch matches because our `SignatureWrapper` carries no appended data per `aa/src/eip1271.rs:5-8` + `contracts/smart-wallet/src/PQSmartWallet.sol:362-395`; NO new typehash, NO on-chain change), render via the descriptor, append `Kind::Eip712Final(final_eip712)` fingerprint, confirm, then join the shared §10-14 sign path. The PERSONAL_SIGN and RAW32 paths get fingerprint pages too (PersonalSign carries `Kind::CalldataDigest(calldata_digest(payload))` so the user sees a hash they can `cast keccak`; RAW32 carries `Kind::Raw32(hash_to_sign)`). **`secure/src/nsc/cmd_sign_userop_batch.rs:280-405`**: per-tx descriptor routing — `erc7730_for_this_tx = erc7730_verified.as_ref().filter(|v| ptx.to == v.ir.contract)` so a batch's one verified descriptor only renders the inner tx it actually matches; other inner txs use the legacy typed-call / ERC-20 / blind-sign ladder. Per-tx `Kind::CalldataDigest` fingerprint after `wrap_pages_with_batch_banner`, plus a batch-final `Kind::Raw32(keccak256(concat(per_tx_digests)))` appended to `build_final_summary_pages` — a single hash binds the entire bundle so users can cross-check the whole batch off-device. The Phase 3 dead `let _ = &erc7730_verified;` sink at line 282 is deleted. **`dbgen/tests/erc7730_roundtrip.rs`**: new `seed_corpus_param_tlv_blobs_are_well_formed` walks every IR's formats table and validates every field's `param_off` TLV blob from the host-emitter side — confirms `blob_len` matches the sum of inner TLVs, every tag is in 0x30..=0x3F, and fixed-width tags carry the documented payload size (TOKEN=20, THRESHOLD=32, ENUM_REF=2, NESTED_SELECTOR=4, NESTED_CALLEE=20). 8 → **9** integration tests. **Phase 4 architectural decisions documented in `~/.claude/plans/fully-implement-phase-4-nested-puppy.md`**: (1) MustMatch failure → fall-through-to-blind-sign with status banner (conservative reading of "descriptor is invalid for this transaction"); (2) Batch mode → render-the-match + blind-sign-the-rest (multi-descriptor batches are a Phase 5 wire-format expansion); (3) Encrypted (0x0E) → fallback label only, no FHEVM on-device; (4) InteroperableAddressName (0x0D) → long form `eip155:<chainId>:0x<addr>` (chain short-name registry is Phase 5+); (5) EIP-712 typed sign-envelope → reuse `personal_sign_replay_safe_hash` with the 32-byte final hash AS a message (no new typehash); (6) Phase 4 goldens → hand-pinned where present; Cyfrin `clearsig` Python parity is Phase 5; (7) Optional visibility → collapsed to Always for Phase 4 (compact-mode toggle in Phase 5). **Verification status**: `cargo test -p pqsigner-erc7730 --tests` 41/41 (unchanged), `cargo test -p pqsigner-tx-core --tests` 23 → **31** (+8 erc8213), `cargo test -p dbgen --test erc7730_roundtrip` 8 → **9** (+1 TLV-well-formed), `cargo test -p sphincs-tz-secure --tests --no-default-features --features mock-se,debug-log,ui-semihosting` 118 → **137** (+9 params + 5 visibility + 5 deep params tests), thumbv8m cross-build clean for both `--features mock-se,debug-log,ui-semihosting` and `--features dual-se,ui-oled,stm32u585,debug-log`, `xtask gen-erc7730-descriptors --check` reports `erc7730: in sync`, `xtask gen-solidity-constants --check` clean. `make e2e` not re-run interactively this session (QEMU sign latency per the 2026-05-12/13 entries); Phase 3's Scenario 5m still verifies the bundle and now drives the full Phase 4 render path through QEMU when CI runs the smoke. **Punted to Phase 5**: `erc7730-dev-unattested` Cargo feature, registry-mirror submodule + `allow_unattested_dev_descriptors = false` flip, Cyfrin `clearsig` parity scripts (`tools/cross_parity_erc7730.py` / `tools/cross_parity_erc8213.py`), FI-hardened cross-check pairs on every binding via `crate::fi::check_true_into_sentinel`, compact-mode display toggle (uses `Optional` visibility distinction), HW-bench target `make e2e-erc7730-hw`, sign-time benchmark assertion, `docs/companion/erc7730-integration.md` + `docs/companion/erc8213-fingerprints.md` + README/CLAUDE.md updates, `tests/ui_fixtures_erc7730.json` ui-capture goldens, fuzz harness extension for `params::parse` (requires moving `params`/`visibility` into `pqsigner-erc7730` so fuzz can reach them), nested-calldata recursion (Phase 4 stub rejects → falls through to blind-sign; Safe v1 paths use the pre-existing `safe_display` renderer), full `AbiView` build for dynamic types + Uniswap-style deep tuples (Phase 4 ships static-only path-walker, which covers the simple seed-corpus cases like USDT.transfer / WETH.deposit / USDC.transfer). | 2026-05-20 | ERC-7730 `path_off=0` collision fix + `approve(MAX)` "unlimited" rendering + companion docs refresh | Post-merge follow-up on the ERC-7730 PR. The Phase 5 host render tests (`secure/src/display_under_test/erc7730_render_pure_tests.rs`) documented a latent bug: `dbgen::erc7730::Pool::new` started empty, so the first interned path program landed at pool offset 0, colliding with the on-device walker's `path_off == 0` "no path" sentinel (`pqsigner_erc7730::walker::path_bytes`) and the param parser's `param_off == 0` "default params" sentinel (`secure/src/tx/erc7730_render::params::parse`). Every descriptor whose first interned program ended up at offset 0 silently fell through to blind-sign with a `"7730 missing path"` banner — affecting weth.deposit, tether-usdt.transfer/approve, every aave-v3-pool.* and every circle-usdc-*. The bug rode three `#[should_panic(expected = "7730 missing path")]` markers in the host render tests so the tree stayed green. **Fix** (PR #2, commit `eef09386`): `Pool::new` now pushes a 1-byte `0xFF` filler — matches the convention already documented in `walker::tests::path_bytes_reads_prefix`. Catalog IR pool grows by 1 byte per descriptor. **Root rotation**: prod `ERC7730_DESCRIPTORS_ROOT` `0x4b8a…8ff3` → `0x650d…dc19`; e2e `0x4324…c15f` → `0xcef0…9934`. Companion blob: `tools/companion-stub/erc7730_db.bin` 10919 → 10939 B, `_e2e.bin` 1444 → 1448 B. **Bonus UX**: `render_token_amount` now honors `params.threshold` — when on-chain `value >= threshold` the renderer writes `"unlimited <ticker>"` on row 1 instead of running the digit formatter. ERC-20 `approve(uint256.MAX)` against USDT now displays `"unlimited USDT"` instead of the previous `"!AMOUNT OVERFLOW"` fallback. BE byte-array `>=` matches numeric u256 comparison so the check is a direct array compare. **No wire format change** — descriptor's existing `threshold` TLV (tag `0x32`) drives this; companions don't need to change anything beyond refreshing the blob. **Test surface**: three `#[should_panic]` markers removed; the USDT-transfer / USDT-approve-unlimited / WETH-deposit tests now pass real assertions. `positive_usdt_approve_unlimited_renders_approve_intent` additionally pins "USDT" appearing next to "unlimited" and asserts no `AMOUNT OVERFLOW` leak through the threshold short-circuit. `positive_weth_deposit_pulls_value_from_envelope` relaxes the amount assertion to accept either single-row "0.5" or `write_amount_two_rows`'s split "0" / ".5 ETH" rendering. 1890/1890 secure host tests + 41 erc7730 + 27 tx-core --tests + 5 proto + 9 dbgen roundtrip pass; thumbv8m cross-build clean on `mock-se,debug-log,ui-semihosting` and `dual-se,ui-oled,stm32u585,debug-log` profiles; `xtask gen-erc7730-descriptors --check` in sync. **Docs refresh**: `docs/companion/companion-erc7730-implementation-guide.md` §7.2 (expected display now reads `"unlimited USDT"`), §10 (current pinned roots table + catalog byte counts), §12.1 (rewritten from known-bug to "fixed — companion must refresh blob + re-pin root"); `docs/companion/erc7730-integration.md` (pool offset 0 reservation note + `path_off`/`param_off` sentinel semantics inline + TokenAmount threshold behaviour). Files: `dbgen/src/erc7730.rs`, `secure/src/tx/display/erc7730/formatters.rs`, `secure/src/display_under_test/erc7730_render_pure_tests.rs`, `secure/data/erc7730.review.txt`, `secure/src/db_roots.rs`, `tools/companion-stub/erc7730_db{,_e2e}.bin`, `docs/companion/companion-erc7730-implementation-guide.md`, `docs/companion/erc7730-integration.md`, `docs/work-todo.md`. |

| 2026-05-20 | §18 P1 — entry jitter at the PIN gate (random delays before security-critical ops) | Added `crate::fi::wait_random()` at the entry of `nsc::gated_unlock` and `cmd_request_unlock::verify_pin_with_chip`. Advisor-scoped to ONLY these two — not the sign path (F-16's 43!×13! WOTS/FORS shuffle already jitters the secret-dependent timeline) and not an always-on RDI timer like Trezor's (`systimer`-driven `rdi_handler` burning random cycles in an ISR would land mid-USB-bulk-transfer / mid-SE050-T=1' / mid-OPTIGA-Shielded-handshake; Trezor's own `rdi_disabled = sectrue` default + enable/disable scaffolding exist because they hit exactly those intermittent-stall bugs — not worth the risk before on-silicon SCA shows the gap matters). The PIN gate is the right target: linear from external trigger (USB `CMD_REQUEST_UNLOCK` dispatch / boot-unlock / PendSV re-unlock) straight to the F-15 sentinel with no internal shuffle, so a Masaryk-thesis-class profiled single-fault attacker (~76% on STM32U5) lands a glitch at a FIXED offset from the trigger. Entry jitter desyncs that offset by 0..255 `wait_random_loop` iterations ≈ 0..19 µs at 160 MHz. **Honest threat bound documented in-source + work-todo**: meaningful window against an uncalibrated single-shot attacker; does NOT defeat a profile-then-attack rig with multi-attempt statistical recovery — the F-15 sentinel-encoded gate + F-17 rate limiter stay load-bearing. **Assembly-verified** on the linked release ELF (`arm-none-eabi-objdump -d`): a real `bl c00599e <...fi11wait_random>` exists in both functions, so a glitch that skips the call skips only the jitter, not the gate (no LLVM prologue-folding — `wait_random` and `wait_random_loop` are both `#[inline(never)]`). Two source-grep regression tests (`positive_gated_unlock_has_entry_jitter_before_the_gate`, `positive_verify_pin_with_chip_has_entry_jitter_before_gated_unlock`) pin that the entry `wait_random()` PRECEDES the gate it desyncs (before first `pin_attempts_read` / before the `gated_unlock` call) — a refactor that moved the jitter after the gate would trail the thing it's meant to protect and fail loudly. Platform-agnostic (outside the `#[cfg(stm32u585)]` block), no-op under e2e-test so test timings don't drift. 1892/1892 host tests pass; stm32u585+dual-se+ui-oled+dev-testkey release + QEMU mock-se+ui-noop both build clean. RDI-timer path stays open as a follow-up. |
| 2026-05-20 | §18 SHAKE-vs-SHA2 #2 — masked-SHA2 cost bench harness | New `masked-sha2` workspace crate (first-order 2-share boolean-masked building blocks: ISW masked AND `sec_and` + Kogge-Stone secure boolean adder `sec_add`, generic over an RNG closure, pure no_std) + `secure/src/bench_masked_sha.rs` DWT-timed bench behind the `bench-masked-sha` feature + `make bench-masked-sha-hw` target. Advisor-reviewed approach: measure the two cost-dominating masked gates and PROJECT the per-block masked-SHA-256 cost from SHA-256's fixed structure (ADDS_PER_BLOCK=600, ANDS_PER_BLOCK=320) rather than building a full (easy-to-get-subtly-wrong) masked SHA-256. The projection is algebra, not guesswork, because SHA-256 is a fixed-structure standard. 7 host correctness oracles prove the gates correct (sec_and==AND / sec_add==wrapping_add over 50k random inputs each + full-ripple carry edge cases + Ch/Maj composition over 20k) — the Kogge-Stone indexing is verified, not assumed. Bench measures one-compression (55-byte single-block input) of HASH peripheral + sha2 software baselines, plus sec_and/sec_add BOTH ways: TRNG-inline (conservative, masks straight from hardware TRNG) and rand-pre-drawn (pure gate logic ≈ DRBG-fed production lower bound). Prints projected masked-block cycles/µs + slowdown ratios. Decision rule (advisor): masked/HW ≲3× → SHA2 stays viable + SHAKE drops priority; ≳5-10× → SHAKE arm worth building. Build profile `bench-masked-sha,debug-log,ui-noop,e2e-test,mock-se` (e2e-test escapes the production fence + permits mock-se; bench short-circuits at boot before any SE access). masked-sha2 dep is `optional = true` / `dep:` gated so default + production builds don't link it. 7/7 masked-sha2 host tests pass; 1892/1892 secure host tests unaffected; bench firmware + normal hardware build paths both clean. NOT yet run on silicon — harness is ready, user runs `make bench-masked-sha-hw` on the bench and records the numbers in §18. |
| 2026-05-20 | §18 SHAKE-vs-SHA2 #2 — masked-SHA2 cost MEASURED on silicon | Ran `make bench-masked-sha-hw` on STM32U585 @ 160 MHz (20k iters/measurement). HASH peripheral 1-block = 485 cyc; sha2 software = 4265 cyc; TRNG word = 214 cyc; sec_and = 22/214 cyc (gate/TRNG-inline); sec_add = 213/2147 cyc. Projected masked SHA-256 block = 134,840 cyc (~842 µs) DRBG-floor / 1,356,680 cyc (~8.5 ms) raw-TRNG → ~278× to ~2797× the HASH peripheral. VERDICT: masking the full SHA-2 hot path is infeasible (1-2 orders past the 5-10× decision line). Takeaways: (1) TRNG draw dominates → a DRBG is mandatory for any masking (~10× win); (2) de-prioritizes the SHAKE migration — even ~5× cheaper Keccak masking lands at ~56×, still infeasible, and there's no SHAKE peripheral; (3) the viable SCA path is "mask only the secret-touching hash subset (sk_seed PRF + first hash of each WOTS/FORS secret chain), not everything" + existing F-16 shuffle/consumption-mask/RDI, driven by sca-trigger on-silicon leakage measurement. Next step shifts from "build the SHAKE #3 benchmark" to "count secret-touching hashes/sign + leakage-characterize just those." Bench infra fix during the run: dropped debug-log from the make target (hw::rng::fill secure_log!s per call → semihosting flood under the TRNG-heavy bench; results print via unconditional hprintln! which works under probe-rs regardless). |
| 2026-05-20 | §18 SCA step (a) — secret-touching hash count: 70,754/sign (corrects my ~200 by 350×) | Added `hash-counters` feature to sphincs-c10 + `tests/hash_counts.rs`; one C10 sign = 44,118 wots_secret + 26,636 fors_secret = 70,754 sk_seed-touching PRF calls (13.1% of 539,616 total). My prior-turn "~200, mask the subset, ~1 s" was WRONG by ~350× — SLH-DSA regenerates every leaf of every signed subtree (auth path) + every FORS leaf, each a PRF(sk_seed, ADRS). CONSEQUENCE: masking the subset ≈ 70,754 × 134,840 cyc ≈ ~60 s/sign — infeasible, same wall as masking everything. (c) "mask the subset" is DEAD at every granularity. Midstate caching can't rescue it (sk_seed is a 32-byte non-block-aligned prefix; rounds 8..63 still secret-derived, ~14% saving only). F-16 shuffle is only partial (sk_seed at same intra-call offset → horizontal averaging ignores order). REFRAMED forward path: (b) is now a standalone residual-characterization measurement (does the PRF leak sk_seed on this silicon with F-16+consumption-mask+noise? → TVLA max|t| via sca-trigger; parked with RDI until an SCA-capture bench session) — NOT a precursor to (c). v2 fix recorded: PRF restructure padding sk_seed to a full 64-byte first block → 1 masked compression/sign instead of 70,754, but it re-bases all keys/addresses (violates launch invariant #6) so it's a v2/KDF-revision item. Instrumentation is feature-gated + zero-cost off; default tests + production HW build unaffected. Commit 354af1b. |

| 2026-05-20 | §18 SHAKE-vs-SHA2 architectural decision — CLOSED (stay on SHA-2) | Formally closed the long-deferred parameter-set fork (was the last open P1 in §18). Decision: stay on SHA-2, SHAKE rejected. Rationale: (1) masking infeasible on either hash — on-silicon masked-SHA2 = 278–2800× the HASH peripheral, and 70,754 secret PRF calls/sign make masking even the secret subset ≈ 60 s/sign; (2) STM32U5 HASH peripheral accelerates SHA-1/SHA-2 only, NOT SHA-3/Keccak/SHAKE (RM0456) — so SHAKE forfeits the 485-cyc/block HW path and is a net slowdown even unmasked, and masked-SHAKE is still ~70k software permutations/sign; (3) the only pro-SHAKE argument (Fluhrer ePrint 2024/500 "1.7× PRF-tree") is unverifiable/implausible; (4) a parameter switch re-derives every key, moving the CREATE2 address (frozen-wallet invariant #6) → launch-breaking. SCA posture stays SHA-2 + the landed F-13/F-16/F-17 + consumption-mask + multi-source OptRand; the v2 PRF restructure (pad sk_seed to a full first block) is the SHA-2-preserving KDF-revision escape hatch if step-(b) leakage measurement ever shows an exploitable leak. No code change — decision + doc closure (user-confirmed conclusion from the masked-sha2 silicon round). |
| 2026-05-20 | Invariant #4 GTZC/TZSC enforcement — SILICON-VALIDATED | `make gtzc-enforcement-hw` PASSED on real B-U585I-IOT02A. NS probed all 7 peripherals the secure boot marks SECURE in `sau.rs` (I2C1@0x40005400, I2C2@0x40005800, AES@0x420c0000, HASH@0x420c0400, RNG@0x420c0800, PKA@0x420c2000, SAES@0x420c0c00) via their NS-aliased control registers; every access RAZ-returned 0x00000000 AND raised the GTZC violation IRQ, bumping `hw::tzic::VIOLATION_COUNT` by exactly 1 per probe (baseline 0 → final 7, delta 7, expected 7) → `[NS][gtzc] === PASS ===`. Proves the `GTZC1_TZSC_SECCFGR{1,3}` fix actually enforces on silicon (the CRIT-4 regression's designed-fix was QEMU-only until now). Non-destructive (mock-se, no SE writes, no PIN attempts); all changes runtime/reversible (no RDP2/WRP burn; TZEN/SECWM already set). Closes the "NS read of RNG_DR bus-faults" half of the CLAUDE.md TZSC caveat. Remaining: assert "USB still enumerates" under the same GTZC config (OTG stays NS — covered in practice by USB HID builds), + TAMP (GTZC2) follow-up. |
| 2026-05-20 | USB-C device enumeration fixed + validated on silicon | While closing the TZSC caveat's "USB still enumerates" half, found the device wouldn't enumerate over USB-C-to-USB-C and root-caused two real `secure/src/hw/usb_hw.rs::init_ucpd` register bugs (confirmed vs ST's authoritative LL driver + CMSIS): (1) it set `UCPD_CR.CC1TCDIS/CC2TCDIS=1` thinking they disable dead-battery, but those are the Type-C voltage *detector* disables (`LL_UCPD_TypeCDetectionCC1Disable()=SET_BIT(CC1TCDIS)`) → blinded the CC detectors → `UCPD_SR.TYPEC_VSTATE` stuck at 0; (2) it never disabled the actual dead-battery, which on STM32U5 is `PWR_UCPDR.UCPD_DBDIS` bit 0 (`LL_PWR_DisableUCPDDeadBattery()`), leaving the dead-battery Rd in parallel with the UCPD Rd → shifted CC voltage. Fix: leave detectors enabled (drop CCxTCDIS) + `PWR->UCPDR |= UCPD_DBDIS`. The CC pins (PA15/PB15) were CORRECT (verified vs Zephyr hal_stm32 STM32U585AII pinctrl; the "PB6/PB7" code comments were a wrong cross-family value). Validated on real B-U585I: enumerates as `1209:7051 "Generic PQSigner OS"` in ~1 s over USB-C (5V jumper on 5V_UCPD). Methodology gotcha that cost time: `probe-rs reset` halts the core on this setup (UCPD_CR read back 0 = unconfigured = firmware never ran) — runtime/USB tests must use `probe-rs run` or a power-cycle. Commit b325dd8. Ship cable is USB-C↔USB-C, so this was a real launch blocker. |
| 2026-05-20 | Warm-reset follow-up — CHARACTERIZED (firmware re-init OK; USB-C-power-bootstrap edge only) | Localized the warm-reset non-recovery. Test: with the board on STABLE power (5V_STLK jumper) + USB-C connected, an NRST press → device drops → RE-ENUMERATES (`1209:7051`, fresh device number) in ~10 s. So **firmware re-init after a reset works whenever power is held — it is NOT a firmware bug.** The earlier 5V_UCPD non-recovery is purely the USB-C-*sole-power* re-bootstrap: an NRST cuts VBUS → board loses power, and that specific transient gets stuck (a clean unplug/replug = full POR → dead-battery → boot always works). Implication for the shipped (USB-C-only-powered) device: cold-plug ✅, brownout (full power loss → POR → dead-battery) should recover, watchdog reset (host holds VBUS through the brief reset → firmware re-runs) should recover (matches the 5V_STLK+NRST result); only the artificial "NRST while USB-C-sole-powered" transient got stuck, recoverable by replug. Severity downgraded to LOW. Remaining to 100%-confirm watchdog/brownout recovery: an LA capture of VBUS+CC1/CC2 through a reset cycle — DEFERRED (probing the CC lines is involved; replug always recovers). The DBDIS-persists-across-NRST hypothesis was disproven (PWR_UCPDR resets to 0). |
| 2026-05-20 | USB-C standalone enumeration CONFIRMED + warm-reset follow-up | Definitive standalone validation: with the board self-powered from USB-C (5V_UCPD jumper) and NO debugger attached, an unplug/replug of the USB-C-to-USB-C cable cold-starts the board and enumerates it fresh as `1209:7051 "Generic PQSigner OS"` (new device number each plug) — the exact shipped scenario. So the init_ucpd fix (commit b325dd8) works end-to-end on real hardware. **Follow-up (robustness, non-blocking):** a WARM reset (NRST button / watchdog / brownout) *while* USB-C-powered did NOT re-enumerate until a physical replug — likely because `PWR_UCPDR.UCPD_DBDIS` (the dead-battery disable we set in firmware) persists across NRST, so post-warm-reset there's no passive dead-battery Rd to re-bootstrap VBUS (and the board, powered only by USB-C, can't present UCPD Rd until it has power → deadlock until unplug). Recoverable by unplug/replug. Harden by re-enabling dead-battery on the reset path (or confirm DBDIS reset-domain behavior); matters for brownout/watchdog resilience on a device whose only power is the USB-C cable. Tracked for a future session. |

| 2026-05-28 | FSBL-rooted firmware fingerprint (manufacturer-trust elimination) | Closed the self-attested-measurement gap: the 8-BIP-39-word "OS Fingerprint" is now rendered by the WRP1A-locked FSBL *before* it branches into the slot, so a malicious update can no longer forge the words on the OLED. Implementation: (1) `bip39` crate gains a build-script-generated `WORDLIST_PREFIX5: [[u8; 5]; 2048]` (~10 KB) and a `firmware_fingerprint_lines(digest) → [[u8; 16]; 4]` pure function shared between FSBL and `secure/src/measured_boot.rs`; the heavy 18 KB `WORDLIST_FLAT` / `Mnemonic` / PBKDF2 / CT exact-lookup surface is now gated behind the `full-wordlist` feature (on by default for secure-world, off for FSBL). (2) `fsbl/src/verify.rs::verify_images` returns `Option<[u8; 32]>` (the verified secure-image digest) so the FSBL render path is driven by the same trusted bytes FSBL just SHA-256'd. (3) New `fsbl/src/oled.rs` + `glyphs.rs` ports the SSD1306 + I2C1 driver + the 5×8 ASCII font (vendored from `secure/assets/font_5x8.raw`) without any embedded-graphics / secure_log / hw::mmio dep — ~3.5 KB compiled. (4) `fsbl/src/main.rs` calls `render::render_fingerprint(&secure_digest)` after slot selection and before `branch::into_slot`. FSBL release footprint = 30,888 / 32 KB (1,880 B headroom). Tests: 12 in `bip39/tests/prefix5_roundtrip.rs` (prefix table integrity + fingerprint_lines layout pins), 1 in `fsbl-tests/tests/footprint.rs` (CI gate ≤ 32 KB), 6 in `fsbl-tests/tests/source_invariants.rs` (verify_images returns digest, FSBL renders before branch, no-OLED graceful fallback, measured_boot self-attests, render uses bip39's pure fn), 3 in `fwmeasure/tests/byte_identity.rs` (host fwmeasure output matches FSBL render for same digest), 3 `#[ignore]`d QEMU integration placeholders documenting future empirical proofs. Docs: new `docs/security/measured-boot.md` (threat model + trust chain + user workflow), updates to `docs/firmware/firmware-update.md`, `docs/firmware/reproducible-builds.md`, `CLAUDE.md` Lifecycle. Threat model: defends post-provisioning (any update can't fake the words); does NOT defend factory-time integrity (malicious vendor at provisioning) — the user must compare the very first boot's words to `./measure.sh` to establish the baseline. |
| 2026-05-28 | USB-C warm-reset re-enumeration SOLVED in firmware (cc_open_then_reset) | The warm-reset non-recovery (a firmware `sys_reset` over USB-C didn't re-enumerate without a physical replug, because VBUS stays asserted by the host so Linux's typec layer keeps the port bound) is now fixed in firmware. Root insight: force a **real Type-C detach** by holding CC fully open long enough (~1.5 s, past tCCDebounce) before the reset — then the post-reset dead-battery Rd re-engages and the host sees a fresh attach. To open CC you must drop BOTH device-side Rd sources (UCPD `CCENABLE`+`ANAMODE` cleared AND `PWR_UCPDR.UCPD_DBDIS=1`) while leaving the TCPP03 EN (PB5) HIGH — driving PB5 low is the trap (it puts the TCPP03 in dead-battery mode = Rd presented, opposite of open; that's why the earlier 200 ms + PB5-low attempts failed). New `hw::usb_hw::cc_open_then_reset()`; wired into `cmd_fw_commit` (reboot into new firmware) + `cmd_fw_begin::arm_wipe_and_reset` (reboot into wiped device). `hw::tzic` stays on the fast `soft_disconnect_then_reset` (IRQ-context). HW-PROVEN via the `fwup-transport-hw-iwdg` wipe-trigger: kernel dmesg logged `USB disconnect` → `new full-speed USB device number N` (1209:7051) re-attach with NO replug, device stable after; observed across multiple runs (device 9→10→11). Latency ≈ 20-25 s (dominated by device boot in the e2e build; production boot is faster). Committed on the dmesg/usbwatch evidence — a single clean automated full-test PASS was rig-limited (the bench USB transport degraded into a flaky heavy-BEGIN hang mid-session, proven NOT cc_open-related: the cc_open-less baseline hangs identically). Re-run `make fwup-transport-hw-iwdg` on a healthy rig for the green checkmark. |
| 2026-05-28 | Evaluated Trezor no-reset FW handoff — REJECTED, we're already at parity | Prompted by the #26 USB-C warm-reset work ("is Trezor's branch-not-reset way better?"). Read `/home/nicola/repos/trezor-firmware`: on **STM32U5** Trezor's firmware→bootloader transition is `NVIC_SystemReset()` (`bootutils.c:161`); only the bootloader→firmware leg is a `jump_to_next_stage()` branch (`bootloader/main.c:551`); the direct `jump_to_vectbl` firmware→verifier path is `#elif STM32F4`-only (they reset, not warm-branch, across the firmware↔verifier boundary on U5 — TrustZone state makes a warm branch unsafe). Their UCPD code is just `HAL_PWREx_DisableUCPDDeadBattery()` (`startup_init.c:259`, == our `UCPD_DBDIS`) with no CCENABLE/ANAMODE/TCPP03 → passive fixed-Rd CC, vs our UCPD-sink + TCPP03. **We already match the U5 pattern**: commit → `sys_reset` → FSBL → branch into the new A/B slot; both re-verify signature every boot. So it's security PARITY, not "we beat Trezor." The only "save-the-reset" variant — a runtime direct slot-A→slot-B branch — was rejected because it bypasses the FSBL per-boot re-verify + FSBL-rooted measured-boot fingerprint (commit `7d0ec47`), i.e. strictly less secure than our current design. The warm-reset re-enumeration gap was purely CC hardware and is already solved by `cc_open_then_reset` (#26). Net: no architectural change. See memory `trezor-fw-handoff-parity`. |
| 2026-05-28 | §18 RDI timer — RE-SCOPED to NOT-MOTIVATED (emulation measurement) | The §18 "always-on RDI" item was gated on first measuring the three non-sign secret CPU paths. Completed the emulation half in `tools/sca/` (rainbow/lascar `mem_address` TVLA, 600 traces, fresh on current tree): AES-GCM entropy wrap (`sca_aesgcm_wrap`), SAES-CMAC(DHUK) KDF wrapper (`sca_saes_cmac`/`sca_saes_kdf_one_block` ×4), and a NEW dual-SE XOR-reconstruction probe (`sca_dual_se_xor`, mirror of `dual_se.rs::xor_32`, added to `kdf_target` + `leakage_seed_derivation.py`) — **all `max\|t\| = 0.00` → flat**. Two facts retire RDI for these paths: (1) the address channel (the only multi-trace channel emulation can test) is already flat on all three; (2) the residual is a value-channel leak that is *single-trace* (boot-once ops, no chosen-input averaging surface), and RDI misaligns *many* traces — it does nothing against single-trace SPA/template. Same disqualifier kills the cheaper per-op `wait_random()` middle step (same mechanism). Consistent with the framework's record: every real single-trace leak (F-22 wordlist, F-24 display, F-23 recovery) was fixed with constant-time code, never RDI. Decision: do NOT build the RDI subsystem. On-silicon value-channel confirmation on these three paths stays tracked under §18b (`sca-trigger`); if it ever shows an exploitable leak, the fix is constant-time/masked code on that op, not a global timer fighting the NS-world USB stack. Ticked §18 RDI `[x]` RE-SCOPED. |
| 2026-05-28 | SE050 silicon-findings closure — A1/A2/A3 documented + harness fixes applied | First on-silicon stress run (commit 67f3bfdb, 6 PASS / 11 FAIL) surfaced three real chip behaviors and four pre-existing harness bugs. None breach the confidentiality invariant — all failures are DoS or test-wrapper assumption breakage; see `docs/secure-elements/se050-silicon-findings.md`. **A1**: `ReadObjectAttributes` is NOT policy-gate-independent on B-U585I-IOT02A — returns SW=0x6986 on a user-PIN-gated UserID with the standard two-entry policy. Doc retracted at `secure/src/se050/mod.rs:485-510`; production `pin_attempt_count_raw` returns `None` and the boot-time reconcile silently skips the SE050 leg (OPTIGA + MCU page-124 still reconcile). **A2**: Failed `write_userid` UPDATE delete-then-creates; chip refuses the create but the delete is already through — any caller with valid SCP03 can DoS-destroy a UserID via one APDU without satisfying any policy. Accepted under the confidentiality-only threat model (BIP-39 backup is the recovery path); `audit_admin_cannot_rotate_user_pin` rewritten to assert silicon truth (refused + destroyed + attacker PIN unusable). **A3**: Failed session ops leave the chip in a session-pending state; subsequent non-session APDUs (`check_exists`, attribute reads) return SW=0x6982 until `Se050::reinit()`. Mitigated in `StressCtx::open_user_session` (reinit-on-failed-verify) + inline in `userid_no_admin_delete`. **S-7d closure**: `apdu.rs::verify_session` now maps both SW=0x6986 and SW=0x6982 to `AuthMethodBlocked` (silicon-observed on the 4th wrong PIN against `max_attempts=3` UserID); replaces the documented-by-deduction prediction. **Harness fixes**: `object_extended_lc_boundary` payload cap dropped 1024→960 B (fits inside the 1024-B ApduBuf with TLV overhead), unchaining cascade failures in `scp03_wtx_endurance` and `trng_quality_basic`. PIN-counter tests P1/P2/P4/P5 reworked: P1 + P2 use behavioral burn-arithmetic inference instead of attribute-read snapshots; P4 repurposed and renamed `pin_attribute_read_refused_on_user_userid` as an A1 silicon regression validator; P5 drops the `(0,0)` marker assertions and relies on the 100-burn loop + a post-burn correct-PIN sanity. Re-run pending: `make se050-stress-destructive` to confirm 17 PASS. |
| 2026-06-10 | CowSwap ZK clear-signing amount-overflow forgery FIXED (circuit + native, both sides) | LLM-found, PoC-verified critical soundness bug (`docs/security/vulns/VULN-cowswap-zk-amount-overflow.md`): `FormatTrimmedAmount` (`circuits/lib/format.circom`) computed `scaled <== raw_amount * scale_factor` in the BLS12-381 scalar field with `raw_amount` bounded only to `< 2²⁵⁴`; the product reached ~2³¹⁴ and wrapped r, so a malicious companion could produce a VALID Groth16 proof (honest circuit + real pinned VK — an under-constrained-circuit forgery, not a verifier/toxic-waste bug) where the trusted UI shows `0.2000 USDC` while the signed `sellAmount` is ~2.6e70 USDC, draining the wallet. **Fixed both sides:** (1) circuit — `Num2Bits(190)` range-check on `raw_amount` before the multiply in BOTH `FormatTrimmedAmount` and `circuits/aave_v3/formatting.circom :: FormatAmount` (`2¹⁹⁰·10¹⁸ < r` over ℤ ⟹ no wrap; lossless, max displayable ≈ 2⁹³); (2) firmware — `amount_within_field_safe_bound()` / `RAW_AMOUNT_FIELD_SAFE_BITS=190` in `secure/src/tx/eip712/cowswap/mod.rs`, called in `verify.rs` step 1b to reject canonical sell/buyAmount ≥ 2¹⁹⁰ before trusting the ZK readable — closes the class on-device even against a future circuit regression. **Regenerated (cowswap):** rebuilt the cowswap_eip712_order zkey/VK with **pot16** (circuit is 17.7k constraints — exceeds the pot14 the repo pins; even the pre-fix circuit did, so the committed zkey was always built out-of-band with pot16), re-pinned `circuit_final.zkey` + `secure/data/vks/cowswap_eip712_order.vk.bin` (`sha256 e0de34d8…`), `cargo run -p dbgen` rotated `VK_DB_ROOT` `0086…`→`3cda5293…` (+ `nonsecure/src/vk_db.bin`); `ERC20_POSEIDON_ROOT` unchanged. Fresh proof verifies end-to-end against the new VK. Fixed the stale `gen_cowswap_eip712_e2e_vector.js` (was missing the v3.1 `is_sub_precision` inputs, 415 vs 417). **Tests:** native `extra_tests.rs` (PoC 254-bit amount rejected, 2¹⁹⁰ boundary, 10²⁸ max-displayable accepted) + circuit-level `docs/security/cowswap-zk-poc/run_overflow_negative_test.sh` (forged witness fails witness-gen, benign passes). 2007/2008 secure host tests pass (the 1 failure — `usb_hw … negative_usb_hw_raw_volatile_only…` — is pre-existing and unrelated). **Ship-blocker tracked:** aave `FormatAmount` source is fixed but its VK was NOT regenerated (would invalidate the upstream-ZKlarity proof in the host Groth16 tests; needs the `../zk_clear_signing` witness + `tools/export_zk_constants.js`); regenerate the aave VK against the fixed circuit before production. Files: `circuits/lib/format.circom`, `circuits/aave_v3/formatting.circom`, `circuits/test/format_trimmed_overflow_test.circom`, `circuits/scripts/gen_cowswap_eip712_e2e_vector.js`, `circuits/circuits.json`, `circuits/cowswap/eip712_order/circuit_final.zkey`, `secure/src/tx/eip712/cowswap/{mod,verify,extra_tests}.rs`, `secure/data/vks/cowswap_eip712_order.vk.bin`, `secure/src/db_roots.rs`, `nonsecure/src/vk_db.bin`, `docs/security/vulns/VULN-cowswap-zk-amount-overflow.md`, `docs/security/cowswap-zk-poc/`. |

| 2026-06-10 | ERC-7730 walker slot-confusion clear-signing forgery FIXED (`docs/security/vulns/VULN-erc7730-walker-slot-confusion.md`) | LLM-found, proof-test-verified trusted-display divergence: the on-device calldata path walker computed a head-word slot by SUMMING logical field ordinals × 32, correct only when every preceding ABI field is one 32-byte word. A field preceded by a multi-word static type (fixed array `T[N]`, non-leading static tuple) resolved to a DIFFERENT head word than the EVM decodes → OLED shows a benign recipient/amount while the contract executes on the attacker's, no fault injection, no warning banner. Latent in the current single-word corpus (masked by luck; Uniswap `exactInputSingle` tuple is leading + single-word), structurally reachable the moment a normal `T[N]`/non-leading-tuple descriptor is curated. **Fixed at the source + fenced so the class cannot silently recur (4 closures):** (1) `dbgen::erc7730::compile_structured_contract_path` (new) emits `FieldIdx` ops whose summed args equal the field's true ABI **head-word** offset via a new recursive `static_head_words` width helper (`T[N]`=N words, static tuple=Σ members, dynamic=1 offset word); the on-device walker is unchanged — its sum is now correct by construction. Context-aware: EIP-712 `encodeData` (one word/member) and `@`/`$` roots keep the prior ordinal/keccak-discriminator encoding — only the `#` contract-calldata root uses ABI widths, so every existing descriptor (incl. the Uniswap nested tuple) compiles byte-identically. (2) dbgen **refuses to pin** a hazardous descriptor — hard error on array index/slice, dynamic-tuple descent, dynamic or multi-word-static terminal field, or a name absent from the signature (keccak fallback retained ONLY for `@`/`$` envelope discriminators). (3) IR `SCHEMA_VER` `0x01→0x02` (calldata `FieldIdx` changed meaning); `Erc7730Ir::parse` strict-rejects old slot-confusable descriptors. (4) On-device head-bound guard: each format header carries `static_head_words`; `head_bounded_body` (`secure/src/tx/display/erc7730/mod.rs`) truncates the body to the static head so any out-of-head slot fails the walker bounds check (rejected, never silently rendered). On-chain verifier, `pk_root`, CREATE2 addresses unaffected; `ERC7730_DESCRIPTORS_ROOT` (prod `99a8a25a…` + e2e) regenerated via `cargo run -p dbgen`, `gen-erc7730-descriptors --check` in sync. **Tests:** `formatters.rs` proof module flipped `walker_slot_confusion_proof`→`walker_slot_confusion_fixed` (walker reads EVM-decoded word; head-bound guard rejects out-of-head/short-head); dbgen golden-value unit tests for multi-word-array/non-leading-tuple/nested-tuple/dynamic-predecessor/EIP-712-ordinal/`@`-discriminator + array-index/unknown-name/dynamic-target/multi-word-target refusals. Seed corpus recompiles + round-trips; firmware builds clean for thumbv8m; 2013/2014 secure host tests pass (the 1 failure — `usb_hw … negative_usb_hw_raw_volatile_only…` — is pre-existing/unrelated, the in-progress MMIO refactor). Files: `dbgen/src/erc7730.rs`, `pqsigner-erc7730/src/ir.rs`, `secure/src/tx/display/erc7730/{mod,formatters}.rs`, `secure/src/db_roots.rs`, `secure/data/erc7730.review.txt`, `tools/companion-stub/erc7730_db{,_e2e}.bin`, `docs/security/vulns/VULN-erc7730-walker-slot-confusion.md`. |

Implemented generic multiSend clear-signing, fail-closed at every step (a DELEGATECALL is never blind-signed): (1) **allowlist** — op=1 accepted ONLY against the 3 canonical `MultiSendCallOnly` deployments (v1.3.0 canonical/eip155 + v1.4.1, verified against safe-deployments; (3) **strict decoder** (new host-testable `secure/src/tx/eip712/safe/multi_send.rs`): canonical ABI framing only (offset==0x20, exact total, zero padding), packed-record iterator with exact-consumption, per-record `operation==0` (no nested delegatecall), 1..=6 records, ≤1 presign-claiming record (one zk_v3 binds one record); (5) **handler gate** — one shared `multisend_sign_gate` (verdict + exact page-budget check, refuses `msend too long` instead of truncating — a record the user never sees is the attack class) called by single §7d + batch render loop; (6) **render** — extracted the per-kind inner arms into `append_inner_kind_pages` (single-call byte-identical) and loop records through them with divider pages ("MSend rec i/N" + target), per-record value pages, per-record erc20 metadata address-match, honest `Op: MultiSend` row (was hardcoded "Op: Call"), pinned-relayer "CoW VaultRelayer" spender label, CoW record renders the standard banner+order body; `MAX_PAGES` 22→24 (+ scaffold mirror + atomic 2-page pre-check in `append_fingerprint_page`); (7) **erc20 metadata routing** — §7a + batch ERC20 arm + picker accept record-level address matches so the approve record renders with symbol/decimals. | 2026-06-18 | CoW clear-signing: on-device ERC-20 decode replaces the Groth16/Poseidon registry (lifts the 256-token cap) | Every valid ERC-20 token (symbol ≤64 on-device, decimals ≤18) now gets full "SELL 0.2 USDC for at least 0.0004 WETH" CoW rendering, with NO token-count cap, by decoding both swap legs on-device against the firmware-pinned SHA-256 `ERC20_DB_ROOT` (2^32-leaf Merkle DB) — the same machinery the ERC-20 transfer clear-sign path uses. The CoW v3 Groth16 circuit + Poseidon `ERC20_POSEIDON_ROOT` registry are RETIRED: they were a display-formatting offload, not a trust anchor — the GPv2Order `canonical` is bound to the signed setPreSignature calldata natively by the keccak `cross_check_setpresig_calldata` orderDigest compare (unchanged), which is what the pre-existing AddrOnly fallback already relied on. Trailer kind 3 repurposed: payload is now `canonical(204) [|| sell_len(u16)||sell_bundle || buy_len(u16)||buy_bundle]`; each present leg's bundle is Merkle-verified on-device and its `(contract, chain_id)` cross-checked vs the canonical leg token+chain before symbol/decimals reach the OLED; a missing/garbage/mismatched bundle DEGRADES that leg to AddrHex (raw addr + uint256 hex), never a reject. New `CowLeg{Decoded|AddrHex}` result type threaded through the direct, Safe-wrapped, multiSend and batch CoW render paths; `verify` module ungated (no longer pulls `crate::zk`) so the trailer→cross-check→leg-decode pipeline now has host unit coverage. Side benefit: the in-circuit scale-factor-multiply that caused the amount-overflow + bytepack-non-binding vuln classes, and the pot16 trusted-setup dependency, are gone with the circuit. Kept intact: Aave v1 2-pub ZK path, `poseidon` primitive, `amount_within_field_safe_bound` (still used by the Aave path), `cowswap_set_pre_signature` VK. Regenerated `VK_DB_ROOT` (1380fc86…→0531197f…) via dbgen after dropping the cowswap_eip712_order VK. Existing AddrOnly e2e scenarios stay byte-compatible (204-byte trailer → both legs AddrHex). Validation: secure firmware builds (QEMU mock-se AND full dual-SE hardware-hardened feature set), nonsecure builds, `cargo test -p sphincs-tz-secure` 2088 passed (8 new decode-path tests), `cargo run -p dbgen` clean. Files: `proto/src/lib.rs`, `secure/src/tx/eip712/cowswap/{verify.rs,mod.rs}`, `secure/src/tx/eip712/cowswap_display.rs`, `secure/src/tx/display/{mod.rs,safe_display.rs,primitives.rs}`, `secure/src/nsc/{cmd_sign_userop.rs,batch_trailers.rs}`, `nonsecure/src/usb/commands.rs`, `secure/src/zk/mod.rs`, `dbgen/src/{erc20.rs,main.rs}`, `secure/data/vks.json`, `circuits/circuits.json`, deleted `circuits/cowswap/eip712_order/` + `circuits/generated/erc20_poseidon_tree.json`. |

| 2026-06-30 | ERC-7730 enum formatter (FormatOp 0x08) end-to-end + descriptor-pack expansion + CI drift gate | Lit up the `enum` formatter — the host (dbgen `encode_enum_table` + `format:"enum"` accept + `PARAM_ENUM_REF` emit) and crate (`params.rs` `enum_ref` parse) halves were already built; only the device renderer + a matching table decoder were missing, and **no descriptor had ever referenced an enum** so the whole path was unexercised. NEW `pqsigner-erc7730/src/render/enums.rs::lookup_enum_label` decodes the interned value→label table (`[u8 count]` then per-entry `[8B BE u64 key][u8 len][ascii label]`): strict bounds/ASCII (malformed ⇒ Reject), value-wider-than-u64 ⇒ None, unknown-value ⇒ None ⇒ caller declines-to-blind (audit M-7: never render the bare index), full-table validation even after a match; 9 unit tests + 2 Kani harnesses (`enum_single_entry_value_sound` value-soundness over a symbolic value/key + `enum_lookup_panic_free` panic/OOB-freedom over a symbolic pool) — **both VERIFICATION SUCCESSFUL** via `cargo kani -p pqsigner-erc7730`. The high-24-bytes-zero key gate is read as integer windows (memcpy + compares, not a per-byte loop) — cleaner than `iter().all()` and keeps the CBMC unwind small. Device `render_enum` (was an unconditional `Reject`) now resolves `Slot32`→label, declining-to-blind on container/missing-ref/unknown-value/over-long-label. First descriptor to exercise it: Aave V3 `borrow(...)` `interestRateMode` (the previously declared-but-dead enum). **Pack expansion:** added `wsteth.json` (wrap/unwrap/approve/transfer, fixed-token via metadata constants). **Host RENDER tests** (the dbgen round-trip + Kani never render a page — the flagged blind spot): `interestRateMode=2`→"variable", `=7`→decline-to-blind, wstETH `wrap`→"Sign: Wrap stETH". Regenerated `ERC7730_DESCRIPTORS_ROOT`; **wired the drift gate** (`xtask gen-erc7730-descriptors --check`) + the dbgen round-trip into CI `host-tests` so a stale root / un-regenerated catalog can't merge. Secure suite **2083/0**; thumbv8m QEMU (`mock-se`) + real-HW (`dual-se,stm32u585,usb`) build clean. The **dynamic-array walker** (the other half of the "1 and 3" ask) is deliberately **FIREWALLED to a design doc** `docs/erc7730-dynamic-array-walker-design.md` — it relaxes the `head_bounded_body` slot-confusion defense (`VULN-erc7730-walker-slot-confusion.md`, the reason for the 0x01→0x02 schema bump) and needs its own adversarial review + Kani harnesses + VULN re-validation; recommended foundation is reusing the hardened `typed_call::abi::walk` rather than hand-rolling tail-following. Next remaining lever = the dynamic-array walker (~38: 1inch swaps, aave multicall, batch deposits). | 2026-06-30 | ERC-7730 dynamic-array walker (sole `<arg>.[]` render-all) — the firewalled security-critical build | Closed the highest-remaining registry-coverage lever (~38 descriptors: 1inch swaps, aave multicall, batch deposits) AND the security-critical one: the on-device renderer now follows a sole top-level dynamic array's calldata TAIL to show EVERY element (Lido `requestWithdrawals(uint256[] _amounts, address _owner)`), instead of blind-signing. This is the slot-confusion ("show X, sign Y") attack surface, so it got design-first + adversarial-review treatment. **Design (`docs/erc7730-dynamic-array-walker-design.md`):** a pre-impl deep-dive corrected the scope — single-index `arr[i]`/`[-1]` is UNSAFE (hides the other elements = the array-tail-hiding HIGH), so v1 is **ArrayAll** (render all, page-budget-bounded, decline-on-overflow). **Implementation:** dbgen gate `compile_array_all_path` (sole-dynamic-arg, static-primitive element, refuses single-index/slice/nested/non-sole-dynamic/EIP-712-array) emits `FieldIdx(offset-slot)+ArrayAll`; device `resolve_array` follows the tail reusing `walk`'s Kani-proven `read_offset_word`/`read_length_word` + TWO exact-placement equalities (`offset==head_end` AND `offset+32+count*32==body.len()`) that pin the array as the whole tail → byte-identical-or-stricter than the EVM decoder. **Scalar path BYTE-IDENTICAL** (only `render_array` gets the full body; every other field stays on `head_bounded_body`; the `walker_slot_confusion_fixed` tests pass unchanged). **Verification (advisor-blessed "make super sure"):** (1) a 5-lens **adversarial-review workflow** (WYSIWYS / element-hiding / panic / slot-confusion-reval / dbgen-gate) → verdict **SHIP, 0 confirmed breaks**, every attack refuted by the catching check; (2) the **`walk` differential** (resolver agrees exactly with the Kani-proven decoder); (3) an **11-case adversarial body suite** (offset/length/count tampering, top-28-nonzero, non-32-align, over-cap, short-head — all decline-to-blind, no panic); (4) **Kani proof** — `resolve_array` extracted to the host crate (`pqsigner-erc7730/src/render/array.rs`) + `resolve_array_panic_free_and_in_bounds` VERIFICATION SUCCESSFUL (panic/OOB/overflow-free + in-bounds element span over symbolic body). **Hardening from the review (closed the 2 depth-1 SPOFs):** dbgen now refuses array ops on the EIP-712/`@`/`$` branch (not just CTX_CONTRACT); `path_ends_with_array_all` validates the program STRUCTURE not just `last()==0x24` (a scalar FieldIdx low-byte 0x24 no longer misroutes). No SCHEMA_VER bump (ArrayAll 0x24 is a reserved op; old firmware declines-to-blind). Secure suite **2088/0**, crate 78/0, dbgen roundtrip 9/0, thumbv8m QEMU+real-HW clean, drift in sync, Kani SUCCESSFUL. Residual follow-ups (review non-blocking): page-budget-mid-loop test; element-type/FormatOp coupling gate (already depth-2 via EVM-revert + overflow banner). | 2026-06-30 | CMD_OFFCHAIN_SYNC page-123 exhaustion → permanent-brick (HIGH availability) — FIXED | Untrusted companion could spray ≥512 distinct seed-independent `(account,chain,slot)` tuples via `CMD_OFFCHAIN_SYNC` (PIN-only, no confirm) → fill page-123 with >256 distinct slot keys → compaction fail-closes forever → every sign path returns `InternalError`; seed-survivable permanent brick (only JTAG reflash recovers). Two-layer fix: **L1** confirm-gate new-slot SYNC (`cmd_offchain_sync` requires trusted-display `confirm()` when `!offchain_count_is_registered`; new `tx/display/offchain_sync.rs` 2-page "SYNC COUNTER?"; mirrors `cmd_sign_offchain` MEDIUM-3 defer-write-until-after-confirm). **L2** structural distinct-slot cap `offchain_state::MAX_DISTINCT_SLOTS=128` + pure `may_create_distinct_slot`, enforced at the single flash chokepoint `write_entry` (new `distinct_slot_count_capped`, ~1KB scan, new-slot branch only; sits above `compact_page` so existing slots always re-compact) + mock backend table-size parity → `128×3=384≤512 ∧ 128≤256` ⇒ compaction provably can never fail ⇒ un-wedgeable by any caller. NO eviction/erase (would reset the non-evictable `userop_sigs` few-time tally — HIGH-1/F3). Graceful refuse-not-brick at the cap; device-lifetime budget on distinct tuples (struct. max 170). Tests: 5 new (exhaustive policy, structural const-proof, mock cap pin, flash wiring pin, handler confirm-gate + ordering pin); secure suite **2016/0**, `stm32u585` debug check + release+link HW build clean. | 2026-07-01 | ERC-7730 general ABI resolver — C1/C2/C3 coverage increments (FollowOffset) | Critical reassessment (user: "don't stay married to a subpar implementation... support as many protocols as possible") found the device resolver was static-head-only — the ARCHITECTURE, not shape complexity/reviewability, was the coverage ceiling (`send(string)`/`mint(bytes)` declined solely because the value is in the tail; my earlier "not human-reviewable / 100% isn't the goal" framing was partly a rationalization — corrected by a 94-descriptor blocker-histogram workflow, `docs/erc7730-coverage-blocker-analysis-2026-07.md`). Unlock = ONE capability, **`PathOp::FollowOffset` (0x25)** + a region-tracking interpreter (`pqsigner-erc7730/render/resolve.rs::resolve_structured`, Kani-harnessed) that follows an ABI offset word at a signature-fixed head slot into the tail → reads the SAME position the contract decodes (WYSIWYS by construction; dbgen already computes correct width-aware head slots, so it just emits FollowOffset where it used to reject). Landed in 3 committed increments (advisor-gated: differential value-equality vs an independent decode, NOT just Kani panic-freedom, since `walk` declines tuples and can't be the oracle): **C1** dynamic bytes/string (string→ASCII; opaque→length+loud, never a hex wall) `601057a3`; **C2** dynamic-tuple members `d4c24cad`; **C3** multi-dynamic — scalar/bytes free via C1, arrays via `resolve_array_multi` (a `TailPlacement::MultiInTail` sibling relaxing the two exact-placement equalities to bounds; `resolve_array` stays byte-identical + Kani-proven — generalize-by-ADDITION) + a `Unit` array arm `2e714e13`. **Corpus 776 → 806 leaves** (root `61a0e379`); secure 2031/0; drift in sync; thumbv8m QEMU+real-HW clean. Synthetic fixtures for the differentials: `synthetic-dynamic-tuple.json` (0x..0B), `synthetic-multi-array.json` (0x..0C). FOLLOW-UPS: C2 nested field-GROUPS (Morpho `marketParams`/sub-`fields` — separate dbgen parser feature, the Morpho Blue unlock); run `make kani` (harnesses added); 5-lens adversarial-review-workflow for the relaxed multi-array placement; HARD-slice (53 DEX swap fns) is a separate higher-risk byte-extraction engine, deliberately not built. | 2026-07-01 | ERC-7730 follow-up — nested field-GROUP flattening unlocks Morpho Blue (the C2 tuple-nav headline) | The C2 increment covered FLAT dynamic-tuple paths but not the ERC-7730 nested field-GROUP syntax (`{path:"#.marketParams", fields:[...]}`) Morpho Blue uses. `FieldDef` had no `fields` sub-array → serde dropped the members → the group reached the compiler as a bare multi-word static tuple → rejected → Morpho blind-signed. Fix is a pure **dbgen parser** feature (`flatten_field_groups`, `7325e9f0`): expand a group into per-member leaf fields with combined paths (`#.marketParams`+`loanToken` → `#.marketParams.loanToken`) ONCE up front (before enum pre-pass AND compile loop). The combined paths ride the EXISTING width-aware `compile_structured_contract_path` (already does static-tuple descent + the slot-confusion fix) + the SAME completeness/visibility/2-level gates — flattening is purely SYNTACTIC, bypasses no gate: a group over a dynamic tuple/array-of-tuple/>2-levels compiles to a path the gates reject → drops to blind-sign. Morpho's `marketParams` is a STATIC tuple (4 addr + lltv) so members sit at fixed head slots 0..5 and every post-tuple arg lands width-aware: `assets` at head word 5 (not ordinal 1), `supply`/`repay` `bytes data` at word 8 → FieldIdx(8)+FollowOffset (C1). mhaas's `visible:"never"` gate SATISFIED (oracle/irm shown) → no allowlist. **Corpus 706 → 708 (+2, Morpho mainnet+Base ONLY, zero regression** — flatten is identity on flat fields); the other 9 nested-group registry descriptors (paraswap/uniswap/flare) correctly stay declined (dynamic/array-of-tuple/EIP-712). Root `f537aa0b`→`3203b873`; e2e+other roots unchanged; drift in sync. Tests: dbgen +7 (per-member+post-tuple slots, bytes-after-tuple FollowOffset at word 8, flatten-before-gates, purely-additive, array-of-tuple/no-path/depth-bound rejects) = 113/0 + roundtrip 10/0; secure +1 `morpho_borrow_nested_tuple_group_renders_exact_values` (drives the REAL registry leaf; WYSIWYS differential value-equality every member+post-tuple arg from its exact head-word slot) = 2033/0; thumbv8m QEMU + stm32u585(ui-lcd,usb) builds.
